- **Look** *(M16, GEN-R25)*: the same rubber sheet, drawn as continuous braille curves: every mesh line is a polyline through its projected samples on a `Dots` lattice (no box-drawing glyphs, so no stair steps or crossing glyphs), with more samples per mesh edge (4 along x, 5 along y, about 3–4 dots apart; stored as `f32` for the live-heap ceiling) so the late, short waves stay resolved (the phase moves under 1.4 rad between samples at the shortest wave shown) and the receding lines bend smoothly. The sheet is evaluated once per drawn frame in `prepare` (GEN-R20). *(M16 round 3)* Each mesh segment is drawn in pieces of about 2 dots, each coloured by the wave phase `cos 2(φ − Φ(t − r/c))` at its middle (interpolated along the shorter way round between samples): only crests are tinted, `Wave` 230 where the phase cosine is ≥ 0.8 and the amplitude `f(r)·s(a)` is at least 0.35 of the largest on the sheet now, so each crest reads as one thin spiral ring round the binary; troughs and everything else are a quiet `Structure` 70–110 stepped by nearness; no tint while the peak amplitude is below 0.05 (after the merger). The phase rather than the strain is thresholded: with the strain at 0.8 of the peak the `1/√r` fall-off would keep every crest within `r ≈ 1.3` of the binary. Captions `inspiral`, `merger` (from `a = 0.45 a₀`, where the strain tapers, to `a = 0`) and `merged` (the remnant; the model emits no ringdown, so the act is not called one). The binary is two small dot discs, `Sun` (the heavier) and `Hot`, each with an orbit trail: its positions over the last 0.7 orbit, laid on the sheet, in three dimmer steps. **Chirp to merger**: the Peters inspiral now runs to `a = 0` at `τc` (the phase stays finite) and is followed by `POST = 4 s` with one merged mass at the centre while the last wave train runs out; the strain factor `a₀/a` is tapered to 0 between `a = 0.45 a₀` and `0.25 a₀`, where the waves get shorter than the mesh can show; the next binary fades in over 1 s at the start of its cycle (its masses grow while the merged mass shrinks, so the wells cross-fade). The phase is continuous across cycles. Supersedes the inks and the reset at `0.55 a₀` above.
- **Acceptance**: `SPT-A1` for a single mass the depth is radially symmetric and strictly increases in magnitude toward the mass; `SPT-A2` the binary keeps its centre of mass fixed and its separation constant (non-inspiral mode); `SPT-A3` the wave term has period π in the azimuth and its phase at radius r equals the source phase at `t − r/c`; `SPT-A4` *(new, M16)* a frame is braille only (no box-drawing glyphs), shows `Wave` crests (*changed, M16 round 3*: no `Cold`, troughs are untinted; *M17*: the sheet layer has no `Cold`, the cloud's negative lobe is `Cold`), the masses (*M17*: `Star` rims and `Hot` trails), and most cells blank; `SPT-A5` *(new, M16)* the separation falls monotonically to 0 at `τc`, stays 0 (one merged mass, no wave emitted) for `POST`, and the next cycle starts at `a₀` with the phase continuous; the strain factor is 0 below `0.25 a₀`; `SPT-A6` *(new, M16)* each mass's trail lies on its past orbit: the trail's points are the mass's positions at earlier times of the same cycle; `SPT-A7` *(new, M16)* at 200×50, 5 s in, every 8-connected group of ≥ 6 `Wave` crest cells away from the near zone (mean sheet radius ≥ 0.5, the cells mapped back onto the sheet) lies in a band: the spread of its radii is < 0.15 of their mean, and at least 3 such bands are seen; `SPT-A8` *(new, M16)* the caption is `inspiral` through the inspiral and the next pair's fade-in, `merger` from `0.45 a₀` to the merger, and `merged` after it; `SPT-A9` *(new, M16)* at 200×50, 5 s in, fewer than 25 % of the grid cells are tinted, and along the radius (sectors of 30°, radial bins 0.08 wide from r = 0.4 to 1.8, cells mapped back onto the sheet) the tinted share swings from below 0.1 to above 0.6 in at least 8 of the 12 sectors.

---
- **Look** *(M17, GEN-R26, quadrupole cloud; the sheet and its ripples stay exactly as in M16)*: around the binary, a stipple cloud of the near-zone `h₊` quadrupole pattern on the sheet: dot probability ∝ `|cos 2(φ − Φ_orb)|` times a radial envelope (peaking a little outside the orbit, fading by ≈ 0.25 of the sheet width), so the cloud has the four lobes of a d-orbital; lobes are `Hot` where `h₊ > 0` and `Cold` where `h₊ < 0`; the pattern turns at twice the orbital frequency, and tightens and brightens through the chirp. The two black holes are tiny dark-cored dots with spiralling decaying trails coloured `Hot` → `Sun` by speed. At merger the lobes collapse into one small `Accent` remnant cloud that fades. The cloud is drawn on the sheet (projected with the same camera), never over grid lines outside its envelope.
- **Model (M17)**: a compact d-orbital jewel: keep probability `amp·cos²2(φ − Φ_orb)·x⁴e^{4(1−x)}·(1 − (r/cut)²)²`, `x = r/r₀`, `r₀ = 0.04 + 0.7a` (≈ 1.4× the lighter hole's orbit), `cut = 1.7 r₀` (≈ 0.15 of the sheet width at `a₀`), `amp = min(0.8(a₀/a)^0.4, 0.95)·fade`, so each lobe is a teardrop densest on its axis; levels 190, 255 above `cos²·E = 0.4`; evaluated once per cell, each dot with its own fixed threshold, on the plane of the sheet under the whole mass at the centre (so it does not wobble with every turn). Inside the cut the sheet yields: its segments there are `Structure` 45, untinted, and the cloud, holes and trails are the upper layer, so the cloud is the brightest thing at the centre; outside the cut the sheet is exactly M16's. Holes: dark-cored `Star` rims (255, 230) of radius 2 and 1.5 dots (remnant 2.5), with a 3.2-dot gap in the cloud; trails 0.8 orbit in 28 samples, level 225 → 60, `Hot`, `Sun` once `√(a₀/a) ≥ 1.3`. Remnant: `Accent` 235, `0.8·w·exp(−(r/0.12)²)`, cut 0.36, `w` 1 → 0 over 3 s after the merger. *Stillness*: the unchanged M16 sheet alone changes 10.1 % of cells per frame at t = 10–12 s (seed 42); with the M17 centre 10.3 % (lead's exception: ≤ 11 %).
- **Acceptance** *(M17)*: `SPT-A10` a cloud dot at sheet azimuth `φ` is `Hot` iff `cos 2(φ − Φ_orb) > 0` and `Cold` otherwise; `SPT-A11` the pattern phase `2Φ_orb` advances by `2Ω Δt`, so the lobes turn with the pair (the field at a point turned by `ΩΔt` equals the field before) and `h₊` at a fixed point oscillates at `2Ω`; the cloud tightens (`r₀` falls) and brightens (`amp` rises) through the chirp; `SPT-A12` outside the cloud envelope the grid's dots and inks equal a render without the cloud; `SPT-A13` after merger the cloud is a single `Accent` remnant that has faded to nothing before the loop restarts.
## Batch D — physics sims & narrative scenes (M7)
Shared helpers: `ode` (RK4), `timeline` + `ease` (CORE-R37..R39) plus `particles`, `grid`, `draw`. Narrative scenes loop forever: every phase has a fixed duration in simulated seconds, so a run is deterministic per seed and resizes never desynchronise the story.

### double_pendulum  (Forces & Waves, M7, M12, M15)
- **Intent**: chaos made visible — two double pendulums released from almost identical angles swing together, then diverge wildly, each tracing a glowing path.
- **Model**: exact Lagrangian equations for point masses `m₁, m₂` on massless rods `L₁, L₂` (rows, aspect-correct on screen), `g` scaled so the upper arm alone has a small-angle period of 2.2 s (the coupled slow mode is ≈ 2.78 s); physics runs in model units (`L₁ = 1`) with a separate screen scale, so motion is identical at any terminal size; both twins share one pivot; state `(θ₁, θ₂, ω₁, ω₂)` advanced with `ode::integrate` at `h ≤ 1/240 s`; the twin starts with `Δθ₂ = 10⁻³` rad; high-energy seeded initial angles (both arms above horizontal ± variety); trails of the lower bobs (`Trail`, ~2 s); a divergence readout (`|Δθ| e-folding`) and energy drift; restart every 45 s with new seeded angles.
- **Inks**: pendulum A `Cold`, B `Hot`, rods `Structure`, pivots `Text`, trails fading by age.
- **Acceptance**: `DP-A1` total energy drifts < 0.5 % over 20 s; `DP-A2` for small angles the two normal-mode frequencies match the linearised analytic values within 1 %; `DP-A3` from a seeded chaotic start the twins' separation (max so far — it saturates near π) grows ≥ 100× within 30 s; `DP-A4` rods keep their lengths exactly (bob positions derived from angles).

### mars_rover  (Voyages, M7, M12, M15)
- **Intent**: a Perseverance-style rover crossing rust-red dunes under a butterscotch sky: parallax ridges, dust devils, a stop to drill a sample and beam data to an orbiter, as the sol passes from day to dusk (blue Martian sunset).
- **Model**: world-x terrain from seeded fractal value noise (≥ 3 octaves) that is a pure function of world x (deterministic, continuous); 3 parallax layers; rocker–bogie suspension: 6 wheels each placed on the terrain surface, body height/pitch from the rocker and bogie pivots; a `Timeline` sol: drive (18 s) → stop & deploy arm (3 s) → drill with dust (4 s) → stow (2 s) → transmit to an orbiter crossing the sky (4 s) → drive … ; dust particles from wheels and drill (`ParticlePool`); 0–2 dust devils drifting; Phobos crossing fast; sky/sun tint changes over a slower 90 s sol cycle (solar elevation at Jezero's latitude; Phobos on its real synodic period, scaled). Pose is solved by Newton iteration on the three wheel-contact residuals (rigid links, body pitch = rocker angle); far-side wheels coincide with the near ones in the side view. Dust over solid fills lightens rather than replaces the cell (a "veil" compositor), since cells have no background colour.
- **Inks**: terrain `Hot` (near) → `Dim` (far layers), day haze `Sun` (low levels), blue sunset glow `Cold`, dusk band/orbiter `Accent`, dust devils `Hot`, rover body `Text`, wheels/arm `Structure`, dust `Particle`, signal `Wave`.
- **Acceptance**: `MR-A1` every wheel contact point lies on the terrain surface (within 0.5 row); `MR-A2` terrain height is a pure deterministic function of world x — identical across scrolls, resizes and chunk boundaries, and continuous (adjacent columns differ by ≤ a bound); `MR-A3` the sol timeline visits its phases in order with their durations and loops.

### voyager_journey  (Voyages, M7, M12, M15)
- **Intent**: Voyager 2's Grand Tour, 1977–1989 in 48 s: launch from Earth, gravity assists at Jupiter, Saturn, Uranus and Neptune with flyby flashes and captions, then out past the heliopause to a closing "Pale Blue Dot" moment.
- **Model**: heliocentric top-down map with a monotone radial compression (e.g. `ρ = R·log(1 + r/r₀)/log(1 + r_max/r₀)`, AU→rows) so Earth…Neptune all fit; planets on circular orbits with real mean motions and J2000-ish mean longitudes; real dates: launch 1977-08-20, Jupiter 1979-07-09, Saturn 1981-08-26, Uranus 1986-01-24, Neptune 1989-08-25; the spacecraft path is a smooth curve through the planets' positions at those dates (e.g. Hermite/polar interpolation in (r, φ) between encounter points with monotone r), time compression with slow-downs near encounters (via `ease`), a date/distance readout; 48 s mission + 8 s epilogue, then loop. Planet elements from JPL/Standish "Keplerian Elements for Approximate Positions" (J2000); the path is a monotone cubic Hermite (PCHIP) curve through each planet's model position on its encounter date (a shape, not a propagated trajectory). Captions sit at the top (Uranus/Neptune are near the bottom in the real geometry). The epilogue credits the Pale Blue Dot to Voyager 1 (which took it); the sight line from Voyager 2 is artistic licence. **Stylisation**: the path is interpolated through the real encounter geometry; the gravity assists the title names ("planets fling it on") are depicted, not integrated.
- **Inks**: Sun `Sun`, orbits `Dim`, planets `Planet`, spacecraft `Accent` with `Structure` trail, captions `Text`, flyby flash `Hot`.
- **Acceptance**: `VOY-A1` at each encounter date the spacecraft coincides with the planet (same model position, and within 0.5 cell on screen); `VOY-A2` planet angular speeds follow Kepler (period ratios `(a₁/a₂)^{3/2}`) and match real periods within 1 %; `VOY-A3` mission time → calendar date is monotone and prints the exact encounter dates at encounters; `VOY-A4` the radial mapping is strictly monotone and fits Neptune's orbit on screen.

### moon_landing  (Voyages, M7, M12, M15)
- **Intent**: Apollo 11's powered descent: the LM pitching over, throttling its descent engine above a cratered Mare Tranquillitatis, kicking up dust, "CONTACT LIGHT", "The Eagle has landed", then the flag and an earthrise.
- **Model**: 2-D point-mass LM with thrust `T ≤ T_max` along its attitude, `ṁ = −T/(I_sp g₀)` (I_sp 311 s), lunar `g = 1.62 m/s²`, scaled distances; a guidance law (e.g. gravity-turn then a constant-deceleration / polynomial guidance to a target point with touchdown speed ≤ 1 m/s) that always lands for the seeded start set; cratered terrain (seeded crater field on a gently rolling surface, pure function of x); dust `ParticlePool` when altitude < ~30 m (scaled) blown outward from beneath the engine; `Timeline`: descent (≈ 22 s) → touchdown & dust settles (4 s) → flag & caption (5 s) → earthrise hold (4 s) → loop with a new seeded approach; readout of altitude, vertical speed and fuel %, "CONTACT LIGHT" at probe contact. Guidance: P64 closed-form E-guidance to a gate 8 m above the site, then P66 sink-rate profile with position hold. "Touchdown" is footpad contact; the engine runs until then (a historical cut at CONTACT LIGHT would free-fall 1.7 m to ≈ 2.4 m/s and break ML-A1). Earth appears only in the earthrise phase.
- **Inks**: LM `Text`/`Structure`, flame `Hot`, dust `Particle`, terrain `Dim`/`Structure`, Earth `Cold`, captions `Text`.
- **Acceptance**: `ML-A1` for a seeded set of start conditions the guidance lands with vertical speed ≤ 1 m/s, horizontal ≤ 0.5 m/s and fuel ≥ 0; `ML-A2` with constant thrust the integrated velocity matches the rocket equation `Δv = I_sp g₀ ln(m₀/m) − g t` (vertical burn) within 0.5 %; `ML-A3` the LM never penetrates the terrain; `ML-A4` the timeline phases occur in order and loop.

## Batch E — scientific art: fields, gravity, chemistry & biology (M10)
Design: `docs/design/M10-scientific-art-batch.md`; ADR-0010 (layered raster: `raster::Dots` braille, `raster::Pixels` half-block with `Cell.bg`), ADR-0011 (colormap roles `Seq`/`Div`), ADR-0012 (simulation lattice decoupled from the display, `grid::lattice_for`).
New core helpers (CORE-R40..R59, RND-R6/R7): `raster::{Dots, Pixels, Density}`, `grid::{resample_bilinear, resample_nearest, lattice_for, SampleMap, laplacian9}`, `ode::{verlet_step, verlet_integrate, tridiag_solve}`, `geom::{Camera, Projector}`, `ink::{seq, div, soft_clip, seq_value, div_value}`, `text::StackText`, `draw::text_mode`, `particles::{SpatialHash, InverseCdf}`, `rng::SplitMix64`, `Category::{Biology, Chemistry}`; the post-M10 audit lifted more shared code into core (CORE-R60..R70); plus the existing `draw`, `particles`, `grid`, `ode`, `timeline`/`ease` and `geom` helpers.
The `gray_scott` upgrade is specified in place in Batch C above (`CHEM-A5`..`CHEM-A8`).

### action_potential  (Physics of Life, M10, M12, M15, M20)
- **Intent**: a nerve impulse made visible. A stylised neuron rests in the dark: soma, branching dendrites, a long unmyelinated axon and a fan of synaptic terminals, all drawn as dim membrane outlines. A stimulus fires the soma, and a bright pulse of depolarisation runs down the axon with a cool after-hyperpolarisation wake behind it. It lights the terminals, which release a small puff of transmitter. An oscilloscope inset, wired to the recording electrode by a faint dotted lead, draws the classic spike waveform at the moment the pulse passes the electrode. A looping protocol shows four things in turn: a single spike, a train that slows as it runs into its own refractory wake, two spikes annihilating in collision, and the all-or-none threshold.
- **Model**: Hodgkin & Huxley (1952) squid giant axon, in the modern rest = −65 mV convention (Dayan & Abbott 2001).
  - **Cable equation**: `C_m ∂V/∂t = (a / 2R_i) ∂²V/∂x² − ḡ_Na m³h (V − E_Na) − ḡ_K n⁴ (V − E_K) − ḡ_L (V − E_L) + I_stim`, with `C_m = 1 μF/cm²`, `ḡ_Na = 120`, `ḡ_K = 36`, `ḡ_L = 0.3 mS/cm²`, `E_Na = +50`, `E_K = −77` and `E_L = −54.387 mV`.
  - **Rates** (ms⁻¹, V in mV): `α_m = 0.1(V + 40)/(1 − e^{−(V+40)/10})`, `β_m = 4 e^{−(V+65)/18}`, `α_h = 0.07 e^{−(V+65)/20}`, `β_h = 1/(1 + e^{−(V+35)/10})`, `α_n = 0.01(V + 55)/(1 − e^{−(V+55)/10})`, `β_n = 0.125 e^{−(V+65)/80}`. All are scaled by `φ = 3^{(T − 6.3)/10}` with `T = 6.3 °C`, so `φ = 1`. The removable singularities at −40 and −55 mV go through an `x/(1 − e^{−x})` helper with a series branch.
  - **Geometry**: axon radius `a = 238 μm` and `R_i = 35.4 Ω·cm` (HH 1952) give `D = a/(2 R_i C_m) = 0.336 cm²/ms`. The cable is `L = 10 cm` in `N = 200` compartments (`Δx = 0.5 mm`, ≈ 1/14 of the 7 mm resting length constant), with sealed (no-flux) ends.
  - **Sub-step (10 μs)**: first a Rush–Larsen (1978) exponential update of m, h and n, which is exact for frozen V and stays in `[0, 1]`. Then backward-Euler V with the ionic conductances frozen (Hines 1984), solved as a tridiagonal M-matrix by the Thomas algorithm into preallocated scratch. This is unconditionally stable; explicit Euler would need a step ≤ 3.7 μs and RK4 ≤ 5 μs. Against a converged fine-grid run (Crank–Nicolson, Δx = 0.05 mm, 0.5 μs: 12.31 and 18.72 m/s) the velocity error is 0.4 % at 6.3 °C and 0.9 % at 18.5 °C.
  - **Clock**: it counts integer sub-steps (`h = 0.01 ms`, through an accumulator with 1e-9 slack), so how frames chop time cannot change results. Slow motion is 4 ms of axon time per simulated second (250×).
  - **Measured physiology** (converged): propagated peak +37.9 mV; after-hyperpolarisation minimum −75.9 mV; V > −40 mV for 2.3 ms at a point (a ≈ 2.9 cm footprint); conduction velocity 12.3 m/s at 6.3 °C and 18.7 m/s at 18.5 °C (HH computed 18.8 m/s and measured 21.2 m/s); threshold ≈ 90 μA/cm² for a 0.3 ms pulse over the first 0.25 cm.
  - **Protocol**: a looping `Timeline` of 18 s (= 72 ms). `single` 3 s: a soma pulse at 0.2 s. `train` 8 s: pulses at 3.0, 5.5 and 8.0 s, 10 ms apart. `collision` 4.5 s: soma and terminal end stimulated together at 13.0 s, the neurophysiologist's collision test. `threshold` 2.5 s: a 0.5× pulse at 16.0 s. Strong pulses are 270 μA/cm² (3× threshold) for 0.3 ms over the first (or last) 0.25 cm, and the weak pulse is 45 μA/cm².
  - **Refractory timing**: every strong pulse starts ≥ 9.5 ms after the last spike passed its site. That is beyond the ≈ 6–8 ms refractory limit, so every strong pulse fires, but inside the relative refractory period, so each train spike runs slower than the last (measured 10.9, 10.1 and 9.8 m/s, against 12.3 isolated). The model state runs continuously across loops, and the second loop repeats the first to within 0.01 s. The first loop starts from rest; every later one starts 20 ms after the collision, in its supernormal phase (at 3–7 cm `h` is ≈ 0.01 above and `n` ≈ 0.006 below their resting values), so its single spike runs 0.8 % faster: 12.36 against 12.26 m/s. Moving the weak pulse (15.2–17.0 s) does not change this. Later loops repeat one another to 1e-6 m/s.
  - **Weak pulse**: it lands on the after-hyperpolarisation left by the collision and lifts the soma to ≈ −61 mV (≈ −57 mV from rest). It spreads passively ≈ 1 cm and fades.
  - **Stimulus markers**: each pulse lights a stimulus marker for 0.25 s, at a level proportional to its amplitude, so the weak kick visibly happens. The marker is `>` left of the soma, and `<` just before the terminals for the far-end pulse.
  - **Stylisation**: the soma, dendrites and terminal arbor are drawn, not simulated; the real giant axon is a syncytium grown from the stellate ganglion. The soma shows V of compartment 0, and the terminal arbor fans out the last 0.8 cm of the cable.
  - **Vesicles**: at each 0 mV up-crossing at the cable end, every bouton emits 4 vesicle dots (`ParticlePool`, capacity 20 = 5 boutons × 4). They drift 1–2 cells across the cleft and fade within 0.4 s.
- **Motion**: at t = 0 the neuron rests, showing only its outline. The stimulus marker and soma flash at 0.2 s. By 1 s the pulse is a third of the way along the axon (GEN-R9). It reaches the terminals at ≈ 2.3 s, with a bouton flash and a vesicle puff. The trace scrolls continuously (a 20 ms = 5 s window) and draws each spike as the pulse passes the electrode (compartment 100, 5 cm, ≈ 1.1 s after the stimulus). In the train, each spike leaves the soma as the previous one reaches the terminals, and each is visibly slower. In the collision beat, two pulses converge and vanish near mid-axon at ≈ 14.1 s. In the threshold beat the marker blinks and a faint soma glow spreads about a centimetre and fades within ≈ 1 s. Nothing travels, and the trace stays flat. The weak kick lands 12 ms after the collision spike left the soma, on its after-hyperpolarisation (≈ −69 mV), so its half-threshold amplitude and the residual refractoriness both hold it below threshold; AP-A5 checks that the same pulse also fails from rest. The loop is 18 s.
- **Resize**: the simulation does not depend on size, so no model state is touched. Geometry is rebuilt from normalised parameters:
  - the soma centre and radius;
  - the axon path, stored as a per-pixel table of arclength `s`, with V linearly interpolated between compartments;
  - the dendrite tree. Its topology is drawn from `ctx.rng` on the first resize only and stored in a fixed array of ≤ 40 segments, each ending at a node `(angle, ρ)`: polar about the soma centre, ρ soma radii beyond the rim, drawn at `r + k·ρ`. The tree is radial and planar. Three primaries leave the rim radially within a ±1.45 rad cone pointing left: one up (−1.15 rad), one down (+1.15) and one at ±0.55 on a random side, each jittered by ±0.08, so the stimulus marker straight left stays clear. Each primary owns the wedge of the cone between the midpoints to its neighbours. Every fork splits its wedge at the parent's angle and turns each child 22–38° off the parent's radial line (measured at the child's end), clamped inside its half-wedge. Since subtrees keep to disjoint convex wedges about the soma centre, no two branches ever cross. Radial reach by depth is 0.9, 0.7, 0.55 and 0.45 soma radii (±20 %). Primaries and their children always fork and about half the depth-2 twigs fork again. On resize each primary's subtree gets its own scale k, the largest that fits left of, above or below the soma (at most 1.3 r and 1.6× the tightest subtree's k: the room is mostly above and below, and terminal rows are coarse). The drawn depth follows the soma radius r: depth 1 below r = 4 cells, depth 2 below 8, depth 3 from 8 up, so a small neuron draws a few long branches instead of a scribble. A twig spanning under 2 cells (`max(|Δx|/2, |Δy|/4)` in dots) is dropped with its subtree; a short primary is stretched to 2 cells instead;
  - the terminal branches, the electrode and stimulus markers, and the trace panel rectangle.

  Tiers: the full neuron when w ≥ 24 and h ≥ 8; otherwise only the axon, as one Pixels row spanning the width. The tube holds 4 pixel rows of axoplasm (a 3-cell tube) when h ≥ 40, else 2. The far-end marker `<` sits just right of the boutons, clamped on screen. The trace panel appears only when w ≥ 56 and h ≥ 16, starting at row 1 and column 0.625 w, and ideally ending at row 1 + 0.27 h. The trace's after-hyperpolarisation dip lies on or just above the panel's bottom row, so that row ends at least two rows above the top bouton, with one blank row between, and the panel shrinks to fit (at least 4 rows). If 4 rows do not fit, the panel keeps its height and ends three columns left of the arbor's first column (the trunk end), provided it keeps at least 12 columns; otherwise there is no panel. It always ends at least two columns from the right edge (x1 ≤ w − 3), the boutons' margin. A dotted lead joins the electrode to the scope. It climbs from the cell above the electrode to the trace's resting baseline and then runs along the baseline's dot row to one blank cell before the `-65` tick. Where the electrode sits under the tick or the panel, the lead stops one row below them. Drawing happens in `update` and `resize` (no `RefCell`): the axon, soma and boutons go into a `raster::Pixels`, the dendrites, arbor and trace into one `raster::Dots` and the vesicles into another; `render` only blits them and writes markers and labels.
- **Inks**: axoplasm, soma fill and boutons use `Role::Div`. For V ≥ rest the value is `x = √((V − V_rest)/105 mV)`, peaking at ≈ +0.99; the square root keeps millivolt-scale subthreshold responses visible. Below rest it is `x = −0.55·((V_rest − V)/12 mV)^1.5` (≈ −0.48 at the −75.9 mV minimum): the shallow tail of the after-hyperpolarisation stays near the background and only the deep, recent wake reads cool, quieter than the spike. Filled regions (axoplasm, soma fill) are left blank within 6 Div levels above rest (≈ +0.2 mV) and 12 below (≈ −3.7 mV), so a resting axon reads as its two membrane lines and the half-block glyphs change as a spike passes. Rest (level 128) sits near the background, so outlines carry the form: `Structure` levels 60–110 for the membrane lines above and below the axon, the soma rim, dendrites and terminal branches. The recording electrode, stimulus markers and trace are `Accent`; the electrode's lead is `Dim` 60 and dotted (every other dot); the trace baseline and the `+40`/`-65` ticks are `Dim`, and `+40` shows only while the window holds a sample above −20 mV (it labels a spike's scale and would float alone over a flat baseline); vesicles are `Particle`; the label is `Text`.
- **ASCII**: in the `Pixels::blit` closure, Div pixels map by level: `≥ 242` → `@` (V ≳ +20 mV), `≥ 211` → `#` (V ≳ −20 mV), `≥ 145` → `+` (V ≳ −63 mV), `< 84` → `~` (V ≲ −74 mV, the deep, recent after-hyperpolarisation only), else rest. At rest the axon is `=`. Structure-only cells are `=` on the axon membrane and `o` on the boutons. The soma is drawn at cell resolution: its rim is the ellipse of radii r × r/2 cells (cells are two pixels tall), rim cells being those inside with a 4-neighbour outside, drawn `o` whatever the voltage; cells inside show the fill's voltage glyph. Dendrites, the terminal arbor and the trace are line art, one glyph per cell. Dendrites and arbor use slope glyphs (`-` `/` `\` `|`). Dendrites are drawn shallowest first, each child skipping its first cell (its parent's last), and never overwrite a drawn cell, so the parent's stroke wins every junction. An arbor cell above rest shows its voltage glyph (`+` `#` `@`), except at the junction with the trunk. Below rest an arbor cell keeps its stroke and a bouton keeps its `o`, in the `Div` ink, so the cool colour carries the after-hyperpolarisation and the fan never dissolves into `~`. The `~` of the deep wake is kept for the axon and soma fill, where the pulse pattern below depends on it. The trace uses `|` where it crosses a cell top to bottom, `/` or `\` where it rises or falls within one, and `_` or `-` for a flat run in a cell's lower or upper half; the baseline shows as `.` ticks only where the trace has left it. Vesicles use the Dots density fallback. The electrode is `v` and the stimulus markers are `>` and `<`. The lead is `:` in every row of its climb and `.` in every other column along the baseline, drawn first so that everything else overwrites it. A spike on the axon reads `==~~~~~~~~===++++#####@@@@#+++===` moving right: `~` marks only the deep, recent wake, so the resting `=` frames each pulse.
- **Label**: right-aligned on the last row with `draw::label_right` (GEN-R13), drawn only when h ≥ 3. The form depends on the room right of the title (`title::readout_room`); each threshold is the form's length with the longest beat, so the form never flickers with the beat:
  - room ≥ 62: `Hodgkin-Huxley 1952 | squid axon 6.3C | v=12.3 m/s | collision`;
  - room ≥ 38: `HH squid 6.3C | v=12.3 m/s | collision`;
  - room ≥ 32: `HH 6.3C | v=12.3 m/s | collision`;
  - room ≥ 7: `HH 6.3C`;
  - none below.

  Every form with a velocity keeps the temperature: 12.3 m/s is right for 6.3 °C, not the textbook 21 m/s at 18.5 °C. `v` is measured live from the latest orthodromic spike's 0 mV crossings at 3 cm and 7 cm. The 3 cm crossing must come first, with the 7 cm crossing 1.5–6 ms later, so antidromic and colliding spikes are ignored. The readout shows `v=--.- m/s` before the first spike, then 12.3 after the first loop's single spike (12.4 in every later loop, in the collision's supernormal wake; see Refractory timing) and 10.9 → 9.8 along the train, and holds through the collision and threshold beats. It returns to `--.-` when the loop wraps, so the slowest train spike never sits beside `single`. The last field is the current beat name. Everything is ASCII, formatted in a stack buffer.
- **Perf**: 6.67 sub-steps × 200 compartments ≈ 1.3k compartment updates per frame (9 `exp`, ~40 flops and a Thomas sweep each). Rasterising covers ≈ 800 axon pixels, ≈ 150 soma pixels, ≤ 40 dendrite segments and ≤ 400 trace columns, then the blits. Measured ≈ 0.09 ms per `update(1/60)` + `render` at 200×50 (criterion, 2026-09-27: 89.8 μs). `dt = 0.25` costs 100 sub-steps, ≈ 1.5 ms. If the bench ever shows > 0.4 ms, tabulate the six rates at build time (0.1 mV steps on `[−100, +60]` mV, with linear interpolation).
- **Acceptance**: `AP-A1` with gates at steady state, `I_ion(V) = 0` has its root at `−65.0 ± 0.1 mV` (bisection on `[−80, −50]`), an unstimulated cable stays within ±0.05 mV of rest for 50 ms, and the α helpers are finite and continuous at −40 and −55 mV (`|α(V₀ ± 1e-7) − α(V₀)| < 1e-6`); `AP-A2` the propagated spike at 5 cm (strong pulse) peaks in `[+34, +42]` mV (reference +37.9), dips to a minimum in `[−77.0, −74.5]` mV (−75.9) and is back within 0.5 mV of rest 25 ms after the peak, and over one full 18 s protocol every gate stays in `[0, 1]` and every compartment's V in `[E_K − 0.5, E_Na]` (prototype −76.2 to +42.2 mV; ≈ 1 s in a debug build); `AP-A3` the conduction velocity between the 0 mV crossings at 3 cm and 7 cm is `12.3 m/s ± 3 %` at 6.3 °C with the scene discretisation (fine-grid reference 12.32, scene scheme 12.26); `AP-A4` with `φ = 3^{(18.5 − 6.3)/10}` the same solver gives `18.8 m/s ± 3 %`, HH 1952's computed velocity (scene scheme 18.55); `AP-A5` all-or-none: the scene's weak pulse (45 μA/cm²) from rest keeps V at 3 cm below −60 mV, while strong pulses at 1.5×, 3× and 6× the 90 μA/cm² threshold give peaks at 5 cm within 0.3 mV of one another; `AP-A6` refractoriness and collision: strong pulses 5 ms apart give exactly one 0 mV crossing at 7 cm, pulses 10 ms apart give two (the second slower), and simultaneous pulses at both ends give exactly one crossing at each end and at mid-axon, with max V < −60 mV 10 ms after the pulses meet (≈ 1 s in a debug build); `AP-A7` `60 × update(1/60)` and `4 × update(0.25)` leave bitwise-identical V and gate arrays (both runs execute the same 400 sub-steps), and each 18 s loop issues exactly the scripted pulses (0.2, 3.0, 5.5, 8.0 s; 13.0 s at both ends; 16.0 s weak) in order, repeating identically in the next loop; `AP-A8` over one protocol loop the live velocity readout prints exactly `12.3` after the first loop's single spike, then 10.9, 10.1 and 9.8 m/s after the three train spikes (each ± 0.3 m/s), and the collision and threshold beats leave it unchanged; the readout returns to `--.-` when the loop wraps, and the second and third loops' single spikes both print exactly `12.4`; `AP-A9` the drawn dendrites, for seeds 1–24 at 24×8, 40×12, 56×16, 80×24, 120×40 and 200×50: exactly three primaries are drawn, every drawn segment spans at least 2 cells, and no two drawn segments cross (a shared endpoint is not a crossing).
- **Look (M20)** (GEN-R25, membrane threads + ion dust; supersedes **Inks** and **ASCII** above where they disagree): model, protocol, clock, trace panel, markers, label and tiers are unchanged. Everything except the label, markers and trace axis text is drawn on one `raster::Dots` lattice in `prepare`, so there are no half-block cells. *Neuron*: the soma is a 1-px `Structure` membrane outline shaped as a rounded teardrop (a circle of the old radius whose right side tapers over ≈ 1 radius into the axon hillock, meeting the axon membranes without a gap). Inside sits a nucleus: a `Dim` stipple atom of σ ≈ 0.3 r with a 1-dot `Structure` nucleolus. The axon is two 1-px `Structure` membrane threads at the old tube edges. Dendrites keep the seeded, non-crossing radial tree but are drawn as gently curved 1-px braille threads (each segment a quadratic Bézier bowing 8–15 % of its length to the side its wedge leaves free), tapering in level with depth (`Structure` 110 → 60), with short spines: ≈ one 1–2-dot stub per 6 dots of segment beyond depth 1. The terminal arbor is curved threads ending in boutons that are `Structure` stipple atoms (σ ≈ 1.2 dots) and flash `Hot` (peak raised) for 0.4 s when the spike arrives; vesicles stay as single dots. *Voltage as ion dust*: the axoplasm and soma interior are stippled with fixed per-dot thresholds (`Dots::stipple`, CORE-R107) at a density that follows the local V: `p = 0.03` at rest; above rest `p = 0.03 + 0.85·x` in `Hot` with x as in **Inks**; below rest `p = 0.03 + 0.5·|x|` in `Cold`. So a resting axon is sparse cool dust, the spike a dense bright puff that travels, and the after-hyperpolarisation a cool wake. *Ion flux*: where a compartment depolarises fast (dV/dt > 20 mV/ms), Na⁺ ions are emitted as `Hot` dots at the membrane drifting inward over 0.25 s; where it repolarises fast (dV/dt < −10 mV/ms), K⁺ ions as `Cold` dots drift outward across the membrane over 0.25 s. A fixed `ParticlePool` (capacity 160) is filled in `update` from `ctx.rng`, so it is deterministic and allocation-free. The trace stays a braille thread (`Accent`), its axes `Dim`.
- **Acceptance** (M20): `AP-A10` at 200×50 after 0.5, 1.5 and 9 s no cell is a half-block cell, and at least 85 % of lit cells outside the label row are braille; `AP-A11` the stipple density is a pure function of V: 0.03 at rest, non-decreasing in V above rest and in `V_rest − V` below it, `Hot` above rest and `Cold` below; at 1.0 s the dots lit inside the axon within 0.5 cm of the spike peak are at least 8× as dense as in the resting far half; `AP-A12` ion dots: `Hot` Na⁺ ions only appear where dV/dt > 20 mV/ms and move inward (towards the axon axis), `Cold` K⁺ ions only where dV/dt < −10 mV/ms and move outward; none exist at rest (t < 0.2 s); the pool never exceeds 160; `AP-A13` the soma outline is closed and joins both axon membranes (no gap ≥ 2 dots), the nucleus lies inside it, and dendrite threads still never cross (the existing wedge rule).

### aurora  (Earth & Sky, M10, M12, M15)
- **Intent**: a subauroral night, looking north over a still lake. A quiet arc low in the north brightens suddenly and breaks into folding, rayed curtains that climb and fill the sky, green below and capped with crimson above, with a pink hem along the lower border at the substorm's peak. It then fades into softly pulsating patches. Between the curtains the sky stays black: light is drawn as coherent arcs, rayed curtains and patches, never as scattered speckle. The focal point is the lower border of the brightest curtain, which is the sharpest and brightest edge in the frame. A black spruce ridge and its rippling reflection anchor the bottom, and the sparse stars never compete. At 200×50 the shore is row 40. At scene start the arch's crest stands ≈ 18° up (≈ 12 rows above the shore) with both ends sagging into the horizon, and at the expansion peak the curtains fill rows ≈ 2–38. At 80×24 the shore is row 19 and the crest starts ≈ 6 rows above it.
- **Model**: *View*: every object lives in sky angles (azimuth α, east positive; elevation e) or in world km, projected equirectangularly onto a full-screen `Pixels` lattice (P = 2h pixel rows). The horizon row is `y_h = round(0.80·P)` with a lake below it, or `round(0.92·P)` with no lake when h < 14. The scale is `f = max(y_h/62°, W/π)` px/rad, with `x = W/2 + f·α` and `y = y_h − f·e`, so the frame is ≈ 155° × 62° at 200×50 and ≈ 130° × 62° at 80×24. *Curtains*: 1–3 thin vertical sheets aligned east–west. Sheet c lies at northward ground distance `D_c(s,t) = D0_c(t) + ξ_c(s,t)` of the along-arc coordinate s (east positive). It is sampled at M = 480 fixed points `s_j = 300 km·tan α_j`, with α_j uniform in ±85°. The displacement is `ξ = Σ_{k=1..4} w_kj·a_k sin(κ_k s + θ_k)` with λ = 900/240/70/18 km (bend/surge/fold/curl), `a_1 = 40 km` and `a_k = F(t)·σ_k/κ_k` for slope amplitudes σ = 0.5/1.3/0.8. The weight `w_kj = clamp(λ_k/(2Δs_j) − 1, 0, 1)` fades a mode out wherever it has fewer than 4 samples per wavelength, so flank folds never alias. Phases integrate as `θ_k −= κ_k c_k dt`, so mode k drifts at velocity c_k, with c = +0.3, −v_s(t), +1.2, −0.8 km/s (negative is westward; v_s is the westward-travelling surge). Speed changes therefore never make the pattern jump. Each sample lies at ground distance `d = hypot(s, D)` and azimuth `α = atan2(s, D)`. An altitude z maps to `e = atan((z − d²/2R⊕)/d)` (flat earth plus curvature drop, R⊕ = 6371 km). A straight east–west arc is therefore an arch that sags into both horizons. The sheet doubles back on screen wherever `ξ' > cot|α|`; for the fold band's slope of 1.3 that happens only beyond |α| ≈ 38°, which is how real folds read in the oblique sky. *Precipitation*: `Φ = A_c(t)·B·((1 − r) + r·ρ/ρ̄)`. B is a westward-drifting bulge `1 + b(t)·exp(−((α − α_b)/w_b)²)`. The ray field is `ρ = ⅓Σ_m max(0, cos(κ_m(s − v_m t) + φ_m))⁶` with λ_m = 23/37/61 km and `v_m = v_r(t)·{1, −0.6, 0.8}`. Its mean is ρ̄ = 5/32, so switching rays on moves precipitation into the rays without changing its average; the ray-averaged flux is `Φ̄ = A·B`. The mean energy E0(t) sets the lower border `z_b = 131 − 25·log10(E0/1 keV)` km, clamped to [85, 150]. This is a fit to the peak energy-deposition altitudes of Rees (1963). *Emission* at altitude z has three components (rays also raise the green column by up to 1.8×, since H grows inside a ray and the profile is not renormalised). Green OI 557.7 nm is `G = Φ·S·g(z − z_b)` with `g(u) = (1 − e^{−(u+2)/2})·e^{−u/H}` for u ≥ −2 km (else 0). It peaks ≈ 3 km above z_b, and `H = 22 km·(1 + 0.8 r ρ)` so that rays reach higher. Green is prompt (the O(¹S) lifetime is 0.74 s). Red OI 630.0 nm is `R = R_j·min(S, 2)·0.4·exp(1 − y − e^{−y})/(1 + e^{−(z−190)/12})` with y = (z − 240)/50 km: a Chapman production layer times collisional quenching of O(¹D) below ~200 km. O(¹D) atoms diffuse tens of km during their 110 s lifetime, so the red layer is thick: its slant gain is capped at 2, and the red is smoothed along the arc by a ≈ 4° Gaussian (three running-sum box passes per pixel row) before it is thresholded, so where folds double back the red ramps instead of standing as a wall. It is displayed at 0.27× (the photopic ratio V(630)/V(557.7)), so the diffuse fringe stays a fringe. The red source relaxes exactly as `R_j ← Φ + (R_j − Φ)·e^{−dt/τ_R}` with τ_R = 3 s. That is the 110 s radiative lifetime compressed only enough to keep the lag visible (on the 144× substorm clock it would be 0.76 s), so the red is smooth, rayless and lingers. The pink N₂ 1PG hem marks type-B aurora, where fast electrons reach heights at which O(¹S) is quenched. It is `P = Φ·S·clamp((E0 − 8)/12, 0, 1)` for z_b − 4 ≤ z ≤ z_b + 1 km, splatted for the brightest curtain only, and it always includes the layer's lowest lit pixel, so it never vanishes at small sizes. A pixel is pink only where P ≥ 0.25 G and the pink's own tone `1 − e^{−P/E_sat}` (after extinction) is ≥ 0.6; isolated hem pixels with no hem neighbour in the adjacent columns are demoted to green (keeping their level), so the hem reads as a run. The slant factor `S = min(1/|n̂·l̂|, 6)` uses the sheet normal n ∝ (−ξ', 1, 0) (east, north, up) and the line of sight l̂ to the sample's lower border. It is the path length through a thin sheet, so folds seen edge-on become bright caustic "rays". *Simplifications*: sheets and rays are vertical (field-aligned; valid for the northern sky seen from a subauroral site) and arcs are strictly east–west. The red source ignores E0 (real 630 nm weakens as precipitation hardens), and its 0.4 normalisation makes the red column ≈ 2.2× the green one, 10–45× the photometric I630/I557.7 of keV aurora, for a vivid fringe; the photopic weight 0.27 brings the displayed ratio to ≈ 0.6. *Raster*: consecutive samples are splatted as column spans into per-column layers {d, z_b, H, G/R/P amplitudes}, at most 6 per column (one crossing per curtain plus room for three more where folds double back; a seventh merges into the nearest d). A fold that doubles back gives a column several layers, and they add. Pixel row y samples each layer at `z = d·tan e_y + d²/2R⊕`. This uses a per-resize tan e row table, a 0.5-km green LUT in u with H quantised to 16 steps, and a 1-km red LUT in z. The sum is multiplied by the extinction `exp(−0.12(1/sin max(e, 2°) − 1))` and tone-mapped as `1 − e^{−E/E_sat}` (E_sat = 0.9) through a LUT. Emission fades in under the top edge over 20 % of the pixel rows (at least 3 px) by smoothstep, so a crown overhead thins into dark sky and the label stays in clear air. *Thresholds, not dither*: the theme floors lit roles at 30 % brightness, so faint light cannot be shown by level; it is left dark rather than scattered as a dither. The pulsating-patch green Q (see *Recovery*) adds to the tone but is kept apart from the curtains' green G. Where the curtains' own green tone is < 0.03 the red is dropped (below notice), so patch light never opens the crown's gate. A pixel whose red share R/(G+Q+R) is ≤ ½ is green if its tone is ≥ 0.14, at level `12 + 243·(tone − 0.14)/0.86`. A red-dominant pixel is the crown if its tone is ≥ 0.2 *and* the curtains' green tone there is ≥ 0.03, at level `12 + 98·(tone − 0.2)/0.8` in `Accent`, so every lit level starts from the core floor `ink::MIN_VISIBLE_LEVEL` = 12 (CORE-R66): the crown caps each ray and fold with crimson and so follows the curtains' envelope; diffuse red far above the green would be cut flat by the top fade (a lid) and stand as a slab over the sky. A hem pixel is `Accent` at `190 + 65·(tone − 0.14)/0.86`. Every lit run thus starts from the theme's dimmest shade of its role, each column lights contiguous runs where its emission profile crosses the threshold, and rays that reach higher end higher, so a curtain's top edge is combed into vertical streaks. Finally specks are dropped: a lit sky pixel with no lit neighbour (of 8), and a pair whose two pixels neighbour only each other, so a fading arc end or a distant arc on the horizon never crumbles into grit. *Setting*: a far-shore ridge (3-octave value noise in α, 0.8–4° high) is topped by seeded spruce spires (1.5–4° apart, 1.2–3° tall, half-width 0.28× height); above f = 60 px/rad a second, nearer forest of fewer, taller spires (2.5–6° apart, 2.5–4° tall, half-width 0.35×) replaces them so each shows triangular flanks. Below it the far spires are stretched upward (widths unchanged, so they get spikier) until a 3° spire (the height cap) stands 3 px: at 80×24 a 3° spruce is under 2 px and the comb would read as a ruled line. Silhouette pixels are never lit. A `Seq` skyglow lights every sky pixel below the glow height `max(f·8°, tallest silhouette + 1.5 px, 3 px)`, from 0.2 at the horizon falling quadratically to 0.02 at its top, solid throughout: the ramp's black end is a dark navy, so the glow stops just above it rather than being carried into it, and the ridge and spires cut a crisp black edge into it. The lake mirrors the sky about y_h at 0.45× level, with their roles: the skyglow, and each column's lit sky runs whole wherever a run's brightest pixel has level ≥ 40 (a fainter run's copy could not be dimmer than the theme's floor, while cutting the dim ends off brighter runs would leave shards), emission copies never below level 12; the ridge and spires mirror as a black band cut out of the reflected glow. Its ripple is `δx = round(0.04 d·sin(0.9d − 1.7t + 0.13x))`, where d is the number of pixel rows below the shore, so the wavefronts travel toward the viewer while the water stays still. Whole rows drop out where `sin(0.9d − 1.7t) > 0.9`, so mirrored rays read as vertical streaks cut by horizontal ripple gaps. Stars come from a seeded 1200-star catalogue in (α, e) with magnitudes. They are shown brightest-first until 2 % of sky cells hold one, so the limiting magnitude adapts to resolution. They twinkle more toward the horizon and are drawn only in cells whose two pixels are empty.
- **Motion**: a `Timeline` runs growth 12 s → onset 3 s → expansion 15 s → recovery 20 s. The 50 s loop compresses a ~2 h substorm ≈ 144× (Akasofu 1964), while folds and rays move at real km/s speeds. Keyframes are smoothstep-interpolated, except that every segment starting at the onset uses `ease::out_quad` (the sudden breakup), and every one returns to its growth-start value by the end of recovery. The scene clock starts at t = 8 s, so breakup lands 4 s into a default 15 s visit. *Growth*: two faint homogeneous arcs drift equatorward, toward the viewer (D0₁ 520 → 260 km, A₁ 0.35 → 0.4, A₂ 0.3, E0 2 → 4 keV, F 0.15, r 0.04, v_r 1 km/s), each a clear band above its threshold rather than a broken line hovering at it. *Onset*: the equatorward arc brightens 2.5× within 1.5 s (`ease::out_quad`). E0 rises to 20 keV, so the lower border drops ≈ 18 km and the pink hem appears. Rays switch on (r → 0.8, v_r → 12 km/s). *Expansion*: the bulge swells both ways. Arc 1 moves toward the zenith (D0₁ → 170 km, base ≈ 31°) while arcs 2–3 light up poleward, arc 2 at D0₁ + 150 km closing to + 110 km within 9 s, arc 3 at D0₁ + 330 km. F rises to 1.0, so folds double back in the oblique sky. The bulge surges west (α_b +10° → −40°, v_s → 6 km/s), and E0 settles to 10 keV. *Recovery*: the arcs dim and retreat poleward (D0 → 520 km; E0, F, r, v_r back to their growth values), and the red fringe outlives the green by τ_R. Six pulsating patches fade in and out: seeded one per 15° stratum across α ±45° (±3° jitter, so neighbours drifting together keep apart instead of fusing into one blob) and at e 25–45° (mid-sky, clear of the top fade), soft Gaussian 6–12° × 3–6° ellipses cut at 1.6 half-extents, with half-extents floored at min(5 px, 0.07 W) × min(3 px, P/12) (at 80×24 a 6° patch is only 4 columns, a smudge; the floor is 5 × 3 px there and at 200×50 and 2.8 × 2 px at 40×12, so a small frame's patches never swell into clouds half the sky wide), period 4–12 s, on-fraction 0.4 with 0.4 s edges, drifting east 0.5°/s, green at 0.45× the arc peak, so a patch at full pulse stays dimmer than the recovery arc. Pulsating aurora has no red cap: patch light is kept out of the crown's gate (*Thresholds*), so a patch in the lingering red dome raises no crimson hood. Activity a(t) runs 0.02 → 0.15 (end of growth) → 0.55 (end of onset) → 1.0 (t = 22 s) → 0.75 (end of expansion) → 0.02 and sets `AL = −(30 + 800a)` nT. In any 1 s window the lake ripple alone changes pixel occupancy (GEN-R9 compares glyphs), and rays and folds add more.
- **Resize**: there is no simulation lattice, so ADR-0012 bucketing does not apply. All state lives in world or sky coordinates and survives any resize untouched: fold and ray phases, red sources, clock, star catalogue, trees and patches. Resize rebuilds only the view: f, y_h, the tan e and extinction row tables, the per-column silhouette, the visible-star list, the layer scratch and `Pixels`. A resize re-frames the same sky. At 1×1 the view is sky-only and nothing panics.
- **Inks**: green `Particle` 12–255; red crown `Accent` 12–110 (a deep magenta-crimson: dark `Hot` is a rust brown and bright `Hot` an orange, while the 630 nm crown reads crimson over green in photographs); pink hem `Accent` ≥ 190, well clear of the crown; skyglow `Seq` 0.02–0.2; stars `Star`; ASCII ridge `Dim` 70; label `Text`. Reflections reuse the source role at 0.45× level (emission copies at least 12). Silhouettes stay empty (black).
- **ASCII**: every cell takes the brighter emission pixel of its pair (a hem pixel wins, so the hem stays a line); skyglow returns `None`. Sky: green is `|` (≥ 200), `!` (≥ 130), `:` (≥ 70), else `,` (so a quiet arc never reads as the dim stars `.`); the crown is `"` (≥ 60), else `'`; the hem is `=`. Lake cells are `~` (≥ 60), else `-`. After the blit, in ASCII mode only, the ridge top is drawn as one continuous `_` line (the cell holding each column's top ridge pixel), with a spire tip `^` in the cell just above it wherever a spire rises above the line (one comb however tall the stretched spires), in still-blank cells, `Dim` 70. Stars are `.`, with `*` for the brightest 10 %, and also need a still-blank cell. At 80×24 this reads as `,:!|` columns capped with `'"`, arching over a `^^_` shore and `~` water.
- **Label**: top-left `AL -620 nT  expansion` (`Text` 150). AL is rounded to 10 nT and written digit by digit into a stack buffer, and the phase word is the `Timeline` beat label. The phase word is dropped when w < 60, and the whole label when w < 24 or h < 10.
- **Perf** at 200×50: update ≈ 0.35 ms. Sampling costs ≈ 0.09 ms (3 × 480 samples × 4 fold sines, 3 ray cos⁶ and a red relaxation, with one exp per call), independent of terminal size; the rest is the rasterisation, which runs in `update`: projection and spans ≈ 0.08 ms; sky shading ≈ 16k px × ≤ 2 live layers of LUT reads ≈ 0.15 ms, skipping rows above the emission top; lake via per-row ripple tables ≈ 0.02 ms; silhouette. Render (blit, stars, the ASCII ridge and the label) ≈ 0.05 ms. Total ≈ 0.45 ms (measured 0.47 ms in criterion at load ≈ 14, 2026-09-27, after the patch and whole-run lake rework), with no allocation after resize. Thresholding and the speck pass cost ≈ 0.02 ms, the lake's per-column run-peak scan ≈ 0.01 ms; nothing is precomputed per process.
- **Acceptance**: `AUR-A1` `lower_border_km(e0)` is strictly decreasing on [1, 60] keV, stays within [85, 150] km for any input, and gives 113.5, 106.0, 98.5 and 88.5 km (±0.1) at 5, 10, 20 and 50 keV; `AUR-A2` with H = 22 km (ρ = 0) and E0 ∈ {3, 10, 20} keV, the green profile peaks within [z_b, z_b + 6] km, is 0 below z_b − 2 km and is < 5 % of its peak above z_b + 80 km; the red profile peaks in [220, 260] km and is < 5 % of its peak below 170 km; with equal sources, red > green for all z ∈ [200, 450] km and red < 0.05·green for all z ∈ [z_b, 150] km; the pink profile is 0 outside [z_b − 4, z_b + 1] km and 0 for E0 ≤ 8 keV; `AUR-A3` from a unit step, `red_relax(r, target, dt)` reaches 1 − e⁻¹ (±1e-12) after τ_R = 3 s, composes exactly (0.1 s then 0.2 s equals 0.3 s within 1e-12) and never overshoots; along the onset keyframes A(t) at least doubles within 1.5 s of the onset start, while a red source relaxed toward A(t) in 1/60 s steps covers < 45 % of the jump at that moment; the mean of ρ over s ∈ [0, 10⁴] km at fixed t is 5/32 ± 0.005; `AUR-A4` `elevation_deg(105, 600)` is 7.29 ± 0.05°, `elevation_deg(105, 180)` is 29.65 ± 0.05°, and `altitude_km(elevation_deg(z, d), d)` returns z within 1e-9 km; with F = 0 and a_1 = 0, a curtain at D0 = 300 km projects to an arch whose lower-border elevation is maximal at α = 0 and strictly decreasing in |α|; `AUR-A5` (200×50 view, curtain at D0 = 300 km): with F = 0 and a_1 = 0, every column where the lower border is above the horizon gets exactly one layer; with only the fold band (σ = 1.3, F = 1), no column with |α| < 37° gets 2 layers from that curtain and at least one column with |α| > 38° does; S is ≥ 1, never exceeds 6, and equals 1/cos e_b (1e-9) for an unfolded arc at α = 0; `AUR-A6` the beats are growth 12 s → onset 3 s → expansion 15 s → recovery 20 s in order, the loop is 50 s, and a fresh scene starts at t = 8 s; every keyframed parameter is continuous across the wrap (|Δ| < 1e-9); `al_nt(t)` stays in [−150, −30] nT throughout growth and ≤ −450 nT throughout expansion; `patch_pulse(t, P)` is P-periodic (1e-12) with on-fraction (value > 0.5) 0.40 ± 0.02, and patches are 0 outside recovery; `AUR-A7` at 200×50, after 56 × `update(0.25)` (scene t = 22 s) and one render (< 1 s in debug): no silhouette pixel is lit, lit pixels are 15–70 % of non-silhouette sky pixels, every lit lake pixel has level ≤ 0.5× the brightest sky level, every star cell has both pixels empty, and at least one hem pixel (`Accent` ≥ 190) exists, while after 4 updates (t = 9 s) there is none; at t = 19, 21 and 22 s a hem exists, every hem pixel (`Accent` ≥ 190) lies within 4 px above its column's lowest pink-layer border (never inside a higher layer) and has a hem neighbour in an adjacent column; at t = 20, 22, 28 and 36 s every lake row where `sin(0.9d − 1.7t) > 0.9` is blank, every other lit lake pixel is a same-role copy at 0.45× level (emission copies at least 12) of a mirrored sky pixel (skyglow, or any pixel of a column's lit run whose brightest pixel has level ≥ 40) within 3 columns of its mirror, and the lake is 0.9–1.1× as densely lit as its mirrored source rows; every hem pixel has level ≥ 190; `AUR-A8` (rendering): below its threshold (0.14 green, 0.2 red-dominant) a pixel is dark, at it the level is 12 (`ink::MIN_VISIBLE_LEVEL`), never lower above it, at full tone 255 (green) or 110 (crown), monotone between; a red-dominant pixel over green tone < 0.03 is dark; a hem pixel is `Accent` ≥ 190; at 80×24 and 200×50, at visit t = 0, 5, 12, 20, 26, 34 and 42 s, pixels in specks (8-connected groups of ≤ 3) are ≤ 3 % of the lit sky; at scene start every column over the frame's middle third is lit and its lower border steps by ≤ 2 px between neighbouring columns; at visit t = 14, 18 and 22 s at 200×50 the crown has ≥ 100 pixels, ≥ 95 % of them with green lit within 12 px below in the same column, and its top edge spans ≥ 6 rows; the patch floor is 5 × 3 px at 80×24 and 200×50 and 2.8 × 2 px at 40×12, and over seeds 0–15 every patch is seeded within 3° of its stratum's centre; during recovery at 80×24 and 40×12 every live patch's half-extents are ≥ the floor and ≤ max(6° × 3°, the floor), and at full pulse it has a solid core (5 × 3 px at 80×24, 3 × 1 px at 40×12); at visit t = 32, 34 and 36 s at 80×24 and 200×50 the centre of every patch at full pulse is dimmer than the brightest curtain pixel outside every patch's support; at 200×50 at visit t = 26, 27 and 28 s (seeds 7 and 42) no crown pixel lies within 2 px of a live patch's ellipse unless curtain green is lit within 12 px below it in the same column; at 40×12, 80×24 and 200×50 some spire rises ≥ 2 px over the bare ridge, the glow height is ≥ the tallest silhouette + 1.5 px, the sky pixel above every silhouette column is lit (glow or aurora), and no silhouette pixel's mirror in the lake holds skyglow.

### belousov_zhabotinsky  (Reactions, M10, M12, M15, M16)
- **Intent**: a Belousov–Zhabotinsky dish seen from above. Thin, pale oxidation fronts sweep a dark medium. Each trails a tail that cools through orange, red and violet to black as the medium recovers. Broken wavefronts curl at their free ends into spirals: counter-rotating pairs, or a single spiral where one end is pinned to the dish wall. A pacemaker nucleus pulses slower target rings, which the spiral wave trains gradually push back. Every 62 s a light quench, as in the photosensitive Ru-catalysed BZ (Kuhnert, Agladze & Krinsky 1989), puts every wave out, and fresh broken fronts restart the dish. `gray_scott` shows Gray–Scott blobs that grow in place and `liesegang_rings` shows static bands; here every feature travels and the resting medium is black.
- **Model**: Barkley (1991) excitable medium on a no-flux (Neumann) rectangle: `∂u/∂t = ∇²u + ε⁻¹ u(1 − u)(u − u_th)`, `u_th = (v + b)/a`, `∂v/∂t = u − v`, with `a = 0.75`, `b = 0.06`, `ε = 0.02` (rigidly rotating spirals; the EZ-Spiral default). `u` stands for the autocatalyst HBrO₂. `v` stands for the oxidised catalyst (ferriin / Ru(bpy)₃³⁺), whose reduction releases the Br⁻ inhibitor; this is the two-variable Oregonator picture. The node spacing is fixed at `h = 0.5` space units (su), so the pattern scale is fixed in nodes. Each sub-step `τ = 0.0125` time units (tu) is Lie-split into three stages. (1) Explicit diffusion, using the isotropic 9-point Laplacian (Patra–Karttunen, as in `gray_scott`) with mirrored ghost nodes. The stencil sums mirror pairs first, `(W+E)`, `(N+S)`, `(NW+NE)` and `(SW+SE)`, so the update is bitwise left–right symmetric. (2) The reaction, with Barkley's (1991) implicit update on the decaying branch, `u ← u / (1 − (τ/ε)(1 − u)(u − u_th))` if `u < u_th`, and an explicit growth branch, `u ← u + (τ/ε) u(1 − u)(u − u_th)` otherwise; the growth branch stays bounded because `τ/ε ≤ 1` (Barkley and EZ-Spiral take it implicitly too). (3) `v ← v + τ(u − v)`. Because `(10/3)·τ/h² = 0.17 ≤ 1`, `τ/ε = 0.625 ≤ 1` and `τ ≤ 1`, every stage is a convex combination or a shrinking factor. So `u, v ∈ [0, 1]` holds exactly for any `b ≥ 0`, with no clamps. Measured on this exact scheme (NumPy prototype):
  - planar pulse speed `c = 3.58` su/tu, which is 0.85 `c_N`, where `c_N = √(2/ε)(½ − b/a) = 4.2` is the Nagumo limit at `v = 0`; the continuum value at `h = 0.1` is ≈ 3.9, and the O(ε) recovery effect accounts for the rest of the gap;
  - spiral period 3.95–4.2 tu;
  - spiral wavelength λ ≈ 25 nodes;
  - excited plateau ≈ 8 nodes long.

  Pacing is `RATE = 1.5` tu per simulated second, exactly 2 sub-steps per 1/60 s, through an accumulator that handles any `dt` (30 sub-steps at `dt = 0.25`). Seeding draws from `ctx.rng` once per cycle and places up to `k = clamp(round(nodes / 2000), 1, 4)` broken fronts. Each front is a straight segment:
  - its length is `L ∈ [1.2, 2.0]·λ` at a uniform random angle;
  - its midpoint is uniform in the central 60 % of each axis;
  - if a free end falls within 4 nodes of a wall, or the segment comes within λ/2 of an earlier segment, `L` shrinks in 10 % steps down to 0.8 λ; if it still does not fit, the draw is rejected. The distance between segments is 0 if they cross, else the nearest endpoint-to-segment distance. Each painted band reaches at most 6 nodes from its segment, so bands never touch or cross. A front still rejected after 32 tries is dropped, and a crowded dish gets fewer fronts rather than two tangled ones (≤ 1 in 10 on the tested lattices). The first front has no earlier segments to avoid; if even it fails the walls, the last draw is shrunk as far as needed, but not below 3 nodes (a shorter front cannot launch a wave, BZ-A2), and otherwise clipped to the lattice;
  - with probability 0.3 the segment is instead extended to a wall from its end nearer one, which leaves one free end and gives a single spiral. Extending the far end would draw a front across the whole dish and leave no room for the others;
  - it is a 2-node strip of `u = 1` backed by a 6-node strip whose `v` ramps 0.69 → 0.36 away from the front. Every `v ≥ 0.315` gives `u_th > ½`, so the strip blocks backward propagation, and the ramp already shades as a tail.

  There is 1 pacemaker (2 when nodes ≥ 8000), at a normalised position in `[0.15, 0.85]²`. It first fires 1 tu into each cycle and then every `T_pm = 6` tu (≈ 1.5 × the spiral period), setting `u = 1` where `v < 0.1` in a disc of radius 2.5 nodes. The higher-frequency source wins (Zaikin & Zhabotinsky 1970; Winfree 1972), so the spiral domain grows into the target pattern. The story is a looping `Timeline [("waves", 60 s), ("quench", 2 s)]`. During the quench, `b` ramps 0.06 → 0.45 with `ease::smoothstep` over 0.5 s, so `u_th ≥ 0.6 > ½`. Fronts retreat, excited regions collapse once `v > a − b = 0.3`, pacemakers stay off, and `v` decays as `e^{−t}`. There is no excited node 1 s into the quench, and max `v` is ≈ 0.047 at its end (3 tu of `e^{−t}` decay), which shades nothing (`s = 0.55·(v/0.69)² < 0.06`). At the cycle boundary `b` resets and a new seed set is painted over the dark field.
- **Motion**: fronts advance ≈ 11 nodes/s, and a lattice node is ≈ 1 pixel column. Spirals turn every ≈ 2.8 s. At t = 0 the seeded fronts already read as short comets. By 1 s their free ends visibly curl, and by 5 s the spirals have formed. Over the minute, spiral wave trains push the target rings back toward their nucleus. On small dishes the takeover is quick: the 80×48 lattice at 80×24 is ≈ 3 × 2 wavelengths and fronts cross it in ≈ 7 s, so the spirals reach the pacemaker within seconds, after which it only fires into gaps between their fronts (5–9 of its 15 beats per cycle find recovered medium in its disc, mean 7.6 over seeds 1–15 and 42); the rings play out longer on large dishes. The quench fades the dish to black within ≈ 1 s and it stays dark for ≈ 1 s, then new fronts appear, brightening from dark over 0.5 s (display only: the model paints them at full strength). The very first seeding shows at once. The loop is 62 s.
- **Resize**: the lattice is `grid::lattice_for(Size(w, 2h), 20_000, 1.25)`, since Pixels are square, `w × 2h`. That gives 197×100 at 200×50 and exactly 80×48 at 80×24: below the cap the per-axis clamp makes the lattice the pixel grid itself. Above the cap, nodes are stretched relative to pixels by at most √1.25 (≤ 12 %). An unchanged lattice leaves the state untouched. Otherwise `u` and `v` are `resample_bilinear`ed into the new lattice (its convex weights keep `[0, 1]`), and the pattern relaxes to its natural λ within a few rotations. Pacemaker and seed positions are stored normalised in `[0, 1]²`. The first resize seeds. Resize also rebuilds per-axis sampling tables (source index and weight per pixel column and per pixel row). Shading happens in `update` and `resize`, with no `RefCell`: `u` and `v` are sampled bilinearly per pixel (the identity when the lattice equals the pixel grid) and shaded into a `raster::Pixels` without allocating; `render` only blits it and writes the label.
- **Inks**: `Role::Seq` encodes the *phase of the excitation cycle*, not a concentration. Let `r = clamp(v / (a − b₀), 0, 1)` with the unquenched `b₀ = 0.06`, so the quench ramp never brightens the tails. Excited cells (`u ≥ ½`) get `s = 1 − 0.45·r`, which is palest at the fresh front and orange at the back of the plateau. Refractory cells get `s = 0.55·r²`, going red → violet → dark as the medium recovers. `s < 0.06` gives `Ink::BLANK`, so resting medium is the terminal background. Every other pixel is `ink::seq(s)`. The prototype measured ≈ 3–6 % of pixels pale (`s ≥ 0.85`) and ≈ 40–52 % lit, so there are thin bright edges, dim tails and real dark gaps. The label is `Text`.
- **Look** *(M16, GEN-R25; supersedes the Inks and ASCII below and the half-block shading in Resize; no longer pinned by GEN-R22)*: no bands, no ground: each wave is braille threads one dot thick on black, traced on the lattice by marching squares (`Dots::contour_by`, CORE-R105; one node per 2×2 dots below the cap, the lattice rule unchanged) and drawn once per frame in `prepare` (GEN-R20). The crest is the level `u = ½` in the cells whose mean `v` is below 0.25, i.e. the leading edge of each front (the back of the excited plateau, where `v` is near `a − b`, is not drawn): a bright `Hot` 230 thread. Behind it, where all four corners have `u < ½` (recovering medium), two `Cold` tail lines trail the crest at the levels `v = 0.42` (`Cold` 190) and `v = 0.2` (`Cold` 150; round 2, was 120), so the recovery reads as a fading wake with black between the lines; resting medium and the quenched dish are black. A reseed's fade-in scales the levels (a line below level 12 is not drawn). ASCII falls back by braille dot count.
- **ASCII**: the `Pixels::blit` closure takes the larger Seq level of the two pixels: `≥ 217` → `@` (`s ≥ 0.85`), `≥ 179` → `#` (0.70), `≥ 102` → `+` (0.40), `≥ 51` → `:` (0.20), any other non-blank pixel → `.`, and two blank pixels → nothing. Fronts read as `@@##++::..` comets curling on white space.
- **Label**: right-aligned on row `h − 1` with `draw::label_right` (a blank cell each side, so it never touches the field; GEN-R13); there is no label when h < 4 or w < 14. The room is `title::readout_room`, which never exceeds half the row (CORE-R76). `{word}` is the beat: `waves`, or during the quench `light quench` (room ≥ 12) or `quench`. The name is not repeated: the title names the reaction.
  - room ≥ 44: `{word}  Barkley a=0.75 b=0.06 eps=0.02`;
  - room ≥ 20: `{word}  b=0.06`;
  - room ≥ 6: `{word}`.

  `b` is printed live to 2 decimals, so it reads 0.45 during the quench: the on-screen sign that the quench is a loss of excitability. The label is ASCII and written into a stack buffer without allocation.
- **Perf**: at 200×50 the lattice has 19.7k nodes × 2 sub-steps of ~30 f32 flops each, plus 20k bilinear pixel samples, the shading and the blit. Measured ≈ 0.31 ms (criterion, 2026-09-27: 310 μs) per `update(1/60)` + `render` at 200×50 (aim < 1 ms). `dt = 0.25` costs ≈ 2 ms (30 sub-steps). Reseeding paints ≤ 4 segments (≈ 2k nodes). There are no allocations after resize.
- **Acceptance**: `BZ-A1` `bz_step` maps any field with `u, v ∈ [0, 1]` back into `[0, 1]` with no clamping in the kernel (64 seeded random 24×16 fields, 50 sub-steps each, at `b = 0.06` and at `b = 0.45`), and the rest state `(0, 0)` is an exact fixed point; `BZ-A2` excitability is all-or-none on a 64×64 lattice: a disc of radius 4 nodes at `u = 0.9·b/a` (`v = 0`) decays to `max u < 1e-3` within 2 tu, while `u = 1` discs of radius 1.5 and 4 nodes each launch a ring whose outermost excited node (`u ≥ ½`) lies ≥ 12 nodes from the centre at 2 tu (prototype 13.0 and 17.0); `BZ-A3` a planar pulse on a 3×160 strip (scene `h` and `τ`; the `u = ½` front is fitted over nodes 40–120) travels at `c ∈ [0.75, 0.92]·c_N` with `c_N = 4.2` (prototype 0.85), and a pacemaker of period 6 tu at the strip's end emits fronts spaced `c·T_pm/h ± 10 %` apart, excluding the leading front (prototype 42–44 nodes against 43); `BZ-A4` a single spiral grown from the scene's wall-anchored broken front (from the top wall to the centre of a 64×64 lattice) rotates rigidly: after a 20 tu transient its period (`u = ½` up-crossings at a probe ≥ λ from the core) lies in `[3.7, 4.5]` tu (prototype 3.95–4.21), and in every 4 tu window up to 50 tu, the trailing 48–50 tu one included, its tip (`u = ½` ∩ `v = a/2 − b`, the EZ-Spiral definition, located per 2×2 block) stays inside a 6×6-node box (prototype ≤ 5×5; the core drifts ≈ 1 node per turn away from the walls, hence the window), with the probe ≥ λ from the window's mean tip (≈ 1–1.5 s in a debug build); `BZ-A5` a horizontal broken front with two free ends, centred on a 72×48 lattice, becomes a counter-rotating pair: at every sub-step up to 12 tu, `u` and `v` are bitwise mirror-symmetric about the line between columns 35 and 36, and at 12 tu the half-plane behind the seed holds excited nodes (`u ≥ ½`) on both sides of that line; `BZ-A6` the lattice never exceeds 20 000 nodes or the pixel grid in either axis (displays 1×1 to 400×120), a resize between two displays in the same aspect bucket that are both at or above the cap (240×60 → 250×62) leaves `u` and `v` bitwise unchanged, and any seeded resize sequence keeps `u, v ∈ [0, 1]`; `BZ-A7` at the end of the 2 s quench beat `max u < 1e-3` and `max v < 0.06` (prototype ≈ 0.047; every pixel is then blank, since `s = 0.55·(v/0.69)² < 0.06`), no pacemaker fires during the quench, each cycle re-seeds deterministically from `ctx.rng` (same seed ⇒ same fronts and pacemakers; consecutive cycles differ), on lattices of at least 64×40 (50 seed sets each at 64×40, 80×48, 197×100 and 100×64) every segment that is not wall-anchored keeps both ends ≥ 4 nodes from the walls, no two segments come within λ/2 of each other, and at most 1 in 10 fronts is dropped for want of room, while a 20×8 lattice still gets its one front; `segment_gap` is 0 for crossing segments and the nearest endpoint-to-segment distance otherwise; and a reseed after the first shows dark on screen one sub-step in (brightest level < 51) while the model holds it at full strength (`max u > 0.9`), then brightens to full brightness (level ≥ 217) within 0.5 s, its brightest level never falling from one frame to the next; `BZ-A8` *(changed, M16)* composition at 80×24 with seed 42: at t = 10, 20 and 30 s between 8 % and 50 % of the field's cells are lit, all `Hot` or `Cold` braille on a blank background, and ≥ 15 % of them are crest (`Hot`) cells; at the end of the quench nothing is lit; `BZ-A9` *(new, M16)* a fresh front (u = 1 into resting medium) draws only a `Hot` thread within one node of its edge, the back of a plateau (high `v`) draws nothing, and recovering medium draws only `Cold` dots on the two tail levels, the fresher (`v = 0.42`) brighter.

### boids_flocking  (Living Systems, M10, M12, M15)
- **Intent**: a starling murmuration at dusk. About a thousand specks pour and fold like smoke above the roost, and the flock's texture shifts as it banks. Every 14 s a falcon, the only warm glyph on screen, stoops into it. A bright agitation wave then runs through the flock faster than any bird flies, and the murmuration bulges away from the attack and closes behind it. Birds copy a neighbour's escape manoeuvre (Hemelrijk et al. 2015), so the wave outruns any bird, as Potts (1984) observed in dunlin flocks when he found manoeuvres passing between neighbours faster than a bird's startle reaction (his chorus-line hypothesis).
- **Model**: Reynolds (1987) boids in 2-D (the sky plane) on the square braille dot lattice `W × H = 2w × 4h` dots (CORE-R41; 1 dot ≈ 0.3 m at scale σ = 1). The open sky is `W × H_s`, with `H_s = H − 4` (the label row) when the label is drawn and `H_s = H` otherwise. Population `N = clamp(round(0.1·w·h), 24, 1000)` (80×24 ⇒ 192, 200×50 ⇒ 1000). Length scale `σ = clamp(√(8wh / (80 N)), 0.5, 4)`, which is 1 whenever N is uncapped, so the flock always covers the same share of the sky. **Small sky**: `ρ = clamp((min(W, H_s) − 44)/16, 0, 1)` is 0 up to a 44-dot shorter side (a 12-row terminal with its label) and 1 from 60 dots (16 rows) up. At full size and speed a flock crosses a small sky in about a second and is pressed against its walls, so there the scene uses `σ·lerp(0.7, 1, ρ)` (clamped to [0.5, 4] again), roost stiffness `lerp(2.0, κ, ρ)` s⁻², margin fraction `lerp(0.2, 0.25, ρ)`, and roost amplitudes multiplied by ρ. At 80×24 and larger ρ = 1, and nothing changes. Every length, speed and acceleration below is multiplied by σ, except the wall margin `m`. **Neighbourhood**: the topological `k = 7` nearest within a metric horizon `R = 12` dots (3.6 m), after Ballerini et al. (2008, `n_c ≈ 6.5`) and Hemelrijk & Hildenbrandt (2011, StarDisplay). The core `SpatialHash` (CORE-R54) with cell size `R/4` is rebuilt every substep. Each query first tries the disc reaching the farthest of the bird's previous k neighbours at their current positions (its radius padded by 1e-9 relative, so the square root and the re-squaring never drop that farthest neighbour), and falls back to the full horizon R when that disc holds fewer than k birds. Once a disc holds k birds everything outside it is strictly farther, so the answer is exact for any hint. It keeps the k best in a fixed `[(d², idx); 7]` insertion array, with ties broken by index. The lists `nbr[N][7]` are built once per substep and shared by forces, contagion and rendering. **Acceleration of bird i** is the sum of five terms. (1) Separation `A_s Σ_{j∈N_i, d<r_s} (1 − d/r_s)(x_i − x_j)/d` with `A_s = 150` dots/s² and `r_s = 4` dots (1.2 m). A pair with `d < 1e-6` pushes along ±x instead, and the lower index goes −x. (2) Alignment `β(v̄_N − v_i)` with `β = 5` s⁻¹. (3) Cohesion `γ(x̄_N − x_i)` with `γ = 1` s⁻². (4) Speed relaxation `(v₀ − |v|)/τ_v · v̂` with `v₀ = 30` dots/s (9 m/s, starling cruise) and `τ_v = 0.5` s. (5) Roost attraction `κ(x_roost − x̄) + κ_i(x̄ − x_i)`, where `x̄` is the flock's centroid. The first part, with `κ = 0.45` s⁻², is the same for every bird, so it steers the whole flock around the roost (period `2π/√κ ≈ 9.4` s) without squeezing it. A polarised flock cruising at v₀ orbits the roost at radius `v₀/√κ ≈ 45` dots, which fits the 96-dot height of an 80×24 sky (κ = 0.3 would give 55). The second part keeps strays with the flock: `κ_i = κ` for N ≤ 300, falling log-linearly in N to 0.03 s⁻² at N = 1000, because a trap as stiff as κ on every bird of a large flock squeezes it into a round, evenly grainy ball (BOID-A7). The roost moves on a Lissajous path `(W/2 + a_x sin 2πt/23, H_s/2 + a_y sin 2πt/37)` with `a_x = min(0.25 W, max(0, W/2 − R_f − m))` and `a_y = min(0.2 H_s, max(0, H_s/2 − R_f − m))`, where `R_f = √(12N/π)·σ` is the flock's radius, so a flock at the roost clears the soft-bound margin. Soft bounds add `A_b·min(((m − d_edge)/m)², 4)` inward (`A_b = 200` dots/s²) within `m = 0.25·min(W, H_s)` dots of an edge of the sky; this wide, gentle cushion turns the flock back through the middle of the sky instead of piling it against the frame. The total is capped at `|a| ≤ 250` dots/s². After the velocity update, the velocity is turned by heading noise `θ ~ gaussian(0, √(2 D_θ h))` with `D_θ = 0.1` rad²/s, applied as the small-angle rotation `(v + θ v⊥)/√(1 + θ²)`, and `|v|` is clamped to `[0.5, 2]·v₀`. **Integration**: synchronous semi-implicit Euler at `h = SIM_DT = 1/60` s (all accelerations come from the old state and go into `vel_next`, then the buffers swap), with the substep count from the core `FixedStep(SIM_DT, 15)` (CORE-R9..R11). A specular wall at the edges of the sky `[0, W] × [0, H_s]` (the label row is not sky) reflects the position and negates the normal velocity. It is only a safety net; the soft bounds do the work (BOID-A4). **Falcon**: a looping 14 s `Timeline`. Patrol 7 s: slow 4 s circles of radius `r_c = 0.8 v₀ · 4 s / 2π` (≈ 15 dots) around an anchor over whichever upper corner is farther from the flock, inset `max(0.12 W, r_c + 4σ)` from the side wall and `max(0.2 H_s, r_c + 4σ)` from the top (never past the middle of the sky), at 0.8 v₀ and turning ≤ 2 rad/s. Stoop 2.5 s: speed eased from 0.8 to 3 v₀ with `ease::in_out_cubic`, steering toward the flock centroid predicted 0.5 s ahead, turning ≤ 3 rad/s. Pursue 2 s: 2 v₀, turning ≤ 1.5 rad/s, so the birds can out-turn it. Climb 2.5 s: 1.5 v₀ back onto the patrol circle, which it joins tangentially instead of overshooting the anchor. In every beat it heeds the walls: within `r_c` of an edge of the sky, `3·min(((r_c − d)/r_c)², 4)` along the inward normal (the birds' soft-bound profile with margin `r_c`) is added to its unit desired heading, and while heading into a wall its speed is divided by `1 + 2·max(0, −v̂·push)` (not below 0.8 v₀). Should it still reach an edge of the sky, it reflects specularly, so it never slides along the frame (BOID-A4). It never removes birds. **Escape wave**: Hemelrijk et al. (2015) showed that agitation waves arise only when birds repeat an escape manoeuvre, and that the dark band comes from orientation, not density. Each stoop is an attack with an id, and alarms can fire only inside that attack's stoop and pursue windows. A calm bird fires at most once per attack, in one of two ways. Directly: the falcon is within `r_flee = 12` dots, and the turn sign points away from it. By contagion: a topological neighbour fired at least `L = 2` substeps (33 ms) earlier and is still alarmed; the sign is copied from the lowest-index such neighbour. Fire steps are stored, so the outcome does not depend on update order. An alarmed bird (age < `T_a = 0.35` s = 21 substeps) adds a lateral `160` dots/s² with its sign and relaxes toward `1.3 v₀`. While the falcon is inside `r_flee` it also flees at `200(1 − d/r_flee)` dots/s². The front advances about one neighbour spacing every L substeps, ≈ 100–150 dots/s (30–45 m/s, 3–5 v₀), which is faster than any bird flies; Procaccini et al. (2011) measured wave speeds often above the flock's own speed. **Banking**: the coordinated-turn roll `φ = atan(a_⊥ / (gσ))` is signed (+ = right wing down), with `g = 32.7` dots/s² (9.81 m/s²), and is smoothed as such by an EMA (τ = 0.1 s), so the flickering pushes of separation average out and only a sustained turn banks a bird; the ink and the wings use `|φ|`. An alarmed bird's 160 dots/s² turn banks it past 75°. **Start**: a disc of radius `min(R_f, 0.3·min(W, H_s))` at the roost with uniformly random headings at v₀, so each visit opens with order emerging from chaos. All randomness comes from `ctx.rng` in index order.
- **Motion**: birds cross ≈ 15 cells/s at 200×50, so every step changes the frame. Within 1 s the random disc visibly breaks into aligned streams, and by ≈ 5 s it is one flock (BOID-A3). The flock swings around the wandering roost with a period of ≈ 9 s; at 200×50 it stretches into sheets and folds with dense edge ridges rather than a round ball (BOID-A7). Stoops begin at t ≡ 7 s (mod 14). The wave crosses the flock in under 1 s, and the flock re-closes within ≈ 2 s. The scene never resets, and its state is bounded.
- **Resize**: positions and the falcon are scaled by `(W'/W, H'/H)` and velocities by `σ'/σ`. When N shrinks, the first N' birds are kept. When it grows, new birds are cloned from bird `j mod N` with the same velocity and a `gaussian2` jitter of `r_s·σ'` truncated at `2.5·r_s·σ'` (clamped into the box), and they start calm. σ, `κ_i`, the sky height, the small-sky factor ρ and the margin `m`, the hash and `Dots` are rebuilt, and any bird scaled below the new sky's bottom edge is reflected back by the wall. Bird arrays are allocated at capacity 1000 in `build`, so only the hash buckets and `Dots` reallocate. Timeline time is untouched.
- **Inks**: calm birds are `Role::Structure` (grey-blue dusk silhouettes) at level `80 + 105·min(|φ|/60°, 1)`, which spans 80–185. Alarmed birds are `Role::Star` at 200–255, fading with alarm age. Because `Dots` keeps the brightest dot per cell, the wave always wins over calm neighbours. The falcon is `Role::Hot` 240, the only warm object, with a 0.3 s dot streak in `Role::Hot` 90. Label `Role::Text` 110.
- **ASCII**: each bird is a heading-aware stroke on `Dots`. A level bird lights 2 dots: the head at x and the tail at `x − 1.5σ v̂`. A bird banked past 35° adds its two wing dots at ±1 dot perpendicular to the heading, for 4 dots. Dots that round into the label row are not drawn, so a tail or wing never reaches it. Alarmed birds are banked, so the wave is literally denser, which matches Hemelrijk's orientational bands. The `Dots` fallback (1 `.`, 2 `:`, 3–4 `+`, ≥ 5 `#`) turns the flock into a `.`/`:` haze with `+`/`#` ridges (about a quarter of the lit cells at 200×50) and a `+`/`#` band where the wave passes. The falcon is an 8-way heading glyph `→ ↘ ↓ ↙ ← ↖ ↑ ↗` chosen by `round(atan2(vy, vx)/(π/4))`, with ASCII `> \ v / < \ ^ /`. It is drawn with `Canvas::put` over the blit, in the sky's last row at most.
- **Label**: `N 192  k=7` (ASCII, preformatted in `resize`) right-aligned on the last row (GEN-R13), drawn only when `w ≥ 32` and `h ≥ 10` and it fits right of the title, with a blank cell either side.
- **Perf**: at 200×50 (N = 1000, hash of 134×67 buckets of 3 dots), `update(1/60)` is one substep, dominated by the hinted kNN; with the padded hint no bird falls back to the full horizon. Rasterising ≤ 4000 dots into `Dots` happens inside `update`, and `render` only blits. Criterion on a loaded machine (load average ≈ 30–45, where molecular_dynamics measured 0.73 ms against its 0.55 ms quiet figure): 0.40–0.64 ms at 200×50 and 0.40–0.61 ms at 400×100 (0.40 ms at load ≈ 18; 0.68 ms at load ≈ 80–99 after the margin and ρ were cached in `resize`, with 0.37 ms measured at M10 integration under load ≈ 6–25), against the < 1 ms aim. At dt = 0.25 it takes 15 substeps, but GEN-R8 is measured at dt = 1/60. Steady state is allocation-free.
