- **Acceptance**: `BOID-A1` for seeded clouds (N ∈ {1, 7, 8, 500}, including coincident points and points outside the box), the spatial hash's `k_nearest(i, 7, R)` returns exactly the brute-force set of ≤ 7 nearest within R, excluding i, sorted by (d², index), for any hint radius; and for a seeded 1000-bird cloud, the hint disc `reach` built from each bird's exact 7-neighbour list holds all 7 (no hinted query falls back to R); `BOID-A2` `N`/`σ` follow the formulas (80×24 ⇒ 192/1.0, 200×50 ⇒ 1000/1.0, 400×100 ⇒ 1000/2.0, 1×1 ⇒ 24/0.5), N is constant across updates, a resize from 200×50 to 80×24 keeps the first 192 birds with positions scaled by (W'/W, H'/H), and a resize from 80×24 to 200×50 yields 1000 birds with every clone within `3·r_s·σ'` of its scaled source; `BOID-A3` order emerges: in the seeded 100×30 scene (N = 300; seeds 0, 1 and 2) with the falcon disabled, global polarisation `Φ = |Σ v̂|/N` is < 0.2 at t = 0, and over 1 s samples in t ∈ [8, 20] s the median local alignment `⟨v̂_i · (1/k)Σ_{j∈N_i} v̂_j⟩` (the mean of the unit headings, not renormalised) is ≥ 0.9 and the median order parameter `max(Φ, M)` is ≥ 0.75, where `M = |Σ r̂_i × v̂_i|/N` about the centroid is the milling parameter, since a flock circling its roost is ordered although its Φ is low (wild flocks reach Φ ≈ 0.96, Cavagna et al. 2010); the global bound depends on the seed (a flock bent part-way into a mill, Φ ≈ 0.5 and M ≈ 0.6, misses it on about 2 of seeds 0–11), while median local alignment is ≥ 0.95 on all of them (~3 s in debug); `BOID-A4` over 20 s at 80×24 with attacks (seed 42), every bird is finite and inside the sky `[0, W] × [0, H_s]` after every substep, speed stays in `[0.5, 2]·v₀σ`, the specular wall acts on < 2 % of bird-substeps after t = 5 s, at every 1 s sample after 5 s the median distance to each bird's true nearest neighbour (brute force, at the current positions) is in `[0.35, 1.0]·r_s` (1.4–4 dots ≈ 0.4–1.2 m; Ballerini et al. 2008 report ≈ 0.7–1.5 m), at ≥ 90 % of the 0.5 s samples after 5 s the flock's 2–98 % extent stays at least one cell (2 dots across, 4 down) inside the sky, and the falcon is within 1 dot of an edge of the sky in < 3 % of substeps (~1.2 s in debug); and in a small sky (40×12 and 32×10, seeds 0–2, 60 s each at dt = 1/30, 0.5 s samples) σ is 0.7, the bottom row shows exactly the label with a blank cell either side, and the mean share of birds within 4 dots of a wall of the sky is ≤ 7 % at 40×12 and ≤ 16 % at 32×10 (with ρ forced to 1: ≈ 12 % and ≈ 21 %) (~2 s in debug); `BOID-A5` the alarm is an excitable wave: in the pure `alarm_step` on a chain graph (bird j's only neighbour is j − 1, T_a = 21 substeps), firing bird 0 at step 0 fires bird n at exactly step `n·L` for n ≤ 10, the sign propagates unchanged, and re-exposure within the same attack never refires a bird; in the seeded 100×30 scene, ≥ 80 % of birds have fired within 1.0 s of the first stoop's first alarm and ≤ 20 % of those fired directly, and the front outruns any bird: the least-squares slope of each firing's distance from where the first alarm fired against its time since then exceeds `V_MAX·v₀σ = 2 v₀σ` (≈ 132 dots/s ≈ 4.4 v₀ for seed 21); `BOID-A6` in the seeded 100×30 scene the falcon's beats are patrol [0, 7) / stoop [7, 9.5) / pursue [9.5, 11.5) / climb [11.5, 14) s and repeat at 14 s, every recorded fire step over 30 s lies inside a stoop or pursue window, and in each of the first two attacks the falcon's minimum distance to the flock centroid during stoop plus pursue is < 0.25 of that distance at stoop start; `BOID-A7` a large flock is not a round ball: `κ_i(N)` equals κ for N ≤ 300, never increases with N and equals 0.03 s⁻² at N = 1000; and in the seeded 200×50 scene (seed 42, with attacks), over 0.5 s samples in t ∈ [8, 40] s, the median principal-axis ratio `√(λ_max/λ_min)` of the positions' covariance is ≥ 1.5 and `+`/`#` make up ≤ 40 % of the lit cells of the ASCII frames (a per-bird trap of κ on every bird gives ≈ 55 %).

### dna_helix  (Molecules of Life, M10, M12, M15)
- **Intent**: the double helix of Watson and Crick's 1953 figure: two backbones winding right-handed around a ladder of coloured base pairs and turning in space. Then a replication fork runs through it. A helicase ring unzips the ladder, and each separated strand is copied into a daughter helix that is half old (grey) and half new (magenta): the leading strand continuously, the lagging strand in Okazaki fragments.
- **Model**: B-DNA in Å, with world +y up (CORE-R45 maps it to screen up) and the axis along +x.
  - **Helix geometry**:
    - Twist is `Ω = 2π/10.5` per bp, i.e. 34.29° (Wang 1979), with a rise of 3.4 Å, so the pitch is 35.7 Å.
    - The phosphorus backbone radius is `R = 8.9 Å` (Arnott & Hukins 1972).
    - The Watson angle is `θ_W(x, t) = φ(t) + 2πx/35.7 Å`. Crick is `θ_C = θ_W + Δ`, with `Δ = 2.2 rad` from C1′–C1′ = 10.5 Å at r = 5.9 Å (Saenger 1984). That gives axial groove widths of 12.5 Å (minor) and 23.2 Å (major).
    - A backbone point is `(x, y₀ + R cos θ, R sin θ)` about its duplex axis at height `y₀`. θ increases with x, so the helix is right-handed. Watson runs 5′→3′ along +x.
  - **Layout**: base pair `i` sits at `xᵢ = x_L + (i − 4.5)·3.4 Å`, where `x_L` is the world x at column 0, so column 0 falls midway between bp 4 and bp 5. Four pairs lie beyond each screen edge, so the camera sway never exposes a blunt end.
  - **Rungs**: a chord at `xᵢ` between the two backbones (the phosphorus positions), split at its midpoint so each half shows that strand's base. Rungs are drawn only every `stride`-th pair, so at least 2 columns separate them, and a rung seen end-on at a backbone crossing (a slanted chord under 2 rows, over 4 rows, or flatter than 1 column per 2 rows) is culled rather than drawn as a diagonal along a row. *Simplifications*: joining phosphorus positions puts each chord ≈ 4 Å off the axis on the minor-groove side (real B-DNA base pairs sit on it), and the helicase is a symbolic collar, not to scale.
  - **Sequence**: `N_MAX = 512` Watson bases in a stack array (enough to span the width at any aspect ratio up to w/h ≈ 50; beyond that the height fit wins and the duplex ends short of the right edge). The GC count is a seeded uniform integer in [⌈0.35·N_MAX⌉, ⌊0.65·N_MAX⌋] (each G or C at random), placed by a Fisher–Yates shuffle from `ctx.rng`. Crick is the complement.
  - **Spin**: `φ(t) = φ₀ − ω·t` on loop-local time, which wraps at the `reset` midpoint (where the fade is 0), so the apex angle is identical in every loop, with `ω = 2π·v_f/10.5 = 1.7952 rad/s`. That is the unwinding a fork at `v_f = 3 bp/s` needs, so the apex angle stays fixed. The unreplicated duplex spins as if its far end were free; in vivo it cannot rotate, and topoisomerases instead relax the positive supercoils that build ahead of the fork.
  - **Fork**: the apex is at `f(τ) = v_f·τ` bp, where τ is the time since `unwind` began. It keeps advancing through `hold` and is frozen during `reset`. There is no fork during `helix`. `v_f` is ≈ 6–17× slower than human forks (1–3 kb/min) and ≈ 300× slower than *E. coli* (≈ 1 kb/s).
  - **Arms**: behind the fork (`s = f − i > 0`) the parental strands separate into two arms, displaced by `±Y(s) = ±1.7R·smoothstep(s/6)` in world y.
    - The upper arm (+y) is the Watson template, which is the lagging-strand template. A DnaB-style helicase ring encircles it at the apex, translocating 5′→3′.
    - The lower arm is the Crick template (the leading strand).
    - Each new strand takes its partner's angular slot (Watson + Δ, or Crick − Δ), so each daughter is a B duplex like the parent. Keeping B geometry in the arms is artistic licence; real single-stranded DNA at the fork is unwound.
  - **Synthesis** is 5′→3′ only:
    - Leading: new base `j` is made at `f(τ) − 2 = j`, i.e. `τ = (j + 2)/3` (the polymerase trails the helicase by 2 bp).
    - Lagging: Okazaki fragments of `L_ok = 8` bp (scaled down from 100–200 nt in eukaryotes and 1–2 kb in bacteria) cover `[8k, 8k + 8)`. Fragment `k` is primed at its high-x end when `f ≥ 8k + 9` (`τ_k = (8k + 9)/3`) and extended toward decreasing x at `v_pol = 8 bp/s`, so base `j` is made at `τ_k + (8k + 7 − j)/8`. The fragment is complete 7/8 s after priming, and ligase seals the nick 0.5 s after completion.
    - Template bases that are not yet paired show as half-rungs.
  - Every quantity is a closed-form function of scene time; nothing is integrated.
- **Motion**: a `Timeline` of `helix` 4 s → `unwind` 14 s → `hold` 4 s → `reset` 2 s, looping every 24 s.
  - **Spin**: from the first frame the spin reads as a barber-pole drift along +x at 10.2 Å/s. That equals the fork speed, so the fork travels with the pattern. It is ≈ 7 columns/s at 80×24 (≈ 11 while zoomed) and ≈ 16 at 200×50.
  - **Camera**:
    - Yaw is `0.2·sin(2πt/19 s)`, capped at `asin(13 Å / visible half-width)` so the edges swing ≤ 13 Å in depth; pitch is 0.18 rad, and distance is `max(40R, 6·visible half-width)` (gentle perspective).
    - A zoom `z(t)` is 2.2 during `helix`. It eases to 1 over the first 3 s of `unwind` (`in_out_cubic`) and stays 1 until the `reset` midpoint, where it steps back to 2.2 while every level is 0.
    - The zoom is anchored at the left edge, so column 0 stays between bp 4 and bp 5, and the dolly-out opens room for the arms.
  - The fork enters at τ ≈ 1.3 s and leaves the screen at τ ≈ 12.4 s at 80×24 (≈ 14.1 s at 200×50). Every visible fragment is sealed before `reset` at both sizes.
  - Fresh nucleotides are drawn at level 255 and fade linearly to their normal level over 0.4 s.
  - `reset` fades the daughters out (1 s). At its midpoint, while the level is zero, it draws a new sequence, and it then fades the parental helix back in (1 s).
- **Resize**: view only; there is no lattice (ADR-0012).
  - The zoom-1 scale is `min(max(h − 2, 1)/(6.6·R), w/(2·35.7))` rows/Å, floored so N_MAX pairs still span the width at extreme aspect ratios. The 6.6 covers the open fork's ±2.7R plus pitch, sway and perspective, as DNA-A4 checks. The width term keeps at least one turn visible on narrow, tall terminals.
  - The pair count is `n = min(⌈w/cols_per_bp + 0.5⌉ + 8, 512)`: ≈ 42 at 80×24 (2.4 columns/bp, R ≈ 3.1 rows) and ≈ 47 at 200×50 (5.2 columns/bp, R ≈ 6.9 rows).
  - The two `Dots` layers are resized.
  - Scene time and the sequence are kept, and column 0 stays between bp 4 and bp 5.
  - Small views: the fork runs only when the zoom-1 backbone radius `R·scale` is ≥ 1.75 rows (h ≳ 14; 50×14 has it, 40×12 does not). Below that the arms, daughters and helicase share too few rows and knot into braille, so the view shows the intact helix at zoom 2.2 all cycle: no fork, arms or helicase, while the `reset` fade and the new sequence at its midpoint still run. The `Story` itself is unchanged; only what the view shows is.
  - Large views: from `R·scale` ≥ 4.5 rows (h ≳ 32, e.g. 120×36 and 200×50) near-layer backbone lines get a twin one dot along the screen normal, so the near strands are 2 dots wide. A 1-dot thread would otherwise be outweighed by the full-cell `│` rungs, and the near/far depth cue needs the area. Far strands stay 1 dot, so they recede.
- **Inks**:
  - Backbones: parental `Structure` and new `Accent`, so semiconservative replication (Meselson & Stahl 1958) shows as two colours.
  - Bases follow the four-dye sequencing convention: A `Particle` (green), T `Hot` (red), G `Sun` (yellow), C `Cold` (blue).
  - Helicase `Wave`.
  - The backbone depth cue is `level = 70 + 160·(z_local + R)/(2R)`, where `z_local` is the camera-space depth of the point minus that of its duplex axis at the same x. Rungs are drawn at `max(0.9 × backbone level, 110)`.
  - Every level is multiplied by the `reset` fade.
  - Label `Text`, with the bases in their base inks.
- **ASCII**: backbones are two `Dots` layers (far `z_local < 0`, near `≥ 0`), which fall back to `. : + #` by dot count. Rungs are `|`, or `/` and `\` when the sway slants them. The helicase is a 0.35R braille collar split across the far and near layers, marked in ASCII by two `o` cells at its top and bottom.
  - Draw order: far dots → rungs (painter-sorted by depth) → helicase → near dots → label.
  - At 80×24 the frame shows dotted strands braiding across the screen over a comb of `|` rungs; in the fork phase they split into a sideways Y.
- **Label**: top-left (row 0, col 1), e.g. `5'-GATTACAGCCTAGGTC-3'`, in ASCII. It shows 16 Watson bases when the width is ≥ 60, or 8 when it is ≥ 30.
  - The window starts at the first on-screen pair (bp 5), 5′ first, and is fixed for the cycle: it changes only at the `reset` midpoint, with the new sequence.
  - It is dropped below 30 columns or 6 rows and is never clipped.
- **Perf**: at 200×50 the backbones are sampled about one dot of arc apart: ≈ 800 samples per strand × 4 strands in the fork ≈ 3.2k projections and plots ≈ 70 µs, plus a twin line for each near segment on bold views (≈ 1.6k more lines, no extra projections).
  - Rungs: up to ≈ 94 (two daughters) × ≈ 12 cells, plus a sort of the visible rungs in a `Vec` reserved in `resize` (allocation-free after it).
  - Two layer blits ≈ 20 µs.
  - The `Dots` are rasterised at the end of `update` and `resize`, because `render` takes `&self`.
  - Total ≈ 0.2 ms per frame at 30 FPS, and ≈ 1.5 ms per frame in the 15-step low-power worst case. There is no heap use after resize.
- **Acceptance**: `DNA-A1` B-DNA geometry: the twist is `2π/10.5` per bp (34.2857°), the rise 3.4 Å and the pitch 35.7 Å; with `R = 8.9 Å` and `Δ = 2.2 rad` the axial groove widths are 12.50 ± 0.05 Å (minor) and 23.20 ± 0.05 Å (major), summing to the pitch, and the C1′ chord `2·5.9·sin(Δ/2)` is 10.5 ± 0.1 Å; both strands are right-handed, i.e. the triple product `(a × b)·c` of any three successive world chord vectors at 0.5 bp steps is > 0; `DNA-A2` rendering is not mirrored: through the scene's own projection at `yaw = 0`, `pitch = 0.18`, zoom 1, every parental backbone sample with `z_local ≥ 0.25R` lies on a segment with screen slope `dy/dx > 0` (a `\` slant with y down, as a right-handed helix along +x requires), world +y projects to a smaller row, and at equal x a near sample's level exceeds a far sample's; at 200×50 and 120×36 the near braille layer lights ≥ 1.5× as many dots as the far layer (bold near strands), and at 80×24 and 40×12 ≤ 1.3× (both 1 dot wide); `DNA-A3` Watson–Crick pairing: sequences use only A, C, G, T; `comp` is an involution with A↔T and G↔C; `Crick[i] = comp(Watson[i])`, so the duplex has #A = #T and #G = #C (Chargaff); for 64 seeds, every drawn sequence's realised GC fraction over all N_MAX = 512 bp lies in [0.35, 0.65]; once synthesis at bp `i` is complete, both daughter pairs at `i` equal the parental pair `(Watson[i], Crick[i])`, and each daughter holds exactly one parental strand (semiconservative); `DNA-A4` fork kinematics: `f(τ) = 3τ`, and the spin `ω = 2π·3/10.5 = 1.79520 rad/s` keeps the apex backbone angle constant through `unwind` and `hold` (`|Δθ| < 1e-9` rad); no pair with `i ≥ f(τ)` is ever separated; `Y(s)` is 0 for `s ≤ 0`, non-decreasing, and equal to `1.7R` for `s ≥ 6`; at 200 sampled times over a cycle, at 80×24 and 200×50, every projected backbone sample of every visible strand lies within rows `[1, h)`, and bp 0 projects left of column 0; views with `R·scale` < 1.75 rows (40×12, 40×13, 200×10) show no fork at any time (no τ, zoom 2.2, the story's fade and draws kept, no half-rungs or helicase in mid-`unwind`), while 50×14 and 80×24 show the story unchanged; `DNA-A5` synthesis is 5′→3′ only: leading base `j` is made at `τ = (j + 2)/3`, so leading synthesis times increase strictly with `j` and no leading base is within 2 bp of the apex; lagging bases come in 8 bp fragments, each primed only once `f ≥ 8k + 9`, with synthesis times strictly decreasing in `j` within a fragment; every fragment is complete before its successor is primed, and each nick is sealed exactly 0.5 s after completion; at 80×24 and 200×50 every visible base of both daughters is synthesised and every visible nick sealed before `reset` begins; `DNA-A6` the story is a pure function of time: driving the pure `Story` state (fork index, synthesised bases with their ages, sealed nicks, phase, zoom, fade) with 1/60 s steps and with a seeded irregular sequence of `dt ∈ [0, 0.25]` to the same time gives identical state and identical RNG position; the timeline is `helix` 4 → `unwind` 14 → `hold` 4 → `reset` 2 s and loops at 24 s; exactly one new sequence is drawn per cycle, at the `reset` midpoint, and for seed 42 consecutive cycles' sequences differ; the zoom changes discontinuously only at an instant where the fade level is 0.

### electron_orbitals  (States of Matter, M10, M12, M15)
- **Intent**: the hydrogen atom as its quantum states actually look. A Monte-Carlo cloud of electron positions is drawn from |ψ_nlm|² and turns slowly in 3-D. Lobes are coloured by the sign of the wavefunction, and the nucleus glints through the nodes. Between states the atom passes through a real superposition, and the charge cloud sloshes from lobe to lobe in a calm breath: the true Bohr beat, slowed by a factor the label states. That oscillating dipole is how the atom emits or absorbs Lyman-α, Hα or Paschen-α light. The cloud is centred. The r₉₀ surface of 4f spans 0.75 of the height, and smaller n is drawn smaller but never as a dot. Point density is matched to each orbital's on-screen size, so 1s is a speckled ball, not a solid disc, and 4f's lobes are not sparse. The eye reads lobe shape first, then the colour of the sign, then the corner label. Nothing else is on screen.
- **Model**: atomic units, a₀ = 1. `ψ = R_nl(r)·Y(r̂)` with `R_nl = √((2/n)³(n−l−1)!/(2n(n+l)!)) e^{−ρ/2} ρ^l L^{(2l+1)}_{n−l−1}(ρ)`, ρ = 2r/n (generalised Laguerre by its three-term recurrence). Real harmonics on the unit direction (x, y, z): `s = 1/√(4π)`, `p_z = √(3/4π) z`, `p_x = √(3/4π) x`, `d_z² = √(5/16π)(3z² − 1)`, `d_xz = √(15/4π) xz`, `d_xy = √(15/4π) xy`, `f_z³ = √(7/16π)(5z³ − 3z)`, `f_xyz = √(105/4π) xyz`. The gallery is a closed dipole-allowed loop (Δl = ±1): 1s → 2p_z → 3d_z² → 4f_z³ → 3d_xz → 4f_xyz → 3d_xy → 2p_x → 1s. Energies are `E_n = −R_H/n²` with `R_H = R∞hc·μ/mₑ = 13.5983 eV` (reduced mass). Lines are `λ = hc/ΔE` with hc = 1239.842 eV·nm: Lyα 121.57 nm, Hα 656.47 nm, Pα 1875.6 nm (vacuum). Sampling is exact and separable, because every real harmonic here is a product Θ(θ)·Φ(φ): `|ψ|² d³r = [r²R² dr]·[Θ² d cosθ]·[Φ² dφ]`. Each factor is tabulated once as a 1024-knot inverse CDF (r ∈ [0, 1.5·r₉₉.₉], cos θ ∈ [−1, 1], φ ∈ [0, 2π)). One sample is three linear-interpolated lookups driven by `ctx.rng` uniforms. Each point also stores fixed uniforms `u_i` (visibility) and `v_i` (mixture), plus ψ of its own orbital and of its partner (f32 storage). Superposition a → b: `Ψ = c_a ψ_a e^{−iE_a t} + c_b ψ_b e^{−iE_b t}` with `c_a = cos χ`, `c_b = sin χ`, so `|Ψ|² = c_a²ψ_a² + c_b²ψ_b² + 2c_a c_b ψ_a ψ_b cos ωt`. Display sampler (exact intensity, per-orbital density): orbital k uses a prefix of `M_k = clamp(round(0.8·π(4ρ_k)²), 256, 16384)` iid points, where ρ_k is its on-screen r₉₀ radius in rows (View), which gives ≈ 0.4 visible points per dot inside r₉₀. For a → b: `M(χ) = (c_a²/M_a + c_b²/M_b)⁻¹`, `p_a = M(χ)c_a²/M_a` and `p_b = M(χ)c_b²/M_b`, so p_a + p_b = 1. Point i < M_a of slot S_a is proposed iff `v_i < p_a`, and point i < M_b of S_b iff `v_i < p_b`, so proposals have intensity exactly `M(χ)·q` with `q = c_a²ψ_a² + c_b²ψ_b²`. A proposed point is visible iff `u_i < w/2`, where `w = |Ψ|²/q = 1 + 2c_a c_b ψ_a ψ_b cos ωt / q ∈ [0, 2]` (AM–GM), evaluated in f64 (q ≤ 0 or non-finite ⇒ hidden). The visible points therefore have expected density exactly `(M(χ)/2)·|Ψ(t)|²` at every instant, and their count glides from M_a/2 to M_b/2. Display fade: on screen a point is proposed iff `v_i < p_k·τ(r)`, where τ is 1 inside `r_in` and falls linearly to 0 at `r_out`. The window is (r₈₈, r₉₅) of the states on screen (the wider (r₉₀, r₉₇) left lone single-dot braille cells a few columns out from the lobes, which read as grain rather than cloud), blended geometrically (`exp(lerp(ln r_a, ln r_b, c_b²))` componentwise, exactly a's at c_b² = 0 and b's at 1) and shared by both slots, so the displayed density is exactly `τ(r)·(M(χ)/2)|Ψ|²`, and the far dust ends inside the frame. Given a proposal, `v_i/p_k` is a fixed uniform independent of `u_i`, so the fade is shimmer-free. Because u and v are fixed per point, the cloud changes only where thresholds are crossed: no shimmer, no pops. At the endpoints c² (and therefore p) is set to exactly 0 or 1. The centroid follows `⟨r⟩(t) = sin 2χ·d_ab cos ωt` with `d_ab = ⟨a|r|b⟩`: 0.7449, 2.452, 5.188, 2.995, 3.867, 3.867, 2.123 and 0.7449 a₀ around the loop. Time is slowed, and the label says by how much: the beat is the true Bohr frequency ΔE/h (h = 4.135667696×10⁻¹⁵ eV·s; Lyα 2.466 PHz, Hα 456.7 THz, Pα 159.8 THz) divided by a round per-line factor S = 4×10¹⁵ / 10¹⁵ / 5×10¹⁴, so `f_disp = ΔE/(h·S)` = 0.62 / 0.46 / 0.32 Hz for Lyα / Hα / Pα: a calm breath with 1.8 / 1.4 / 1.0 cycles per 3 s beat, still ordered by ΔE as the physics is. (The previous `2.0 Hz·√(ΔE/ΔE_Lyα)` compression made 1s–2p_z flip every 0.25 s, a vibrating blob 2–4× faster than anything else in the scene.) ω = 2π f_disp with `t` counted from the rise start; a pair that is not a gallery α line would have no line, no factor and no beat. Timeline per orbital: `hold` 6 s (pure; this is χ = π/2 of the (prev, a) pair and χ = 0 of the (a, next) pair, which give the same visible set), `rise` 2 s (χ 0 → π/4, smoothstep), `beat` 3 s (χ = π/4, full sloshing), `fall` 2 s (χ π/4 → π/2). That is 13 s per orbital and a 104 s loop, starting at a seeded orbital's hold. Two-slot ring: during a's hold the free slot is filled with S_next, and S_a's partner ψ is rewritten to ψ_next (≤ 256 points per 1/60 s of dt; any remainder completes at the rise boundary). View: orbital (x, y, z) maps to world (x, z, −y), so orbital z is screen-up. One world unit is the orbital's r₉₀ times `g_k = (r₉₀,k/r₉₀,4f)^{0.3}` (r₉₀ = 2.66 / 7.99 / 15.80 / 25.99 a₀ for 1s / 2p / 3d / 4f), so `ρ_k = R·g_k` with `R = fit_radius(stage, 0.75)`, which is 0.50 R, 0.70 R, 0.86 R and R. The stage is every row above the label (all rows when the label is hidden), and the nucleus sits in its middle cell with the projected origin at that cell's centre, so a nodal plane through the nucleus empties one whole row. During transitions ln r₉₀ and ln g are lerped with the weight `glide(c_b²)`, so the larger state of the pair sets the scale while it holds at least half the weight: `1 − (1 − 2w)₊²` into a larger state, `(2w − 1)₊²` out of one. A plain c_b² would draw the larger state bigger than in its own hold (× 1.47 for 1s → 2p_z at w = ½) and push its dust off the frame. Camera: `geom::Camera` with distance 6 and a per-state pose (α_k yaw offset, p_k pitch, rock amplitude, phase φ_k), lerped component-wise with weight c_b²: 1s, 2p_z, 3d_z² and 4f_z³ (0, 0.15, 0.6, 0); 2p_x and 3d_xz (0, 0.15, 0.25, π/2); 4f_xyz (π/4, 0, 0.8, 0); 3d_xy (0, 1.05, 0.6, 0). On the Timeline clock t, with θ = 2π(t − 3 s)/13 s + φ_k, `yaw = α_k + rock·cos θ` and `pitch = p_k − 0.10·sin θ`. One rock period is one orbital's 13 s visit, so the 104 s loop is exactly periodic and every hold sees the same part of the rock: the rock peaks at mid-hold (3 s in), and states with φ_k = π/2 pass through broadside there. The pitch wobble is in quadrature, so the camera always turns at ≥ 0.10·2π/13 s ≈ 2.8°/s and never stalls. Pose rationale: m = 0 states sit low, so their nodal planes stay open (at pitch 0.32 the 2p_z gap fills in). 2p_x and 3d_xz have a vertical nodal plane that is edge-on at yaw 0 and fills in when turned further (at ±0.6 the 2p_x node column holds up to 0.86 of the lobe column's dots, ≤ 0.26 at ±0.25). 4f_xyz is turned 45° and kept level, so its z = 0 nodal plane stays an open gap through the nucleus between two tiers of lobes; at mid-hold the rock turns it to yaw π/4 + 0.8 ≈ π/2, where its vertical nodal planes are edge-on and the lobes stand as four `+#` blocks (±0.6 never separates them). 3d_xy lies in the horizontal plane and is seen from above. The camera only rotates and scales the whole atom, so none of this touches the physics. References: Bethe & Salpeter (1957) for ⟨r⟩, ⟨r²⟩ and d(1s–2p).
- **Motion**: the camera rocks about each state's pose, never slower than 2.8°/s and at up to 0.6·2π/13 s ≈ 17°/s of yaw (0.8 rad for 4f_xyz: ≈ 22°/s), with the pitch nodding in quadrature. Even a 1s or m = 0 state, whose shape is invariant under yaw, visibly turns because the finite sample rotates, so the frame changes within the first second (GEN-R9). Otherwise a stationary state stays still, as it does in nature; there is no resampling shimmer. Every 13 s there is a transition. The second lobe grows in, the cloud breathes between lobes at f_disp (1s–2p_z swells up and down once every 1.6 s, the 3d–4f pairs roll over once in about 3 s), and then the new orbital settles and the framing eases to its size. Every 15 s visit sees at least one transition.
- **Resize**: samples live in atomic units and don't depend on the terminal size. They are drawn in the first resize (current and next slot) and then during holds. A resize rescales the view, resizes `Dots` and recomputes every M_k. When an M_k grows past the sampled count, the resize tops up that live slot (allocation is allowed there). Timeline, samples and uniforms are kept. Zero-area sizes make plotting a no-op. ADR-0012 needs no lattice.
- **Inks**: the cloud uses `Role::Div`. The sign is that of the dominant component at the point, `sign(c_kψ_k)` with `k = argmax_k |c_kψ_k|`; in holds this is exactly the orbital's phase sign, and it never blinks at ω. The level is `ink::div(±(0.44 + 0.56·near))`, i.e. `128 ± (56 + 71·near)`, so both signs stay clear of the dark zero and nearer dots are more saturated: cyan negative, orange positive. Each cell keeps its most intense dot by `|level − 128|` (CORE-R41), so a negative-lobe cell shows its nearest dot. The nucleus is `Accent` 180 (`Glyph::new('•', 'o')`), drawn before the cloud so it shows only through nodes (l ≥ 1). While a state with l ≥ 1 is on screen it is drawn again over the cloud when at most 2 of its cell's 8 dots are lit, since a lobe dot or two from the tilt would otherwise hide it; the 1s hold keeps it covered. Row h−1 takes no dots while the label shows. The label is `Text` 150.
- **ASCII**: `Dots` fallback by dot count (`.`, `:`, `+`, `#`). 1s is a speckled round ball, `+#` at the core fading through `:` to `.`. 2p_z is two stacked `+#` lobes with a sparse `.` equatorial band and the `o` nucleus between them. 3d_z² is two lobes plus a `.:` equatorial ring. 4f_z³ is two polar lobes and two conical `+` rings. 4f_xyz shows its eight cube-diagonal lobes as two tiers split by an open gap through the `o` nucleus, and at mid-hold as four `+#` blocks split by a cross. Labels use their ASCII variants.
- **Label**: right-aligned on row h−1 (GEN-R13), static strings only (GEN-R11). Hold: `3d_z²` (ASCII `3d_z2`). Transition: `2p_z → 3d_z²  Hα 656.5 nm  slowed 10¹⁵` (ASCII `2p_z -> 3d_z2  H-a 656.5 nm  slowed 1e15`), and likewise `Lyα 121.6 nm  slowed 4×10¹⁵` / `Ly-a … slowed 4e15` and `Pα 1876 nm  slowed 5×10¹⁴` / `P-a … slowed 5e14`: the one liberty the scene takes with time is stated on screen. Parts are dropped, never clipped, to fit `title::readout_room`: first the slow-down part, then the line part, then the arrow part (leaving the current state's name), and the whole label when `w < 14` or `h < 8`.
- **Perf**: estimated at 200×50 with M_k ≤ 16384. The v-test runs over ≤ 2 × 16384 f32 SoA points, weights are computed for about half of them, and ≤ 8 k visible points are projected and plotted: ≈ 0.15 ms. The camera rotation is hoisted out of the loop (`Camera::projector`). `Dots` clear and blit of 10 k cells ≈ 0.05 ms. Slot preparation of ≤ 256 samples plus ψ pairs per 1/60 s ≈ 0.08 ms. Total ≈ 0.3 ms (aim < 1 ms; measured 0.16–0.31 ms at 200×50 on a loaded machine). The worst frame is a resize late in a hold, which forces synchronous completion of a 16384-point slot (≈ 2 ms in release, still < 5 ms). Memory: two SoA slots of 7 × 16384 f32 (≈ 0.9 MB) and 192 KB of tables (8 samplers × 3 `InverseCdf<1024>`).
- **Acceptance**: `ORB-A1` every gallery orbital satisfies ∫R²r² dr = 1 (trapezoid, h = 0.01, r ≤ 90; 1e-6) and ∫Y² dΩ = 1 (48-point Gauss–Legendre × 96 φ, 1e-9), and distinct gallery orbitals are orthogonal (|⟨i|j⟩| < 1e-9); `ORB-A2` quadrature gives ⟨r⟩ = (3n² − l(l+1))/2 (1.5 / 5 / 10.5 / 18 a₀) and ⟨r²⟩ = n²(5n² + 1 − 3l(l+1))/2 (1e-6 relative), and 20 000 sampler draws per orbital give a mean r within 4σ of ⟨r⟩ and ⟨cos²θ⟩ within 4σ of 3/5 (2p_z) and 11/21 (3d_z²); `ORB-A3` ⟨1s|z|2p_z⟩ = 2^{15/2}/3⁵ = 0.74494 a₀ (1e-4), every consecutive gallery pair including the wrap has |⟨a|r|b⟩| equal to the Model's d_ab table (1e-3) and > 0.5 a₀, and the line wavelengths are 121.57, 656.47 and 1875.6 nm within 0.1 % and agree with the label strings as rounded, and every consecutive gallery pair (wrap included) is labelled with its own line (a pair that is not an α line shows no line part and has no beat rather than a wrong one); ΔE_Lyα/h = 2.466 PHz (0.1 %), and each line's beat is exactly ΔE/(h·S) with S the factor its label prints (ASCII `4e15` parses to S, Unicode `4×10¹⁵` / `10¹⁵` matches it): 0.6165 / 0.4567 / 0.3197 Hz (1e-3), each ≤ 0.7 Hz, decreasing with ΔE, and ≥ 0.9 cycles per 3 s beat; `ORB-A4` exact superposition: for 1s–2p_z at χ = π/4 with M_a = M_b = 40 000, the visible count is 20 000 within 4σ and the visible centroid z̄ equals 0.7449·cos ωt within 4 SE at cos ωt ∈ {1, 0, −1}, with x̄ and ȳ zero within 4 SE; with M_a = 10 000 and M_b = 40 000 the visible count is M(χ)/2 = 8 000 within 4σ; the display fade is exact: for 1s–2p_z at χ = π/4, cos ωt = 1 and M_a = M_b = 40 000 with the c_b² = ½ window, the displayed count matches `(M/2)∫τ|Ψ|²` = 10 000·(∫τR_1s²r² dr + ∫τR_2p²r² dr) within 4σ and z̄ matches `(∫τR_1sR_2p r³ dr)/√3` over half that bracket within 4 SE (both by quadrature; per-slot windows missed by 23σ and 4.5 SE); after a slot switch the live slot carries its new partner's ψ (at the 2p_z → 3d_z² beat z̄ = ±⟨2p_z|z|3d_z²⟩ = ±2.452 a₀ within 4 SE); `ORB-A5` no pops: the displayed set is identical at the end of each phase and the start of the next (hold→rise and fall→hold, including the slot switch), and over a full 1s → 2p_z transition at 200×50 (dt = 1/60) no step toggles more than 6 % of the sampler's visible count (the expected bound is sin 2χ·(ω dt/2)·∫|ψ_aψ_b| d³r ≤ 1.35 % of M(χ), i.e. 2.7 % of the visible count at Lyα's 0.62 Hz with ∫|ψ_1sψ_2p| = 0.419, plus ≤ 2 % from χ drift; measured 4.7 %), nor more than 7 % of the displayed count, which adds the drift of the fade window as it widens 3× (measured 5.1 %); `ORB-A6` the Timeline visits 1s, 2p_z, 3d_z², 4f_z³, 3d_xz, 4f_xyz, 3d_xy, 2p_x in that order with 6 / 2 / 3 / 2 s phases and a 104 s loop, and the label is pure ASCII in ASCII mode, complete at 80×24 (slow-down included), right-aligned on row h−1 within `title::readout_room` chars: at h = 12 the slow-down part needs w ≥ 80, the line part w ≥ 54 beside the phenomenon and w ≥ 64 once the full title shows (from w = 55), and 3d_xz → 4f_xyz w ≥ 34 (33 in Unicode), and it is absent when `w < 14` or `h < 8`; over one loop at dt = 0.25 no dot lands on the label row (80×24, 40×12, 14×8); `ORB-A7` composition at 80×24 (seed 42; the first three at yaw 0.3, pitch 0.15): in the 1s hold ≤ 5 % of lit cells are full 8-dot cells (measured ≈ 2 %); in the 4f_z³ hold ≥ 16 rows hold ≥ 3 lit cells; in the 2p_z hold the nucleus row (the nodal plane) has ≤ 60 % of the lit cells of the fullest row (measured ≈ 40 %). With each hold's own camera swept over a whole rock cycle: 3d_xy spans ≥ 0.6 of 4f_z³'s rows, and a ring at 0.5ρ_k about the nucleus meets ≥ 4 sign runs for 3d_xy and 3d_xz (clovers); the 2p_x nucleus column holds ≤ 40 % of the fullest column within ±3 (measured ≤ 26 %), and 2p_x is broadside (yaw 0) at mid-hold; the nucleus glyph shows through the 2p_z node in ≥ 80 % of frames; the 4f_xyz nucleus row holds ≤ 50 % of the fullest row's lit cells; its centre band (nucleus column ± 1) empties to ≤ 15 % of the fullest lobe column for ≥ 16 of 52 clocks; and at its mid-hold yaw is π/2 within 0.02 and the nucleus column holds ≤ 60 % of the fullest lobe column (four blocks, not two slabs), with the 104 s loop a whole number of rock cycles. Every pose's camera turns at ≥ 2°/s throughout. No hold leaves more than 1 lit cell per frame beyond 1.25ρ_k (the far dust fades out). In the 2p_z hold every Div cell has |div value| ≥ 0.43, and both signs light > 20 cells. Over one loop at dt = 0.1 (80×24 and 200×50) no dot reaches the outermost stage rows in a hold or beat, and the χ ramps leave ≤ 0.1 cells per frame on them.

### globular_cluster  (Deep Cosmos, M10, M12, M15, M16)
- **Intent**: an ancient globular cluster seen through a slowly turning telescope. A blazing, unresolved core thins into a sparse halo of single stars, with a handful of orange giants glinting among them. Every visible halo star is a live gravitating body: the swarm breathes, and the camera's slow turn gives it depth through parallax. It is calm, dense and three-dimensional, the stellar counterpart to the flat `starfield` and the disk-shaped `galaxy_collision`.
- **Model**:
  - **Units and softening**: direct N-body in Hénon units (`G = M = 1`, virial radius 1, `E ≈ −1/4`), equal masses `m = 1/N`, Plummer softening `Φ_ij = −G m²/√(r² + ε²)` with `ε = 0.05` (0.085 of the Plummer scale radius `a = 3π/16`).
  - **Forces and integration**: an O(N²/2) Newton-3 symmetric pair loop, so `Σ m a = 0` to rounding. `ode::verlet_integrate` (KDK leapfrog, CORE-R44) runs with `h_max = 1/128` N-body units at a rate of `0.4` N-body units per simulated second: one force evaluation per frame at `dt = 1/60`, 13 at `dt = 0.25`. The crossing time `t_cr = 2√2` is 7.1 s.
  - **ICs**: Plummer sphere `ρ ∝ (1 + r²/a²)^{−5/2}`, sampled per Aarseth, Hénon & Wielen (1974) in `G = M = a = 1`:
    - Radius `r = (X₁^{−2/3} − 1)^{−1/2}` with mass cut `X₁ < 0.999` (so `r ≤ 38.7 a`).
    - Isotropic directions (`z = 1 − 2u`, `φ = 2πu`).
    - Speed `v = q·√2(1 + r²)^{−1/4}`, with `q` from `g(q) = q²(1 − q²)^{7/2}` by rejection under the bound 0.1 (the maximum is 0.0923).
    - COM position and velocity subtracted.
    - Positions scaled so the unsoftened `W = −1/2` (then `a ≈ 3π/16` and `r_h ≈ 0.769`).
    - The density centre moved to the origin: 4 passes that each subtract the mean position of the bodies inside the half-mass radius. The heavy tail (`r_rms ≈ 3.0 a` with the 0.999 cut) puts the COM ≈ `r_rms/√N` off the body core (0.08 units at N = 500, 0.22 at N = 64). The view and the glow are centred on the origin, so without this the core would sit off the glow and swing around it as the camera turns. It is a translation, so `W` and the dynamics are unchanged, and the momentum stays zero, so the cluster does not drift.
    - Velocities scaled so `2K = |W_ε|` (virial equilibrium in the softened potential), giving `E ≈ −0.248`.
  - **N**: the largest bucket in `{64, 96, 128, 192, 256, 384, 500}` that is `≤ max(area/5, 64)`. 80×24 gives 384, and 200×50 and anything larger gives 500 (the ADR-0012 cap).
  - **Unresolved light**: each body stands for ~10³ real stars, so 500 bodies cannot fill a braille core (at 200×50 they light only ≈ 1.5 of 8 dots per core cell). The core glow is drawn from the Plummer surface brightness `g(R) = (1 + R²/a_g²)^{−2}`, where `R` is the projected distance from the centre and `a_g = r_h/1.3048`. Here `r_h` is the body system's 3-D half-mass radius about the origin, smoothed with `τ = 4 s` and reset at every reseed, so the glow follows the real core contraction.
  - **Luminosity tags** (visual only): the first `g_n = clamp(N/50, 3, 10)` bodies (sample order is random) are red giants. The dynamics stay equal-mass, so there is no mass segregation.
- **Motion**:
  - Bodies orbit in the self-consistent potential: the core crosses in about 2 s and halo stars drift slowly.
  - `geom::Camera` yaws at `2π/90 s`, with pitch `0.30 + 0.15·sin(2πt/67 s)` and perspective `distance = 10` units, so near-side stars slide against far-side ones. The camera alone moves an `r = 1.5` star ≈ 4 dots/s at 200×50.
  - A looping `Timeline`: `evolve` 120 s (48 N-body units ≈ 17 t_cr). Spitzer's `t_rh ≈ 0.138 N r_h^{3/2}/ln(0.4N)` gives 8.8 units (22 s) at N = 500; softening at ε = 0.05 cuts ln Λ to ≈ ln(r_h/ε) ≈ 2.7, so a cycle spans ≈ 3–5.5 t_rh. Then a 1.5 s `fade`, a fresh Plummer sample from `ctx.rng`, and a 1.5 s `rise`: 123 s per loop.
  - Within a cycle the core contracts slightly and the odd star escapes. Core collapse needs ≈ 15 t_rh and is never reached. The reseed keeps the halo populated and bounds the accumulated energy error.
- **Resize** (ADR-0012 analogue, with N as the cap):
  - Within the same N bucket a resize only changes the view: `scale = draw::fit_radius(size, 0.92)/2.2` rows per unit, so `R = 2.2` units (≈ 93 % of the projected mass) fills the frame, and `Dots` is resized. Pos/vel are untouched.
  - On a bucket change, shrinking keeps the first N′ bodies (an unbiased subsample). Growing appends fresh AHW samples, scaled by the same length and velocity factors as the build sample. Then `m = 1/N′`, the momentum is re-zeroed (the COM position is not re-centred: that would break GC-A6's bit-identity and jump the view; the subsample's small COM offset, ≈ `r_rms/√N′`, persists until the next reseed), and velocities are re-virialised to `2K = |W_ε|` (one O(N²) pass). The cluster never visibly resets while you drag the window.
- **Inks**:
  - Everything except the giants is one `Dots` layer in `Role::Star`.
  - Glow: a dot at projected radius `R` is lit iff `p(R) > u(x, y)`, where `p = clamp(1.7·(g − 0.08)/0.92, 0, 1)` and `u` is a fixed per-dot hash in `[0, 1)` salted once from `ctx.rng`. So the core is solid for `R ≤ 0.518 a_g`, thins through a speckled ring, and ends at `R = 1.59 a_g`. A cell whose centre has `p = 1` is lit whole (all 8 dots), as a saturated detector pixel is, so the core reads as a solid knot. Glow level is `90 + 165·g`.
  - Bodies: level `(100 + 140·√g(R) + 20·clamp(z/2, −1, 1))·f_eye(z)` (nearer is brighter), clamped to `[0, 255]`. The eye at distance 10 sits inside the far halo (`r ≤ 38.7 a ≈ 23` units), so a near-side halo star would be magnified without bound and streak across the screen like a meteor. `f_eye = 1 − smoothstep((z − 2.5)/(10/3 − 2.5))` therefore fades near-eye stars out between depth `z = 2.5` and `z = 10/3`, where perspective magnifies them 1.33–1.5×.
  - Giants: `Glyph::new('✦', '*')` in `Role::Hot`, drawn over the blit, each twinkling slowly at level `200 + 30·sin(2πt/P_k + φ_k)` with `P_k ∈ [5, 9]` s and `φ_k` drawn from the salt (visual only). Giants projected where the glow probability is `p ≥ 0.5` (`R < 0.83 a_g`) are drawn as ordinary `Star` dots, lost in the core's glare as in a real exposure.
  - The label is `Text` 140.
  - `Seq` was deliberately not used: a false-colour cluster reads as a heatmap, not a starfield.
- **Look** *(M16, GEN-R25; supersedes the glow, the `Star`-only bodies and the `✦` giants above)*: every body is a braille dot coloured by its place on the colour–magnitude diagram, with a short one-dot orbit trail, on black; there is no unresolved glow (no grey fog): the core is dense because its stars and their trails crowd it. The tags are render-only (the dynamics stay equal-mass): the first `g_n` bodies are red giants, a `Hot` cross of five dots twinkling at `225 ± 30`; the first `s_n = clamp(N/100, 2, 5)` of the rest that start inside 0.5 units (≈ 0.65 `r_h`; heavy merger products sink to the core) are blue stragglers, `Cold` 255; every other body is a main-sequence star whose display mass is a fixed hash of `(salt, index)` through `dN/dm ∝ m^{−1.3}` on 0.15–0.8 M☉, in three brightness steps by mass: the turn-off and subgiants (`m ≥ 0.6`, ≈ 14 %) white `Star` 245, the upper main sequence (0.35–0.6, ≈ 29 %) `Sun` 240, the lower (< 0.35) `Sun` 190. Each body's trail is a one-dot thread through its last 4 positions, stored every 4 updates (≈ 0.27 s, in `update`, so the trail does not depend on the draw cadence), one step dimmer (0.75 of the head's level); a segment longer than 12 dots is left out. A heavier kind is drawn at a higher level, so it wins a shared braille cell. Near-eye stars with `f_eye < ½` are left out. The tags are re-drawn and the trails restarted at every reseed and bucket change. ASCII: by braille count. *(M16 review, supersedes the levels, giant mark, trail length and label above)*: giants are the most luminous and the largest mark, a 2 × 2 dot block in `Hot` twinkling at `245 ± 10`; turn-off and subgiants `Star` 230, blue stragglers `Cold` 230, upper main sequence `Sun` 190, lower main sequence single `Sun` 150 dots; trails 2 snapshots (≈ 0.13 s), so the halo reads as points rather than confetti. The label drops the scene's name, which the title already gives: `N=500  3 crossings` while it takes at most half the width, else `N=500` while it fits in `w − 2`, else nothing.
- **ASCII**: the `Dots` count fallback gives the classic look. At 80×24 there is a `#` core block (≈ 3 × 6 cells), a `+`/`:` ring out to ≈ 5 rows, `.` halo stars, and giants as `*`. The label is pure ASCII.
- **Label**: top-left (row 0, column 1), e.g. `globular cluster  N=500  3 crossings`: the body count and the whole crossing times (`t_cr = 7.1 s`, `1 crossing` in the singular) since the last reseed, so it changes only every 7.1 s. Before the first crossing the clause is left out (`globular cluster  N=500`), so the label never shows a debug-like `0 crossings`. It is written into a stack `StackText` without allocating. The full form is used only when it takes at most half the width (`2·len ≤ w`), so it stays a quiet caption rather than a banner. Otherwise the label is `globular cluster` when that fits in `w − 2`, else nothing. Column 0, the label and one trailing cell of row 0 are kept clear of glow, bodies and giants, so no star reads as part of the text.
- **Perf**: at 200×50 with N = 500 a frame costs ≈ 0.4 ms.
  - Force: 124,750 pairs × (1 sqrt + 1 div + ~20 flops) ≈ 0.25 ms scalar.
  - Projection: 500 × `Camera::project` ≈ 30 µs.
  - Glow: ≈ 12k dots inside `1.59 a_g`, each one hash and compare, ≈ 25 µs.
  - `Dots` clear and blit ≈ 25 µs.
  - `dt = 0.25` gives 13 substeps (≈ 13× the force cost, a few ms). Only the GEN-R4 contract reaches it: the runtime feeds fixed 1/60 steps (ADR-0003), and GEN-R8 covers `update(1/60)`.
  - Pos/vel/acc `Vec<f64>`s have capacity 1500 from `build`, and the cluster's half-mass scratch has capacity 500. The reseed at 121.5 s refills these in place (GC-A6), and the glow needs no buffer, so update and render do not allocate.
  - Debug note: the contract suite's 600-step 200×50 run is ≈ 75 M pair evaluations, ≈ 1.5–2.5 s unoptimised (flagged, like `molecular_dynamics`).
- **Acceptance**: `GC-A1` the AHW sampler (`G = M = a = 1`, cut 0.999): a seeded 4000-star sample has KS distance < 0.03 from the truncated Plummer mass profile `M(r) = r³/(1 + r²)^{3/2}/0.999`, median radius within 4 % of `1.3048 a`, the speed fractions `q = v/v_esc(r)` (`v_esc = √2(1 + r²)^{−1/4}`) all in `[0, 1)` with KS distance < 0.03 from the CDF of `g(q) ∝ q²(1 − q²)^{7/2}` (the regularised incomplete beta `I_{q²}(3/2, 9/2)`, integrated numerically), while exponents 5/2 and 9/2 give > 0.03, and the raw N = 1500 virial ratio `2K/|W|` in `[0.9, 1.1]`; `GC-A2` after `plummer` for seeds 1–6 and N = 64, 256, 500, the density centre (mean position inside the half-mass radius) lies within 0.03 units of the origin and `|Σ m v| < 1e-14`; for N = 256 the unsoftened `W = −1/2` within 1e-12, `2K = |W_ε|` within 1e-12, and `E = −1/4` within 0.01; `GC-A3` for N = 64 seeded, the Newton-3 pair loop equals a naive per-star O(N²) sum within 1e-12 relative, `|Σ m a| < 1e-14`, each acceleration equals `−∇Φ_ε/m` by central differences within 1e-6 relative, and two coincident stars give a finite, zero mutual force; `GC-A4` for N = 128 seeded, `ε = 0.05`, `h = 1/128`, 10 N-body units (1280 steps, ≈ 10 M pair evaluations): `|Σ m v| < 1e-14` throughout and max `|ΔE/E₀| < 5e-4` (measured ≤ 4.6e-5); `GC-A5` in the same run, `r_h(t)/r_h(0) ∈ [0.7, 1.3]` at every 0.5-unit sample with time mean in `[0.9, 1.1]`, and the time-mean `2K/|W_ε|` is in `[0.9, 1.1]`; `GC-A6` N follows the bucket rule (80×24 ⇒ 384, 200×50 ⇒ 500, 1×1 and 0×0 ⇒ 64, never > 500), a resize within a bucket leaves pos/vel bit-identical, and a bucket change keeps the first `min(N, N′)` positions bit-identical while restoring `|Σ m v| < 1e-14` and `2K = |W_ε|` within 1e-12, and the reseed at 200×50 draws a fresh N = 500 sample into pos/vel/acc/scratch without changing their capacities; `GC-A7` *(changed, M16)* at 200×50 (seed 42, t = 3 s, Unicode) every lit cell below the label row is a braille cell in a kind's head ink or its trail step (giants: `Hot` at ≥ 0.75·195), at least one giant shows, and the lit-dot density within 0.4 model units of the projected centre is ≥ 6× that between 1.2 and 1.8 units and below 60 % (dense, not filled); `f_eye` is 1 up to `z = 2.5`, strictly decreasing, and 0 from `z = 10/3`; *(M15, superseded)* every cell within `R ≤ a_g/2` shows `⣿` or a giant glyph, no glow beyond `R = 1.6 a_g`, the core ≥ 50 levels brighter than the halo and at most `g_n` giant glyphs, none within `R < 0.83 a_g`; `GC-A8` *(changed, M16)* `g_n` = 10, 7, 3 and `s_n` = 5, 3, 2 for N = 500, 384, 64; the display mass spans 0.15–0.8 M☉ and rises with its uniform; heavier main-sequence kinds are brighter (`Sun` / `Star`), stragglers `Cold`, giants `Hot`, and a trail is > 20 levels below its head; for a seeded N = 500 sample the first 10 bodies are giants, exactly 5 stragglers all start inside 0.5 units, the turn-off / upper / lower shares are within 0.07 of 0.14 / 0.29 / 0.57, and the tags are a pure function of positions and salt; *(M15, superseded)* the glow probability `p(R)` and the per-dot hash; `GC-A9` the label reads `globular cluster  N=384  4 crossings` at age 4.5 `t_cr` (80×24), `1 crossing` at 1.99 `t_cr` and `globular cluster  N=384` before the first crossing; the full form shows down to `w = 2·len` and falls back to `globular cluster` one column below (so at 40 columns) and down to `w = 18`, and the label is empty below; at 30×10, 40×12 and 80×24 (seed 42, t = 0, 5, 12 s) no dot is lit in row 0's cells `0..=len + 1` (so no giant lies there); each giant slot's level stays in `[195, 255]` *(changed, M16; was `[170, 230]`)*, swings by ≥ 40 over 9 s, with `P_k ∈ [5, 9]` s, and the slots differ; `GC-A10` *(new, M16)* at 120×40 (seed 42) the dots after 120 updates are identical whether `prepare` runs after every update or after every second one, the trail then holds 4 snapshots spanning ≤ 0.3 s, and a reseed restarts it with one snapshot at the new positions. `GC-A11` *(new, M16 review)* the faintest giant twinkle (235) is at least every other kind's head level, at 200×50 (seed 42, t = 3 s) the mean level of the `Hot` cells is at least that of the `Star`, `Sun` and `Cold` cells, and every giant on screen is a lit 2 × 2 dot block; GC-A9 *(changed, M16 review)*: the label is `N=384  4 crossings`, `N=384  1 crossing`, `N=384` before the first crossing, `N=384` one column below the full form and down to `w = 7`, empty below; each giant slot's level stays in `[235, 255]` and swings by ≥ 15.

### ising_model  (States of Matter, M10, M12, M15, M16, M17)
- **Intent**: the ferromagnetic phase transition as a living mosaic. A quench turns static into two colours of domain that swell, round off and straighten. Reheating melts them into a dim boil, and a slow cool through the critical point leaves clusters of every size breathing (critical opalescence). *(changed, M16)* Only the domain walls are drawn, so the eye follows the shrinking bubbles and the straightening walls, and the froth of walls that boils up near the critical point.
- **Model**: 2-D Ising `H = −J Σ⟨ij⟩ sᵢsⱼ`, `sᵢ = ±1`, `J = k_B = 1`, zero field, periodic (torus) boundaries. The lattice is capped: `L = grid::lattice_for(Size(w, 2h), 16 384, 1.25)` in square pixel units, so 200×50 → 178×91 (≈ 1.1 px per spin) and 80×24 → 80×48 (below the cap the lattice is the pixel grid itself). Kinetics are single-spin Glauber heat-bath (Glauber 1963), random-sequential, with one `u64` per attempt from a scene-local SplitMix64 seeded once from `ctx.rng` at the first resize (GEN-R7). Bits 32–47 and 48–63 pick `x = (r₁₆·Lw)>>16` and `y = (r₁₆·Lh)>>16` (≤ 0.3 % non-uniform, harmless because every heat-bath move leaves the Boltzmann distribution invariant). The local field is `h = Σ_nn sⱼ ∈ {−4,−2,0,2,4}`; set `sᵢ = +1` iff the low 32 bits are `< thr[h]`, where `thr = round(p₊·2³²)` as u64 (so `p = 0, 1` are exact) and `p₊(h) = 1/(1 + e^{−2h/T})`. The 5-entry table is rebuilt once per update with T clamped to `[0.05, 100]` (NaN ⇒ T_c). This is the same model-A equilibrium, universality and coarsening class as Metropolis; it replaces checkerboard Metropolis because deterministic sublattice sweeps flip every ΔE = 0 spin at high T (a period-2 strobe) and because random-sequential updates give smooth fractional sweeps per frame at any lattice size. The rate is `max(60·N/16 384, 1)` Monte Carlo sweeps (MCS) per simulated second (N = lattice sites; one MCS per 1/60 s step at the cap), realised as `⌊acc⌋` attempts from an accumulator gaining `rate·N·dt`. Domain size relative to the lattice scales as `√(MCS/N)`, so coarsening looks the same at every terminal size. Temperature is in units of J/k_B with `T_c = 2/ln(1+√2) = 2.269 185 314` (Onsager 1944). References: `m₀(T) = (1 − sinh(2/T)⁻⁴)^{1/8}` below T_c and 0 above (Yang 1952); exact energy per spin `e(T) = −coth 2β·[1 + (2/π)·k′·K(k)]` with `k′ = 2 tanh² 2β − 1` (signed complementary modulus, computed directly to avoid cancellation), `K = π/(2·AGM(1, |k′|))`, and the product `k′·K` taken as its limit 0 when `k′ = 0`, so `e(T_c) = −√2`. T/T_c follows a looping `Timeline` of 58 s: `coarsen` 20 s at 0.60 → `reheat` 8 s, smoothstep 0.60→1.25 → `hot` 5 s at 1.25 (ξ ≈ 2–3, a boil rather than a per-frame fizz) → `cool` 15 s, `1 + 0.25(1−p)²` (eases into T_c so most of the phase sits near criticality) → `critical` 8 s at 1.00 → `quench` 2 s, smoothstep 1.00→0.60; it is continuous at every boundary. The first resize seeds a T = ∞ state (i.i.d. ±1), so t = 0 is the textbook quench. Magnetisation `M = Σs` is tracked incrementally and recomputed on resize.
- **Motion**: within 1 s (≈ 60 MCS at the cap) static condenses into domains 10–20 spins across; coarsening follows `ℓ ∝ t^{1/2}` (Allen & Cahn 1979; Bray 1994). Over `coarsen`, about 1200 MCS grow domains to lattice scale: bubbles shrink and vanish, walls straighten, and the phase often ends in a few domains or one stripe (≈ 1/3 of torus quenches end in stripes). In `reheat`, walls roughen and thermal clusters eat the domains as T crosses T_c. `hot` is a dim boil of small clusters. In `cool`, clusters grow fractal and slow down (critical slowing, z ≈ 2.17; Nightingale & Blöte 1996). `critical` breathes at all scales up to `t^{1/z}`. `quench` feeds straight into `coarsen`, so later cycles coarsen from critical clusters and the first from noise.
- **Resize**: if `lattice_for` returns the same lattice, only the display maps are rebuilt (cell-centre nearest-index tables px→lattice, `Vec<u16>`, allocated in `resize`) and the spins are untouched. A different lattice (bucket change, or any change below the cap) triggers `grid::resample_nearest` into a scratch grid and a swap (ADR-0012); spins stay exactly ±1 and M is recomputed. Above the cap the lattice-to-pixel ratio is not an integer (200×50: 178×91 → 200×100 px), so about every 8th pixel column and 11th pixel row repeats a lattice line; cell-centre sampling spreads the repeats evenly, and the resulting ±1 px wall jitter is accepted (an integer ratio would halve the lattice). `resize` never draws from `ctx.rng` after the first seeding. T comes from scene time, so a resize never desyncs the schedule.
- **Inks**: every pixel is `Role::Div` with `v = s·A[n_al]`, `A = [0.06, 0.12, 0.22, 0.38, 0.72]`, where `n_al ∈ 0..4` counts aligned lattice neighbours; the ten possible inks are built once per scene from A and indexed by `(s > 0)·5 + n_al`, so render does no float work. The table is convex because even at 1.25 T_c most spins still have 3–4 aligned neighbours (⟨sᵢsⱼ⟩ ≈ 0.45), so a linear map could not dim the hot phase: only n_al = 4 is bright. Domain interiors are ±0.72 (astro: orange up, blue down). Walls are ±0.22–0.38, a darker seam that outlines domains without drawing lines. Thermal or isolated spins are ±0.06–0.12, so the hot phase sinks toward the background and ordering visibly brightens the screen. The label is `Role::Text` 200.
- **Look** *(M16, GEN-R25; domain walls only; supersedes the Inks and ASCII below and the pixel display maps in Resize; no longer pinned by GEN-R22)*: black ground, no half-blocks: only the domain walls, as braille threads one dot thick. Each spin's block magnetisation `b` (the mean of its periodic 3×3 block, in steps of 2/9) is traced over the lattice by marching squares (`Dots::contour`, CORE-R105; nodes map to dots as the lattice to the old pixels) at `+0.56` (`Hot` 220, the up side of every wall) and `−0.56` (`Cold` 210, the down side) *(round 2: was ±4/9; just above the block value 5/9, so a wall's two threads sit further apart and never pass through a node)*, so each wall is a warm and a cool thread side by side, bubbles of one sign are rings inside the other's colour, and a lone thermal spin (its block stays at ±7/9) draws nothing. The torus seam is a wall like any other where the spins differ across it. Spin flips blink: a spin whose value changed during the latest update, in an ordered neighbourhood (`|b| ≥ 5/9` after the flip, a thermal flip inside a domain, not the boil), is one dot at its site for that update, `Hot` 110 if it flipped up and `Cold` 110 if down (round 2, was 140) (bound to updates, not frames: GEN-R20). After the quench the walls coarsen and straighten; near and above T_c they roughen into a froth of small rings. ASCII falls back by braille dot count. The label is unchanged.
- **ASCII**: the `Pixels::blit` closure decodes each pixel's signed value with the exact inverse of `ink::div` (`(level − 128)/128` below 128, `/127` above), takes the cell mean m and applies `draw::ramp((m/0.72 + 1)/2, [. , : + #])` (so interiors reach the ramp ends) with ink `ink::div(m)`. Up interiors are `#`, down interiors `.`, up walls `+`, down walls `,`, and cells split across a wall `:`. The 80×24 snapshots show five-glyph noise at t = 0, blobs about 5 cells wide at t = 1 (≈ 14 MCS) and `#` islands with `+` rims in a `.` sea at t = 5 (≈ 70 MCS).
- **Label**: top-left (col 1, row 0) `T/Tc 0.93  |m| 0.71`. T/T_c comes from the schedule and `|m| = |M|/N` is measured live; after a quench it visibly lags the equilibrium m₀ (for example 0.71 vs 0.86 at 0.93). `put` resets bg, so the label sits on a small dark chip. It is written from a stack buffer; the `  |m| …` part is dropped when w < 24, and the whole label when w < 12 or h < 4.
- **Perf** (200×50): update is 16.2 k heat-bath attempts × ≈ 4–5 ns (one u64 draw, table-driven wrapped neighbours, 4 L1 loads, compare, store) ≈ 75 µs. Render is 20 k pixels × (2 map lookups, 5 lattice loads, 1 ink-table lookup) ≈ 50 µs plus blit ≈ 40 µs. Total ≈ 0.17 ms. Attempts per step scale as `N²/16 384` below the cap (80×24 ≈ 0.03 ms), and `dt = 0.25` at the cap costs ≈ 1.2 ms of update.
- **Acceptance**: `ISING-A1` for h ∈ {−4,−2,0,2,4} and T ∈ {0.5, 1, T_c, 3, 10}, `p₊(h)/p₊(−h) = e^{2h/T}` within 1e-12 relative (detailed balance against Boltzmann), `p₊(h) + p₊(−h) = 1` within 1e-15, `p₊(0) = ½` and `thr(0) = 2³¹` exactly, thresholds are non-decreasing in h, and T ∈ {0, −1, NaN, ±∞} gives probabilities in [0, 1] with no NaN; `ISING-A2` `T_c = 2.269185314` (1e-9), `m₀(2.0) = 0.911319`, `m₀(1.5) = 0.986500`, `e(2.0) = −1.745565`, `e(3.0) = −0.817310` (all 1e-6), `e(T_c) = −√2` (1e-9) and `|e(T_c ± 1e-6) + √2| < 1e-3`, m₀ = 0 for T ≥ T_c, m₀ is non-increasing on (0, T_c) and strictly decreasing on [1, T_c); `ISING-A3` on a seeded 32² torus, from a cold start at T = 2.0 after 1000 MCS the means over the next 3000 MCS (one sample per MCS) are ⟨|m|⟩ = 0.9113 ± 0.015 and ⟨e⟩ = −1.7456 ± 0.015, and from a random start at T = 3.0 they are ⟨|m|⟩ < 0.2 and ⟨e⟩ = −0.8173 ± 0.015; `ISING-A4` on a seeded 192² random start at T = 0.6·T_c the excess wall density `ρ − ρ_eq` (ρ = fraction of antiparallel bonds, `ρ_eq = (1 + e/2)/2 = 0.006678`) strictly decreases across 24, 48, 96 and 192 MCS and its least-squares log–log slope over those four points lies in [−0.62, −0.32] (Allen–Cahn −½); `ISING-A5` spins stay in {−1, +1} and the tracked M equals Σs after any sequence of updates (dt ∈ [0, 0.25]) and resizes including 1×1, a resize between two displays in the same `lattice_for` bucket at or above the cap leaves the lattice bit-identical, a bucket change makes every new site equal to its `resample_nearest` source, and 60 updates of 1/60 s perform `rate·N` attempts within ±1 with `rate = max(60·N/16 384, 1)`; `ISING-A6` T/T_c(t) is 0.60 at t = 0 and 10 s, 1.25 at 30 s and 1.00 at 50 s, is periodic with period 58 s and changes by ≤ 0.01 per 1/60 s step over a whole loop, and the label for (0.93, 0.71) is exactly `T/Tc 0.93  |m| 0.71`, is `T/Tc 0.93` for widths 12–23, and is absent below 12 columns or 4 rows. `ISING-A7` *(new, M16)* walls only: an up stripe in a down sea draws `Hot` dots only within 1.5 sites on the up side of its walls and `Cold` only within 1.5 sites on the down side; an ordered domain with lone flipped spins draws nothing; the hot boil (t = 30 s, 200×50) draws at least twice the wall dots of the coarsened domains at t = 20 s; no cell has a half-block glyph or a background. `ISING-A8` *(new, M16)* a repaint shows no blinks, one spin flipped down inside an up domain is exactly one `Cold` dot, and the blinks depend only on the updates (a scene prepared every step and one prepared once after 90 steps show the same dots).
- **Look** *(M17, GEN-R26, stipple domains + wall threads; supersedes the M16 paired walls)*: each domain is a sparse stipple of its spins, `Hot` on up sites and `Cold` on down sites (the two signs of an orbital lobe), dense near a wall and thinning into the bulk (probability falls with distance from the nearest wall, from ≈ 0.25 to a floor ≈ 0.008 over ≈ 3.5 dots, so walls carry a soft coloured fringe and domains stay mostly black; the distance from a cheap two-pass chamfer transform, recomputed in `prepare` only when spins changed); each wall is one crisp bright `Star` thread, the zero iso-line of the spin field coarse-grained over periodic 5×5 blocks and 0.1 s of updates (so clusters of a few thermal spins are no walls; refined, CORE-R106), and the walls the stipple gathers at are the same coarse-grained ones; flips into the minority inside ordered regions flash as single `Star` dots for ≈ 0.2 s, at a fixed 5 % sample of sites so thermal flips sparkle rather than snow; walls that move on leave a faint `Star` afterglow (≈ 0.5 s, dissolving through fixed thresholds, below the domain stipple in a cell) so coarsening reads as motion; near `T_c` the stipple boils.
- **Acceptance** *(M17)*: `ISING-A9` stipple dots are `Hot` only on up sites and `Cold` only on down sites; `ISING-A10` stipple density decreases with distance from the nearest wall; `ISING-A11` each wall is drawn as a single thread, not a pair; `ISING-A12` with the spins frozen the afterglow decays to nothing within 1 s.

### liesegang_rings  (Reactions, M10, M12, M15)
- **Intent**: the rhythm of periodic precipitation. A rack of gel columns stands side by side, each with its outer electrolyte poured on top. In each gel a faint supersaturation haze sinks and, every few seconds, snaps into a bright precipitate band. Bands grow wider, brighter and farther apart with depth (Jablczynski's spacing law). Each tube keeps its own tempo: the concentrated one packs bands tight (forming its first band later and deeper), the dilute one wide and slow, so the Matalon–Packter law reads as a visual polyrhythm. Then a redissolution front wipes the bands top-down and the rack regrows. ("Liesegang rings" names the phenomenon; in tubes it forms bands.)
- **Model**: per tube, a 1-D reaction–diffusion system with nucleation-and-growth precipitation. The ingredients are Ostwald's (1897) supersaturation theory, the nucleation threshold of Keller & Rubinow (1981) and the aggregation threshold of Chopard, Luthi & Droz (1994). The reactions are A + B → C (soluble product, idealised 1:1) and C → D (immobile precipitate), as in Liesegang's 1896 experiment of AgNO₃ diffusing into K₂Cr₂O₇-loaded gelatin to precipitate silver dichromate. The equations are `∂a/∂t = a'' − k·a·b`, `∂b/∂t = b'' − k·a·b`, `∂c/∂t = D_c·c'' + k·a·b − P`, `∂d/∂t = P`, `P = λ·max(c − c_s, 0)·[nᵢ]`. After each Euler step the per-node nucleation flag nᵢ is set irreversibly when `cᵢ ≥ c*` (homogeneous nucleation at supersaturation) or when `cᵢ ≥ c_g` and a neighbour was nucleated before the step (growth on adjacent precipitate; a Jacobi update, so the result is sweep-order-free). The step time is stored in `t_nuc[i]`. Parameters (dimensionless) are `D_a = D_b = 1`, `D_c = 0.3`, `k = 1`, `λ = 0.05`, `c_s = 0.01`, `c_g = 0.037`, `c* = 0.04`, `b₀ = 0.05`, and five presets `a₀ ∈ {0.7, 1.0, 1.5, 2.0, 3.0}` (a₀/b₀ = 14–60). One unit ≈ 0.2 mm and one time unit ≈ ℓ²/D_A ≈ 27 s (D_A ≈ 1.5·10⁻⁹ m²/s), so the displayed 100 units are 2 cm grown in about 9 h, which are real Liesegang scales. The discretisation is part of the model: band positions shift about 10 % with τ while p changes by ≤ 0.02 between τ = 0.15 and 0.075. It uses a 3-point Laplacian and explicit Euler with `τ = 0.15`; positivity holds exactly since `τ(2 + k·a₀,max) = 0.75 ≤ 1` and `τ(2D_c + λ) ≤ 1`. Fields are f64 on 160-node columns, and node i sits at depth `x = i + 1` (the reservoir ghost is x = 0). The top ghost is `a = a₀` (reservoir) with no flux for b and c; the bottom ghost is `a = 0, b = b₀,c, c = 0` (semi-infinite gel; a closed bottom starves the front of b). All five tubes are always simulated, whatever is shown. Sim time is a warp of scene time s: `t_sim = T_END·(s/36 s)^{1.5}` with `T_END = 1200` through `grow`, then continuing at its final rate of 50 units/s, so `t_sim = 1500` when `hold` ends. Euler steps are taken whole from an accumulator, so the scheme always uses exactly τ. `scan_bands(d)` runs every 0.25 s: nodes with `d ≥ 5 %` of the column max form contiguous runs, each run is a band with d-weighted centroid x, width = run length and birth time `t_n = min t_nuc` over its nodes, and the first run is the junction zone, excluded from every law. Reference behaviour (a₀ = 2, t = 1500): bands at x = 8.9 (junction zone, 17 wide), 21.3, 28.3, 36.9, 48.6, 63.9, 83.6 and 106.8, ratios 1.33, 1.31, 1.32, 1.32, 1.31, 1.28 (last five mean 1.304), widths 5 → 12 (width law), and x_n/√t_n rising to 2.9 (last four mean 2.84; time law, Morse & Pierce 1903). Across the presets at t = 1400, p = 0.507, 0.406, 0.341, 0.299, 0.264, which fits `p = F + G·b₀/a₀` with F = 0.190, G = 4.41, R² = 0.999 (Matalon & Packter 1955; Antal et al. 1998). The looping `Timeline` (50 s) is `grow` 36 s → `hold` 6 s (bands thicken and the fronts leave the view) → `dissolve` 8 s. Dissolve is a scripted redissolution with the reaction frozen, like an ammonia front complexing the silver salt to [Ag(NH₃)₂]⁺ (or Co(OH)₂ in excess NH₃). The front descends as `y_e = 120·√progress` nodes (it passes the view bottom at 69 % of the phase), and above it `c, d ← ·e^{−3·dt}`; a, b and the flags do not change. At the loop, state resets to `(a, b, c, d, n, t_nuc) = (0, b₀,c, 0, 0, false, ∞)` with `b₀,c = b₀·(1 + 0.03ξ)`, where ξ ∈ [−1, 1] is drawn from `ctx.rng` at the loop (ξ = 0 in the first cycle). `regrow` is simply the next cycle, with slightly different bands.
- **Motion**: in the first second (at s ≈ 0.7 s) each junction zone precipitates as a dim, cloudy cap under the meniscus, and a dim-red haze starts to sink. Bands are then born 2–5 s apart, staggered across tubes (a₀ = 2: s ≈ 6, 8, 11, 15, 20, 28 s). At each birth a thin faint line appears just ahead of the haze, then brightens and thickens over about 1 s as it drains the haze, leaving a dark depletion gap between the band and the sinking haze. During `dissolve` a clear front sweeps down and erases the bands top-down; the tubes rest clear for about 2.5 s before the next cycle.
- **Resize**: the chemistry never depends on the terminal. The five 160-node columns are the capped lattice, decoupled from the display (ADR-0012). 1-D columns have no aspect, so there is one bucket and nothing to resample; no resize touches state or draws from `ctx.rng`. Only the layout is rebuilt: `n = clamp(⌊(w + 4)/24⌋, 1, 5)` visible tubes, showing presets {2}, {1,3}, {0,2,4}, {0,1,3,4} or {0..4} (always dilute → concentrated). The interior width is `clamp(round(w/(2.6n)), 1, 16)` columns with even gaps. Row 0 is the header, row 1 the liquid, and the gel runs to the bottom edge (tubes run off-screen, no base). The gel's `D_px = 2·max(h − 2, 0)` pixel rows show nodes [0, V) with `V = clamp(2.4·D_px, 50, 100)` through linear box-filter weights built in `resize`: V = 100 from h = 23 (≈ 2.3 nodes/px at 80×24, ≈ 1.1 at 200×50), and short terminals zoom in on the top of the gel (V = 50 at 40×12) rather than merge bands and gaps into one striped column; the chemistry and the ratios always use the full column. A concave meniscus gives each pixel the depth `x = y − y₀ − sag·(1 − u²)`, with u ∈ [−1, 1] across the tube and sag = 0 below h = 22 (fewer than 40 gel rows, where a 1 px sag would only notch single pixels), 1 px for 22 ≤ h < 40 and 2 px from h = 40; pixels with x < 0 are liquid, so the interface and the bands curve gently. Cylinder shading is `× (0.8 + 0.2·√(1 − u²))`.
- **Inks**: gel pixels are `Role::Seq` at the highest of three levels, times the cylinder shading. Precipitate is `(0.30 + 0.70·min(d/0.2, 1)^{0.75})·clamp(d/0.02, 0, 1)` from the box-filtered d, a continuous fade-in, so a newborn band is a thin faint line that brightens and a dissolving band fades fully out; it is not drawn below d = 0.002. Early bands are ≈ 0.6 and deep mature bands → 1.0 (pale yellow). The junction zone is scaled by 0.45 (keeping its cloudy gradient) so the eye goes to the bands. Haze is `0.25·clamp((c/c* − 0.5)/0.5, 0, 1)` (dim red, only above half the nucleation threshold, c ≥ c*/2 = 2·c_s). Clear gel is not drawn (terminal background), so depletion gaps and virgin gel are both dark. The liquid row and meniscus pixels are `Role::Wave` level 60, the glass walls are cell glyphs in `Role::Structure` level 100, and the header is `Role::Text` (ratios 170, legend 110).
- **ASCII**: walls are `|` (Unicode `▕` left and `▏` right, hugging the gel) and liquid cells are `~`. The `Pixels::blit` closure classes each gel pixel from the fields, not the shaded ink: band iff the box-filtered `d ≥ 0.04` outside the junction zone; faint iff otherwise `d > 0.002` (faint precipitate and the whole junction zone); haze iff otherwise haze > 0 (clear gel carrying only the supersaturation haze); otherwise empty. band/band ⇒ `=`, band on top only ⇒ `-`, band on bottom only ⇒ `_`, faint/faint ⇒ `:`, one faint or any haze ⇒ `.`, empty ⇒ space, and the ink carries the level. The haze is thus a light stipple: its large sinking cloud never outweighs the `-`/`=` bands or the `:` junction cap. Wavy bands read at half-cell resolution. The 80×24 snapshots show three `|` tubes under a `~` row; at t = 5 each tube shows a `:` junction cloud over a sinking `.` haze; the first `-`/`=` bands appear from t ≈ 8 (the dilute tube first, the concentrated one at t ≈ 12).
- **Label**: the header is on row 0. Centred over each tube is its live ratio of its two deepest complete bands (a band is complete once a deeper band has nucleated), for example `1.31`, blank until that exists. The legend sits at column 1 while any ratio is shown, keeping a 2-column gap before the first tube's ratio column r₀ (the walls start below the header, so nothing else can collide): `x(n+1)/x(n)` when r₀ ≥ 14 (80×24, 200×50 and 40×12), `xn+1/xn` when 10 ≤ r₀ < 14, otherwise none. During `dissolve` a tube's ratio leaves once the front `y_e` passes the end of the deeper band it measures (or the view bottom), so the numbers follow the wipe and the rest shows clear tubes under an empty header; the legend leaves with the last ratio. Numbers are dropped when the tube interior is < 4 columns, and the header when h < 6. Written from a stack buffer.
- **Perf**: update is ≤ 5 tubes × 160 nodes × ≤ 6 Euler sub-steps per 1/60 s (the peak comes in `hold`; 1–2 early) ≈ 4.8 k node updates ≈ 25 µs, plus a band scan of 5 × 160 nodes every 0.25 s. `dt = 0.25` costs ≤ 84 sub-steps ≈ 0.35 ms. Render at 200×50 is ≈ 7 k gel pixels ≈ 25 µs plus blit ≈ 30 µs. Total ≈ 0.1 ms, independent of terminal size; all state is fixed-size arrays, so nothing allocates after `resize`.
- **Acceptance**: `LIES-A1` for any sequence of `update(dt ∈ [0, 0.25])` over two full loops all fields stay finite with `0 ≤ a ≤ a₀`, `0 ≤ b ≤ b₀,c`, `c ≥ 0`, `d ≥ 0`, and during grow and hold every node's d is non-decreasing and its nucleation flag and `t_nuc` never change once set; `LIES-A2` on a closed 96-node column (no flux at both ends, a = 1 on the top 40 nodes, b = b₀ everywhere), after 10⁴ Euler steps `Σ(b + c + d) = 96·b₀` and `Σ(a − b) = 40 − 96·b₀` within 1e-9 relative (A + B → C → D stoichiometry); `LIES-A3` preset a₀ = 2 on a 160-node column run to t = 1500 gives ≥ 7 bands (junction zone included) with x ≤ 112, and excluding the junction zone the last five ratios `x_{n+1}/x_n` lie within ±4 % of their mean, the mean is in [1.27, 1.35] (reference 1.304), and the deepest band is wider than the first band after the junction zone (reference 12 vs 5 nodes; spacing and width laws); `LIES-A4` in the same run `x_n/√t_n` for the four deepest bands lies within ±8 % of their mean and the mean is in [2.65, 3.0] (reference 2.84; time law); `LIES-A5` across the five presets (160 nodes, t = 1400), p = mean of the last three ratios − 1 is strictly decreasing in a₀, and the least-squares fit `p = F + G·b₀/a₀` has R² ≥ 0.98, G ∈ [3.5, 5.5] and F ∈ [0.12, 0.26] (reference 0.190, 4.41, 0.999; Matalon–Packter); `LIES-A6` the phases grow 36 / hold 6 / dissolve 8 s loop at 50 s, `t_sim(36 s) = 1200` and `t_sim(42 s) = 1500` (1e-9) and t_sim strictly increases through grow and hold, during dissolve a, b and the flags are unchanged and Σd over nodes [0, 100) is non-increasing in every tube and ends below 2 % of its starting value, and at the loop every field equals the reset state exactly with `|b₀,c/b₀ − 1| ≤ 0.03`; `LIES-A7` two seeded scenes driven by the same update sequence across a loop boundary, one of them resized through 1×1, 7×3, 80×24 and 200×50 in between, have bit-identical tube states, and the visible presets follow the n(w) table (w = 20 ⇒ {2}, 80 ⇒ {0,2,4}, 200 ⇒ all five); `LIES-A8` the legend is `x(n+1)/x(n)` for r₀ ≥ 14, `xn+1/xn` for 10 ≤ r₀ < 14 and absent below, and the long form shows at 80×24, 200×50 and 40×12; at 80×24 (seed 42) the header shows the legend and three ratios at s = 41.9 (hold) and is blank at s = 49.9 (end of dissolve); a pixel with `d ≤ 0.002` and haze > 0 is class haze and any cell with haze but no faint, band or liquid pixel is `.`, while faint/faint stays `:`; and V = 100 at 80×24 and 200×50, 50 at 40×12 and `2.4·D_px` at 80×16, with the deepest window of 40×12 ending at node 50.

### molecule_viewer  (Reactions, M10, M12, M15)
- **Intent**: a slowly tumbling ball-and-stick model of a real molecule, lit like a physical model on a dark bench. Near atoms are bright and far atoms recede. The gallery runs water, methane, ethanol, benzene, caffeine, then buckminsterfullerene. Each molecule grows out of a point, turns, and collapses back into the point before the next one grows.
- **Model**: static tables in the module. Each holds element, position (Å) and bonds with an order of `Single`, `Double` or `Aromatic` (1.5). Benzene uses `Aromatic`; caffeine uses Kekulé single and double bonds. Planar molecules are stored with their plane in world xy (normal along z), so yaw takes them through face-on and edge-on views. Geometries:
  - water: r(OH) 0.9572 Å, ∠HOH 104.52° (Benedict, Gailar & Plyler 1956).
  - methane: T_d, r(CH) 1.087 Å.
  - ethanol: trans (anti H–O–C–C) conformer, r(CC) 1.512, r(CO) 1.431, r(OH) 0.971 Å (NIST CCCBDB experimental); the remaining C–H bonds and angles come from CCCBDB, or are tetrahedral with r(CH) 1.09 Å.
  - benzene: D₆h, r(CC) 1.397 Å, r(CH) 1.084 Å (Herzberg 1966).
  - caffeine C8H10N4O2: the 3-D conformer of PubChem CID 2519 (24 atoms, 25 bonds, planar purine), with double bonds C2=O, C6=O, C4=C5 and C8=N9.
  - C60: generated at build, not typed in. Each of the 30 edges of an icosahedron carries two atoms at fractions `t` and `1 − t`. The icosahedron's vertices are the cyclic permutations of (0, ±1, ±φ); its edge is 2, so it is scaled by `a/2`. With `a = 2·r₅₆ + r₆₆` and `t = r₅₆/a`, the pentagon (5:6) bonds are r₅₆ = 1.458 Å and the hexagon-fusing (6:6) bonds, which lie along the old edges, are r₆₆ = 1.401 Å (Hedberg et al. 1991, gas-phase electron diffraction). Every atom then sits 3.562 Å from the centre.

  Every table is recentred on its centre of mass (H 1.008, C 12.011, N 14.007, O 15.999 u).

  Balls and bonds:
  - Ball radius is 0.20 × the Bondi (1964) van der Waals radius (H 1.20, C 1.70, N 1.55, O 1.52 Å), which keeps ≥ 0.5 Å of visible stick on C60's 1.40–1.46 Å bonds, so the cage reads as rings of balls rather than a heap of blobs.
  - Bodies are chosen once per molecule and size, per class (heavy atoms, hydrogens), from the class's largest unmagnified ball at full size: *Dot* (the centre cell only) below 0.95 rows, *Compact* (a 3-cell lozenge on the centre row) below 1.2 rows, *Round* (a shaded `draw::disc`) above; a body is floored at 0.8·0.95·s rows so small far atoms keep a few lit cells. At 80×24 only caffeine and C60 are dot-and-stick.
  - Bonds are drawn only while the gallery scale `s ≥ 0.5` (below it the atoms crowd the centroid and sticks only thicken the clump). A bond is a Bresenham stroke between the two projected centres. It skips every cell that `draw::disc` would paint for either end ball, so sticks run from ball edge to ball edge.
  - A double bond is drawn as two parallel strokes, one cell either side of the axis, when its projected length is ≥ 6 cells and neither end is a Dot body. Otherwise, and for aromatic bonds, it is one stroke.

  View:
  - The camera is `geom::Camera { yaw, pitch, distance: 5·r_eff }`, with `r_eff = max(maxᵢ(|rᵢ| + r_ball,ᵢ), 1.25 Å)` so water (raw ≈ 1.16 Å) is framed but not ballooned.
  - The canvas below the label row (height `h − 1`, floored at 1) is centred at row `1 + (h − 1)/2`.
  - Scale (rows/Å) is `s(t) · draw::fit_radius(w × (h − 1), 0.86) / (1.05·r_eff)`. A sphere of radius `r_eff` seen from `5·r_eff` projects to at most `5/√24` ≈ 1.021 of its radius, and the ≈ 3 % on top covers floored bodies and rounded cells, so no orientation leaves the frame; `s(t)` is the gallery scale (Motion). (The on-axis bound 5/(5 − 1) = 1.25 would leave water and ethanol at ≈ 75 % of the framing radius.)

  Painter's order: in every `render`, all atoms and bonds (≤ 150 items; a bond's depth is the mean of its end depths) are sorted far → near. They sit in fixed-size stack arrays (`[u16; 150]`, `[f32; 150]`), sorted with `sort_unstable_by` on `(depth.total_cmp, index)`, which is deterministic and uses no heap.

  Shading: the sphere normal is `n = (nx, −ny, √(1 − nx² − ny²))` from `draw::disc`, where screen y points down. Light comes from `L = normalise(−0.45, 0.55, 0.70)` (upper left, front). Intensity is `I = 0.35 + 0.65·max(0, n·L)`, and cells with `n·H > 0.95` are specular (Blinn–Phong, `H = normalise(L + V)` for a viewer on +z, so the highlight sits ≈ 0.39 of the radius up-left of centre).
- **Motion**: everything is a closed-form function of scene time `t`.
  - The gallery is a `Timeline` of `grow` 0.6 s → `hold` 8.8 s → `shrink` 0.6 s (10 s per molecule). The molecule is `(start + timeline.cycle(t)) mod 6`, where `start` is seeded.
  - Scene time begins at the start of the first `hold`, so the first frame shows a full molecule, and successive 15 s `sim` visits show different molecules.
  - The gallery scale `s` is `ease::smoothstep(progress)` in `grow`, 1 in `hold` and `1 − smoothstep(progress)` in `shrink`. Atoms converge on the centroid and their levels dim with `s²`. The molecule and label change only at the slot boundary, where `s = 0`.
  - Yaw is `ω·τ − 0.78 rad` with `ω = 2π/10 s`, where τ is the time since the slot began, so each molecule reaches full size in a three-quarter view (≈ −0.4 rad). Pitch is `0.30 + 0.30·sin(2πt/17 s)` rad.
  - C60's equator sweeps up to ≈ 9 columns per second at 80×24 (≈ 19 at 200×50).
- **Resize**: view only; there is no simulation state (ADR-0012). The centre, scale and label tier are recomputed. Scene time, slot and orientation are kept, and the seeded `start` is drawn only on the first resize.
- **Inks**: CPK colours mapped to roles: H `Text` (white), C `Structure` (grey), N `Cold` (blue), O `Hot` (red). Each bond is split at its projected midpoint, and each half takes its atom's role at 0.8× that atom's level.
  - The depth cue is `level = s²·(100 + 155·ν)·I`, with `ν = (depth + r_eff)/(2·r_eff) ∈ [0, 1]`.
  - The projected ball radius is `r_ball · scale · d/(d − depth)`.
  - Specular cells are `█` at level 255. The label is `Text`.
- **ASCII**: every atom's centre cell shows its element symbol, upper-case in the near half (`ν ≥ 0.5`) and lower-case in the far half (`C`/`c`, `H`/`h`). Ball bodies (Compact and Round) are `: + * # @` by intensity `I`, dim to bright (the dimmest tier is `:`, so a ball's shadow rim stays distinct from rear bonds). Bonds use `- | / \` by projected slope; a bond whose ends are both in the far half (ν < 0.5) is dotted `.` (Unicode `·`), so C60's rear lattice recedes. At 80×24 the frame reads like a textbook structure drawn in letters.

  Unicode uses `█▓▒░` bodies by `I`, `●` for small heavy atoms and `•` for small H, with `─ │ ╱ ╲` bonds.
- **Label**: top-left (row 0, col 1): `caffeine  C8H10N4O2  194.19 g/mol`, all in ASCII. The Hill formula (C first, then H, then the rest alphabetically; H2O for water) and the molar mass (2 decimals) are checked against the table. Tiers, used in order when the label does not fit `width − 2`: drop the mass, then the name, then show nothing. The label is never clipped and switches only at the slot boundary.
- **Perf**: `update` only advances `t` (O(1)). The worst case is `render` of C60 at 200×50 (≈ 5.1 rows/Å, C ball ≈ 1.75 rows):
  - 60 `Camera::project` calls;
  - a 150-item stack sort (≈ 3 µs);
  - ≈ 60 discs × 20 cells + 90 bonds × 15 cells ≈ 2.5k cell writes.

  That is ≈ 0.05 ms, with no heap use in `update` or `render`.
- **Acceptance**: `MOL-A1` every table is chemically consistent: with the covalent radii of Cordero et al. (2008) (H 0.31, C 0.76, N 0.71, O 0.66 Å), bonded pairs satisfy `0.80 ≤ d/(rᵢ + rⱼ) ≤ 1.12` and every non-bonded pair has `d/(rᵢ + rⱼ) ≥ 1.35`, so the bond list equals the distance-derived connectivity;
