- **Motion**: `STEPS_PER_S = 270` lattice steps per simulated second: 4.5 per 60 Hz frame through a fractional accumulator, and at most 68 per `update` at dt = 0.25. The shedding period `P ≈ D/(St U)` is ≈ 400–450 steps at the reference lattices (St ≈ 0.20–0.21 on the free stream), or ≈ 1.5–1.7 s. Eddies advect ≈ 25–29 lattice nodes/s (≈ 0.85 U), which is ≈ 40 columns/s at 200×50 and ≈ 18 columns/s at 80×24, so motion is obvious within 1 s. Without forcing the flow would be an exact limit cycle (frames 25 s apart nearly identical); the 47 s breathing inlet makes the shedding period and eddy spacing drift by ±8 %, so a long visit evolves. It loops forever without a Timeline, and each seed opens on a different street phase.
- **Resize**: `lattice_size(display)` decides everything. If the lattice is unchanged (same aspect bucket), only the view mapping and `Pixels` are rebuilt and the populations stay bit-identical. On a bucket change, ρ, uₓ and u_y are resampled onto the new lattice with `grid::resample_bilinear` (ADR-0012); every lattice runs at the same U₀, so the moments need no rescaling. Solid nodes of the new cylinder get `u = 0`, `FlowParams` are recomputed, `f = f^eq(ρ, u)` is rebuilt and the probe history is cleared. Non-equilibrium populations are never resampled. The first resize initialises the flow from the analytic street and pre-rolls it. Every buffer (lattice planes, vorticity grid, `SampleMap`, `Pixels`) is sized here. The view is refreshed at the end of both `resize` and `update`, so `render` only blits and writes the label.
- **Inks**: vorticity `ω = ∂ₓu_y − ∂_y uₓ` (y up on screen) is taken by central differences on the visible lattice and normalised as `x = ω D/(2.5 U)`. It is soft-clipped with a rational tanh-like squash, `s = clamp(x(27 + x²)/(27 + 9x²), −1, 1)` (`ink::soft_clip`), which is odd, monotone and exactly 1 at |x| = 3, so no transcendental function runs per pixel. The vorticity grid is resampled bilinearly to pixel size, and each pixel gets `ink::div(s)` (`Role::Div`): CW (upper row, s < 0) is cyan and CCW (lower row) is orange. Pixels with |s| < 0.06 stay `Ink::BLANK`. The Scully core peak `ω_max = Γ/(π r_c²) ≈ 4.7 U/D` maps to |s| ≈ 0.975, so eddy cores are graded while the thin shear layers saturate. **Hysteresis** (as in Canny's 1986 edge detector): a 4-connected group of drawn `Div` pixels stays only if one of its pixels reaches |s| ≥ 0.12, the ASCII blank band, so a faint structure shows in both glyph modes or in neither. The faint fringes of eddies and shear layers hang on to their cores, while isolated threshold-level specks with nothing behind them are blanked: start-up transients (a detached 4-pixel strip below the cylinder at 80×24, t = 0.2 s; a dotted diagonal trailing the upper shear layer at 200×50, t = 1 s) and staircase noise, ≈ 1–2 groups per frame at any time, ≈ 0.2 % of the drawn pixels. Dropping every faint pixel not next to a strong one would instead strip ≈ 11 % of the drawn pixels at 200×50, the soft edges of the eddies. It is a flood fill from the seeds over the pixel buffer, with scratch buffers sized in `resize`. The fluid is drawn first, the hysteresis runs on it, and the cylinder goes on top; pixels centred inside the rim are not fluid, so the wall's own shear never seeds a group. Cylinder pixels are those whose centre, mapped into lattice coordinates, lies within D/2 of (x_c, y_c), so the outline is round rather than the staircase. The rim is anti-aliased by coverage `c = clamp(½ − d, 0, 1)`, with `d` the signed distance of the pixel centre outside the rim in pixel widths along the radius: `Role::Structure` level 200 where c = 1 and `120 + 80c` otherwise, i.e. 160–199 for the rim pixels whose centre is inside, and 120–159 for pixels partly covered from outside, which show the wall only where the fluid is undrawn, so they never hide the boundary layer (at 80×24 a hard 7×6-pixel disc read as a rounded square). On the upstream half (dx < 0), weak vorticity (|s| < 0.2) is not drawn within 1 node of the rim, where the staircase's corners stick out of the round mask, nor, when its sign is opposite to the boundary layer's (CW above, CCW below), within 0.6 D of the rim, where the steps' odd-even noise is washed along the wall: at Re ≲ 190 the only vorticity there is the attached boundary layer, far stronger, and opposite-signed secondary vorticity only forms behind separation. These removed single-pixel brown and navy specks at the cylinder's upstream shoulders. The label is `Role::Text` level 150.
- **ASCII**: the `Pixels::blit` closure decides per cell. A cell whose two halves are both cylinder, `Structure` at level ≥ 160 (centre inside the rim), gives `@`; so does a cell with one such half whose column centre lies within 0.35 D of x_c (the disc's top and bottom caps, columns computed in `resize`). Elsewhere a single cylinder half, and anywhere an anti-aliased outside-edge half (< 160), falls through to the other half's `Div` value, so the shoulders show the boundary layer instead of a one-pixel sliver. At 80×24 the disc is then four rows of 5, 7, 7 and 5 `@`, round at 2:1 cells; with both halves required it was two rows of 7–8, a 2:1 bar in square units inside the wake. (At 40×12 the disc is only ≈ 3.7 × 1.7 cells and stays a 2×2 block.) `s` is the mean of the `Div` halves: |s| < 0.12 is left blank, |s| < 0.25 gives `.`, s < 0 gives `x` (|s| < 0.6) or `X`, and s > 0 gives `o` or `O`, each with the averaged `Div` ink (the Unicode threshold stays 0.06; the wider blank band keeps decaying far-wake eddies from reading as clouds of dots). A cell with both halves empty is left untouched. Snapshots show a symmetric, round `@` disc, an `x/X` upper row and an `o/O` lower row alternating downstream on a blank field.
- **Look** *(M16, GEN-R25)*: thin braille contours of the vorticity on black, replacing the half-block field, its hysteresis pass, the graded rim and the glyph ASCII above (the model, the vorticity `ω`, its normalisation and soft clip are unchanged). The soft-clipped `s` on the visible lattice nodes is contoured by marching squares (`Dots::contour_cell`, CORE-R105) at three levels per sign, `|s| = 0.15, 0.4, 0.75`: positive (counter-clockwise, lower row) `Hot`, negative (clockwise, upper row) `Cold`, the levels stepped 170, 215, 255 inward, so an eddy reads as a few nested rings and a shear layer as a few parallel filaments. Each lattice cell the level crosses is refined into `k × k` bilinear sub-cells (`Dots::contour_cell_refined`, CORE-R106), `k = clamp(⌈max(sx, sy)/2.2⌉, 1, 5)` with `sx, sy` dots per node, so curves stay smooth at large displays; cells whose corners all lie below 0.15 are culled first. Before contouring, nodes upstream of the cylinder get the old staircase-noise rule (weak `|s| < 0.3` — 0.2 before, for the bilinear pixels — within 1 node of the rim, or opposite-signed within 0.6 D, is set to 0; on the nodes themselves start-up transients reach 0.24 there). The cylinder is a one-dot ring, `Structure` 255, of radius D/2 in square dots (M16 round 2; a filled disc before), drawn last so its cells stay grey; the attached boundary layer shows as contours hugging it. ASCII falls back by dot count (GEN-R10). Rasterised in `prepare` (GEN-R20).
- **Label**: `Re≈140` (ASCII `Re~140`, the current Re = Re₀·breath(t) rounded to the nearest 5) at column 1 of row 0, above the calm inflow. `FlowParams::re` is `U₀D/ν` rounded to 1e-6: ν = (0.53 − 0.5)/3 comes out slightly above 0.01 in f64, so Re₀ = 137.4999… rounded down to `Re≈135` at t = 0 and flipped to `Re≈140` within 0.5 s as the breath rose; now the first number read is 140. At width ≥ 40, `  St≈0.18` (ASCII `St~0.18`) is appended once ≥ 3 upward zero-crossings of u_y, with ±0.02 U hysteresis, have been recorded at the probe `(x_c + 2.5 D, round(y_c))`. The probe keeps the last ≤ 5 crossings (a core `Trail`), and `St = D/(U·P̄)`, where P̄ is the mean of their ≤ 4 intervals and U the inlet speed U₀·breath at the middle of that window: the breath drifts by up to ≈ 3 % over the ≈ 6 s window, so the speed at the latest crossing would bias the reading by as much. St is based on the free-stream U, like Re. With y periodic at blockage D/H ≈ 0.15 the fluid passes a row of cylinders, the gap speed is U·H/(H − D) ≈ 1.17 U, and that St reads above the unconfined value: over t = 20–140 s the reference lattices average 0.207 (80×24, Re₀ 137.5) and 0.200 (200×50, Re₀ 125), ranging ±6 % with the breathing inlet. The label shows St on the gap speed, `St·(1 − D/H)`, 0.177 and 0.170. This is a heuristic confinement correction, calibrated empirically, not an established blockage correction: Maskell- or Allen–Vincenti-type corrections for a walled channel at this blockage typically give a smaller factor (≈ 0.9 against 0.855), and a y-periodic row of in-phase cylinders is not a walled channel. On these lattices it matches Williamson's unconfined St(137.5) ≈ 0.179 and St(125) ≈ 0.175 to within ≈ 3 %, and gives 0.148 against 0.149 at Re 75 (VOR-A5), so a viewer comparing it with the literature reads about the right number instead of one ≈ 15 % high. There is no label below 16 columns or 6 rows, and it is never clipped. It is written through a stack buffer.
- **Perf**: 10 200 nodes × 4.5 steps ≈ 46 k lattice updates per frame. The interior uses a branch-free row-sliced kernel as in `gs_step`; only the cylinder's bounding rows and the inlet, outlet and sponge columns take the special paths. At 60–100 MLUPS single-threaded that estimate is 0.45–0.75 ms, plus vorticity ≈ 0.02 ms, resampling to 20 k pixels ≈ 0.08 ms, ink mapping ≈ 0.04 ms and the blit ≈ 0.05 ms. Measured (`cargo bench`, frame = update + render): 0.39 ms at 200×50, 0.76 ms at 400×100 (the view grows with the display; the lattice does not), i.e. the kernel runs at ≈ 150 MLUPS. The hysteresis pass adds ≈ 0.6 M retired instructions per frame at 200×50 (+10 %), mostly the per-pixel mark written while shading; reading the pixels back through `Pixels::get` instead cost ≈ 0.1 ms. The fastest frame measured 0.36 ms against 0.33 ms without the pass (M10 polish, same machine, under heavy load). 80×24 costs about the same as 200×50 because the lattice does not depend on display size (GEN-R8 is 5 ms). `render` and steady `update` do not allocate. Every `update(1/60)` costs ≈ 46 k lattice updates at any size, including 1×1; the debug suites stay fast because `sciart-generators` builds at opt-level 1 in dev.
- **Acceptance**: `VOR-A1` `feq(ρ, u)` for 100 seeded states (ρ ∈ [0.9, 1.1], |u| ≤ 0.2) has moments Σf = ρ, Σf c = ρu and Σf c_α c_β = ρ(δ_αβ/3 + u_α u_β) to 1e-6, and `feq(1, 0) = w`; `collide(f, ω⁺, ω⁻)` conserves ρ and ρu to 1e-6, scales the even non-equilibrium part by (1 − ω⁺) and the odd part by (1 − ω⁻) to 1e-4 of each part plus 5e-8 (perturbations ±2e-2·w_i; f32 round-off is ≈ 1e-8), while an ω⁻ off by 1e-3 fails in ≥ 95 of 100 states, equals BGK when ω⁻ = ω⁺, and `tau_minus(0.53, 1/4)` = 8.8333 ± 1e-4; `VOR-A2` on a fully periodic 32×16 lattice a uniform state (ρ = 1, u = (0.1, 0)) stays uniform (all nodes bit-identical to each other) with ρ and u within 1e-6 of the start after 1 000 steps, and with a D = 6 cylinder at (10, 8) (halfway bounce-back, fully periodic, no inlet, outlet or sponge, u₀ = (0.05, 0)) the total mass Σρ (summed in f64) is conserved to 1e-5 relative over 300 steps; `VOR-A3` a shear wave uₓ = 0.01 sin(2πy/64) on a periodic 4×64 lattice decays as exp(−ν k² t), with ν fitted by Fourier projection between steps 100 and 2 100 at τ = 0.53 and between steps 100 and 1 100 at τ = 0.8, within 2 % of (τ − ½)/3 (validates the τ → ν → Re chain under TRT); `VOR-A4` for every display with w ∈ 1..=480 (step 7) and h ∈ 1..=160 (step 3), `lattice_size` gives between 9 500 and 10 240 nodes, displays whose clamped, sponge-widened aspects round to the same power of 1.12 map to one lattice, and for display aspects in [1, 4] the visible lattice aspect is within 7 % of the display's; `flow_params` gives ω⁺ = 1/0.53, U₀ = 0.125 (Ma = 0.217), D = clamp(round(min(0.14 H, W_vis/9)), 6, 16) with D ≤ 12 and D/H ≤ 0.15, Re = U₀ D/ν ∈ [87, 150], x_c = max(round(0.15 W_vis), round(1.65 D)) with the front x_c − D/2 ≥ 1.1 D, and y_c = (H − 1)/2, and 200×50, 80×24 and 100×50 give exactly the 150×68 (D = 10, x_c = 20, Re = 125), 134×76 (D = 11, x_c = 18, Re = 137.5) and 107×95 (D = 11, x_c = 18, Re = 137.5) lattices; `VOR-A5` on a 96×36 test lattice (`flow_params` gives D = 6, U = 0.125, Re = 75) started from the seeded analytic street, after 1 000 warm-up steps the next 1 400 steps give a probe Strouhal number in [0.165, 0.195], ±8 % around the gap-speed heuristic 0.149/(1 − D/H) = 0.149/(1 − 1/6) ≈ 0.179 from Williamson's (1988) unconfined St(75) ≈ 0.149 (measured 0.177) with u_y amplitude > 0.1 U, time-mean ω < 0 over the upper near wake and > 0 over the lower one (x_c … x_c + 2D, above and below y_c), ρ ∈ [0.9, 1.1] and |u| ≤ 2.5 U from the first step on, and no watchdog reinit (≈ 8.3 M lattice updates, the one slow test, ≈ 2–4 s in debug); `VOR-A6` resizing 200×50 → 190×48 → 200×50 (the lattice stays 150×68) leaves every population bit-identical, and 200×50 → 100×50 (150×68 → 107×95, D 10 → 11, U₀ unchanged) resamples with mean ρ within 2e-3 of before and the fluid-mean uₓ/U within 3 % of before, no non-finite value, and after 200 further steps ρ ∈ [0.9, 1.1] with no watchdog reinit; `VOR-A7` *(changed, M16)* a seed-42 80×24 frame at t = 0.5 s: the `Structure` cells form one 8-connected blob (a ring of cells) whose centroid is within 1.5 columns of column 11.3 and within 0.5 rows of row 11.5, mirrored rows (y, 23 − y) hold the same number of them, at least four rows; every cell below the label row is blank or braille and inked `Hot`, `Cold` or `Structure` (the label `Text`); columns 0–5 of rows 1–23 are blank; right of the blob there are both `Hot` and `Cold` cells, more `Cold` than `Hot` above row 11.5 and more `Hot` than `Cold` below it, and the ASCII frame is pure ASCII; in the developed flow at 80×24 (seeds 42 and 7), in five frames spanning one shedding period from t = 20 s (every 0.4 s), there are same-sign eddies (8-connected `Hot` or `Cold` cell groups of ≥ 4 cells) of both signs whose centroids lie right of the display column of x_c + 3D in every frame, and ≥ 3 of them in at least one; the calm inflow stays calm from the first frame on: at 100×50 (seeds 3 and 42), 100×40, 80×24 and 200×50 (seed 42), at t = 0, 0.1, 0.2, 0.3, 1, 20, 30 and 45 s, no contoured node (|s| ≥ 0.15 after the upstream rule) lies left of x_c − 0.6 D outside the band |y − y_c| ≤ 0.6 D, none on the upstream shoulders (dx < 0, |dy| > D/4, within 1.1 D of the centre) has the sign opposite to the boundary layer's, and no watchdog reinit in any of those runs nor in 45 s at 13×5 (lattice 119×85, D = 12, Re₀ = 150, the highest Re); `VOR-A8` `breath(0) = 1`, its extremes are 1.08 and 0.92 at a quarter and three quarters of 47 s, and it is 47 s periodic; for every display of VOR-A4, 0.92·Re₀ > 47, 1.08·Re₀ < 190 and 1.08·U₀√3 ≤ 0.234; in a 200×50 scene after 1.5 s the centre-row inlet velocity is breath(t) times its t = 0 value and the outlet sponge's target is U₀·breath(t), both within 1e-3, with no watchdog reinit; at t = 0 every inlet row carries the potential flow's velocity and its Bernoulli density `1 + 1.5 (U₀² − |u_pot|²)`, within 1e-6; the label reads Re₀·breath(t) rounded to 5: `flow_params` gives Re₀ = 137.5 exactly at 80×24, the label reads Re≈140 at t = 0 and at t = 0.5 s and Re≈150 at t = 10 s, when it also shows St on the gap speed, St·(1 − D/H), in [0.16, 0.19]; `strouhal` reads nothing before 3 crossings, uses the last ≤ 4 periods, and divides by the inlet speed at the middle of their window (VOR-A5 uses the same function); `VOR-A9` *(new, M16)* the vorticity is drawn as threads, not fills: in the developed flow at 200×50 (t = 20 s) `Hot`/`Cold` cells hold on average ≤ 4.5 of their 8 dots and ≤ 15 % of them are full, they use exactly the levels 170, 215 and 255, each of them somewhere.
## Batch M13 — new scenes
Design: [`docs/design/M13-scenes.md`](../design/M13-scenes.md) (picks, palettes, batches, research sources), `docs/design/M11-titles-taxonomy.md` §6–§7. Every scene here: < 2 ms worst frame (step + render) at 200×50 (`pixi run rs-worst`), `render` and steady-state `update` allocation-free (GEN-R11), growing structures in fixed-capacity pools, live heap under the TEST-R7 ceiling at 500×150, trigonometry through `Trig` (GEN-R6), title per GEN-R12 and category per ADR-0013. Shared `pub(crate)` models live in `src/shared/` (GEN-R3, GEN-R16): `vortex_advect` (jupiter_storms, hurricane), `plume` (io_volcanoes, comet_breakup), `excitable` (heart_fibrillation, dicty_aggregation; belousov_zhabotinsky's Barkley step and pacemaker now call it, snapshots unchanged), `laplacian_growth` (lichtenberg_discharge), `cahn_hilliard` (spinodal_decomposition), `skyline` (total_solar_eclipse, rainbow, meteor_shower, noctilucent_clouds, star_trails) and `earth_globe` (satellite_orbit, moon_landing); `lbm` is lifted into core with kelvin_helmholtz (CORE-R98, CORE-R99).

### spiral_galaxy  (Deep Cosmos, M13, M16, M17, M18)
- **Intent** *(M14: arms on a dark disc; M15, ADR-0018: in M11's flat steps; M16: four arms of star streams)*: a face-on grand-design spiral, tilted slightly. Stars flow steadily through four arms that rotate slower than they do, so the arms stay while the stars pass; dark dust lanes hug the inner edge of each arm, blue young stars and a few pink star-forming knots light up just downstream. No inter-arm haze.
- **Model**: Kalnajs kinematic density waves. Star `i` has a guiding radius `r_i` (exponential disc, scale 0.3 R, 20k stars capped at `0.8·area`) and moves on a closed oval about the centre, `r = r_i(1 − e sin ψ)` with `ψ = 2(α + ln r_i / tan p)` (`α` its angle in the pattern frame, `e` 0.08–0.12, pitch `p = 18°`), so nested ovals crowd into two trailing logarithmic arms. The ovals' orientation turns at the pattern speed `Ω_p`; each star advances along its oval at `Ω(r) = v_flat / r` (flat rotation curve, softened within 0.1 R). Closed form per frame: no integration. Stars are splatted into a `raster::Glow` (the outer disc's stars dimmed so the rim reads smooth) with a Gaussian bulge (σ 0.08 R) and the unresolved disc light, modulated by the same crowding as `√(1 − a²)/(1 − a cos φ)` (a = 0.85) so the arms read without shot noise. Arm phase `φ = 2(θ + ln r / tan p − Ω_p t)`: dust lanes are an attenuation mask at `φ ∈ [−0.6, −0.2]` rad (the inner, concave edge); H II knots (pool 48) are spawned at `φ ≈ +0.25` with a 3 s life, where gas leaving the shock forms stars, and drawn as single pixels *(M14; were 5 × 5 soft glows)*: only those of size ≥ 0.87 (about a third), and not beside another knot's pixel, so no two merge into a blob; young stars of luminosity ≥ 0.12 *(M15; 0.2 at M14, 0.06 before)* show in the band `φ ∈ [−0.15, 0.7]`, thinned towards its upstream edge. *(M14)* Before the tone, the light less `0.8×` the unresolved disc light at that radius (the arm factor averages 1) is kept: the inter-arm disc falls to black and the arms and bulge stand on it.
- **Motion**: stars stream at `Ω(r)` (inner ones lap the pattern), the pattern turns at `Ω_p` (corotation at 0.6 R); knots flare and fade; 1 revolution of the pattern ≈ 120 s.
- **Resize**: the star list (guiding radii, phases, seeded) is kept in units of R; R, the projection (inclination 25°, aspect-corrected) and the `Glow`/`Pixels` buffers are rebuilt.
- **Inks** *(M15, ADR-0018: M11's roles in flat steps; were `Ramp(Starlight)`, `Blackbody` and `Ramp(HAlpha)`)*: the toned disc and bulge light in three flat steps, old starlight grading red → yellow → white: from toned 70 a dim `Hot` 70 (the faint outer disc), from 140 `Sun` 150 (the arms), from 205 `Star` 210 (the arm crests and the bulge); young stars near the arms single `Cold` pixels at 255 (the arms read slightly blue); knots single `Accent` pixels, 140 while flaring or fading and 230 at their peak (envelope ≥ 0.5; hidden below 0.15); field stars `Dim` 120 or, one in five, `Star` 170; dust dims only. ASCII: by level (`ascii_ramp_cell`).
- **Title**: `Spiral galaxy · density waves`.
- **Look** *(M16, GEN-R25; supersedes the Model's rendering, the toned light and the Inks above where they disagree)*: star streams on black, all braille dots. The wave has four arms (`m = 4`): `ψ = 4(α + ln r_g / tan p)`, arm phase `φ = 4(θ + ln r / tan p − Ω_p t)`, pitch 22° (open arms; was 18° with two), eccentricities 0.06–0.08 (so the crowding `e·m·cot p` is 0.6–0.8, below orbit crossing). 0.4 stars per cell, capped at 12k; 8 % are an old bulge (Rayleigh radius, σ 0.07 R, ≤ 0.3 R, circular orbits) and 40 % of the disc stars are young. Each star is one dot with a stream thread: the chord back along its circular motion over 0.6 s (≈ 3 dots), in its population's ink one step dimmer: old disc stars `Sun` 150 (thread `Sun` 70); the bulge half `Hot` 215 (130), half `Sun` 235 (140); a young star only while it crosses an arm's leading edge, `φ ∈ [−0.1, 0.9]` (thinned toward the upstream edge by a fixed hash), `Cold` 255 (thread 160), and not at all elsewhere (a massive young star lives only just downstream of the arm that made it). A disc star whose dust weight (the raised cosine at `φ = −0.4 ± 0.25` times the radial weight) exceeds 0.4 is hidden: the lanes are gaps. The arms emerge where the dots crowd, traced in blue. H II knots are two `Accent` dots (150 flaring or fading, 235 at peak); field stars single `Dim` 120 / `Star` 170 dots. A brighter population wins a shared braille cell. No glow, tone or unresolved disc light. The arm phase of each star comes from its own orbit phase, `φ ≈ ψ + m·cot p·ln(1 − e sin ψ)` (second-order log series), not a per-star `atan2`. ASCII: by braille count. *(M16 review)*: the four arms carry the old disc: an old disc star is drawn in full (`Sun` 150, thread 70) only on an arm, arm phase `φ ∈ [−0.2, 1.0]` (the crest to the end of the young-star band, the dust lane just upstream stays a black gap); between the arms only a fixed 35 % of them are drawn (a per-star hash), one step dimmer, `Sun` 110 (thread 50), so the arms stand out by surface brightness. Young stars are `Cold` 230 (thread 150). At least 1400 stars are used, so a small frame (80×24) still traces four arms.
- **Stylisation**: kinematic (Kalnajs) density wave, not a self-gravitating N-body disc: the radial oscillation is forced at `2(Ω − Ω_p)` (kinematic-wave approximation) rather than at the epicyclic frequency κ, and the inside-corotation ordering of the ovals is kept out to the rim; time-lapse (one pattern turn ≈ 250 Myr); dust as an attenuation mask; star count far below reality.
- **Acceptance**: `SPG-A1` a star's orbit is closed: after one epicycle period its position returns within 1e-9 R (closed form); `SPG-A2` the density of stars (binned in `φ`) peaks within ±0.3 rad of the arm phase and the arm/inter-arm contrast is ≥ 1.8 at 200×50; `SPG-A3` the arm pattern (angle of peak density at 0.5 R) turns at `Ω_p ± 5 %` while individual stars at 0.3 R turn faster than `Ω_p` and at 0.9 R slower; `SPG-A4` *(changed, M16)* the dust lanes lie on the inner side of the arm crest (mean `φ` offset < 0), a star at `φ = −0.4` and 0.5 R is hidden while one on the crest or in the bulge is not, and at 200×50 the lit-dot share in the lane (0.3–0.7 R) is below half that just downstream of the crest; *(superseded)* dust-lane pixels are dimmer than the crest; `SPG-A5` knots appear only at `φ ∈ [0, 0.8]` and the pool never exceeds 48. `SPG-A6` *(changed, M16)*: at 200×50 after 2 s every lit cell is a braille cell in one of the populations' head or thread inks, the knots' or the field stars', with > 20 young-star cells; between the arms (`||φ| − π| < 0.4`, 0.3–0.7 R, > 400 dots) under 30 % of the dots are lit, and just downstream of the crests at least 1.5× that share; *(M15, superseded)* light steps, single-pixel knots and ≥ 70 % dark inter-arm pixels; `SPG-A7` *(new, M16)* at 200×50 every bulge star is `Hot` or `Sun` and within 0.3 R, every star drawn young lies in the band `φ ∈ [−0.1, 0.9]`, other disc stars are drawn in the old disc's inks unless hidden in a lane; the per-star position and arm phase match the closed form (within 1e-6 R and 0.02 rad). `SPG-A8` *(new, M16 review)* at 160×45 (seed 42) the lit-dot density on the arms (`φ ∈ [−0.2, 1.0]`, 0.25–0.9 R) is ≥ 2.5× that between them (`|φ| > 1.6`), and the interarm old disc is the dimmer step; SPG-A7 *(changed, M16 review)*: an old disc star is drawn in the arm inks on an arm, in the interarm inks or not at all between the arms.
- **Look** *(M17, GEN-R26, Kalnajs orbit ellipses; supersedes the M16 star streams)*: the density-wave picture made visible: about 40 nested elliptical star orbits (axis ratio ≈ 0.8) as faint `Dim`/`Structure` threads, each orbit's major axis turned a little more than the one inside it, so two grand-design arms appear where the orbits crowd; stars ride the ellipses as dots faster than the pattern turns (inside corotation), so the arms are a traffic jam, not material; young stars are bright `Cold` dots where the crowding is highest, the bulge a `Sun`/`Hot` stipple cloud densest at the centre, HII regions small `Accent` knots on the arm ridges, dust lanes a dark gap on the arms' inner edge; the pattern turns slowly at `Ω_p`, seen at a slight tilt.
- **Model (M17)** *(supersedes the M16 Model's m = 4, pitch and corotation)*: two arms (`m = 2`): 24 orbits, log-spaced guiding radii 0.18–1.0 R, `r = r_j(1 − ε cos 2(α − θ_j))`, `ε = 1/9` (axis ratio 0.8), `θ_j = cot p·ln r_j`, `cot p = 3.2` (each ellipse turned 0.24 rad from the one inside); crowding `1/((1 − ε cos ψ) − 2ε cot p sin ψ)`, `ψ = 2(α − θ_j)`, peak ≈ 3.5 at `tan ψ* = 2 cot p` (no orbit crossing). Pattern turns once in 150 s; corotation at 1.08 R, outside the disc, so every star overtakes the pattern. Threads: sparse dotted orbits, one `Dim` dot per sample ~2.6 dots apart (phase-offset per orbit so no spokes), 50, or 85 where the crowding is ≥ 2; every other orbit below 60 dots of disc radius; hidden in the dust lane `ψ = ψ* + 0.55 ± 0.22` (the inner edge). Stars: 30–110 per orbit (a fixed share drawn below 90 dots of disc radius, ≥ 30 %), 45 % young (`Cold` 255 only where the crowding is ≥ 2; H II knots `Accent` 235, two dots, for a tenth of them where it is ≥ 3), old `Sun` 130 only where the crowding is ≥ 1.25 (the inter-arm disc nearly black). Bulge: compact stipple `0.95 exp(−r/0.04)` within 0.14 R, `Sun` 235 inside 0.04 R, `Hot` 205 beyond. The M16 contracts of `SPG-A2` and `A5`–`A8` are restated for the ellipses (tests chained with `A9`–`A12`): arm/inter-arm contrast ≥ 2.5 (A2, A8), knots only where the crowding is ≥ 3 and outside the lane (A5), braille-only frames in the listed inks with > 20 young cells (A6), a `Sun`/`Hot` bulge (A7); `A1`, `A3`, `A4` hold for the ellipses.
- **Acceptance** *(M17)*: `SPG-A9` at mid radius the star-dot density on the arm ridge is at least twice the inter-arm density; `SPG-A10` stars' angular speed exceeds the pattern speed inside corotation; `SPG-A11` orbit threads are inked at lower levels than star dots; `SPG-A12` the bulge stipple density falls with radius.
- **Look** *(M18, GEN-R26, 6-fold Kalnajs rosettes; supersedes the M17 two-arm ellipses where they disagree)*: as in M17, but with six arms: each orbit is a faint dotted six-lobed rosette, each turned a little from the one inside, so six grand-design arms appear where the rosettes crowd; stars ride the rosettes faster than the pattern turns; young `Cold` stars on the ridges, `Accent` HII knots (two dots), the `Sun`/`Hot` bulge stipple and a dust gap on each arm's inner edge, all as in M17.
- **Model (M18)** *(supersedes the M17 Model's m = 2 and its constants)*: `m = 6`, orbits `r = r_j(1 − ε cos 6(α − θ_j))`, `θ_j = cot p·ln r_j`; crowding `1/((1 − ε cos ψ) − 6ε cot p sin ψ)`, `ψ = 6(α − θ_j)`; the constants are chosen so the orbits never cross (`6ε cot p ≤ 0.75`, peak crowding ≈ 3–4) and the arms are well resolved: about 36 orbits log-spaced over 0.22–1.0 R (at least six orbits per arm spacing in `ln r`, `2π/(6 cot p)`), pitch 35–40° (open arms; at 25° each arm wound 225° and the disc read as rings). The pattern turns once in 150 s as before; corotation stays outside the disc. The thread, star, knot, bulge and lane rules of M17 apply with the crowding thresholds re-tuned for `m = 6`, recorded here. *Recorded*: 36 orbits over 0.22–1.0 R; `ε = 0.09`, `cot p = 1.3` (pitch 37.6°, each arm winds ≈ 110°), so `6ε cot p = 0.70` and the peak crowding ≈ 3.4; corotation at 1.12 R, outside the rosettes' reach `1 + ε`. Ridge (young stars, brighter threads) at crowding ≥ 2.5, knots ≥ 3.1, old stars ≥ 1.7; 60–220 stars per orbit; ridge threads `Dim` 110, others `Dim` 50. Small frames draw every 2nd–4th orbit thread (drawn threads ≥ 3.5 dots apart at 0.5 R) and a share `(R_px/75)²` (≥ 0.25) of the stars.
- **Acceptance** *(M18)*: `SPG-A13` the star-dot density at mid radius, binned in angle, has its strongest Fourier mode at `m = 6`, at least twice any of `m = 1…5`; `SPG-A14` the orbits never cross: `6ε cot p < 1` and, sampled along every orbit, each rosette stays strictly inside the next one out; SPG-A1–A12 hold for the rosettes (A2/A9 arm contrast, A10 star speed, A11 thread levels, A12 bulge).

### cosmic_web  (Deep Cosmos, M13, M16)
- **Intent** *(M14: a thin web on black; M15, ADR-0018: in M11's stepped `Seq`)*: a faint mottling in the dark gathers under gravity into a connected web of faint links, thin filaments and small bright knots: the cosmic web as seen in the Millennium simulation, then gently dissolves and replays.
- **Model**: Zel'dovich approximation `x = q + D(t)·s(q)`, `s = −∇ψ`, with ψ a sum of 48 plane waves (spectrum `P_δ(k) ∝ k^{-2}` over wavelengths of 1/22 to 1/1.6 of the box, periodic, seeded) built on resize as separable sin/cos tables along each axis (the CMB trick, no FFT). One particle per node of the display's pixel lattice capped at 16k (ADR-0012); the next cycle's displacement table is filled a chunk per step. Adhesion-like freeze: each particle stops at its own shell crossing, `x = q + min(D, D_c(q))·s(q)` with `D_c = −1/λ_min(∂s/∂q)`, so pancakes and filaments stay sharp instead of streaming through and smearing back to uniform; `D_max` is 2.5× the first shell crossing (`CROSSING_AT` = 0.4). Periodic wrap and a slow pan across the box. Particles are deposited cloud-in-cell into the lattice, blurred (three `[1 2 1]` passes) where sparse and kept sharp where matter has gathered, sampled to the pixels and tone-mapped with an asinh `Tone` over the density minus a floor, then a gamma 2.5, so the voids end in black on a smooth ramp (no dither). *(M14)* The floor and the scale adapt to each frame: the floor is the density above which 22 % of the lattice lies (at least the mean, 1), the tone's white the density above which 0.3 % lies, and the densest 0.4 % are the knots (white at the densest 0.15 %); so the web shows at every stage (the early fog as faint mottling, the collapsed web as links, filaments and knots). The quantiles come from one fine histogram (2048 bins over densities 0–32) interpolated inside the bin, so they are smooth in time (no pumping from frame to frame) and independent of the render cadence.
- **Motion**: `D(t)` rises over 20 s (fast at first: a faint web, then sheets → filaments → knots), creeps on for 5 s while the view pans, then eases back over 5 s to `D = 0`, where the next cycle's phase-shifted modes take over invisibly. The scene starts at `D ≈ 0.3 D_max`.
- **Resize**: modes stay in box units; particle grid, sin/cos tables and buffers rebuilt for the size.
- **Inks** *(M15, ADR-0018: M11's stepped field; was a smooth `Ramp(Plasma)`)*: a brightness `b` (0–255) as before: faint links at about 20, easing in from black by `smoothstep(t/0.12)` of the toned value `t`, rising as `t^2.5` to 110 in the filaments' cores; the knots continue from 110 at the knot threshold (the densest 0.4 %) to 250 at the densest 0.15 % of the frame. It is drawn as a `Seq` field in four flat steps, like M11's reaction fields: `b` ≥ 15 → `Seq` 60 (violet links), ≥ 50 → 115 (red filaments), ≥ 112 → 180 (orange halos), ≥ 200 → 235 (the densest knots, yellow); below 15 black. ASCII by level.
- **Look** *(M16, GEN-R25; supersedes the toned `Seq` field above)*: the particles themselves are drawn, one braille dot each (the same Zel'dovich positions, frozen at shell crossing, panned and wrapped), on black; no fill. The density lattice is still deposited and smoothed, but only to ink each particle by the density where it sits, in four classes ranked so a brighter one wins a shared braille cell: knot halos (the densest 0.6 % of the lattice, and at least 3× the mean) `Sun` 245; filaments (the densest 5 %, at least 2.2×) `Accent` 200; sheets (≥ 1.4×) `Accent` 110; the rest is fog (or a void's last stragglers), of which a fixed 40 % (a per-particle hash) is drawn `Accent` 40. So the early fog is a faint stipple that collapses into magenta sheets and filaments with yellow knots, and the voids empty to black. Each particle is drawn with a fixed sub-cell jitter (≤ 0.45 lattice cells, drawing only), a glass-like start that breaks the start lattice's rows. No trails: the web's motion is a slow drift. ASCII: by braille count. *(M16 review)*: while the web is diffuse the sheets are thinned too: a sheet particle is drawn only if its fixed hash is below `0.5 + 0.5·smoothstep((d − 0.35)/0.35)` of the growth `d` (in `D_max`), so the early fog is stipple, not a blanket.
- **Title**: `Cosmic web · gravity gathers`.
- **Stylisation**: Zel'dovich (first-order Lagrangian) flow with particles frozen at shell crossing (adhesion-like, not the adhesion model's viscous limit); a 2-D flow, not a slice of 3-D; the loop runs time backwards to restart (stated).
- **Acceptance**: `WEB-A1` with `D = 0` particles sit at their grid points, and with `D > 0` a particle's position is `q + min(D, D_c)·s(q)` exactly (tables match the direct sum); `WEB-A2` the density contrast (std/mean of the binned field) rises monotonically with `D` over the collapse phase; `WEB-A3` at `D_max` the fraction of particles in the densest 10 % of bins is ≥ 35 %; `WEB-A4` the displacement field is curl-free (discrete curl of `s` < 1e-9 of its magnitude); `WEB-A5` the loop is periodic: frames at `t` and `t + T` differ only by the mode phase shift; `WEB-A6` the web never smears back: the density contrast mid-hold is at least that at 15 s; `WEB-A7` *(changed, M16)* the web is particles on black: the four inks rise fog < sheet < filament < knot and a particle's class follows the thresholds; at 120×40 after 12 s every lit cell is a braille cell in one of the four inks, under 30 % of the dots are lit, and knot cells exist but are fewer than filament cells; *(M15, superseded)* every lit pixel is `Seq` at one of the four step levels, under 35 % lit; `WEB-A8` *(new, M16)* the fog gathers: at 120×40 the filament and knot cells at 20.5 s are at least twice those at 0.5 s, when fog and sheet cells outnumber them; the fixed fog hash keeps 40 ± 2 % of the fog. WEB-A8 *(changed, M16 review)*: also the sheet share is 0.5 at `d` = 0 and 0.3 and 1 at 0.8.

### supernova_remnant  (Deep Cosmos, M13, M15, M16, M17)
- **Intent** *(M15, ADR-0018: M11's idiom, on black)*: a line-colour portrait of a young remnant like Cassiopeia A: a thin, dim blast-wave line racing outward, and inside it a filamentary shell of ejecta (silicon, iron and oxygen patches) whose Rayleigh–Taylor fingers reach out, with knots lighting up as the reverse shock reaches them.
- **Model**: forward-shock radius `R_f(t)` from the ejecta-dominated `t^{4/7}` (Chevalier 1982, n = 7) to Sedov–Taylor `t^{2/5}`, a smooth blend (Truelove–McKee style), drawn as a one-pixel line (antialiased inward; *M14*: the outward falloff band is gone); reverse shock `R_r` easing from 0.62 to 0.45 `R_f` over the expansion. Contact discontinuity `R_c(θ) = 0.8 R_f·(1 + finger shaping of Σ a_k(t) cos(kθ + φ_k))`, 24 modes with `k` 5–22 growing as `a_k ∝ e^{√(k/k0)·γt}` capped at 0.12 (R–T growth, saturated), pushed out up to 18 % where fingers reach and pulled in at most 5.4 % between them: fingers. The whole remnant is out of round at low angular order where the surrounding gas is lumpy: a shape factor `1 + 0.09·w(θ, t)`, `w` a sum of modes `m` = 2, 3, 4 (relative amplitudes 1, 0.6, 0.35; `m` = 2 dominant, a lopsided shell) normalised to `[−1, 1]`, their phases drifting slowly (≤ 0.05 rad/s), scales `R_f`, `R_c`, `R_r` and the knots alike, so their ordering holds at every angle. The emitting ejecta sit between max(`R_r`, 0.82 `R_c`) and `R_c`, limb-brightened (projected path through the shell), hollow inside, textured by radial streaks (noise fine in angle, slow in radius) and brightest in the fingers; an element map of broad comoving patches picks Si/S, Fe (a sector) or O. Ejecta knots (pool 1500) coast homologously from the explosion, `r ∝ t`; a knot lights when the reverse shock passes it (`r > R_r`) and fades with τ = 1.5 s to a 25 % afterglow, drawn as a soft glow (no tail) that brightens the ejecta it sits in. Emission is computed per `Pixels` pixel with per-angle tables, and the ejecta field (knots included) is smoothed with a 3 × 3 binomial blur before tone mapping, so the R–T fingers read as filaments, not grain; element colour comes from the smooth element map only.
- **Motion**: the blast wave expands over a 36 s loop (fast at first; model time starts at 0.3, so the radius grows ~1.7× between the scene's 1 s and 5 s) from a flash to 0.9 of the half-height, then the remnant fades over 4 s and a new one starts from a flash; the scene opens ~5.4 s after a flash (15 % into the expansion, so the first frame is a grown remnant); fingers grow; knots light and fade continuously.
- **Resize**: modes and knots in units of `R_f`; tables and buffers rebuilt.
- **Inks** *(M15, ADR-0018: M11's roles in flat steps; were M14's `Starlight`/`Sulfur`/`HAlpha`/`Ice` ramps)*: the blast-wave line one flat dim blue, `Cold` 70; the ejecta's toned value `u` (×1.8 exposure) at `250·u²` through a 256-entry table, black below level 12, its bodies (≤ 110) two flat dim orange steps of shocked gas (`Hot` 0 below level 55, 45 above) and the brighter knots and fingertips one flat colour per element: sulfur/silicon `Sun` 210, iron `Hot` 220, oxygen `Wave` 200; knots splat to single pixels, the ejecta smoothed by one `[1 2 1]` blur; the young remnant's exposure (shock and ejecta) is lifted ×3.5 at the start, easing to ×1 over its first 6 model seconds, so it reads from the first second; background stars (one per 200 cells) `Star` 40 or 120; the central compact object a single `Star` 200 dot; the flash flat rings, `Star` 230, `Sun` 220, `Hot` 150, with a `Star` 255 heart. ASCII by level.
- **Look** *(M16, GEN-R25; supersedes the M15 Inks and the element colours)*: a filament shell drawn in braille threads on black, like the filaments of the Crab and Cas A. The shocked ejecta are radial Rayleigh–Taylor filaments, each a one-dot thread from the inner edge of the emitting shell, max(`R_r`, 0.5 `R_c0`) (in practice the reverse shock), out to its fingertip at the contact `R_c(θ)` (long where the R–T fingers reach, short stubs between them), wiggling sideways by a smooth per-filament curve of at most ~2 dots; their angles follow the golden-angle sequence, and the drawn prefix holds one filament per `FIL_SPACING` = 2 dots of the inner edge's circumference (at most 400), so the shell keeps its spacing as it grows and new filaments sprout between old ones. Colour along a filament is the ejecta's temperature, which falls with the time since the reverse shock heated it: the inner 35 % (freshly shocked, next to the reverse shock) `Sun` 220, the rest `Hot` 190 in the fingers and `Hot` 150 on the stubs between them. Knots the reverse shock has just lit are sparks coasting outward on their homologous paths, `r ∝ t`: a head dot (`Star` 255 while its brightness is ≥ 0.75, else `Sun` 220) and a radial trail back along the path it took in the last `SPARK_TRAIL` = 1.2 s (`Sun` 220), drawn while the brightness is ≥ 0.45; afterwards a knot is part of the filament shell. The forward shock is a faint, thin `Cold` 110 circle one dot thick at `R_f(θ)` (dotted: every other dot), outside the filaments. Background stars single `Star` 60/120 dots (one per 200 cells); the compact object one `Star` 200 dot; the flash a braille starburst (a `Sun` 220 ring and `Star` 255 arms that shrink as it fades) round a `Star` 255 heart. In the 4 s fade the drawn filament prefix shrinks with the envelope, so the remnant dissolves thread by thread. No half blocks.
- **Title**: `Supernova remnant · blast wave`.
- **Stylisation**: element → hue on the brightest ejecta only (*M15*: false colour in M11's roles, sulfur yellow, iron red, oxygen cyan; the early exposure lift; the oxygen patches stand for O emission seen mostly in optical light and Chandra's O VIII lines); a 2-D projection of shells; the time scale is ~300 yr in 36 s, and the blast wave reaches the Sedov slope sooner than Cas A does (its expansion index is m ≈ 0.65 today); R–T growth is exponential then clamped, not simulated hydrodynamics.
- **Acceptance**: `SNR-A1` `R_f` is increasing and its log-slope moves from ≥ 0.55 early to ≤ 0.45 late; `SNR-A2` `R_r < R_c < R_f` at every angle; `SNR-A3` a knot is lit only after crossing `R_r` and its brightness is non-increasing afterwards; `SNR-A4` *(changed, M16)* every `Cold` shock dot lies within 1.5 dots of `R_f(θ)`, and the shock is a thin circle (≥ 40 % of its circumference drawn); `SNR-A5` *(changed, M16)* the knot pool never exceeds its capacity and the loop restarts with a flash (the centre cell is the `Star` 255 heart); `SNR-A7` *(changed, M16)* at 200×50 more than 60 cells are lit one second in, and at every time every lit cell is braille in one of the M16 inks (`Cold` 110, `Sun` 220, `Hot` 190/150, `Star`); `SNR-A6` the blast wave is visibly out of round (max/min radius 1.08–1.2 for any seed), at low order (≤ 8 sign changes of the shape round the rim), and reshapes only slowly (< 0.01 per frame); ~~SNR-A8~~ *(superseded by the M17 look)* *(new, M16)* the ejecta are thin radial threads: ≥ 85 % of filament dots have at most 3 lit dots across the radial direction within ±2 dots, the filament count follows the shell circumference (more at 30 s than at 3 s), and every filament dot lies between `R_r` and the blast wave; ~~SNR-A9~~ *(superseded by the M17 look)* *(new, M16)* colour by temperature: `Sun` filament dots sit on average inside `Hot` ones (smaller `r/R_f`); `SNR-A10` *(new, M16)* a spark's trail is radial and behind its head (the head is the farthest point from the centre), its length the distance coasted in `SPARK_TRAIL`.
- **Look** *(M17, GEN-R26, Doppler-shell Cas A; supersedes the M16 radial filament combs)*: the ejecta are a few hundred knots on a slowly turning 3D shell, each knot a small stipple puff (fixed samples, 6–12 dots) coloured by its line-of-sight velocity, as the two signs of an orbital lobe: `Cold` where it approaches us (blueshift), `Hot` where it recedes, `Sun` near the limb where `|v_z|` is small; Rayleigh–Taylor fingers are fine threads trailing inward behind knots; the forward shock is a thin faint `Cold` synchrotron thread with a sparse stipple; the neutron star is a tiny `Star` dot kicked off-centre and drifting; the shell expands with knot afterglow trails. *(Model, M17: knots coast freely until the reverse shock lights them, then decelerate into the shocked shell at 0.92 × the R–T-rippled contact radius, so they gather between the reverse shock and the contact; face-on knots show fewer, dimmer dots than limb knots (limb brightening), so the shell reads as a ring; small screens draw a prefix of the knots.)*
- **Acceptance** *(M17)*: `SNR-A11` a knot is `Cold` iff its line-of-sight velocity points toward the viewer by more than the limb band, `Hot` iff away, `Sun` otherwise; `SNR-A12` every knot lies inside the forward shock; `SNR-A13` the neutron star is off-centre and moves; `SNR-A14` the forward-shock rim is at most two dots thick and sparse (at most half its dots lit).

### relativistic_jet  (Deep Cosmos, M13, M16)
- **Intent**: the jet of M87 streaming from a bright galaxy core into a radio lobe: bright knots race outward at what looks like several times the speed of light and pulse, a hotspot and bow shock glow where the jet stops, and the counter-jet is invisible while its (unbeamed) lobe still shows faintly.
- **Model**: jet at angle `θ = 17°` to the line of sight, bulk speed `β` = 0.98 (γ ≈ 5). A knot moving at `β` appears to move at `β_app = β sin θ / (1 − β cos θ)` (≈ 3–5.4 c for β 0.95–0.99), and is boosted by `δ^{3}` with `δ = 1/(γ(1 − β cos θ))`; the counter-jet has `δ' = 1/(γ(1 + β cos θ))`, so it is dimmed by `(δ/δ')³` (≳ 10³). Knots (pool 64) are launched from HST-1 at seeded times with `β ∈ [0.95, 0.99]`, positions advanced in closed form, their brightness pulsing. The jet body is a conical glow with a helical brightness ripple and stationary brightenings, ending in a compact, bright, pulsing terminal hotspot; the lobes are unbeamed, diffuse turbulent plumes: a cocoon narrow at the hotspot and widening back toward the core, its domain-warped noise texture drifting back toward the core with the backflow, a faint bow-shock arc just ahead of the hotspot (the counter-lobe at 45 %, smaller, joined to the core by a faint radio bridge along the counter-jet's line, in lobe ink: the counter-jet itself stays invisible); the host galaxy is a de Vaucouleurs profile tapered into the sky, with globular clusters and a white core.
- **Motion**: knots move out in apparent-speed units (a readout `v_app 4.6c` right-aligned, updated per knot); HST-1 flares every ~12 s; the helix drifts; knots and hotspot pulse.
- **Resize**: knot state in jet-length units; glows rebuilt.
- **Inks** *(M15, ADR-0018: M11's roles in flat steps; were `Ramp(Plasma)`, `Ramp(Ice)`, `Ramp(Starlight)`, `Blackbody`)*: each toned field in flat steps, the jet over the lobes over the host: the jet's synchrotron light `Accent` 110 (toned ≥ 22) and 200 (≥ 70), its knot peaks `Star` 230 (≥ 190); the lobes `Cold` 90 (≥ 25) and 170 (≥ 80); the host galaxy `Dim` 90 (≥ 45) with a `Sun` 110 centre (≥ 110); globular clusters single `Dim` 110 pixels; the terminal hotspot a 2 × 2 and the core a small disc of `Star` 255. No lobe edge colour. ASCII by level.
- **Look** *(M16, GEN-R25; supersedes the M15 Inks and the M14 look)*: twin jets as helical magnetic field-line threads on black, from a tiny accretion disc round a black core. The disc is a small flat ellipse of `Sun` 210 braille dots across the jet axis (semi-axes max(3, 0.03·dot height) and 0.35 of that) with an unlit core. Each jet is three helical strands, a helix seen from the side: transverse offset `w(s)·sin(K s − Ω t + 2πj/3)`, `j` = 0, 1, 2, inside the cone `w(s) = (0.012 + 0.045 s)·len`, sampled every 0.8 dots, `K` = 30 per jet length, the pattern drifting outward at `Ω/K` = 0.06 jet lengths per second; colour along a strand is its depth: the half of each turn in front (`cos(·) > 0`) `Wave` 200, the half behind `Cold` 110. The counter-jet's strands run from the core to the counter-lobe as a sparse dotted thread, one segment in three, in `Cold` 60: its field structure, not its light (beamed to ≲ 10⁻³, JET-A3). Knots brighter than 0.15 are `Accent` heads (a disc of radius 1 dot, `Accent` 255 while brighter than 0.5, else 200) with a trail along the axis back over the distance they covered in `KNOT_TRAIL` = 1.5 s (`Accent` 150); HST-1 an `Accent` 255 disc of radius 0.5 + 1.5 × the flare. The terminal hotspot a `Star` 255 disc of radius 1 dot. The radio lobes (and the counter-lobe, the bridge and the bow-shock arc) are a faint stipple: on every second dot each way, a dot is lit with probability `0.45·min(1, e/0.25)` of the plume's emissivity `e` by a fixed per-dot hash, in `Cold` 130, so the lobes read as grain whose texture drifts with the backflow. The plume's emissivity is cached on that lattice over the lobes' bounding box and re-evaluated a row at a time: row `j` of `n` shows the plume at `(⌊t/Q + j/n⌋ − j/n)·Q`, `Q` = 0.4 s, so each frame re-evaluates about `Δt/Q` of the rows and the picture depends only on the time, not on the draw cadence (GEN-R20). The host galaxy's light is gone; its globular clusters are single `Dim` 90 dots (one per 150 cells). No half blocks.
- **Title**: `Black hole jet · beamed near c`; readout right-aligned (GEN-R13).
- **Stylisation**: time-lapse (decades in seconds); jet length, lobe and knot sizes not to scale; knots keep their launch speed to the end (in M87 the motion is superluminal near HST-1 and knot D, while the outer knots A and B move at ≲ 1 c); the apparent-speed formula is exact, the knot emissivity is art; the white core is art (the nucleus is synchrotron light); the jet's magenta and the lobes' blue are false colour; the terminal hotspot and bow-shock arc are borrowed from FR II jets (M87 is FR I: its jet decollimates past knot A/C into turbulent radio lobes); the lobes are radio emission shown alongside the optical jet.
- **M14 look**: a narrower jet (opening 0.012, a 1–2-px core); a tighter host (effective radius 0.28, was 0.5); the terminal hotspot a 2 × 2-pixel white point.
- **Acceptance**: `JET-A1` `β_app` from the pure function matches the formula and exceeds 1 for `β = 0.98, θ = 17°`; `JET-A2` a knot's on-screen speed equals `β_app` in the scene's c-units within 2 %; `JET-A3` *(changed, M16)* the counter-jet's emissivity is < 1 % of the jet's, no knot ink (`Accent`) shows behind the core, and the counter-jet's threads are only `Cold` 60, with fewer than a third as many dots as the jet's; `JET-A4` knots never exceed the pool and new ones start at HST-1; `JET-A5` the readout never enters the title span; `JET-A6` the lobes are plumes trailing back from the hotspot: 0.15 jet lengths behind it the plume is over 1.5× as wide as just ahead of it, nothing lies far ahead of the hotspot or beyond the cocoon's tail, the hotspot end is the brightest part, and the texture changes with the backflow; `JET-A7` *(new, M16)* the jet is three helical threads: every jet-strand dot lies within the cone half-width (+1 dot) of the axis, `Wave` where the strand is in front and `Cold` where it is behind, and the pattern moves outward (the strand at `s` and `t` is the strand at `s + Ω Δt/K` and `t + Δt`); `JET-A8` *(new, M16)* a knot's trail lies on the axis behind its head (toward the core), as long as the distance it moves in `KNOT_TRAIL`; `JET-A9` *(new, M16)* the lobes are a stipple, not a fill: at most 45 % of the even-lattice dots inside the plume are lit and none off the even lattice, and every cache row shows the plume at its staggered refresh time, at most `Q` old.

### light_echo  (Deep Cosmos, M13, M15, M16)
- **Intent** *(M15, ADR-0018: thin shells on black in M11's idiom)*: V838 Monocerotis: a star flashes, and over the next seconds thin rings of light sweep outward through the surrounding dust, lighting swirls and cavities that were always there, apparently faster than light, while the previous flashes' echoes fade farther out.
- **Model**: for an observer far away, the scattering surface at time `t` after a flash is the paraboloid `z = ρ²/(2ct) − ct/2` (star at the origin, `z` toward the observer). Dust is 4 static thin sheets `z_k(x, y) = z0_k + h_k(x, y)` (value-noise ripples, one seed, precomputed on resize) with density `n_k(x, y)` (swirly domain-warped noise with a cavity near the star). For each node and sheet, the light arriving now left the star at `t_e = t − Δ(ρ, z_k)/c` with `Δ = √(ρ² + z_k²) − z_k`; brightness `= n_k · L(t_e) · Φ(angle) / r`, `L` a three-peaked light curve (the 2002 shape, linearly interpolated; *M14*: peaks 0.2, 0.25 and 0.3 s wide, were 0.55–0.95 s, so each lit sheet is a thin shell 1–3 px across rather than a broad band), the inverse-square dilution softened to `1/r` (stylised), `Φ` Henyey–Greenstein (g = 0.6); brightness and brightness-weighted temperature are smoothed together by two *(M14; were three)* passes of a separable `[1 2 1]` blur before tone mapping, so the lit sheets read as soft dust, not blocks or stair-steps; the colour temperature is `T(t_e)`, treated as monotone cooling from 8 kK to 3 kK (V838's outburst peaked near 7–8 kK). The echoes of the two previous flashes are added at 55 % and 25 % weight. ≤ 24k lattice nodes (ADR-0012) × 4 sheets × 3 flashes per frame.
- **Motion**: a 0.5 s dark gap, the flash, then the echo rings expand for 21 s, fading as they spread, while the older rings keep expanding beyond them; the loop repeats every 21.5 s over the same dust. *(M16)*: no dead stretch: the loop is retimed (loop length, flash timing and/or the fade of the older echoes) so that over every 0.5 s of the loop the lit ring dots stay ≥ 35 % of their loop mean; the loop still opens on rings already on screen.
- **Resize**: sheet heights and densities rebuilt per size from the seed (same dust, rescaled).
- **Look** *(M16, supersedes the M14/M15 inks below where they disagree; strange_attractors' thread idiom)*: the echoes are drawn as braille dots (`raster::Dots`), not half-block fills: the scattered brightness is computed as before (lattice, light curve, phase function, exposure) and sampled per braille dot; a dot is lit only on a ring's crest, so every echo is a thin ring about one dot thick (a ridge test across the ring, or an equivalent analytic crest band), more dots where the crest is brighter, black between rings. Colour is by age, one ink per cell from the strongest contribution: the newest flash's echo `Sun` 230 on its brightest crests and `Hot` 190 elsewhere, the previous flash's echo `Accent` 150, the oldest `Cold` 130 (warm → cool as the generations recede); at most two flat steps per generation. The flash is visible: while the light curve exceeds 0.3 the star is a braille starburst (a 4-spike cross of dots, arm length growing with `L` up to ~3 cells) in `Star` 255 with a `Sun` 230 inner ring, otherwise a single `Star` 255 dot. The crests are the analytic crest band: marching-squares level sets `Δ = c(t + age − t_p)` of each sheet's lag field at the light curve's crests `t_p`, drawn as one-dot braille lines; dim crests (exposed brightness 0.012–0.06) are thinned by a fixed per-node hash so the pattern holds still as the ring moves. The stripes were the newest flash's 12 shells merging as thick half-block bands, which one-dot threads remove (ECHO-A11). Retimed: the loop repeats every 14 s (a 0.5 s dark gap, the flash, 13.5 s of rings; was 21.5 s); the two previous flashes' echoes at 45 % and 35 % weight (were 55 % and 25 %); the echo weight fades beyond 1.3 half-heights over 0.6 (was 0.95 over 0.5). ASCII: by braille count, as strange_attractors.
- **Inks** *(M14, ADR-0017; were `Ice`/`Ember` fills with a dust-grey fringe and unlit dust ≤ 20)*: an automatic exposure, like a camera's: each `update` finds the peak scattering weight lit now on every 13th lattice node, with the light curve at its envelope (so the exposure follows the shells' geometric dilution, not the flash's own peaks, which brighten and fade the frame as they should), and eases the gain toward `6·2.2 / peak` with time constant 1.5 s of simulated time (smooth, and independent of the render cadence, GEN-R20), clamped to 1–6; just after each flash the exposure is still set for the dim older echoes, so the new flash briefly blooms and settles within ~1.5 s; the toned value `u` is taken to `240·u³` through a 256-entry table (a contrast gamma: a shell's soft skirts fall to black, its crest stays legible) and drawn in three flat steps *(M15, ADR-0018; were M14's `Starlight` with an `Ice` tint on the crests)*: from level 18, 70 and 150 up, light that left the star at ≥ 5.5 kK (the hot first peak, each ring's leading edge) is `Cold` 60, `Cold` 200, `Wave` 230, and the cooler light of the later peaks `Hot` 50, `Hot` 190, `Sun` 230: complementary blue and orange rings; black below. Unlit dust is not drawn. The echo weight still fades beyond 0.95 half-heights over 0.5. The star is a single `Star` 255 pixel (a plus of five while the light curve exceeds 0.3; V838 Mon in fact became a red supergiant). ASCII by level.
- **Title**: `Light echo · flash lights dust`.
- **Stylisation**: dust as 4 sheets, not a 3-D cloud; time compressed (4 yr in ~21 s); the outburst's peaks sharpened so the shells read thin; apparent superluminal expansion is genuine geometry, not moving matter (said in the spec, not the title); the loop repeats the outburst, and the echoes of the two previous loops are kept at 55 % and 25 % so rings are always on screen; V838 Mon erupted once.
- **Acceptance**: `ECHO-A1` the echo paraboloid function satisfies `√(ρ² + z²) − z = c t` for sampled points; `ECHO-A2` for a flat sheet at depth `z0` the lit ring radius is `√(ct(ct + 2 z0))` (increasing, apparent ring speed exceeding `c` early); `ECHO-A3` dust pixels never move: the unlit dust density map is identical at two times; `ECHO-A4` a node lit by one (sheet, flash) pair carries the light curve's temperature at its `t_e` (`T(t_e)` non-increasing in `t_e`, so a ring's leading edge is the hottest); `ECHO-A5` a flash lights no dust before it happens (only older echoes show before loop time 0); `ECHO-A6` *(changed, M16)* every lit cell is a star ink (`Star`, or the flash's `Sun` within 4 cells of the star) or a generation ink (`Sun`/`Hot` newest, `Accent` previous, `Cold` oldest), all three generations show over a loop, and the newest generation's inks dominate; *(M15, superseded)* every pixel is black, the star, or one of the six echo steps, and both the hot light (blue steps) and the cooler scattered light (warm steps) show over a loop, the warm light dominating; `ECHO-A7` *(changed, M16)* the rings are thin: at 120×40 over a loop ≥ 85 % of lit echo dots have at most 3 lit dots in the 5-dot window across their ring (radially), and ≤ 2.5 % of lit dots are isolated specks (no lit 8-neighbour dot); *(M15, superseded)* the lit dust is smooth, not blocky or ragged: at 120×40 over a loop the mean step between 4-neighbouring echo pixels is ≤ 1 of the three flat steps *(M15; M14 bounded the level step by 40)* and ≤ 2.5 % of echo pixels are specks (fewer than two echo 8-neighbours; *M14*: 8, since a thin shell running diagonally touches its neighbours only at corners); `ECHO-A8` *(new, M14)* the exposure gain moves by at most `1 − e^{−dt/1.5 s}` of the way to its target per step, and after 10 s stepping at 1/60 s or 1/30 s gives gains within 5 %. `ECHO-A9` *(new, M16)* the flash is visible: at each light-curve crest and ±0.1 s around it the starburst covers ≥ 9 lit dots and spans ≥ 2 cells each way, and while L < 0.3 the star is one dot; `ECHO-A10` *(new, M16)* no dead stretch: at 120×40, sampling every 0.5 s over a loop, the lit echo dot count never falls below 35 % of its loop mean; `ECHO-A11` *(new, M16)* no stripes: at 120×40 over a loop, the share of lit echo dots in horizontal runs of ≥ 6 lit dots is ≤ 1.5× the share in vertical runs of ≥ 6 (rings are isotropic, so row-aligned runs must not dominate).

### planetary_nebula  (Deep Cosmos, M13, M15, M16)
- **Intent** *(M16: bipolar threads, the AGB shell rings removed)*: a dying Sun-like star's last breaths: the cool giant's slow, dusty wind drifting out, densest round its equator; then a fast wind from the exposed hot core blowing an hourglass of two lobes through it, drawn as the fast wind's streamlines; then the core, now ~100 kK, ionising the lobes, which glow cyan-green inside with red knots at their tips, like the Cat's Eye or NGC 6302.
- **Model** *(M16)*: lengths in the half-height `H`. Slow wind: a pool of 480 dust grains, each launched from the star in a fixed direction relative to the symmetry axis (70 % within a Gaussian of 0.35 rad about the equator, the rest uniform) and coasting at `V_DUST` = 0.045 H/s (±20 %), `r = v·((t + φ) mod T)` out to `R_DUST` = 0.95, a steady outflow. Fast wind: two lobes along the axis, the lobe wall `R(θ) = L·|cos θ|^2.7` (θ from the axis: teardrops pinched to the star at the waist, an hourglass whose length is ≥ 1.4 times its full width), its length `L(t)` inflating quickly to 0.62 `L_FULL` in act 2 then creeping on to `L_FULL` = 0.85 through act 3. The streamlines of the wind, leaving the star sideways and deflected poleward by the dense equatorial gas, are the curves `r = F·L·|cos θ|^p` from the star to the axis at `F·L`: per lobe and side 5 of them, `(p, F)` = (2.7, 1), (3.3, 0.93), (4.2, 0.86), (5.5, 0.79), (7.5, 0.72) (the first is the lobe wall), drawn from 0.22 `L` out (where the streamlines have spread apart); and the Cat's Eye's second, smaller pair of lobes (`L₂` = 0.55 `L`, its axis tilted 20°) with 3 each, (5, 1), (7, 0.85), (10, 0.7), inside the first. The fast wind sweeps the slow wind up: no dust is drawn inside the lobe wall. Ionisation: a front sweeps out through the lobes in normalised radius `q = r/R(θ)` (each pair by its own wall) (to 1.25 over 4 s from the ionised act); low-ionisation knots (FLIERs) sit just beyond both tips (1.12 `L`) and drift outward.
- **Motion**: `Timeline`: slow wind (6 s) → core exposed, the lobes inflate (8 s) → ionised nebula (26 s) → fade (5 s); the dust drifts out all the time; the wind flows along the streamlines as dashes moving outward at `V_FLOW` = 0.06 H/s; the axis turns slowly and precesses (the Cat's Eye's point symmetry); the FLIERs drift outward and flicker.
- **Resize**: all geometry in units of the half-height; buffers rebuilt.
- **Inks** *(M15, ADR-0018: M11's roles in flat steps; were M14's `Ice`/`Starlight`/`HAlpha` ramps)*: [O III] at `210·u^3.5` of its toned value `u` after ×4 exposure (a contrast curve: dark within, bright limbs), drawn green `Particle` 0 from level 12 and `Particle` 100 from 50, and cyan `Wave` 200 on the crests (from 120); [N II] (658 nm, beside Hα) up to level 70, drawn red `Hot` 70, or `Hot` 220 from 40 (the complementary rim), its rim weighted by `c⁴` of the angle to the axis so it glows only toward the ends of the major axis; AGB rings and haze (dust lit by the cool giant) at `180·u^1.2` of their emission `u` after ×1.5, three flat red steps `Hot` 10 from level 40, `Hot` 80 from 70, `Hot` 170 from 120, black below; the haze at 0.25; both curves are 256-entry tables. Before the ionisation front has swept the bubble (front < 1 bubble radius), the fast wind's swept-up rim (q 0.9–1.03, where no [O III] or [N II] shows) is drawn `Cold` 150, `Wave` 200 toward the ends of the axis (`c² > 0.6`): the cold partner to the red shells in every act. Background stars (one per 200 cells) `Star` 40 or 120. The central star is one `Star` 255 pixel. ASCII by level.
- **Look** *(M16, GEN-R25; supersedes the M15 Inks)*: braille threads on black, no shells, rings, fills or haze. The streamlines are one-dot threads drawn as dashes (`DASH` = 0.08 H, 75 % on) that move outward with the flow; colour along a thread is its ionisation: inside the front the outer lobes are [O III] `Wave` 200 and the inner lobes `Particle` 160, outside it (the hot, not yet ionised bubble) `Cold` 150; once the core is hot ([N II] glow ≥ 0.5) the lobe wall beyond 0.82 `L` is [N II] `Hot` 190. The FLIERs are small `Hot` 220 dot clusters (radius 1 dot) with a 3-dot trail toward the star. Dust grains are `Hot` 120 dots with a 2-dot trail behind them, thinning out past 0.6 H, and are not faded by the envelope: the slow wind keeps blowing through the fade and the restart. The central star is one `Star` 255 dot; background stars (one per 200 cells) `Star` 40 or 120. In the fade, whole threads drop out by a fixed per-thread hash as the envelope falls. No half blocks.
- **Title**: `Planetary nebula · winds collide`; captions *(changed, M16)* `slow wind`, `fast wind`, `ionised` replace the mechanism per act.
- **Stylisation**: narrowband false colour; shells are analytic, not hydrodynamics; ~10⁴ yr in 45 s.
- **Acceptance**: `PNE-A1` *(changed, M16)* each dust grain moves radially outward at its speed (0.8–1.2 `V_DUST`), and at least 55 % of the grains lie within 30° of the equator; `PNE-A2` *(changed, M16)* the lobes are an hourglass: length / full width ≥ 1.4 and every streamline lies inside its lobe wall (`r ≤ R(θ)`); `PNE-A3` *(changed, M16)* in the ionised act [O III] dots (`Wave`, `Particle`, > 20) lie inside the outer lobes (q < 1.1) and ≥ 80 % of [N II] thread dots (`Hot` 190) within 3 dots of the lobe wall or of a FLIER; `PNE-A4` *(changed, M16)* the lobes are hollow threads: ≥ 85 % of thread dots have at most 3 lit dots within ±2 dots across their streamline; `PNE-A5` the caption follows the Timeline act; `PNE-A6` *(changed, M16)* at 200×50 at 3, 8, 20 and 30 s every lit cell is braille (no half blocks) in `Hot`, `Star`, `Wave`, `Particle` or `Cold`, exactly one dot cell is `Star` 255 (the star), and no dust is drawn inside the lobe wall; `PNE-A7` *(new, M16)* the flow moves outward: the dash pattern at distance `s` from the star and time `t` is the pattern at `s + V_FLOW·Δt` and `t + Δt`.

### jwst_unfolding  (Voyages, M13, M15, M16)
- **Intent**: the James Webb Space Telescope unfolding after launch: the fairing opens on the folded observatory, the sunshield pallets drop, the mid-booms stretch the shield and its five layers tension one by one, the secondary mirror swings out, the gold mirror wings fold into place, and the observatory cruises into its halo orbit around L2, the Earth and Moon small behind.
- **Model**: a diorama driven by a `Timeline` of deployment beats (the real sequence, compressed). Parts are convex polygons in a 3-D body frame (sunshield layers as two halves of an elongated hexagon each, the primary as an explicit 18-hex honeycomb: three panels (the centre's 12 segments round the open central hole, and the two 3-segment wing columns) rasterised per `Pixels` pixel by mapping the pixel back into the mirror plane (flat-top axial hex lookup), each segment a flat pale-gold fill with a one-pixel dark backplane gap measured in pixel space, the hole dark grey, an edge-on wing a flat gold strip; bus, solar array and fairing halves) projected with `geom::Camera` (then rolled 0.08 rad about the screen centre), painted far to near with per-polygon gradients, a specular band sweeping across the gold (each segment lit as a whole, keeping its flat fill) and a sheen across the Kapton; the secondary a gold dot in a dark baffle on a three-strut tripod of dark struts; edges and struts in braille dots, depth-tested against the painted surfaces. Each beat is an eased angle or extension: pallets fold down, the tower lifts the telescope, the mid-booms extend, then the layers tension from the Sun-facing layer 1 up to layer 5. Final act: a halo orbit drawn as a Lissajous-like loop around L2 in the rotating frame, with the telescope moving along it and the Earth–Moon in the distance.
- **Motion**: beats: fairing (3.5 s), pallets (5 s), tower (3 s), mid-booms and layers tension (10 s), secondary (4 s), wings (7 s), orbit (16 s); the camera sways.
- **Resize**: camera fit rebuilt; state in the Timeline.
- **Layout**: the deployed shield spans ~60–65 % of the width, seen as a thin tilted diamond (camera pitch 0.4 rad, roll 0.08 rad, yaw 0.14 ± 0.12 rad so the mirror faces the viewer) with the five layers' near edges visible under the top sheet (layers drawn lowest-first so the top sheet is never notched); stowed, the camera sees the stack in profile (yaw 1.1 rad, swinging round as the pallets drop): the telescope with its wings folded, the pallets two grey-silver slabs folded up beside it, the bus a box right under their hinge line and the solar array a thin panel hinged on the bus, one connected body.
- **Inks** *(M15, ADR-0018: M11's roles, every shaded surface in four flat steps (`step`: 30 below level 60, 90, 160, 230 from 185); were M14's `Ember`/`Starlight`/`Plasma`/`Cobalt` ramps)*: the mirror gold `Sun` (level 200 ± 9 per segment, the specular glint sweeping every 14 s lifting it a step); the sunshield silver Kapton `Structure` (top layer and layers beneath by their level), the lower layers' shadowed edges `Accent` braille line work only (no magenta fills); the honeycomb's gaps `Star` 24, its central hole 34; stowed pallets, structure, fairing and solar array `Structure` steps; the secondary and trail `Sun`; the Earth `Cold` steps, the Moon `Star`, the distant Sun `Sun` steps; stars `Star` points. ASCII: blocks by level, lines by braille count.
- **Look** *(M16, GEN-R25, blueprint line art; supersedes the M15 Inks and the M14 shading above; geometry, camera and beats unchanged)*: nothing is filled. Every part is still a convex polygon in the body frame, but polygons only write a per-pixel depth buffer (the `w × 2h` lattice of the old half blocks), which hides the line work behind them; what is drawn is braille: the bus and solar array outlines, the tower and the folded pallets in `Structure` 170, the secondary's tripod struts `Structure` 130, the fairing halves' outlines `Wave` 150. Each sunshield layer's outline is coloured by its temperature: layer 5 (facing the telescope, ≈ 40 K) `Cold` 200, then `Wave` 160, `Accent` 150, `Hot` 160, and layer 1 (facing the Sun, ≈ 380 K) `Hot` 230, so the stacked near edges read as the shield's cold-to-hot gradient; folded (before the booms), plain `Structure` lines. The gold mirror is the one solid subject, drawn in dots so it stays braille (GEN-R25's 3 % half-block budget would not hold a half-block mirror against thin line work): every dot inside one of the 18 segments, off the one-pixel seams and the central hole and not behind a nearer surface, lit in the segment's `Sun` step (level 200 ± 9, the glint lifting it), so the honeycomb is a field of gold dots with black seams. The secondary is a small `Sun` 240 dot disc. Stars are single `Star` dots on open sky. The orbit act is braille too: the Earth a small dotted disc (`Cold` 230 day side to the Sun, `Cold` 120 night limb), the Moon a `Star` dot, the halo loop dashed, JWST a `Cold` shield stroke with a gold dot and a dashed gold trail, the Sun a thin `Sun` limb arc off the left edge. ASCII by dot count. *Round 2 (M16)*: frame lines `Structure` 200 (was 170); the tower is one continuous line, not hidden by the shield it passes through; a mirror dot is hidden only by a nearer surface (compared at its pixel's centre, so a steeply seen panel never hides its own dots); the seams are drawn only while a segment's edge spans ≥ 6 dots on screen along its shorter axis, otherwise the honeycomb is solid gold with its hole.
- **Title**: `JWST · unfolding in space`; captions `fairing opens`, `sunshield opens`, `layers tension`, `mirror unfolds`, `halo orbit at L2`.
- **Stylisation**: two weeks in ~48 s (the fairing opened on launch day, the rest over the following two weeks); sizes, layer spacing and orbit not to scale; the halo orbit is an illustrative loop, not a computed CR3BP solution.
- **M14 geometry, M15 inks**: the sunshield a faint silver fill (top layer 48–62, the layers under it 30–42, stowed 70, so its lowest step) whose 1-px braille layer edges carry the shape; the glint the bright accent on the gold.
- **Acceptance**: `JWST-A1` beats happen in the documented order (fairing, pallets, tower, mid-booms, layers, secondary, wings); `JWST-A2` the five layers tension one at a time, Sun-facing layer 1 first; `JWST-A3` the mirror is fully deployed (all 18 hexes visible) at the end of the wings beat, each wing's three hexes showing once it has swung out (folded, a wing hides behind the centre panel); `JWST-A4` captions follow the Timeline; `JWST-A5` after the loop the diorama resets to stowed; `JWST-A6` *(changed, M16)* fully deployed, ≥ 90 % of the mirror's gold (`Sun`) cells are on its upper steps (≥ 110) and the shield's layer outlines span 55–72 % of the width at 80×24, 120×40 and 200×50; `JWST-A7` *(changed, M16)* the primary reads as JWST's honeycomb: walking between ≥ 30 neighbouring segments' centres crosses unlit dots between gilded ones, the centre is an unlit hole and the secondary shows as gold in front of it; stowed (5 s), the camera looks at the stack in profile and the bus joins the stack (the depth buffer is solid from the bus to the mirror); `JWST-A8` *(changed, M16)* deployed, at 80×24, 120×40 and 200×50 every lit cell is a braille cell on black (no fills), and both the cold top layer's `Cold` outline and the Sun-facing layer's `Hot` outline show; `JWST-A9` *(new, M16)* at 120×40 in the fairing beat (1.5 and 2.5 s) ≥ 85 % of the sampled points inside the visible segments (within half a segment radius of their centres) are lit gold dots.

### orbital_rendezvous  (Voyages, M13, M15)
- **Intent**: "slow down to catch up": a chaser spacecraft behind a space station fires its engine *backward*, drops to a lower, faster orbit, overtakes, and climbs back to meet it, shown both over the Earth's limb and in the station's own frame, where the path is a looping cycloid.
- **Model**: Clohessy–Wiltshire equations in the target's LVLH frame, closed form (no integration); impulsive burns at scheduled times: a retrograde phasing burn, cycloid hops (each orbit the chaser returns to the station's altitude further along), a prograde burn that stops the drift at the hold point, then a scripted approach along V-bar to the docking port. Main panel, scaled per axis to the trajectory zoomed onto the V-bar (the station at 76 % of the width, the chaser starting at 8 %, and ~68 % of the height above the limb, the station's altitude at 20 % and the hops' lowest dip at 75 %): the path still to fly a dim solid braille line, the flown path a bright solid braille line fading with age over a faint glow along its last 4 s, a star where each burn fired, the two CW burns labelled with their Δv (`Δv −0.48 m/s`, `Δv +0.48 m/s`: the model's Δv for a 20 km starting gap and a 92.6-min orbit) two rows above, clear of the station, and while a burn fires a plume flash (a small bright glow at the engine end) with a thruster puff (a spreading cone of exhaust dots opposite the Δv) and a Δv arrow; an 8-cell station sprite along V-bar (docking port facing the chaser, pressurised modules, two truss joints each with solar-array wings above and below) and a 6-cell chaser (solar array, hull, nose), held off the station so it docks at the port. Inertial inset (top right, from 100×30): a lit Earth globe (Lambert-shaded toward the Sun, soft terminator, a little cloud) filling 72 % of the station's orbit radius, the orbit and both craft; stars are kept out of the inset and from behind the labels. The Earth's limb along the bottom (a band ~15 % of the height), each pixel shaded by its signed distance to the limb (antialiased below the half-block): a faint airglow, a bright atmospheric rim, a deep ocean under soft cloud streaks (stretched, warped noise, foreshortened toward the limb) streaming past.
- **Motion**: `Timeline` of acts (40 s loop): phasing burn (2 s), overtaking (19 s = two 10 s orbits of cycloid hops), raise orbit (3 s, the prograde burn), docking (16 s: V-bar approach, arrival, braking at the port).
- **Resize**: panel layout and scales rebuilt; state is closed-form in time.
- **Inks** *(M15, ADR-0018: M11's roles in flat steps; were M13's `Aurora`/`Ferroin`/`Ice` and M14's `Starlight` ramps)*: the flown path a green `Particle` braille line (200 over its last 6 s, 80 before), the path still to fly `Dim` 110, the inset's orbit ring `Dim` 120 with the station `Hot` 200 and the chaser `Particle` 230 on it; the station and chaser grey-white pixel-art sprites (`Star` modules 200–225, truss 150, hull 185–215, nose 240) with blue `Cold` 130 solar arrays; the Earth (limb band and inset globe) by its light in flat steps: black below 0.2, `Cold` 0, `Cold` 120 from 0.3, the bright limb and airglow `Wave` 160 from 0.5, white cloud `Star` 170 from 0.75; the burn flash `Sun` 190 (255 at its heart), the puff `Sun` 200 then `Hot` 110 as it ages; burn markers and arrows `Star`, the Δv labels (when shown) `Accent`. ASCII: paths by braille count, craft `-=[##>`, station `=[#+H+#]` with `#` wings.
- **Title**: `Rendezvous · brake to catch up`; captions `phasing burn`, `overtaking`, `raise orbit`, `docking`.
- **Stylisation**: burns impulsive; the final approach is scripted along V-bar (continuous thrust implied), not free CW motion; the main panel's axes are scaled independently (the radial axis exaggerated to fit the height) and distances in the inset are exaggerated; time compressed; the Δv labels assume a 20 km gap and the real orbital period, so they are true to the CW model at that scale.
- **M14 geometry** (kept at M15): a thin-limbed Earth arc (the surface dimmed to 0.45 of its level under the bright limb and airglow); the Earth's clouds soft streaks; the flown path a thin line with no glow under it; the station and the chaser as pixel-art sprites, 2× (two pixel columns and four pixel rows per table cell) from 120 columns, 1× below, the chaser docking clear of the wider station; the burns' Δv labels are not drawn. ASCII keeps the glyph sprites.
- **Acceptance**: `RDV-A1` the CW closed form satisfies the CW ODEs (finite-difference residual < 1e-6); `RDV-A2` after the retrograde phasing burn the chaser's along-track position gains on the target (moves toward it) while its altitude drops; `RDV-A3` with no burns a chaser at the same altitude stays fixed in LVLH; `RDV-A4` the approach ends within 1 % of the docking point; `RDV-A5` captions follow the burns; `RDV-A6` at 80×24 to 500×150 the path spans > 60 % of the width and 60–80 % of the height above the limb, its lowest dip stays above the limb, and the chaser never enters the station's sprite; `RDV-A7` the inset is drawn only from 100×30; `RDV-A8` the view is zoomed onto V-bar: the station sits at 74–78 % of the width, both sprites are 6–8 cells wide, and the path still to fly is a solid line (every planned sample lit); `RDV-A9` *(M14)* `dv_ms(Δv)` is 0.48 m/s, and no Δv label is drawn at 80×24, 120×40 and 200×50.

### catalytic_surface  (Reactions, M13, M16)
- **Intent**: a platinum surface seen atom by atom as carbon monoxide burns on it: CO and oxygen land, meet, and leave as CO₂ in tiny flashes; as the CO fraction in the gas is swept, the surface swings from oxygen-choked to reactive to CO-choked, with islands nucleating and flipping.
- **Model**: Ziff–Gulari–Barshad lattice model on a capped square lattice (≤ 200×100): pick a site; with probability `y` try CO adsorption (one empty site), else O₂ dissociative adsorption (two neighbouring empty sites); adjacent CO + O react to CO₂ which leaves (flash). Rejection-free batches: ≤ 12k attempts per update. `y` sweeps along a `Timeline` through the two kinetic phase transitions (`y₁ ≈ 0.39`, `y₂ ≈ 0.525`), with slow CO desorption so the CO-poisoned phase can recover (a 0.4 % desorption channel per attempt: CO always leaves, O a tenth of the time, so the O-poisoned phase also recovers). The gas is fed as a jet onto the centre: the local `y` falls by 0.14·r from the centre (r the distance over the half-diagonal), so each transition sits on a ring that moves with the sweep. The jet's `y` follows a looping 36 s Timeline *(changed, M16 round 2: was 36.5 s)*: 5 s at 0.545 (CO islands nucleate at the centre), 7 s up to 0.65 (the CO disc grows to the corners), 2.5 s held, 9 s down to 0.53 (O eats the disc back along a ring front), 5 s held (reactive), 4.5 s down to 0.48 (the O layer closes in from the corners; was 0.43 with a 0.5 s hold, which closed nearly every front and left a dead frame) and 3 s back: the O-poisoned caption is a ≈ 5 s beat with O covering ≈ 80–88 % of the surface, and somewhere on the surface the local `y` is always inside the window.
