- **Motion**: the loop opens part-way through the fall from 0.65 (at ≈ 19 s, jet `y` ≈ 0.59): orange CO and blue O domains already meet along a sparking front at t = 0, and the CO disc then recedes as it does every loop; fronts move as `y` sweeps; sparks throughout; the loop takes 36 s.
- **Resize**: lattice regridded (ADR-0012, nearest site) and the gradient re-laid; the first lattice starts split the way the opening `y` splits it (CO where the local `y` exceeds `y₂` = 0.525, ≈ 40 % of the sites, O around it, 10 % vacancies by a fixed hash), is pre-rolled 12 sweeps at that `y`, then runs a tenth of a second of frames so the front already sparks.
- **Inks** (M15, ADR-0018: the lattice is the art, in flat semantic domains like M11's `ising_model`; were `Ramp(Starlight)`/`Ramp(Sienna)`): one half-block pixel per site, nearest (1:1 up to the cap). Empty platinum is the terminal's black, O a dark `Cold` blue (level 20), CO a dim `Hot` orange (70) and its islands' edge sites (CO with a non-CO neighbour, periodic) bright `Hot` (200), so the edges, where the reaction runs, carry the light and the two species read as a complementary pair. A deterministic 4 % sample of the CO₂ events blinks its site white, `Star` 255 while its brightness is above half and 170 below, fading with τ = 0.1 s (gone in ≈ 0.3 s), exposed per update and drawn once per frame (GEN-R20). The O-covered surface is the one filled ground in this batch: it is the lattice itself, as M11's Ising domains are. No blur, glow or time smoothing. ASCII: a density ramp by brightness (`raster::ascii_ramp_cell`). Readout `CO fraction y=0.54  CO2 <count>` (or `y=0.54`).
- **Look** *(M16, GEN-R25; supersedes the M15 inks above)*: no ground and no half-block pixels: only the island boundaries are drawn, as braille threads one dot thick on black. The species field (CO +1, O −1, empty platinum 0) is box-blurred over 2 sites each way (clipped at the lattice edges, so lone sites, vacancies and the screen edge draw nothing) and contoured by marching squares (`Dots::contour`, CORE-R105): the `+0.30` level, on the CO side of every front, is a `Hot` 210 thread and the `−0.45` level, on the O side, a `Cold` 235 thread *(round 2: CO level lowered from +0.45 so small CO islands in the reactive window show warm; Cold raised from 200, which read navy beside Hot)*, so each CO/O front is a complementary pair of threads with the reacting (mostly empty) zone black between them, the CO disc a `Hot` ring inside a `Cold` one, O islands `Cold` loops. The CO₂ blinks are single `Star` dots at their site (255, then 170, gone in ≈ 0.3 s, as before). In the short O-poisoned beat the O layer still holds a few dozen holes, drawn as `Cold` loops, with the blinks between them: no frame is dead (CAT-A10). ASCII by braille dot count.
- **Title**: `Catalysis · CO burns on platinum`; captions `oxygen-poisoned`, `reactive`, `CO-poisoned`.
- **Stylisation**: a lattice-gas model (ZGB), not Pt(110) with its surface reconstruction; the desorption channel is a stylisation so both poisoned phases recover within the loop (plain ZGB poisons irreversibly); only a sample of the reactions is drawn as sparks; the PEEM images are greyscale, the colours are false.
- **Acceptance**: `CAT-A1` at `y = 0.3` the surface fills with O (coverage ≥ 0.9 after the relaxation time) and at `y = 0.6` with CO; `CAT-A2` at `y = 0.45` the CO₂ production rate stays > 0 and coverages stay mixed; `CAT-A3` adsorption never overfills a site and O₂ needs two empty neighbours; `CAT-A4` attempts per update never exceed the cap; `CAT-A5` captions follow the surface: `oxygen-poisoned` when O coverage ≥ 0.8, `CO-poisoned` when CO coverage ≥ 0.8, else `reactive`; the sweep reaches all three; `CAT-A6` over a loop the surface is poisoned (either caption) ≤ 14 s and CO₂ production never stalls (> 0.01 per site per sweep in every half second), and the gas gradient falls by `GRADE` from the centre to the corners; `CAT-A7` *(changed, M16)* nothing is drawn as half-blocks or backgrounds; a blink is one `Star` dot, 255 at full and 170 a step dimmer as it fades, gone below its threshold; at 80×24 the front blinks from the first frame and, with no new events, every blink is gone within 18 updates (0.3 s). `CAT-A8` the loop opens mid-window (jet `y` in [0.52, 0.60]) on balanced domains (CO holds 35–55 % of the occupied sites), with sparks already lit and the `reactive` caption. `CAT-A9` *(new, M16)* threads only at fronts: a lattice split into a CO half and an O half draws `Hot` dots only on the CO side within 3 sites of the front and `Cold` only on the O side, and a uniform O or CO cover with 10 % vacancies draws nothing; `CAT-A10` *(new, M16 round 2)* no dead frame: at 160×45, sampled every 0.25 s over a whole loop, at least 2 % of the field's cells are lit.

### kelvin_helmholtz  (Forces & Waves, M13)
- **Intent**: two layers of fluid sliding past each other: the interface ripples, curls into cat's-eye billows like wave clouds, neighbouring billows pair and merge, and the layer thickens into turbulence before it is reset.
- **Model**: D2Q9 TRT lattice Boltzmann (the vortex_street kernel, lifted into core as `lbm`, CORE-R98/R99) on a capped lattice (≤ 16 384 nodes, the display's square-pixel aspect clamped to [0.75, 4] and bucketed by 1.25), periodic in both axes, so the flow is the classic *double* shear layer `u = U[tanh((y − H/4)/δ) − tanh((y − 3H/4)/δ) − 1]` (no walls to shed their own vorticity; the two rows of billows spin in opposite senses); `U = 0.1`, `τ = 0.508` (Λ = ¼), `δ = max(H/28, 2.5)` (Re = Uδ/ν ≈ 95–200 with the aspect bucket; 121 at 200×50); the node budget is also capped at the display's half-block pixel count (never below 1 200 nodes), so small displays simulate a smaller block with fewer, larger billows instead of a minified one. Each interface is seeded with a small transverse velocity (Gaussian envelope of width 2δ) at the wavenumber nearest `λ ≈ 12δ` with an even count `n` across the width (the fastest-growing mode once the layer has thickened at this resolution) and its subharmonic `n/2` (pairing), phases from `ctx.rng`. A passive dye marks the middle band, advected semi-Lagrangian (clamped Catmull–Rom) with a weak interface sharpening (stylisation). A view whose pixels span more than 1.3 nodes box-filters the picture before sampling. 4 LBM steps per update; 1 s pre-roll in the first resize of a lattice; the act re-seeds after 36 s (before the under-resolved late turbulence can go unstable), or when the watchdog (1 s cadence) finds ρ outside [0.8, 1.2] or |u| ≥ 0.3, or when the row-mean flow keeps less than a quarter of the laid-down shear (the merged vortices have reorganised the block: turbulence, then reset).
- **Motion**: ripples at once, billows by ~3 s, pairing by ~9 s into half as many large cat's-eye vortices, which then turn slowly until the reset.
- **Resize**: within a bucket nothing changes; a new bucket gets its own lattice and a fresh act (the seeded modes depend on the lattice, so resampling the old flow would seed the wrong ones). The first act's 60-update pre-roll runs 15 updates in `resize` and one extra per `update` after it until paid (GEN-R19: ≈ 9 ms instead of ≈ 34 ms at 500×150).
- **Inks** (M15, ADR-0018: M11's complementary pair in flat steps; was a smooth `Ramp(Starlight)` line): lines only, on black. The interfaces, where the passive dye crosses ½, are thin flat lines, the pixels whose distance `d` to the `c = ½` contour (estimated per pixel as `|c − ½| / |∇c|` in pixels) is under 0.9: `Hot` where the sheet's vorticity is positive and `Cold` where it is negative (the two shear layers spin opposite ways, so the upper billows read warm and the lower cool), at 130 and, where |ω| reaches 0.45 of the saturation value, 230. Everything else is the terminal's black (the dye bands are not drawn). Dye, signed spin and slope are sampled bilinearly at any magnification (the line is drawn by its distance, not by the sampling, so a cubic would only double the per-pixel cost at 500×150). A minified display box-filters dye and spin first. ASCII: `#` spinning, `-` calm. Readout `Re 121` right-aligned on the title row when it fits.
- **Title**: `Shear billows · layers roll up`.
- **Stylisation**: 2-D, low Reynolds number, periodic domain; perturbation seeded for a clean first roll-up.
- **Acceptance**: `KH-A1` the lifted `lbm` reproduces the vortex_street results (its lattice after 2 s at 200×50 is bit-identical to the pre-lift kernel's, and its snapshots are unchanged), and the scene steps exactly the core lattice; `KH-A2` the interface perturbation's amplitude grows exponentially early (log-linear fit R² > 0.9); `KH-A3` the number of vortices (vorticity extrema along the interface) decreases after pairing; `KH-A4` total mass is conserved to 1e-6 relative over 10 s (f32 rounding, CORE-R99); `KH-A5` worst frame < 2 ms at 200×50 on the stepping frame (measured by `rs-worst`; a test pins the node cap × steps per update that bounds it); `KH-A6` *(new, M15)* the two rows of billows spin in opposite senses and the line shows it flat: `Hot` for positive spin, `Cold` for negative, the brighter step from 0.45, the same ink across its width, nothing from 0.9 px.

### lichtenberg_discharge  (States of Matter, M13)
- **Intent**: a block of acrylic charged by an electron beam, discharged by a tap: a brilliant branching tree burns inward from the edge, fractal and fern-like; across acts the branching thins from dense coral to sparse lightning, and a final act shows the same Laplacian growth as viscous fingering in a Hele-Shaw cell.
- **Model**: `laplacian_growth` (shared `pub(crate)`): dielectric breakdown model on a capped lattice (≤ 160×80): potential φ solved by warm-started SOR (a few sweeps per update, residual-capped), the tree at φ = 0, the boundary at φ = 1; a perimeter site joins with probability `∝ |∇φ|^η` (η = 0.75 dense coral, 1.5 fern, 3 lightning-like). Tree nodes are a fixed-capacity pool (parent links) with a reset beat when the tree reaches the target or capacity; growth age ink. Viscous-fingering act: same model with η = 1 and an injection point (low-viscosity fluid pushing high). Growth is paced: sites are added two per perimeter snapshot, at most 24 per update, while the tree's depth (lightning: its reach) trails an eased 8.5 s schedule. Dense and fern are tapped at the bottom edge, with the top edge charged and insulating sides; they complete when the depth reaches 0.85 of the block. Lightning comes from the top toward a grounded bottom edge and completes on touch. Fingering injects at the centre with all edges at pressure and completes at 0.9 of the half-height. The scene opens 2 s into the first act with its tree pre-grown in `resize`; a lattice change pre-grows the fresh tree to the act's schedule. SOR is red–black, at most 10 sweeps per update, stopping once the largest update is below 2·10⁻⁴.
- **Motion**: up to 24 new sites per update. Each act is 13 s: growth, a near-white completion flash that cools through violet, a glow, a 0.8 s fade, and the next act's tree starts at once.
- **Resize**: lattice regridded; tree reset.
- **Inks**: one half-block layer: each link is stroked as an antialiased channel whose radius (`0.35 + 1.25·b²` pixels, +0.6 in the fingering act) and level (`b(0.25 + 0.75·b)`) follow b = 0.2 + 0.8·(log current share, the current being the subtree size), wrapped in a halo falling to zero over 1.4 pixels; strokes combine by maximum, not sum, so a dense coral stays a tree of distinct channels: a near-white trunk thinning through lavender to hair-fine indigo tips (`Ramp(Plasma)`; fingering `Ramp(Cobalt)`). New tips flash bright for 0.5 s but keep their width. Acrylic is black. ASCII: the tree by braille dot count (`. : + #`).
- **Title**: `Lichtenberg figure · breakdown`; captions `dense`, `fern`, `lightning`, `fingering`.
- **Stylisation**: DBM on a square lattice (weak lattice anisotropy); the acrylic figure is really a 3-D tree seen in projection; time slowed by ~10⁹ (a nanosecond discharge shown over seconds).
- **M15 look** (ADR-0018; supersedes the Inks above and M14's muted ramp lines): braille 1-dot channel lines in the act's semantic role (`Wave` for the three discharges, `Cold` for the fingering air), in three flat steps by the log current `b` (0.2 + 0.8·log share): twigs 110, limbs (`b` ≥ 0.5) 180, and the links carrying ≥ 0.8 of it (≥ 0.6 fingering), doubled to 2 dots, 255; a fresh tip or the strike flash (heat ≥ ½) is `Star` 255. The act's fade drops each link a step, then out. No halo. The lines are rasterised once per drawn frame (GEN-R20).
- **Acceptance**: `LICH-A1` SOR converges: residual falls below 10⁻³ within 60 sweeps from a warm start after growth; `LICH-A2` the tree stays connected (every node's parent is in the tree); `LICH-A3` the fractal dimension estimate (box counting at the end of growth), summed over 3 seeds, is larger at η = 1 than at η = 3 by > 0.15; `LICH-A4` the pool never exceeds capacity and resets at the beat; `LICH-A5` captions follow η (dense 0.75, fern 1.5, lightning 3, fingering 1). `LICH-A6` *(changed, M15)* twig, limb and trunk links take the act role's three flat steps, a hot tip or the flash is `Star` 255, and the fade steps down, then out; after the first frames at 120×40 every lit cell is the act's role or a hot tip, ≥ 70 % of them below the trunk step, and every background is blank.

### quantum_double_slit  (Forces & Waves, M13, M16)
- **Intent**: electrons from a steady source meet a wall with two slits: the wave spreads through both into a fan of bright lobes and dark nodal rays, and on the screen behind, single dots appear one at a time until the fringes emerge from the clicks. A last act aims the same beam at a thin barrier that it partly tunnels through.
- **Model**: 2-D time-dependent Schrödinger equation on a capped grid (≤ 11 000 nodes, view aspect 1–3) with ADI Crank–Nicolson (unitary), a complex tridiagonal (Thomas) solver with caller-owned scratch (CORE-R99; the line matrices of both potentials factored once per grid; M14: the sweeps solve 8 lines at a time interleaved, bit-identical to one at a time), absorbing complex potential at the edges, 2 ADI steps per update. A soft line source near the left absorber adds `S·g(y)·e^{−iφn}` after every step (`g` Gaussian, σ = 0.15 of the view height, lighting the two slits; `φ` the step's eigenphase for the carrier `k`), so a monochromatic beam drives a steady diffraction pattern; a new grid starts from the analytic incident beam and runs 2× fast for 2 s. Clicks arrive at a rate proportional to the probability current reaching the screen column (as a share of the beam's current) at positions sampled from that current (`j = 2 Im(ψ*ₓψₓ₊₁)`, clamped ≥ 0) with `ctx.rng` via `InverseCdf`; clicks accumulate (pool 4000) and a per-row click histogram glows behind them.
- **Motion**: the fan is up within ~1 s of any start or resize and stays; acts of 20 s: `interference` (full exposure, ~30 clicks/s), `one at a time` (wave held at 92 %, so the fan never reads as fading; ~10 clicks/s, the histogram glow builds the bands), `tunnelling` (the beam on a V₀ = 1.6, 2-node barrier: reflected standing wave left, weak transmitted wave right at the same exposure).
- **Resize**: grid regridded and the beam restarted from the analytic incident beam; click histogram kept in screen fractions.
- **Inks**: the wave as a soft single-hue glow, `Ramp(Aurora)` (a phosphor green): brightness `|ψ|^0.65` against the per-side peak (the source side at 55 %), a soft knee instead of a hard cut, so faint density fades smoothly to black; where the view resolves the carrier a 25 % shimmer of `Re ψ/|ψ|` rides on it, so wavefronts stream through the steady fan of lobes and dark nodal rays. Walls `Ramp(Starlight)` 111, barrier 71, screen 55; the detector strip (≈ 1/6 of the width) glows `Ramp(Starlight)` 18–170 behind the clicks by where they landed (faded in over the first 500 clicks), and the clicks are warm-white dots, 255 settling to 185. ASCII: the glow's level on the ASCII ramp, walls `|`, the strip's glow `.`/`:`.
- **M15 look** (ADR-0018; supersedes the Inks above and M14's muted `Aurora` glow): the wave is `Wave` (cyan) in three flat steps of its brightness `L = 140·vis·t·knee²·shimmer`: 90 from 30, 165 from 62, 255 from 98 (the crests), black below 30, with a 50 % carrier shimmer so the crests stream through the fan as brighter steps (as M11's `wave_interference` shows its crests). The slit wall and the barrier are drawn only at their front face, a 1-px `Structure` line (150 and 100); the screen a dashed 1-px `Dim` line (110). The detector strip is black: the fringes build from the click dots' density alone, `Star` 255 for their first 0.6 s, then 185.
- **Look** *(M16, GEN-R25; supersedes the M15 look and the Inks above)*: braille threads on black, no half-blocks. The wave is drawn as its crest lines: the wavefronts where the carrier peaks, the level `Im ψ = 0` in cells whose corners' `Re ψ` sum is positive, traced over the view's nodes by marching squares (`Dots::contour_by`, CORE-R105). Each crest piece is gated and stepped by its cell's exposed `|ψ|` (the mean of its corners, with M15's per-side peaks, tone and knee, no shimmer) in three flat `Wave` steps, 175 from 25, 230 from 45, 255 from 70 *(round 2: were 140/205/255 from 25/50/75, which left the fan faint; M15's fills were 90/165/255 from 30/62/98)*, so the lobes are bright arcs streaming out of the slits and the dark nodal rays are gaps between them; on the source side the standing wave shows as short crest bars. A view with fewer than 0.8 dots per node (crests ≈ 7 nodes apart would crowd) draws `|ψ|` iso-lines at the three steps instead. The slit wall (or barrier) is a one-dot `Structure` line at its front face (250; barrier 210), the screen a dashed one-dot `Dim` line (150) *(round 2: were 190, 140 and 110)*; clicks unchanged (`Star` 255, then 185). ASCII falls back by braille dot count.
- **Title**: `Double slit · waves of chance`; captions `interference`, `one at a time`, `tunnelling`.
- **Stylisation**: units chosen so fringes are visible; a steady monochromatic beam stands for many electrons, and single clicks are sampled from the arriving current (Born rule): the wave itself is not observable; the density is shown as a glow rather than `arg ψ` (Phase inks carry no brightness).
- **Acceptance**: `QDS-A1` the source-free ADI step preserves the norm to 1e-10 without absorbing boundaries; `QDS-A2` the fringe spacing at the screen (between the dark fringes either side of the centre) matches `λL/d` within 10 %, with the lattice's paraxial wavelength `λ = 2π/sin k`; `QDS-A3` with one slit closed the fringes vanish (visibility < 0.2 over one fringe period on the open slit's axis); `QDS-A4` the click histogram converges to the arriving current j⁺ at the screen (the sampled profile; χ² test on 4000 clicks); `QDS-A5` the share of the beam's current transmitted through the barrier falls with its width; `QDS-A6` the source settles to the analytic beam (within 10 %, steady over a carrier period), and *(changed, M16)* the fan's crests light ≥ 60 % of the cell rows next to the screen within 1.5 s of a start at 80×24, 120×40 and 200×50; `QDS-A7` every act shows the wave at ≥ 90 % of full exposure; `QDS-A8` *(changed, M16)* at 120×40 after 6 s every wave cell is `Wave` braille at one of its three steps (the upper two both showing), every other lit cell a wall, barrier, screen or click ink, and no cell is a half-block or has a background; `QDS-A9` *(new, M16)* the crest lines lie on the carrier's crests: for a plane wave `e^{ikx}` at full exposure ≥ 97 % of the wave dots away from the wall lie within 0.6 node of `kx ≡ 0 (mod 2π)`; `QDS-A10` *(new, M16 round 2)* the fan reads bright: at 120×40 after 6 s at least half of the wave's dots are in cells of level ≥ 230.

### radical_chain  (Reactions, M13, M16)
- **Intent**: hydrogen and oxygen in a vessel: at low pressure a few radicals wander and die on the walls, and nothing happens; step over the explosion limit and every radical makes two, the population doubles again and again, and the vessel flashes pale blue, leaving water behind.
- **Model**: radicals (pool 4000) random-walk in a vessel (a rounded rectangle); per step a radical branches (H + O₂ → OH + O, O + H₂ → OH + H: one becomes two) with probability `k_b(T)·p·g` (Arrhenius; ∝ p, a collision with O₂; `g` the local unburnt gas), terminates at the walls (on contact) or by three-body recombination with probability `k_t·p²` (H + O₂ + M: ∝ [O₂][M]); heat released per event deposits into a coarse temperature grid (≤ 100×50) that relaxes toward the wall temperature, feeding back into `k_b` (thermal runaway). Water product accumulates as a density field. Pressure `p` sweeps along a `Timeline` across the first explosion limit (at p ≈ 1 in these units); the second limit, where three-body recombination (∝ p²) overtakes branching (∝ p), lies at p = K_B0/K_T3 = 12, far above the sweep. The diffusion coefficient falls as 1/p, calibrated so the wall loss of the slowest mode equals the branching rate at p = 1 (the first limit). A radical–radical recombination term K_RR·n/capacity quenches the flash, and fuel is consumed per branching, globally and in a coarse unburnt-gas grid (4.5 branchings spend a cell; burnt gas adds 6/s of recombination), so the explosion runs as a front (a Fisher–KPP pulse) from the spark and burns out behind it. Initiation runs only in the slow phase and once the vent is below the limit (fresh gas flows back in at 1.5/s). The loop is 14.5 s: slow 2.5 s at p = 0.6 (25 seed radicals), a rise over 1.5 s to p = 2.2, burn 5 s (*M16*: was 6.5 s, which left the vessel empty once the drawn afterglow was gone), vent 5.5 s (p falls below the limit within its first third); when the burn begins (p = 2.2) an ignition spark (a Gaussian hot spot, +1.6 in T, with 30 radicals, replacing the stray ones, near the centre) starts the flame there.
- **Motion**: slow chains with fading trails (radicals wander, fork and die at the walls) for the first seconds; a flame kernel at the spark within ≈ 1 s of the burn, a front of forking threads sweeping the vessel by ≈ 4 s; the flame dies out behind it, the vessel rests dark for ≈ 1–2 s while it is vented, and fresh slow chains start (*M16*: the drawn afterglow is gone).
- **Resize**: vessel geometry in vessel units; grids regridded; pools kept.
- **Inks** (M15, ADR-0018: M11's roles in flat steps; were `Ramp(Plasma)` radicals and trails over a smooth `Ramp(Ember)` flash): a black vessel, its wall a dim dotted outline (every third wall dot, `Dim` 90). While fewer than 300 radicals are alive, each is a small plus of braille dots (a disc of radius 1 dot) in `Wave` 255 (pale blue, the subject) over its chain trail (a `Glow` stroke from its last position, kept ×0.72 per update and toned) drawn flat in `Cold` 110 where the toned trail reaches 70. The flash is the gas's heat: `2·(T − 1) + 0.9·afterglow` per heat node (the afterglow decays with τ = 1.8 s), softened by one [1 2 1] pass, Catmull-Rom sampled (CORE-R102) and toned; from toned level 48 (single reactions' heat stays black) it is a `Seq` field in four equal flat steps, levels 70, 130, 185, 235 (violet edge, red, orange, a yellow core), like M11's reaction fields, and inside the flame it wins over the trails. The water mist and the ambient glow are gone. `explosion` latches once the pool passes an eighth of its capacity or T exceeds the spark by 1.5. ASCII by level. The trails are exposed per update and the picture rasterised once per drawn frame (GEN-R20).
- **Look** *(M16, GEN-R25; supersedes the M15 Inks above)*: a branching chain tree on black, all braille dots. Each radical is a `Sun` 255 dot at the head of its thread: its last 6 drawn positions, sampled every 0.06 s (≈ 0.36 s of path), where the drawn position is its position eased with τ = 0.25 s (*round 2*: were 8 and 0.12 s) (a raw random walk is a scribble; smoothed, a thread), coloured by age: `Hot` 230 for the two newest segments, `Hot` 150 for the next three, `Accent` 90 for the tail. A branching child is born on its parent with the parent's trail and a new identity, so every branching forks a thread and the explosion grows as a tree from the spark. While more than 70 radicals live (*round 2*: was 240), only a fixed hashed share of about 70 is drawn (by identity, so a drawn thread stays drawn), so the flame stays a tree of threads, not a wash. A radical lost at the wall leaves a brief `Cold` 200 dot (radius 0.8 dots, 0.4 s) at its last point inside. The wall stays a faint dotted `Dim` 90 outline. The flash fill, the chain-trail glow and the afterglow are gone (temperature, fuel and water remain model state). No half blocks; ASCII by dot count. *Round 2 (M16)*: the vessel is never dark. Initiation (14 radicals/s at full fuel, was 6) runs through the slow phase and the rise; a fresh fill seeds 60 radicals (was 25); at the spark the stray chains are quenched (they no longer branch and die out as before) instead of removed, so the flame still grows from the spark alone; and once the flame has burnt out (a pool that peaked above 200 is below 20) the vessel is vented and refilled at once: the loop jumps to its next cycle (fresh gas at the wall temperature, 60 seed radicals), so the caption is `slow reaction` again with no dark stretch.
- **Title**: `Explosion · radicals multiply`; captions `slow reaction`, `chain branching`, `explosion` (they follow the state: `slow reaction` while p is below the limit; above it `chain branching` until the explosion latches, once the pool passes an eighth of its capacity or T exceeds the spark by 1.5, then `explosion` until the vent takes p back below the limit).
- **Stylisation**: a two-step branching scheme stands for the full H₂–O₂ mechanism; the radical count saturates at the pool (the flash takes over); time scales stretched from milliseconds.
- **Acceptance**: `CHN-A1` with branching off, the radical count never increases; `CHN-A2` when branching exceeds termination the count grows exponentially (log-linear fit, positive rate) and when it is below it decays; `CHN-A3` a radical that reaches the wall is removed; `CHN-A4` the pool never exceeds capacity and branching stops (without panic) when it is full; `CHN-A5` *(changed, M16)* the temperature grid stays finite and is back at the wall value (< 1.1) 1 s after the burnt-out vessel is vented; `CHN-A6` the flame runs as a front: when `explosion` latches, < 25 % of the vessel is hot and < 10 % burnt, and later > 30 % is hot at once (*changed, M16*: the afterglow clause is dropped with the drawn afterglow); `CHN-A7` *(changed, M16)* at 120×40 at 1.5, 4.5, 6 and 12.5 s the ground is black and every lit cell (the readout aside) is a braille cell in one of the thread inks (`Hot` 230/150, `Accent` 90), a radical (`Sun`), a wall death (`Cold`) or the wall (`Dim`), with threads present; thread colour falls with age; and burnt gas quenches branching. `CHN-A8` three-body recombination scales as p² and branching as p, so their ratio doubles when the pressure doubles. `CHN-A9` over two loops the caption is `slow reaction` exactly when p < 1, `explosion` exactly when p ≥ 1 and the explosion has latched, and `chain branching` otherwise; the spark strikes at the start of the burn and the explosion first latches during the burn (*changed, M16*: burnt-out loops are cut short, so two loop lengths hold ≥ 2 flashes, each latching in its burn); `CHN-A10` *(new, M16)* a branching child carries its parent's trail under a different identity (a forked thread); wall deaths are recorded at the radical's last point inside the vessel, show in the first second of the slow phase and vanish within 0.4 s once no radical dies; with a crowded pool (4000) fewer than 1.2 × 70 threads are drawn, and with 70 all are (*changed, M16 round 2*); `CHN-A11` *(new, M16)* at 120×40 over 32 s, sampled every 0.25 s, ≥ 1.5 % of the field cells are lit by threads, radicals or deaths (the wall aside), the caption is never `explosion` with no radicals, and an explosion happens; `CHN-A12` *(new, M16)* from 5 to 7.5 s fewer than 10 % of the lit dots have all four neighbours lit (a tree of threads, not a mesh).

### spinodal_decomposition  (States of Matter, M13)
- **Intent**: a well-mixed grey liquid suddenly unmixes like oil and water: fine worm-like domains appear everywhere, then coarsen, the small ones swallowed by the large ones; a second act at an off-critical mix breaks into droplets that ripen.
- **Model**: `cahn_hilliard` (shared `pub(crate)`): Oono–Puri cell-dynamics system on a capped periodic lattice (≤ 16 000 nodes): `Γ = A tanh ψ − ψ + D(⟨⟨ψ⟩⟩ − ψ)`, `ψ ← ψ − M(⟨⟨Γ⟩⟩ − Γ)`, with `⟨⟨X⟩⟩ − X = 0.3·Δ₉X` (a Δ₉-based variant of the Oono–Puri average) from the isotropic 9-point Laplacian (`grid::laplacian9`, wrap), `A = 1.3`, `D = 0.5`, `M = 1`; tanh by the `soft_clip` Padé approximant. This conserves ψ. The initial ψ is the mean plus ±0.1 noise smoothed by one periodic 3 × 3 box pass (mean kept). Acts reset: critical quench (mean 0) → off-critical (mean 0.35) droplets.
- **Motion**: domains form within the first second (the step rate starts at twice the act's opening rate and eases back over 2 s, so the dim quench mush is brief); length scale grows as t^{1/3}; the step rate rises from 120 to 360 steps/s through each act (at most 6 per update) so late coarsening stays visible; each act 30 s.
- **Resize**: lattice regridded (ADR-0012), conserving the mean.
- **M15 look** (ADR-0018; supersedes the alloy inks below and M14's anti-aliased `Starlight` line): lines only, on black, in M11's complementary pair. Both phases are black; each interface is a flat 2-px line, the pixels within 1.3 px of ψ = 0 (`|ψ|/|∇ψ|` × pixels per node, the slope from central differences, periodic), `Hot` on its ψ > 0 side and `Cold` on its ψ < 0 side, so the contours still tell the phases apart; ψ sampled bilinearly up to 2× magnification and Catmull-Rom above (CORE-R102). The line is 110 while the pattern forms and 220 once formed (the pattern's formedness, the mean `|ψ|/ψ*` mapped from 0.3 to 0.8 onto [0, 1], at 0.6), and not drawn below 0.3. Before that the quench's composition noise shows as braille speckle: one dot, in the lowest step of its side's colour (`Hot` 110 for `ψ − ψ̄ > 0`, `Cold` 110 below), on every blank pixel whose `|ψ − ψ̄|` exceeds 0.3 of the frame's largest, so the unmixing grows out of a visible, mostly black speckle. ASCII: the line `#` (formed) or `+`, the phases blank.
- **Inks**: a two-phase alloy of named ramps, shaded per lattice node: one phase deep teal `Ramp(Aurora)` (its lower 62 %, apart from kelvin_helmholtz's Eosin/Cobalt), the other cream `Ramp(Starlight)` (lifted toward its pale end); on formed domains the magnitude follows the sub-node distance to the interface (`|ψ|/|∇ψ|`, smoothstep from 0.1 to 1.7 nodes), so boundaries are even antialiased dark seams at any angle rather than stair-stepped ψ thresholds (a young quench is dimmed by its small amplitude as before); a domain's interior sits deeper (0.46) and its rim glows lighter (up to +0.34, depth from the blurred |ψ|); a sheen from a fixed upper-left light on that depth makes domains read as rounded liquid. Each act emerges from the terminal's black: pixels stay empty until the domains form. ASCII: `#` `+` for one phase, `:` `.` for the other, blank on interfaces.
- **Title**: `Unmixing · domains coarsen`; captions `spinodal`, `droplets ripen`.
- **Stylisation**: cell-dynamics (Oono–Puri) rather than the full Cahn–Hilliard PDE; lattice units.
- **Acceptance**: `SPIN-A1` the mean of ψ is conserved to f32 rounding: 1e-8 per step and 1e-6 over 300 steps; `SPIN-A2` the characteristic length (first zero of the correlation function) grows, with a log-log slope in [0.2, 0.45] over the act; `SPIN-A3` in the off-critical act the minority phase forms ≥ 10 separate droplets whose count then falls; `SPIN-A4` ψ stays within [−1.2, 1.2]; `SPIN-A5` regridding keeps the mean; `SPIN-A6` *(changed, M15)* on a straight interface at 30° the line has no gap (every pixel within 0.3 px of it is drawn), nothing beyond 1.5 px, `Hot` on the ψ > 0 side and `Cold` on the other (more than 5 pixels each); deep in either phase (ψ = ±ψ*) the pixel is blank; and the domains are formed 1 s after a quench at 120×40 (some |ψ| > ψ*/2); `SPIN-A7` *(new, M15)* the first frame of either act at 120×40 shows the quench as speckle: over 100 cells each of `Hot` 110 and `Cold` 110 and nothing else, under half the art cells lit.

### chemical_garden  (States of Matter, M13, M18)
- **Intent**: a seed crystal of a metal salt dropped in water glass: a membrane forms, swells with water by osmosis, bursts, and a jet of salt solution rises and sheaths itself in new membrane: blue tubes grow upward in jerks into a garden; a final act shows a hydrothermal vent chimney, where such mineral membranes are *hypothesised* to have housed early life.
- **Model**: tubes as a fixed-capacity segment pool (≤ 600) with tips; each tip has an internal osmotic pressure that rises at a rate ∝ concentration difference (C: precipitation) and bursts when it exceeds a membrane strength (seeded ±30 %); a burst emits a buoyant jet (P: buoyancy) that extends the tip upward by a random-walk-biased step and precipitates a new membrane segment; occasional branching; precipitate particles (pool 800) flake and sink. Vent act: a warm plume rising from a chimney with mineral walls growing (same model, different parameters) and a caption naming the hypothesis.
- **Motion**: bursts every 0.3–2 s per tip; garden grows 30 s; vent act 20 s; reset.
- **Resize**: tank geometry in tank units; pools kept.
- **Inks** (M15, ADR-0018: M11's roles in flat steps; were muted named ramps anti-aliased by coverage): a black tank. Each salt keeps its colour in a semantic role: copper blue `Cold`, cobalt pink `Accent`, iron rust `Hot`, nickel green `Particle`. Tubes are flat capsule strokes of radius 0.5–1 px (the chimney wall's five columns 0.75 px, so the stack reads as mineral strands, not a block): a pixel is set when its centre lies within the radius + ¼ px. Levels are flat steps: an old tube 120, a fresh one (first 4 s) 200, the growing tips and seed crystals 255 (a tip swells from a point to a 1-px disc once its pressure passes 70 % of the membrane strength); a garden fading out drops to 120, then 60 for its last half. The floor is one `Dim` 60 pixel row; jets and flakes single pixels in their salt at 90; vent smoke single `Dim` 110 pixels (parcels with life ≥ 0.4), `Hot` 150 at the orifice. Gas bubbles are simulated but not drawn. ASCII: tubes `|` (above 90) or `,`, floor `_`, smoke `.`.
- **Title**: `Chemical garden · osmosis bursts`; captions `osmotic bursts`, `life's vents?` (a question: the vent link is a hypothesis).
- **Stylisation**: phenomenological burst model (no fluid solver); the origin-of-life link is a hypothesis (stated in the caption and here).
- **Acceptance**: `GRD-A7` *(changed, M15)* thin stepped tubes on a black tank: at 80×24 and 200×50, seed 7, at t = 5, 20 and 40 s, every lit pixel is at one of the scene's eight step levels, lit pixels cover 0.2–20 % of the tank, and no cell shows an `o` glyph; `GRD-A1` a tip's pressure rises monotonically between bursts and drops after one; `GRD-A2` tube tips only move upward on average (mean dy < 0 upward over 30 s); `GRD-A3` the segment pool never exceeds capacity and the scene resets when full; `GRD-A4` the vent act's caption is `life's vents?` (a question, never a claim); `GRD-A5` precipitate particles fall; `GRD-A6` a renewed garden fades out over 5 s while the new one grows, and the tank never shows an empty frame (act changes included).
- **As built (batch 5)**: pools 600 segments, 24 tips, 800 particles (jets, flakes, vent fluid); bursts every 0.35–1.7 s per tip (pressure rate 0.6–2.8, strength 1 ± 30 %), branch chance 14 %, 5–6 seed crystals. The vent act starts with a standing chimney (a tapered stack of mineral wall) whose crown keeps growing and venting warm fluid. Precipitate flakes are dim cobalt.
- **Round 2**: tube bursts step 0.028 tank units, 1–2 tips per seed, branch chance 10 %. The vent act's chimney is taller and wider (24 layers × 5 columns, crown at 55 % of the tank); vent fluid (40 parcels per tip per second) rises faster, spreads and billows, and is drawn as a glow: `Ramp(Ember)` at the orifice, grey `Ramp(Starlight)` ≤ 137 smoke above.
- **Round 3**: category States of Matter (precipitation and osmosis; was Life from Matter). A tank: dark `Ramp(Cobalt)` water lit softly from the upper left, a rippled `Ramp(Starlight)` sandy bed. Each seed is one metal salt and its tubes keep its colour: copper blue-green `Ice`, cobalt violet `Plasma`, iron rust `Ember`, nickel green `Aurora`; tubes are lit from the left with mineral grain; growing tips glow with a halo that brightens as the pressure builds; bursts release rising gas bubbles. A garden is renewed when it holds 380 segments or its tips have stopped, and at every act change: the old garden fades out over 5 s while the new seeds sprout, so the tank is never empty (GRD-A6). The vent chimney is rough rust rock (iron sulfides), its crown ember-hot.
- **Look** *(M18, GEN-R26, precipitate bead chains; supersedes the capsule tubes, Inks and GRD-A7 above)*: a black tank; the floor a 1-px `Dim` thread. Each tube is drawn as a growing molecule: every burst precipitates one bead, a small Gaussian stipple atom (`Dots::stipple_puff`, CORE-R108) at the new segment's end, joined to its parent bead by a 1-px bond thread, so a garden is a forest of branched bead chains. Each salt keeps its colour (copper `Cold`, cobalt `Accent`, iron `Hot`, nickel `Particle`); beads keep M15's steps: old 120, fresh (first 4 s) 200, the growing tip 255, a fading garden 120 → off; sticks are a fixed, legible step under their atoms: 200 under a tip or fresh bead, 110 under an old one, 60 in a fading garden. A tip bead swells with its osmotic pressure (σ from 0.7 dots at rest to 1.6 dots at the burst threshold) and, on bursting, leaves a short jet thread (afterglow 0.3 s) to the new tip; a branch is a bead with two children. Seed crystals are slightly larger atoms (σ 1.6 dots) on the floor. Flakes are single falling dots (`Accent` 90). Vent act: the chimney is a tapered column of stacked beads (mineral wall), its crown venting warm fluid as rising `Hot` → `Dim` dots with short afterglow threads. *As built (M18)*: an atom is drawn wherever the path since the last atom reaches 7 dots, and at every tip, branch point and tube end (the sticks between atoms follow the tube's path and stop 2 dots short of each atom). Tube atoms are σ 1.0 with peak 2.5 (a solid round core); tips peak 2.0; chimney wall atoms σ 0.9, peak 2.5. A fading garden's beads step 120 → 60, then are removed. Twin tubes root ±0.015 apart on their seed, and each tube's first stick grows out of the seed crystal (σ 1.6, 255). The jet overshoots the new tip by 0.9 × the burst step: 200 for 0.15 s, then 90 until 0.3 s; droplets and bubbles are simulated but not drawn. The vent chimney is two bead-column walls on one footing with two crown tips on each wall top; the crown branches at 3 %. A fixed 15 % of vent parcels are drawn while life ≥ 0.55, each a dot with a 0.06 s afterglow thread, `Hot` 150 while life > 0.75, then `Dim` 110.
- **Acceptance** *(M18)*: `GRD-A8` frames are braille-only (≤ 3 % half-block cells, as GEN-R26) at 80×24 and 200×50, seed 7, t = 5, 20 and 40 s, and lit dots cover 0.2–20 % of the tank; `GRD-A9` every bead but a seed has exactly one parent bond, and a branching bead has two children; `GRD-A10` between bursts a tip bead's σ rises monotonically with its pressure and drops back after the burst; `GRD-A11` bead inks are the salt's role at one of the step levels (old, fresh, tip, fading), and sticks are never brighter than their bead and strictly dimmer under the tip and old beads (200 / 200 / 110 / 60).

### saturn_rings  (Sun & Planets, M13, M15, M16)
- **Intent** *(M15, ADR-0018: M11's flat steps, on black)*: Saturn at 20° tilt, the rings spanning about three quarters of the frame (was all of it) on black: the dark Cassini Division, the Encke and Keeler gaps, Pan in the Encke gap trailing a wake and edge waves, Mimas orbiting beyond the rings, ghostly spokes and self-gravity clumps drifting across the rings, and the planet's shadow falling on the rings.
- **Model**: optical-depth profile `τ(r)` (C, B, A rings, Cassini Division, Encke and Keeler gaps, Huygens ringlet) as a static LUT built in `build()` from published radii. Per pixel, the inverse projection gives ring-plane `(r, λ)`; brightness `1 − e^{−τ/μ}` (μ = sin tilt), averaged over the pixel's radial footprint and dimmed where the planet's shadow falls (shadow averaged over 4 sub-pixel points). Keplerian speed `Ω(r) ∝ r^{-3/2}`: clumps (value noise in `(r, λ − Ω(r)·age)`, coarse in radius) move at the local Keplerian rate and, like real self-gravity wakes, dissolve and re-form over 10 s (two staggered generations cross-faded by `sin²`), so shear never winds them into radial grain. Pan (r = 2.2135) raises edge waves `∝ e^{−lag}·sin(k·lag)` on the Encke gap and a wake of brightness ripples of azimuthal wavelength `3π|Δr|` beside it, ahead on the faster inner side and behind on the slower outer one, decaying downstream and away from the gap. Mimas (r = 3.078) orbits at its Keplerian rate outside the rings; its 2:1 inner Lindblad resonance, `r_M·2^{−2/3} ≈ 1.94`, holds the B ring's outer edge and so the Cassini Division's inner boundary. Spokes (pool 10) corotate with the planet's magnetic field (`Ω_Saturn`), so they drift against the Keplerian clumps. The planet: an oblate disc (flattening 0.1) with soft zonal bands (bright equatorial zone, equatorial belts near ±19°, temperate belts near ±39°, a duskier polar hood), limb darkening `0.64 + 0.36√μ`, a smooth terminator and a 16-sample coverage limb that drops to space within one pixel (coverage < 0.4 stays space; above it brightness scales only by `0.8 + 0.2·cover`), occluding the far ring side; the rings' shadow bands the southern globe.
- **Motion**: clumps drift and re-form at Keplerian speeds (the inner rings overtake the outer); Pan's wake and edge waves travel with it; Mimas crosses the view (period ≈ 41 s); spokes form, drift, fade (6–10 s). Frames at t = 1 and t = 30 differ clearly.
- **Resize**: projection and buffers rebuilt; the τ LUT is size-independent.
- **Inks** *(M15, ADR-0018: M11's roles in flat steps; were M14's `Starlight` and `Sienna` ramps)*: ring brightness (with a soft shoulder `0.05 + 0.84·b/(1 + 0.12b)`, so the opaque B ring keeps its clumps) in four steps, the bright B ring `Star` 170 from 0.72, grey `Structure` 170 from 0.5, 70 from 0.3, 0 from 0.14, black below (the Cassini Division stays black); the globe's lit brightness `v` in four steps, pale butterscotch `Sun` 190 from 0.78 (zones), `Sun` 110 from 0.68, ochre `Sun` 40 from 0.5, dark rust `Hot` 10 from 0.2 (the terminator and the rings' shadow), black below; belts `0.2·bands` and streaks `0.05·noise` in the tone, limb darkening `0.64 + 0.36√μ`; spokes darken the rings (up to 50 %, soft-edged wedges, one every ~1.5 s, two already on the ansa at the start) and so step them down; Pan and Mimas white `Star` 240 on their lit side, 130 on the other; background stars `Star` 40 or 120. Where ring and globe share a pixel (limb, crepe ring) the pixel takes the owner's steps (the owner is chosen from the static brightness, so clumps never flip it) at the coverage-blended brightness (a globe step stands for its floor). ASCII by level.
- **Look** *(M16, GEN-R25; supersedes the per-pixel ring and globe shading and the Inks above where they disagree)*: everything is braille dots on black. The rings are concentric ringlets, one-dot threads every 3 dots of ring radius at the ansae; where the tilt crowds them on screen (toward the minor axis) only every m-th ringlet is kept, so neighbouring threads stay ≥ 2 dots apart and never fill. A ringlet's brightness is the optical-depth profile averaged over its band, lit, times the Keplerian clumps (as before), Pan's edge waves and wake near the Encke gap (the profile re-averaged at the wave-displaced radius), and the globe's shadow (×0.06, so the shadow is a gap); each of its dots takes the rings' flat steps, brightest first: the B ring `Star` 230 from 0.72, `Structure` 200 from 0.5, 120 from 0.3, 50 from 0.14, dark below (the Cassini Division and the dark material between ringlets stay black; the Encke and Keeler gaps, narrower than the ringlet spacing, darken their ringlet). Spokes are faint grey streaks: ringlet dots inside a spoke's wedge (strength > 0.15) are `Dim` 150. The globe is also threads, not a half-block disc (GEN-R25's 3 % budget leaves no room for a disc at the rings' true scale): its outline ellipse `Sun` 230 on the lit side, 120 toward the terminator, `Dim` 90 on the night side so the outline closes; ten band lines (circles of planetographic latitude ±10°, 19°, 30°, 39°, 55°), zones `Sun` 200 and belts `Hot` 170 (one step dimmer toward the terminator: `Sun` 100, `Hot` 80), drawn only on the lit, visible hemisphere; the near ring hides the globe where it is fairly opaque, and the rings' shadow on the southern globe is a band with no lines. Far-side ring dots behind the globe are left out. Pan is one white dot and Mimas a small white dot disc, `Star` 245; background stars single `Star` 50 / 120 dots in 0.4 % of the cells, clear of the rings and the globe. The globe and stars are built per resize; the ringlet dots (position, angle, static brightness) are tabulated per resize and re-inked per frame. ASCII: by braille count. *(M16 review, supersedes the ringlet spacing above)*: ringlets are 4 dots apart at the ansae (3 on frames with fewer than 45 dots per Saturn radius), and thinned where the tilt crowds them by their true on-screen separation across the ellipse, `gap·sin i/|(sin λ, cos λ·sin i)|`, to ≥ 2.5 dots, so they never hatch into a fill; a ringlet's brightness averages the profile over half its band; a ringlet within 0.75 dots of the Cassini Division, the Encke or the Keeler gap is left out, so each gap is a black lane at least a dot and a half wider than itself (the Cassini Division ≥ 4 dots at 160×45).
- **Title**: `Saturn rings · moons carve gaps`.
- **Stylisation**: Pan's wake, edge waves and spokes exaggerated in contrast; Pan and Mimas enlarged (Mimas ×10); the Encke and Keeler gaps widened to ~0.02 R_S and the Cassini Division ~15 % outward so they show; each pixel averages the profile over its radial footprint (sub-pixel gaps darken rather than flicker); the F ring is omitted; time-lapse ×2000 for the ring motion; clump lifetimes shortened to 10 s; the profile is simplified to ~20 features; spokes shown at 20° tilt (they are seen mainly near equinox).
- **Acceptance**: `RING-A1` the τ LUT has near-zero depth at the Cassini Division, Encke and Keeler radii; `RING-A2` texture at the inner B ring advances faster than at the outer A ring in angle (Keplerian shear, ratio matches `r^{-3/2}` within 5 %); `RING-A3` spokes turn at the planet's rate, not the local Keplerian one; `RING-A4` *(changed, M16)* no ringlet dot lies on the far ring behind the globe, the planet's shadow falls on the far, anti-sunward ring (the shadow function), and at 100×40 the shadowed far ring is dark where its sunward mirror is lit; `RING-A5` Pan's edge-wave amplitude decays with angle downstream of the moon; `RING-A6` *(changed, M16)* at 120×40 the globe is threads: > 20 dots each of limb, zone and belt lines, nothing else but stars; at most 1/16 of the band-line dots (checked at their rounded dots) lie behind the opaque near ring or in the rings' shadow; no band line reaches the night (left) limb; every lit cell is braille; *(M15, superseded)* the globe in flat steps, palest in the middle, limb-darkened; `RING-A7` Mimas turns at `Ω(r_M)`, slower than any ring, its 2:1 resonance lies within 1 % of the Cassini Division's inner edge, and at 120×40 it is on screen at t = 1 and t = 30, in different places *(changed, M16: as a lit dot in a `Star` 245 cell)*; `RING-A8` Pan's wake ripples decay downstream and away from the gap, vanish beyond its reach and tighten towards the moon (wavelength `3π|Δr|`); `RING-A9` the clump texture changes by < 0.3 × its contrast over 0.02 R_S in radius, and at least a fifth of the ringlet dots switch on or off or change ink between t = 1 and t = 30 at 120×40 *(changed, M16; M15: a third of the ring pixels change step)*; `RING-A10` *(new, M16)* at 200×50 after 2 s no lit dot of a ringlet inside the Cassini Division's core (its edges less 0.02 R_S) shows on the ansae, and the ringlets are threads: in the B ring's band (1.55–1.9 R_S) on the ansae fewer than half and more than a fifth of the dots are lit. `RING-A11` *(new, M16 review)* at 80×24, 160×45 and 200×50 (t = 3 s) under 10 % of the lit ringlet dots have all four neighbours lit, no ringlet within 0.75 dots of a gap is drawn, and at 160×45 the drawn ringlets either side of the Cassini Division are ≥ 4 dots apart.

### comet_tails  (Sun & Planets, M13, M15)
- **Intent** *(M15, ADR-0018: flat-stepped tails on black, the white head the accent)*: a bright comet near the Sun (the Sun itself off frame, not drawn): a curved, fanned yellow dust tail and a straight, narrow blue ion tail of fine rippling streamers pointing straight away from the Sun, which occasionally snaps off and regrows (a disconnection event).
- **Model**: dust grains released from the nucleus with small velocity spreads; each moves on an analytic two-body orbit about the Sun under reduced gravity `μ(1 − β)`, β log-uniform in [0.04, 0.9] (< 1 keeps gravity attractive; β > r/2a ≈ 0.015–0.03 everywhere on the shown arc, so every grain is on a hyperbolic orbit), propagated in closed form with the universal-variable Kepler solution (Newton, ≤ 3 iterations warm-started from the previous frame; the scene's own `Orbit`, since core has no Kepler-equation solver). Grains of one β form a syndyne, grains of one release time a synchrone. Dust pool 8000, grains dropped after 20 s; activity ∝ r^{−3/2}. Ion tail: plasma parcels (pool 3000 — room for a whole attached tail, `280/s × 5 s`, plus a whole disconnected one, so a cut never starves the regrowth) launched antisunward at 0.25 AU/s and accelerated at 0.16 AU/s², each riding one of 5 streamers fanned 0.026 rad apart, with a kink (2.8–4 % of the run, 0.35 Hz) that travels down the tail. A disconnection every 20 s (first at 16 s scene time) detaches the tail at the coma — the cut tail keeps its rays, is swept off by the solar wind and fades over 4 s — release pauses 1.6 s and the new tail regrows. The comet (q = 0.35 AU, e = 0.97) sweeps ν = −90° → 95° in a 30 s loop; each new pass starts with the tails it grew on the way in (grains seeded with past release times from the orbit's earlier states — they need no history, being analytic).
- **Motion**: comet sweeps round perihelion (30 s loop, 0.4 s cross-fade at the wrap); the view follows the nucleus (kept off-centre towards the Sun by 0.38 of the frame, orientation fixed; *M14*: the scale is `min(0.36 w, 0.8 h)` px per AU, was `min(0.5 w, 1.1 h)`, so the dust tail stays in the frame; the long ion tail may still run out of it, faded), so the dust tail curves behind the orbit and the ion tail swings to stay antisunward; kinks run down the ion streamers and knots of plasma stream out along them.
- **Resize**: orbit and grain state in AU; view scale rebuilt; dot lattice and pixels resized. *(M14, GEN-R19)* A new comet starts 4 s into its approach with its backlog seeded there and placed by one step (grains are analytic orbits, so that equals having flown there); the first resize no longer runs 4 s of steps (40 ms → 4 ms at 500×150).
- **Inks** *(M15, ADR-0018: M11's line art, braille dots for tails and flat rings for the head; were M14's toned `Starlight` and `Ice` glows)*: the tails are braille `Dots` over the pixel layer. Dust (sunlight reflected): one dot per grain, sun-yellow `Sun` 200 for grains younger than 5 s (the fan near the head) and for fine grains (β > 0.6, which scatter more light per unit mass), dim orange `Hot` 110 for the rest (the broad tail curving behind the orbit); none while the loop's envelope is below ½ except the older step. Ion tail (CO⁺ blue): each streamer a dotted Bresenham line along its ray (48 segments), `Wave` 170 within 0.16 AU of the root, `Cold` 170 to 0.68 AU, `Cold` 40 beyond and on the fan's two faint outer rays (and while the comet fades); attached parcels are moving `Wave` 230 knots (`Cold` 120 beyond 0.68 AU), a cut tail's parcels `Cold` 40 for 60 % of its fade, then gone. No dots within 4.5 px of the nucleus, where the head is drawn. The head, within 4.5 px of the nucleus, is flat rings: a `Star` 255 nucleus (< 1.2 px), `Star` 150 coma (< 3 px), a `Sun` 140 rim, each one step dimmer while the loop's envelope is below ½: the one bright accent. No Sun glow (it sat cut off in a corner). Sky stars `Star` 40. ASCII by level.
- **Title**: `Comet tails · light pushes dust`.
- **Stylisation**: comet brightened; dust offsets from the nucleus drawn ×5 (the real tail grows over weeks); β range and release rates tuned for looks; ion tail kinematic, its streamers and kinks drawn wider than the ~1° real rays.
- **Acceptance**: `CMT-A1` a grain's orbit conserves energy of the reduced-μ problem (closed form) to 1e-9, and on the whole shown arc β_min = 0.04 > r/2a, so even the heaviest grain released at the nucleus's velocity has positive energy (hyperbolic); `CMT-A2` the dust tail lies on the side of the anti-solar direction away from the direction of motion (curves behind); `CMT-A3` attached ion-tail parcels older than 0.3 s are within 8° of anti-solar; `CMT-A4` after a disconnection the tail near the coma (0.08 AU) is empty for ≥ 1 s (from 0.3 s after the cut) and then refills; `CMT-A5` the grain pools never exceed capacity and β < 1 always; `CMT-A6` (regression, M13 review: a cut-off tail used to fill the pool and leave a detached stub) at 120×40 over 70 s, outside a cut's first PAUSE + 1 s and the loop's wrap, the attached tail holds ≥ 60 % of its steady parcel count and its ion dots run unbroken along the axis (a lit ion dot within 1.5 px of every sample) from the head's edge to 0.4 AU or the frame edge, whether or not the Sun is on frame *(changed, M15: was tone level > 20)*; `CMT-A7` *(changed, M15)* the ion dots near the root are a brighter step than those 0.9 AU down, and a cut across the tail at 0.6 AU crosses ≥ 2 separate runs of ion dots (streamers, not one band); `CMT-A8` *(changed, M15)* at 5, 12 and 20 s the pixel layer holds only the head's rings and sky stars (the nucleus `Star` 255, no Sun), and the tails are braille cells, > 20 in `Cold`/`Wave` (ion) and > 20 in `Sun`/`Hot` (dust), nothing else. *(M15: CMT-A9, the far-dust blur, is retired with the blur.)*

### jupiter_storms  (Sun & Planets, M13, M15, M16)
- **Intent** *(M14; M15 look, ADR-0018)*: Jupiter's whole disc on black space: alternating belts and zones sheared by jet streams, the Great Red Spot rolling like a ball bearing, white ovals, and filaments wound into curls, the view following the spot as the planet turns.
- **Model**: `vortex_advect` (shared `pub(crate)`): semi-Lagrangian advection of two dye fields on a capped lattice (≤ 160×80, `lattice_for`) by an analytic velocity: zonal jets `u(y) = U Σ a_j cos(k_j y)` (6–8 jets, alternating) plus Gaussian stream-function vortices `ψ = s·e^{−dx²/a²−dy²/b²}` (GRS anticyclone elongated 1.4:1, its present shape — it was ~2:1 in the Voyager era — and 5 white ovals with dark collars) that drift with the local jet, velocities as the cell differences of ψ (discretely divergence-free); a weak relaxation back to the banded dye keeps it from mixing out; ~30 short-lived small eddies of random sign add filaments; the red chromophore is fed on the GRS's east side so its own rotation winds it into a spiral. A third advected dye, fed by three small slowly drifting sources on the fastest eastward jet at a belt edge and decaying at 0.35/s, is drawn out by the jet into dark festoons. *(M14)* The flow lives on a longitude × latitude strip (lattice aspect 4:1, one lattice height spanning 65° of latitude and of longitude) mapped onto an orthographic, oblate (0.935) disc of radius 0.47 of the frame's smaller side; beyond ±65° the poles fade into a dim featureless hood. Io's shadow crosses the disc every 20 s, starting on it.
- **Motion**: dye streams along jets and curls round vortices; ovals drift; festoons trail along the jet; the GRS slowly turns (period ~6 s); the view follows the GRS, swaying up to 0.5 rad of longitude either side of it; Io's shadow drifts across the disc.
- **Resize**: lattice regridded (ADR-0012); vortex positions in planet units. *(M14, GEN-R19)* The first resize spins the flow up (100 steps) on a coarse lattice of 1/10 of the cap and then regrids it to the full lattice.
- **Inks** *(M15, ADR-0018; was M14's one smooth `Ramp(Sienna)`; superseded by the M16 Look: the shared cloud map `shared::jovian` was removed at M16)*: features blend in one *level* space (0 dark … 1 pale), and the shaded level is drawn in five flat steps of M11's roles, like M11's stepped star: `Hot` 25 below 0.24, `Hot` 105 below 0.4 (rust belts, the Great Red Spot), `Sun` 70 below 0.56, `Sun` 150 below 0.72 (ochre), `Star` 170 above (cream zones). Belts and zones map the zone/belt dye (embossed by a relief term, which with the steps gives the flat, contoured texture), dimmed to 0.82 by the shading with limb darkening `0.35 + 0.65·√μ`, so the limb steps down into dark rust; the polar hood is level 0.33. The GRS is a brick-to-rust oval (darker where the wound-up chromophore is dense) whose outline comes from its vortex (0.8 of its half-axes), in a paler collar; it is dimmed a little less (0.8). The clearing dye only darkens the belt edge (up to 60 %): no blue hot spots. Io's shadow is black. Space is black. ASCII by level. The previous row's dye samples are cached while shading, so most pixels take one bilinear sample.
- **Look** *(M16, GEN-R25; a flow-line planet, superseding the stepped half-block disc and its inks above)*: the same flow and dyes, drawn as braille threads on black. Each drawn frame samples the advected dyes on a screen-space node grid over the projected disc (spacing `max(3.5, 2R/110)` dots; geometry per node fixed in `resize`, only the longitude shift is per frame), blurs the samples twice with a NaN-aware 1-2-1 kernel and traces iso-contours with each crossed cell refined into sub-cells of about 1.4 dots (`Dots::contour_cell_refined`, CORE-R106), so the threads are smooth waves, not lattice-stepped runs with right-angle jogs (M16 round 2). Contours of an advected dye are material lines, so they run along the jets and wind into curls round the vortices. **Bands**: the zone/belt dye's contours at 0.2 and 0.45 (belt side, `Hot` 200, 235) and 0.75 (zone side, `Sun` 235), and the clearing dye's festoon at 0.3; toward the poles the dye fades to 0.5, so the threads end in the hood. **Great Red Spot** *(M16 round 2)*: a kinematic spiral of threads turning counter-clockwise with the vortex's 6 s turnover: two arms of 1.5 turns each from 0.12 to 1.05 of its half-axes, laid on the sphere (about a sixth of the disc wide), `Hot` 255 inside half the radius and `Hot` 205 outside; band threads inside 1.1 of its ellipse are left out. The red chromophore dye is dropped from the model (it only fed the spot's threads). **White ovals**: one-arm `Star` spirals of 1.5 turns (255 inside, 215 outside) out to 1.3 of their half-axes, turning in 4 s; band threads inside 1.43 of their ellipse are left out. **Festoons**: the clearing dye's contour at 0.3 is a thin `Hot` 130 filament. The **limb** is a thin dot ellipse (`Sun` 170, oblate 0.935); threads within `μ < 0.35` of it are one step dimmer (limb darkening). **Io's shadow** is a round gap in the threads. No half-blocks. ASCII by dot count. Rasterised in `prepare` (GEN-R20).
- **Title**: `Jupiter storms · belts shear`.
- **Stylisation**: kinematic flow (prescribed, not solved); enhanced false colour in five flat steps (ADR-0018); speeds time-lapsed; the flow strip repeats every 260° of longitude (at most 180° is ever in view).
- **Acceptance**: `JUP-A1` the velocity field is divergence-free (discrete divergence < 1e-9), and the lattice flow is that field; `JUP-A2` jets alternate in sign between neighbouring bands; `JUP-A3` total dye is conserved by advection within 1 % per second (before relaxation); `JUP-A4` the GRS rotates anticyclonically (counter-clockwise in the southern hemisphere view); `JUP-A5` the lattice stays within its cap at 500×150; `JUP-A6` *(changed, M16)* after 2 s at 100×40 every lit cell is braille inked `Hot`, `Sun` or `Star`, the four corners are blank, the lit cells inside the disc hold on average ≤ 4 of their 8 dots and ≤ 10 % of them are full (threads, not a fill), with at least 10 % of the disc's cells lit; `JUP-A7` the GRS's half-axis ratio is in [1.3, 1.5]; `JUP-A8` *(changed, M16)* at t = 0 the GRS sits on the disc's centre meridian at its latitude, and the lit cells inside its drawn ellipse are mostly `Hot` (≥ 80 %, at least 6 of them); `JUP-A9` *(changed, M16)* the limb is a thin dot ellipse: every one of 36 directions has a lit dot within 1.5 dots of it and none beyond 2 dots outside it, and threads near the limb (μ < 0.35) are one step dimmer than the same thread on the disc; `JUP-A10` *(new, M16)* the belts and zones are threads: the centre column crosses at least 8 separate band threads, `Hot` on the belt side (levels below 0.5) and `Sun` on the zone side (above 0.5); `JUP-A11` *(new, M16)* a white oval on the visible hemisphere is drawn as `Star` threads round its centre; `JUP-A12` *(new, M16)* the threads are smooth: at 200×50 and 100×40 after 12 s at most 5 % of lit dots sit at a corner (exactly two lit neighbours at 90° or less to each other), and at 200×50 at most 5 % are L-step jogs (a side and an up/down neighbour; round 1 had ≈ 10 %); `JUP-A8` *(M16 round 2)* also: the spot's spiral is drawn about a sixth of the disc wide (0.13–0.2 of its diameter) with at least two turns in all.

### solar_eruption  (Sun & Planets, M13, M15, M16, M17)
- **Intent** *(M15, ADR-0018: thin lines on black in M11's complementary colours)*: the Sun's limb: a quiet arcade of thin glowing loops over a black disc edged by a thin red chromosphere, then a twisted flux rope rises, field lines snap and reconnect beneath it, two flare ribbons flash at the footpoints, a coronal mass ejection balloons out, and bright coronal rain drains back down the new loops.
- **Model**: 2.5-D potential field of 4–6 photospheric sources (line dipoles) plus a flux rope (line current) whose height `h(t)` follows a torus-instability curve (slow rise, then exponential takeoff). Field lines are traced with RK2 from footpoints (≤ 20 re-traced per frame, 42 cached: 32 arcade, 10 rope strands kept only while closed) into a `Glow` buffer. Reconnection: lines whose apex passes under the rope's X-point are cut and replaced by shorter post-flare loops (cooling ages); flare ribbons brighten at their footpoints and move apart. CME: a three-part structure (bright front, dark cavity, core) expanding self-similarly. Coronal rain: blobs (pool 200) sliding down loops under projected gravity.
- **Motion**: `Timeline`: quiet (6 s) → rise (8 s) → flare (6 s) → CME (10 s) → rain (12 s) → quiet.
- **Resize**: sources and rope in solar radii; caches invalidated.
- **Drawing** (M13 review): the traced field is a 2.5-D slice, so each arcade line gets a fixed 3-D *pose* (lean ±0.3, footpoint shift ±0.6 L along a neutral line that curves away from the viewer, so loops far along it are rooted up to ~0.26 L behind the limb and their feet are occulted; height ×0.62–1.22): the arcade reads as a row of loops seen at the limb, not concentric arches. The quiet arcade is sparse: only ~45 % of the loops glow (the rest are skipped until reconnection heats them), so isolated loops stand apart with dark corona between them. Hot post-flare loops stand upright on the ribbons and relax into their pose as they cool. Brightness varies along every loop (knots ~0.3 L long drifting along it, siphon flows) and between loops (gain 0.35–1.35 with a cubic spread: a few bright over many faint). The rope's closed field lines are not drawn; instead four strands (two below 26 px per `L`, e.g. 80×24, where more would merge into a blob) wind 2.5 turns round a sheared arch (one shoulder high, the apex leaning over: the limb view of a sigmoid's S), rotating slowly, faint while it forms, brightening as it rises, and gone by 60 % of the CME act so no stretched legs are left in the frame. The CME is a self-similar bubble (radius 0.55 × centre height) that swells out of the rope in the flare and accelerates off the top of the frame (centre 1.05 L → 6 L): a bright front (a lightbulb shell: a crisp outer rim fading softly inward into the cavity, brightest on top and falling off faster down the flanks as the bubble grows, the whole front fading out by 55 % of the CME act so it leaves as a whole, and filamentary: narrow radial streaks modulate its brightness strongly along it), a dark cavity that empties the loops and corona inside it, and the rope as the bright core low in the cavity, its legs still rooted.
- **Inks** *(M15, ADR-0018: M11's complementary roles in flat steps; were M14's `Starlight`/`HAlpha` ramps and M13's `Aurora`)*: the hot loops glow in EUV, which has no visible colour, so they are false-colour cyan (AIA-like); the cool plasma that shines in H-alpha (the flux rope and its core, coronal rain, the flare ribbons) is red, the complementary accent; the CME front is white light (Thomson-scattered), a dim white shell at `120·level/255`. Loops are stroked with a line kernel at every size (centre 1, the four neighbours 0.15, no corners: about a pixel thin), their glow exposed ×1.5 × 1.4 (thin strokes gather less light); a toned value `g` is taken to `top·smoothstep(g/0.35)·(0.5 + 0.5·g)` with top 235 for loops and 150 for H-alpha, plus up to 60 more for H-alpha toned above 0.85 (the rope's dense core only), and drawn in flat steps: loops `Wave` 220 from 150, `Wave` 100 from 60, dim `Cold` 40 below; H-alpha `Hot` 240 from 160, `Hot` 150 from 60, `Hot` 40 below; the front in false-colour magenta `Accent` 190 from 70, `Accent` 70 from 40, nothing below; per pixel the brightest of loops, H-alpha material and front owns it. The flare (ERUP-A11): newly reconnected loops hotter than 0.3 of their `e^{−age/9 s}` also stroke a flare layer (weight `2.4·gain·hot`), and the flare ribbons go there too; its toned level draws `Sun` 240 from 45 and `Star` 255 from 120, over everything else, so at reconnection the loop tops and footpoint ribbons are the brightest pixels of the scene. The disc is black (no chromosphere fill) with a flat red line just inside the limb (`Hot` 90 where the 0.8 px rim's smooth profile reaches 40 % of its peak); the diffuse corona is not drawn; each quiet loop flickering on its own heating cycle, a light rain drizzle in the quiet act; open lines are mirrored about the inversion line (the field is mirror-symmetric); flare ribbons are two lines hugging the limb at the post-flare loops' feet, brightest at their inner edge, in the H-alpha steps. No stars (EUV). ASCII by level.
- **Look** *(M16, GEN-R25; supersedes the M15 Inks and the glow strokes of Drawing)*: every field line, the rope and the CME are one-dot braille threads on black; no glows, fills or blobs. Colour along the field is its topology: closed loops (both feet on the surface) `Hot`, open field lines (leaving the domain, and their mirror twins) `Cold`; a segment's brightness `e` (the loop's weight × its knot brightness, dimmed inside the CME cavity) picks a flat step, 220 from 1.0, 150 from 0.35, 90 from 0.12, and below that the segment is not drawn, so knots and faint loops break into dashes; 30 % of the cool lines (each mirror twin on its own, by a fixed hash) are not drawn at all, so the rising arcade does not tangle; hot post-flare loops always are. The flare: freshly reconnected loops hotter than 0.3 are `Sun` 240, their tops (the middle 12 % of the loop while hotter than 0.8) `Star` 255; the flare ribbons are dotted lines of `Sun` 240 dots (every other dot) hugging the limb at the post-flare loops' feet, the inner third (the newest reconnection) `Star` 255, drawn while the flash × taper is ≥ 0.3. The flux rope's strands are `Hot` 240 where they face the viewer (face ≥ 0.5) and `Hot` 140 behind (`Hot` 90 while the rope's gain is below 0.3), and its prominence core a single bright `Hot` 240 thread along the axis (u 0.28–0.61) in the flare and CME. The CME front is a braille arc at the bubble's radius, drawn where its brightness `amp·flank·(0.25 + 1.8 fil)` is ≥ 0.25 (filamentary dashes), `Accent` 230 from 0.8, else `Accent` 150; 48 plasma dots at fixed places in the bubble's upper half (0.5–0.95 of its radius) ride the self-similar expansion, each an `Accent` 230 head with an `Accent` 110 trail back to where it was `PLASMA_TRAIL` = 0.025 of the act earlier, while the front's amplitude is ≥ 0.2. Coronal rain is `Hot` 240 dots with a short trail along their loop. The limb is a thin `Hot` 100 braille arc just inside it; the disc is black. ASCII by dot count. No half blocks.
- **Title**: `Solar eruption · loops snap`; captions per act: `quiet arcade`, `loops rise`, `flare`, `mass ejection`, `coronal rain` (every act is captioned).
- **Stylisation**: potential + one rope, not MHD; hours compressed into ~45 s; EUV loops drawn in false-colour cyan (they have no visible colour), H-alpha red where the plasma is cool; loop poses are drawn, not traced (the field is a 2.5-D slice); the CME's three-part structure is drawn as seen in white-light coronagraphs, its front in false-colour magenta.
- **Acceptance**: `ERUP-A1` a traced line follows `B`: the angle between its tangent and `B` at sample points is < 2°; `ERUP-A2` the rope height is monotonic in the rise act and its log-slope increases (takeoff); `ERUP-A3` flare-ribbon separation increases during the flare; `ERUP-A4` rain blobs move toward the surface along their loop; `ERUP-A5` at most 20 lines are re-traced per frame; `ERUP-A6` arcade loops vary: at least a fifth lean each way by > 0.08, a quarter are rooted > 0.05 L behind the limb, shifts and height scales each span > 0.3, gains span > 3×, brightness along ≥ 3/4 of the loops varies > 2× and drifts in time, a pose at strength 0 is the identity, 30–60 % of the arcade loops glow in the quiet corona, and loops rooted far along the neutral line (|shift| > 0.35 L) sit on average > 0.08 L further behind the limb than those near its middle; `ERUP-A7` the rope axis is rooted on the surface either side of the inversion line, peaks at `hr` (±2 %), is sheared (shoulder heights at u = 0.3 and 0.7 differ by > 20 % of `hr`, apex off-centre by > 0.1 L), each of ≥ 3 strands crosses the axis ≥ 4 times, its drawn apex height and brightness never fall from the rise through the flare, its brightness is 0 from 65 % of the CME act on, and two strands are drawn at 80×24 scale; `ERUP-A8` the CME is three-part (front at d = 1 over 4× brighter than at d = 0.7 or 1.3, cavity dimming to < 0.3 at d = 0.5, none outside), self-similar, its core below the centre by < half a radius, its top rising monotonically past 4 L by the act's end, and *(changed, M16)* at 80×24 mid-CME every lit cell is braille in one of the M16 inks (`Hot` and `Cold` steps, the rope's `Hot` 240/140/90, the flare's `Star` 255 and `Sun` 240, the front's `Accent` 230/150/110, the limb's `Hot` 100), with some `Hot` 240 (the rope); `ERUP-A9` every act shows a caption from `meta.captions`, the quiet act's first; `ERUP-A10` *(changed, M16)* at 120×40, sampled at ten times through a cycle, every cell whose dots all lie more than 2 dots inside the limb is blank, and > 20 cells are the limb's `Hot` 100 arc; `ERUP-A11` *(changed, M16)* at 120×40 the quiet arcade has no flare cells, and mid-flare more than 10 cells each are the flare's `Star` 255 and `Sun` 240; `ERUP-A12` *(new, M16)* closed field lines are drawn `Hot` and open ones `Cold`, every segment of a line in its topology's role (or the flare's while it is hot); `ERUP-A13` *(changed, M17)* the CME front's stipple lies in the band from max(0.9 r, r − 2 dots) to the rim r, and rises through the act.
- **Look** *(M17, GEN-R26, three-part CME; supersedes the M16 look where they disagree)*: loops stay braille field-line threads (`Hot` closed, `Cold` open) and now carry their plasma as stipple sampled along and beside each loop (probability ∝ emission measure, densest at hot loop tops during the flare); the CME erupts with the classic three-part structure: a bright leading front of sparse stipple (`Star`/`Cold`, expanding), a dark cavity with no dots, and a bright core — the twisted flux rope as two or three helical `Accent`/`Hot` threads; flare ribbons are `Sun` dots at the loop footpoints that separate as reconnection proceeds (M16's `Star` inner third and its `Accent` front thread with 48 plasma dots are gone: that plasma sat in what is now the dark cavity); open field lines stay thinned until the CME; post-flare loops stack and rise beneath; the limb is a thin arc. The `loops rise` act is calmer than M16 (fewer, cleaner threads).
- **Acceptance** *(M17)*: `ERUP-A14` during the CME act no lit dots lie in the cavity between the core and the front; `ERUP-A15` the front is stipple, not a solid line: along it at most half of the dots are lit; `ERUP-A16` ribbons are `Sun` dots at loop footpoints and their separation grows during the flare; `ERUP-A17` in `loops rise` the lit cells are at most 70 % of M16's at the same time (seed 42, 200×50).

### io_volcanoes  (Sun & Planets, M13, M15, M17)
- **Intent** *(M15, ADR-0018: flat M11 fills)*: Io's limb against black space: a yellow ground (ochre plains with brighter sulfur-yellow patches), white SO₂ frost, rust highlands, black paterae and glowing lava pixels, and two volcanoes throwing umbrella-shaped plumes of cyan grains, a giant Pele (300 km) and a squat Prometheus (80 km), whose fallout paints a red-brown ring and a white ring on the surface.
- **Model**: `plume` (shared `pub(crate)`): ballistic particles (pool 6k) in Io's gravity (1.8 m/s², no air), launched from the vent with speed `v₀·U(0.96, 1)` (Pele 1 km/s, Prometheus 0.5 km/s) at zenith angles uniform in solid angle over 0.15–0.9 rad in 28 azimuthal rays; height `v₀²/(2g)`, fall radius from the ballistic range. Landings are splatted bilinearly into a 32² deposit map per vent (±1.5 × the 45° range), fading 2.5 % per second, read relative to the map's densest cell and averaged over a fan one ray wide, so the ring is smooth; the rings are repainted one band of rows in 6 per step. *(M14)* The plume is the live grains themselves: each is one braille dot on its ballistic arc, drawn only against space (the thin plume vanishes over the bright ground, as Pele does on the limb in the Voyager and Galileo frames). Tidal heating is shown as a pulsing vent pixel in phase with Io's orbital eccentricity (period compressed to 20 s). The view: Io fills the lower frame, radius `min(0.95·H, 0.65·W)` pixels, centred one radius below mid-height, lit from the upper right with a soft terminator and gentle limb darkening (the lower-left limb in shade); no Jupiter. Sparse pixel stars (1 in 500 space pixels) dot the sky. The ground is shaded once per resize, the paterae each over their own box (GEN-R19).
- **Motion**: the eruption breathes (launch speed scaled by the breathing factor's square root, so the plume's size by ≈ ±8 %, on 7.3 s and 2.9 s beats), the grains fly along their arcs, vent pixels pulse with the tide, lava lakes flicker, deposit rings build then hold.
- **Resize**: geometry in km mapped to the frame; surface and lava-lake positions rebuilt; deposit maps kept.
- **Inks** *(M15, ADR-0018: M11's roles in flat steps, like mars_rover; were M14's `Starlight`/`Sulfur`/`Sienna` ramps and `Blackbody` lava)*: the light (`io_light`, 0.52–1) in three steps (terminator side, day side from 0.7, sub-solar from 0.88), one step brighter where the mottle noise exceeds 0.62; per step, plains ochre `Sun` 25/65/105 with patches (value noise) of sulfur-yellow `Sun` 120/165/210, SO₂ frost white `Star` 60/120/180, highlands and the redder poles rust `Hot` 10/50/90; paterae black `Dim` 0 with a dark `Hot` 0 rim; each lava lake two pixels, `Hot` 255 flickering to `Sun` 255 (its first pixel), and each vent one pixel pulsing `Sun` 255 → `Star` 255: the hot accents; Pele's short-chain-sulfur ring red `Hot` 120/170/220 (wider and softer on its inner edge), Prometheus's frost ring `Star` 150/195/240; plume grains cyan `Wave` braille dots, 70 below half the apex and 200 above (the canopy); stars `Star` 40. ASCII by level.
- **Title**: `Io volcanoes · tides melt rock`; captions `Pele` / `Prometheus` optional.
- **Stylisation**: plumes drawn ×1.6 relative to Io, heights exact in ratio; time scale ×100; tidal heating shown symbolically (a pulsing vent); the plume is drawn as its ballistic grains only (Pele's real canopy is a shock where gas-borne grains pile up into a bright rim); lava lakes and vents drawn yellow- to white-hot rather than Io's ≈ 1500 K, so they read as the accent; the ground's colours are saturated, flat steps (ADR-0018); Jupiter is left out (less decoration).
- **Acceptance**: `IO-A1` a particle's apex equals `v₀²/(2g)` within 1 %; `IO-A2` Pele's plume is ≥ 3× taller than Prometheus's; `IO-A3` deposits fall in a ring whose radius matches the ballistic range for 45° launches (± 15 %); `IO-A4` the pool never exceeds capacity; `IO-A5` the vent glow pulses with the configured period; ~~IO-A6~~ *(superseded by the M17 look)* *(changed, M15)* at 120×40 and 200×50 the plume is more than 40 braille cells of `Wave` grains (70 or 200), every one over space (not over Io), brighter in its top row than at its foot, and it moves between t = 1 s and 1.5 s; ~~IO-A7~~ *(superseded by the M17 look)* *(changed, M15)* at 160×50 every disc pixel is `Sun`, `Star`, `Hot`, `Dim` or `Wave`, more than a quarter of the disc is yellow `Sun`, ≥ 2 % is frost, there are ≥ 10 black paterae and ≥ 8 lava or vent pixels, and Pele's ring shows as more than 20 red-ring pixels near its vent; `IO-A8` the plume's size breathes by 8–25 % peak to peak, at least a third of the lava lakes change brightness by > 0.1 within 0.6 s, and at 120×40 the plume's top stays in the frame through the breathing (t = 1–5.5 s); `IO-A9` Io is shaded as a sphere: the light at the lower-left limb is ≥ 0.2 below the upper-right disc and below the centre, it changes by < 2 % per 1° across the disc (no hard terminator); *(changed, M15)* it reaches the ground in flat steps, the lower-left limb on the darkest and the sub-solar region on the brightest, and the bare globe uses at most 14 inks.
- **Look** *(M17, GEN-R26, limb + ballistic plume threads; supersedes the half-block globe, Inks and ASCII above)*: Io is a thin `Sun` braille limb arc; its surface is a sparse sulphur stipple (keep probability ≤ 0.15 by terrain: plains `Sun`, red polar and Pele-ring deposits `Hot`, SO₂ frost sparse `Star`), thinning across the terminator; active paterae (Loki and others) are tiny `Hot` knots whose brightness follows their output.
