Plumes (one big Pele-type umbrella, smaller Prometheus-type ones) are hundreds of ballistic particles on exact parabolas under Io's gravity (1.796 m/s², scaled to rows), each with a decaying trail, coloured by state along the flight: `Hot` at the vent, `Sun` while rising, `Cold` near the apex where SO₂ condenses; the umbrella canopy and the red fallout ring emerge from the trajectories, and landed particles add a slowly fading thin stipple ring on the surface (`Hot` for Pele's short-chain sulphur, `Star` for the Prometheus types' SO₂ frost). Three vents: Pele (1 km/s) and two Prometheus types (0.5 and 0.42 km/s), launched from a fixed fan of angles with small jitter, each grain drawing about 4 s of its own parabola as a smooth thread. No flat half-block planet.
- **Acceptance** *(M17)*: `IO-A10` at most 3 % half-block cells; `IO-A11` a plume particle's height after flight time `t` is `v_z t − g t²/2` (exact parabola) and its range is `v² sin 2θ / g`; `IO-A12` the umbrella's top is within one dot row of `v_max² / 2g` and fallout lands at the ring radius; `IO-A13` the surface stipple keeps at most 15 % of disc dots and the limb arc is continuous (no gaps longer than one dot).

### comet_breakup  (Sun & Planets, M13, M15, M16)
- **Intent**: Shoemaker–Levy 9: a comet grazes Jupiter inside the Roche limit and is torn into a string of pearls; two years later (compressed) the fragments strike one after another, raising fireballs on the limb and leaving dark scars.
- **Model**: rubble pile of 240 particles with softened self-gravity and soft damped contacts (N² pairs, 29k per step, ≤ 3 sub-steps of 0.01 τ per frame), in the orbital plane on a Kepler orbit about Jupiter, integrated in the comet's frame with the tidal term (Hill approximation) by velocity Verlet with sub-steps near perijove; inside the Roche distance the pile shears into a chain whose clumps are found by a cheap union-find on neighbours (≤ 21 fragments tracked). Act 2: each fragment's impact at 60 km/s spawns a `plume` (shared) fireball on the limb and a persistent dark scar that Jupiter's rotation carries round.
- **Motion**: `Timeline`: approach (2 s) → breakup (12 s) → string of pearls (12 s, the view follows the chain) → impacts (16 s) → scars (6 s), with orbit time advancing 0.7, 0.9 and 1.1 τ per second in the first three acts from τ = −2.6 (the approach is short so the tide visibly stretches the pile by t ≈ 5 s). Roche distance 2.5 R_J in the model's units, perijove 1.33 R_J. On the run-in the chain's spacing is shrunk if needed so the last fragment strikes by 13 s into the impacts act.
- **Resize**: dynamics in Jupiter radii; view rebuilt.
- **Inks** *(M15, ADR-0018: M11's roles in flat steps, no halos; were M14's `Starlight`/`Ice`/`Sienna` ramps and a `Blackbody` heart)*: nuclei are tight single-pixel-kernel splats; every third grain trails a dust tail (0.8 R_J, fading as `(1 − s)^1.5`) pointing away from a Sun to the lower left, and a narrow ion tail (1.4 R_J, straight anti-Sun, 12° from the dust tail); no coma halo. Their tone levels are drawn in flat steps: white `Star` 255 heads (from 170), pale `Star` 90 (from 100), then sun-yellow `Sun` 110 and dim orange `Hot` 40 down the dust tail (from 50 and 22); the ion tail, where no dust step is drawn, blue `Cold` 120 and `Cold` 20 (from 110 and 40). Each fragment trails its own short dust tail and ion streak. *(M15; superseded by the M16 Look, `shared::jovian` removed)* Jupiter used the same stepped cloud palette as `jupiter_storms`: the belt/zone latitude profile (EZ, NEB/SEB, tropical and temperate zones and belts, polar hoods), its edges made wavy and mottled by value noise fixed in planet longitude (so the texture turns with the planet and the scars), a small Great Red Spot (brick oval with a faint paler collar, feathered in its own units), limb darkening `μ^0.9` with no floor (the limb steps down into dark rust). The impact is a radial Gaussian glow on the limb (0.16 R_J, e-folding 0.7 s, gone by 2.5 s) plus the plume's grains (ramped in over their first 0.4 s so the vent never saturates), in flat steps from tone level 30: `Hot` 70, `Hot` 200 from 90, `Sun` 220 from 170, a `Star` 255 heart from 238; over the clouds a glow below level 140 lifts them one cloud step instead of replacing them. The falling plume reads as a crescent on the limb; each strike leaves a persistent dark scar (dark core in a crescent) that steps the clouds under it down (one step, three in its core, below the darkest belt into `Dim` 0) and rotates with the planet. The Roche circle is dashed `Dim` 140; sky stars `Star` 40. The view leans half-way to the comet on the approach, locks onto the pile early in the breakup and zooms out (to ≥ 40 % of the home scale) as the chain lengthens so the whole string of pearls stays in frame. ASCII by level.
- **Look** *(M16, GEN-R25; supersedes the Inks and the strike point above)*: braille threads on black, no half blocks or glows. Every grain of the pile (and, in the impacts act, every fragment as a small dot disc sized by its mass) is a white `Star` 255 dot. Tails are straight one-dot threads from the grains: a dust thread from every 16th grain (0.8 R_J, away from the Sun to the lower left), `Sun` 220 over its inner half and `Hot` 160 over its outer half (the dust ages and reddens outward), and an ion streamer from every 40th grain (1.4 R_J, straight anti-Sun), `Cold` 200 then `Cold` 120; in the impacts act each fragment trails one of each, scaled by its size. So the whole pile wears a few threads and each pearl its own. Jupiter is a thin `Sun` 190 braille limb circle with two `Hot` 110 belt lines (NEB and SEB edges); the scars are small grey `Dim` 200 dot rings (0.7 of the old scar size, foreshortened toward the limb) carried round by the rotation. The fireball's grains are dots coloured by heat `exp(−age/1.4 s)`: `Star` 240 from 0.6, `Sun` 220 from 0.3, then `Hot` 170; the flash is a starburst of 8 `Sun` 230 rays over the limb (length 2·0.16 R_J, fading with `e^{−t/0.7 s}`); the strike a white dot disc for its first 0.6 s. The Roche circle stays dashed `Dim` 140; background stars single `Star` 60 dots in 0.4 % of the cells. A brighter ink wins a shared braille cell (dust over ion near the nucleus). ASCII: by braille count. *(M16 review)*: during the breakup and the string of pearls every tail thread is capped at 12 dots, so the breaking pile's many tails stay threads.
- **Title**: `Comet breakup · tides pull apart`; captions `Roche limit`, `string of pearls`, `impacts`.
- **Stylisation**: the pile is ~10³× coarser than reality; the 2-year gap is compressed to seconds; scars exaggerated; the orbit is drawn as a parabola, an approximation of SL9's actual bound (highly eccentric, ~2-year) Jovian orbit, which near perijove it matches closely; the fragments' final run-in is a straight line to the impact site.
- **Strike point** (M14, kept): at each strike a 2 × 2-pixel white `Star` 255 point sits at the limb for its first 0.6 s, the impact's accent over the existing flash and fireball. `shared::plume` is unchanged, so io_volcanoes keeps its look.
- **Acceptance**: `BRK-A1` outside 3 Roche radii the pile stays bound (its radius changes < 10 %); `BRK-A2` after perijove inside the Roche limit ≥ 8 fragments form and they are ordered along the orbit (a chain: the principal axis ratio ≥ 5); `BRK-A3` the pool and the fragment table stay within capacity; `BRK-A4` impacts happen in fragment order and every fragment strikes within the act; `BRK-A5` scars move with Jupiter's rotation; `BRK-A6` by t = 5 s the pile's principal axis ratio is ≥ 2; *(changed, M16)* at 100×30 from t = 0.1 s, within a cell of the nucleus there is a white `Star` 255 cell, a quarter of a dust-tail length out a `Sun` 220 thread cell, at 0.8 lengths a `Hot` 160 one, no dust ink 0.45 lengths sunward, a `Cold` cell half an ion-tail length out along the anti-Sun line, and every lit cell is braille; *(M15, superseded)* stepped pixel tails; `BRK-A7` *(changed, M16)* Jupiter is a thin limb: ≥ 90 % of the points of its limb circle are lit dots, and inside it (0.85 R_J tall, 0.5 R_J either side of the centre, the belt lines left out) at most 2 % of the dots are lit; *(M15, superseded)* ≥ 80 % of its central pixels in the stepped cloud palette. `BRK-A8` *(new, M16 review)* at 100×30, 6 s and 20 s in, every lit dot outside Jupiter, the Roche circle and the stars lies within 12 + 1.5 dots of a grain.

### hurricane  (Earth & Sky, M13, M15, M16)
- **Intent** *(M15, ADR-0018)*: a mature hurricane seen from above on a black sea: a small clear eye inside a solid white central dense overcast, broad ragged rain bands winding counter-clockwise into it and faint cirrus outflow fanning clockwise at the edge, and an infrared act in the classic enhancement curve, the coldest cloud tops rising through blue, cyan, green and yellow to orange.
- **Model**: `vortex_advect` (shared with jupiter_storms): semi-Lagrangian cloud-water field on a capped lattice advected by a Holland-profile vortex (tangential wind peaking at the radius of maximum wind) plus radial inflow near the surface that tightens bands into logarithmic spirals; convective sources in the eyewall ring (0.75–1.5 radii of maximum wind), a solid central dense overcast out to 2.2 radii (fading over 0.8 more) and 3 broad spiral bands (noise-modulated cells) that switch on over one radius past 2.4 radii so they merge into the overcast; the visible picture adds a subtle fixed convective-tower texture (overshooting tops, turning slowly with the storm) with its own sunlit and shadowed sides where cloud is thick; the eye (r < 0.6 radii of maximum wind) is kept clear by a sink (subsidence). Display only (not advected), refreshed every 6 steps: faint cirrus outflow streaks spiralling out clockwise (anticyclonic aloft) at 0.7–1.5 storm radii. Brightness is blurred once (1-2-1) for soft 2–4 px cloud edges. On a coarse lattice (height ≤ 64, i.e. 80×24 and smaller) the secondary band and the tower texture drop out, the bands start at 3.2 radii, the eyewall cells are weaker and the picture is blurred twice, so a small frame shows a few broad arms rather than thin stripes. Northern hemisphere: cyclonic = counter-clockwise (Coriolis).
- **Motion**: clouds rotate and spiral inward; bands pulse; `Timeline` switches visible → IR act every 25 s; the whole storm drifts slowly.
- **Resize**: lattice regridded (ADR-0012); vortex in storm radii. *(M14, GEN-R19)* The first resize spins the storm up (180 steps) on a coarse lattice of 1/24 of the cap and then regrids it to the full lattice; the convective-tower tile is size-independent and built once.
- **Inks** *(M15, ADR-0018: M11's roles in flat steps; were M14's `Starlight` and muted `Infrared` ramps)*: visible: the open sea and the eye are black (no ocean colour or sunglint). Cloud lightness is `0.16 + 0.84·√b` of the cloud-top brightness `b`, with sun-facing relief and the tower texture where the cloud has body, times an opacity `smoothstep((b − 0.012)/0.128)`, scaled by 0.8, and drawn in four flat grey-to-white steps: black below level 14, the thin veil `Dim` 0 below 0.28, the deck `Structure` 120 below 0.5, `Star` 110 below 0.66, sunlit tops `Star` 220. Rows of the cubic lattice sample are cached while shading. IR act (25 s visible, 12 s infrared, 1.5 s cross-fades: a dip through black, the visible picture fading out while the IR weight rises to ½ and the IR one fading in after, uniform in space): coldness `c` (the enhancement curve's position, from cloud-top brightness standing in for height, hence coldness, times a height factor, 1 inside the overcast (2.2 radii of maximum wind) falling to 0.62 at 0.9 storm radii, so the overcast and eyewall are the coldest), times the thin-cloud opacity and the cross-fade, in six flat steps of the classic enhancement curve: warm low cloud `Dim` 0 below 0.18, `Cold` 90 below 0.36, `Wave` 150 below 0.52, `Particle` 130 below 0.66, `Sun` 200 below 0.8, the coldest tops `Hot` 230; black where the opacity × cross-fade is below level 14. ASCII by level, the sea left blank; in Unicode a cell whose two pixels match is a space on that background.
- **Look** *(M16, GEN-R25; spiral band threads, superseding the half-block cloud picture, its relief and tower texture, and the inks above)*: the same storm model, drawn as braille threads on black. The blurred cloud-top brightness on the lattice is contoured by marching squares at `b = 0.3, 0.5, 0.7` (below 0.3 the band cells' noise, near the lattice scale, only frays into specks), each crossed lattice cell refined into `k × k` bilinear sub-cells (`Dots::contour_cell_refined`, CORE-R106) (`k = clamp(⌈max(sx, sy)/2.2⌉, 1, 5)`, `sx, sy` dots per node), so the threads are smooth. *(M16 round 2)* Inside 1.8 radii of maximum wind only the 0.5 and 0.7 contours are drawn: two eyewall rings round the clear eye. Outside it each rain band is one thread along its centre line: the zero of the brightness's radial derivative (central differences per node) where it falls outward, in cloud of at least 0.4, so the bands are single logarithmic-spiral threads winding counter-clockwise into the eye. **Visible act**: colour by radius, outer bands (beyond 3.2 radii of maximum wind) `Cold`, the inner bands and overcast edge (1.8–3.2) `Wave`, the eyewall (inside 1.8) `Star`, each stepped 150, 200, 245 by contour level. **Inflow tracers**: 90 dots carried by the low-level wind (the model's tangential wind plus inflow, storm-relative), each with a trail of its last 0.4 s (`Star` 255 head, `Cold` 150/110 trail), respawned at 0.5–1 storm radii when they reach the eye or after 12–20 s, so they spiral in counter-clockwise. **Cirrus**: the faint outflow stays in the brightness (below the lowest contour) and is not drawn; as threads it only hatched the storm's flanks. **IR act**: the threads recolour by the coldness of their cloud tops (the enhancement curve of the contour level, or of a band ridge's own brightness, times the height factor, so the inner bands recolour too): warm `Cold` 170 below 0.4, `Wave` 210 below 0.7, the coldest tops `Accent` 245; the cross-fade dips through black (no threads while its weight is below 0.25, one step dimmer below 0.6). ASCII by dot count. Rasterised in `prepare` (GEN-R20).
- **Title**: `Hurricane · Coriolis spirals`; captions `visible`, `infrared`.
- **Stylisation**: kinematic vortex, not a moist-convection model; time-lapse; brightness stands in for cloud-top temperature in the IR act, and the enhancement curve is drawn in six flat semantic steps; the sea is drawn black; seen from above (Earth's weather seen from above is Earth & Sky: ADR-0013 addendum, M13).
- **Acceptance**: `HUR-A1` the tangential wind profile peaks at the radius of maximum wind and turns counter-clockwise; `HUR-A2` a tracer released at 0.8 R spirals inward (radius decreasing, angle increasing CCW); `HUR-A3` the eye (r < 0.5 radii of maximum wind) holds < 10 % of the eyewall's cloud; `HUR-A4` *(changed, M16)* in the IR act every thread ink is one of the three IR steps (`Cold`, `Wave`, `Accent`, the tracers aside), the coldest tops reach `Accent`, and the steps run `Cold`, `Wave`, `Accent` with coldness; `HUR-A5` the lattice stays within its cap at 500×150; `HUR-A6`/`HUR-A7` *(changed, M16)* at 120×40 and 80×24 the visible act is braille threads in `Cold`, `Wave` and `Star` on black: lit cells hold on average ≤ 4.5 of their 8 dots, and more than half of the cells are blank; `HUR-A8` *(changed, M16)* at 80×24, the mean cloud-top brightness of the lattice nodes in 36 angular bins round annuli at 2.5, 3.5 and 4.5 radii of maximum wind shows at most 4 arms (peaks with 0.08 hysteresis); `HUR-A9` *(changed, M16)* at 120×40 (t = 2 s and 9 s) the central dense overcast is unbroken: all 36 angular bins of the 1.2–2 radii annulus of the lattice brightness average at least the top contour level (0.7) and ≥ 0.75 of the brightest, and `Star` eyewall threads ring the eye: every one of 12 directions has a `Star` cell within 0.5–2 radii of maximum wind; `HUR-A10` *(changed, M16)* in the IR act at 120×40 there are `Accent` threads inside 3 radii of maximum wind (the overcast's top contour; inside the eye the lower contours are thin, warmer cloud), and fewer than a quarter of the band thread cells at 4.5–6 radii are `Accent` (coldest tops in the overcast, not the bands); `HUR-A11` *(new, M16)* the inflow tracers spiral in: over 1 s the tracers that were not respawned turn counter-clockwise and move inward on average, and each trail point is a position its tracer held in the last 0.4 s.

### total_solar_eclipse  (Earth & Sky, M13, M15, M16)
- **Intent**: totality seen from the ground: on a black sky over a dim skyline, the last sunlight breaks into Baily's beads and a diamond ring, then the pearly corona blooms with long streamers and small pink prominences at the limb; then it runs in reverse.
- **Model**: geometry in solar radii about the Sun's centre. The Moon (radius 1.03 R☉) moves along a tilted chord through the Sun's centre; its offset is piecewise linear in the `Timeline` (fast in the partial phases, slow through the beads and totality). Uncovered fraction `F`: the exact circle–circle lens area of the Moon's mountain-top envelope; once the envelope covers the Sun, `F` is the light through the limb valleys. The limb is a seeded valley profile (360 samples, 14 valleys up to 0.02 R☉ deep): a valley shows a bead when its floor still dips inside the photosphere, by its penetration depth. Beads and the diamond (the brightest bead, flaring most when it is nearly alone) are glare splats (`Glow`, `halo_kernel` 2 and 3) centred 0.8 px outside the lunar limb and masked by the Moon's disc, so the diamond is a small bead beside an intact dark disc. Photosphere: `raster::disc_cover` of Sun and Moon, `stellar::limb_darkening` (its colour shift softened, √I, so the limb is warm rather than an orange ring) and `brightness_temperature`; partly covered rim pixels at either disc's edge fade as pale `Starlight` (anti-aliased by brightness, not by a redder colour, and never in the sky's blue: no cyan rim on the crescent). Corona, precomputed per pixel in `resize`: a power law `r^{−3.3}` (toned with `asinh`, knee 0.35, with no taper or cut-off: it thins into the sky's light as far as it reaches) modulated by 6–10 helmet streamers (angular Gaussians crowding the equator, longer there: the solar-minimum shape, tapering and bending toward the equator with height), polar plumes and fine radial striations; shown only as the photosphere's glare falls. Prominences: 3–6 small arches on the limb. Sky brightness grows with `F^0.8` (dimming toward deep twilight through the partial phases) plus glare round the uncovered Sun; the Moon a black silhouette closing in, its rim anti-aliased against the air, and in the partial phases the Moon's shadow darkens the side of the sky it comes from first (and leaves last); a sunset glow along the bottom, strongest in totality; low hills (`Skyline`) in silhouette; Venus, Jupiter and two bright stars come out one by one below `F` = 0.12, Venus first.
- **Motion**: `Timeline`: partial (8 s) → beads + diamond ring (4 s) → totality (14 s) → diamond ring + beads (4 s) → partial (8 s), symmetric.
- **Resize**: geometry in solar radii centred; corona and prominence buffers rebuilt; the limb profile reseeded.
- **Inks** *(M15, ADR-0018: M11's roles in flat steps, no halos; were M14's `Starlight`/`HAlpha`/`Blackbody` inks and a glare halo)*: the sky is black in every act (no glare round the uncovered Sun: M14's glare table, twilight air, sunset glow and `Ice` sky are removed). The corona, the beads' glare (their kernels radius 2 and, for the diamond, 3) and the crescent's partly covered rim pixels are summed as light and tone-mapped (linear to level 140, rolling off exponentially toward 245), then drawn in four flat steps: `Star` 230 from 170, `Star` 110 from 95, grey `Structure` 100 from 45, `Structure` 0 from 18, black below, so the corona steps down into black. Bead cores (toned ≥ 200) `Star` 255. The diamond ring: through both diamond-ring acts the last light is a white `Star` 255 bead of 3 pixels on the limb at the contact point (the Sun's limb straight away from the Moon's centre, 0.8 px out), with 8 short braille spikes (reach max(8 dots, 1.6 solar radii in dots): `Star` 230 along the axes, `Sun` 200 at 0.55 of that on the diagonals), kept off the Moon's disc; meanwhile the corona is held back one step, blooming fully in totality. Prominences `Accent` 200 (small pink arches; the accent). The photosphere is stepped like M11's exoplanet star: `Sun` 255 at the centre (limb-darkened brightness √I > 0.93), `Sun` 170 (> 0.8), an orange `Hot` 200 limb, and pixels more than 45 % lit at the crescent's edge also `Hot` 200 (no pale fringe). The hills are black under a 1-px `Dim` 60 skyline; Venus, Jupiter and two bright stars `Star` 255 or 110 on black. ASCII: the Sun, beads and corona by level (`raster::ascii_ramp_cell`). The corona and prominence tables are filled 2 pixel rows per frame while the corona is hidden (`F ≥ 0.01`), or all at once when it is needed sooner, so a resize costs only the land table (GEN-R19); the picture is the same either way.
- **Look** *(M16, GEN-R25; supersedes the M15 Inks and the per-pixel corona of Model)*: braille on black, no half blocks. The corona is long streamer threads that follow a dipole-like field (solar minimum): a dipole whose equator is the seeded `equator` angle, closed loops `r = L sin²θ` (θ the colatitude, `L` = 1.2, 1.45, 1.75, 2.05 and the cusp `L_max`, seeded 2.2–2.6 per side, so the two helmets differ) under each helmet, and per pole and side 9 open field lines from footpoint colatitudes `θ₀ = s·θ_c` (`θ_c` the last closed footpoint, `sin²θ_c = 1/L_max`; `s` evenly spread with a seeded jitter, three crowding the separatrix as the streamer stalk) that fan out along the separatrix, `θ(r) = s·θ_sep(r)`, `θ_sep = asin √(min(r, L_max)/L_max)`, and run radially beyond the cusp out to 4 R☉: polar plumes bending toward the equator, helmet streamers drawn to a point. Colour along a thread is its brightness, falling outward as `e = gain·vis·r^−2.2` (gain seeded 0.5–1.0 per line, ×1.6 on the stalk lines, ×1.3 on closed loops): `Star` 230 from 0.55, `Star` 190 from 0.25, `Star` 150 from 0.1, `Star` 120 from 0.035, not drawn below, so the corona blooms outward from the limb as the visibility `vis` rises and each thread steps down into black; in the diamond-ring acts one step darker. In totality (vis > 0.5) the Moon's limb is a thin `Star` 200 ring (0.6 dots outside it). Prominences are tiny dotted arches of `Accent` 200 dots (every other dot). The uncovered photosphere is a checkerboard of braille dots (half the dots) in the limb-darkening steps `Sun` 255 (√I > 0.93), `Sun` 170 (> 0.8) and `Hot` 200, so the Moon's black disc bites a crisp crescent out of it. Beads are small `Star` 255 dot discs (radius 0.6 + 1.4 × penetration/valley depth) 0.8 px beyond the lunar limb; the diamond ring a `Star` 255 disc of radius 2 dots at the contact point with the 8 braille spikes, reach max(16 dots, 1.6 solar radii in dots), the inner 40 % of the axis spikes `Star` 255. The hills are black under a skyline of `Dim` 60 dots (one per dot column); Venus, Jupiter and two bright stars `Star` 255 or 110 dots. Nothing is drawn on the Moon's disc.
- **Title**: `Total eclipse · Moon hides Sun`; captions `partial`, `diamond ring`, `totality`.
- **Stylisation**: totality lengthened relative to the partial phases (real partial phases last ~25× longer than totality; here 8 s against 14 s); the Moon's apparent motion is uneven, ~180× faster in the partial phases than through totality (really uniform); limb valleys ~10× real lunar relief; streamers procedural; the corona's power law steepened to `r^{−3.3}` so it reads on a terminal; a black daytime sky.
- **Acceptance**: `ECL-A1` the uncovered fraction is 1 at the start, 0 at mid-totality and symmetric in time (the smooth crescent exactly; the bead light, which differs between second and third contact, to within 0.005); `ECL-A2` beads appear only when the uncovered fraction is in (0, 0.005) and lie on the Moon's limb (within the valley depth); `ECL-A3` *(changed, M16)* the corona is drawn only while the uncovered fraction < 0.01 (its visibility is 0 above that, and no corona thread is drawn then); `ECL-A4` *(changed, M16)* corona brightness falls outward along every thread (its step never rises with `r`); `ECL-A5` captions follow the Timeline; `ECL-A6` *(changed, M16)* in totality consecutive segments of a thread differ by at most one corona step (black counting as the step below the faintest); `ECL-A7` *(changed, M16)* the sky's corner stays black through the partial phase, and no dot is lit on the Moon's disc (1.5 dots inside its limb) through the beads and diamond ring; `ECL-A8` the timing stylisation holds (8 s partial, 14 s totality, a partial-to-totality speed ratio of 150–210); `ECL-A9` *(changed, M16)* in the partial phase every photosphere dot is `Sun` 255, `Sun` 170 or `Hot` 200 and lies on the uncovered disc; the stalk threads reach 3.5 R☉ in totality (no cut-off); open threads bend toward the equator (their colatitude grows outward) and stay outside the closed loops. `ECL-A10` *(changed, M16)* black overhead: in every act the top quarter's corner cells are blank (but for the planets and stars), the land holds only its skyline (at most one lit cell per column below the horizon's top), and in totality between 1.3 and 3 R☉ every lit cell is a corona step, a prominence or a `Star`; `ECL-A11` *(changed, M16)* mid diamond-ring act (11 s) the diamond's bead and ≥ 4 braille spike cells show and no corona segment is the corona's brightest step, and in totality (20 s) there are no spikes; `ECL-A12` *(new, M16)* the corona threads follow the field: every closed-loop point satisfies `r = L sin²θ` (to 1e-9), every thread is one dot thick (≥ 85 % of its dots have at most 3 lit dots across it within ±2), and in totality the Moon's limb is a thin `Star` 200 ring.

### rainbow  (Earth & Sky, M13, M15, M16)
- **Intent** *(M15, ADR-0018)*: a rain shower lit by a low Sun behind the viewer, under a dark storm sky: a primary bow (red outside, the full spectrum to violet inside), a fainter secondary with reversed colours, black sky between them (Alexander's band), faint supernumerary ripples inside the primary, and drifting rain curtains that brighten and dim the bow along its arc, while the Sun behind the viewer passes in and out of cloud.
- **Model**: per pixel, `cos θ` from the antisolar point (per-column `cos az` and per-row `sin/cos el` tables, so a pixel costs two products and a look-up). A size-independent LUT over `cos θ` (8192 bins, built once): Descartes' minimum-deviation angles for k = 1 and k = 2 internal reflections (`cos² i = (n² − 1)/(k(k + 2))`, Cauchy `n(λ)` of water fitted at 404 and 700 nm) at 700 and 400 nm, their spread about 550 nm widened ×2.5 (primary) and ×1.15 (secondary), give each band's red and violet edges; between them an analytic angle → wavelength map (700 → 400 nm) gives smooth bands, with Gaussian shoulders beyond the red rim (0.14 band widths) and the violet edge (0.32), and a body weighted by the eye's spectral response. Supernumeraries: `sin²` ripples inside the violet spaced as Airy's first two maxima of `Ai(−z)²` (drop radius 0.5 mm, `z ∝ (a/λ)^{2/3} Δθ`), fading over 0.55 band widths, drawn as lightness only (a fringe a pixel or two wide would alias into coloured specks). The secondary at 0.45 of the primary. The sunlit rain's smooth light: bright inside the primary (brightest near it), dim outside the secondary, darkest between (Alexander's band). Rain curtains: two octaves of drifting value noise along `x − 0.15 y` (slanted with the rain), refreshed per drawn frame into a per-column table; they modulate the sunlit rain and so the bow along its arc (×0.55–1). *(M14: the anticrepuscular shafts, the cloud-base mottling and the rain streaks are removed, ADR-0017.)* Sun breaks: the sunlight on the rain is value noise in time (6 s cells) mapped to 0.4–1, dimming and brightening the whole bow and the sunlit rain. The bow's strength is its band light × 1.35 × (0.35 + 0.65 · min(1, sunlit rain / 0.6)), the sunlit rain being rain strength × Sun × curtain factor.
- **Motion**: the Sun sinks from 18° to 8° over a 50 s loop (the scene opens 12 % in, with the shower under way), so the bow rises; the rain swells and ebbs and thins at the loop's seam, so the Sun's jump back is never seen; curtains drift across, brightening and dimming the bow along its arc; the Sun breaks through and hides over seconds, brightening and dimming the whole bow.
- **Resize**: the LUT is size-independent; view tables, curtain table and hills rebuilt.
- **Inks** *(M15, ADR-0018: M11's saturated roles in flat steps; were M14's six named ramps and `Starlight` sky)*: the bow is drawn where its band's light exceeds 0.3 of its own peak (a property of the angle), in the nearest spectral role: orange-red `Hot` (≥ 610 nm), yellow `Sun` (≥ 570), green `Particle` (≥ 505), cyan `Wave` (≥ 475), blue `Cold` (≥ 435), violet `Accent`; its light `a = (1 − e^{−1.6x})/(1 − e^{−1.6})` of its strength `x` in three flat steps (230 from 0.8, 150 from 0.55, 70 below), and only where `200·a` is at least level 10 and above the sky's. The supernumeraries' pastel fringes (lightness only) and the sky: the sky's light `16·low²` (low = the pixel's depth toward the horizon) plus the sunlit rain's lift `8·sky light·sunlit rain` inside the primary and the supernumeraries' lift up to 18, in two dim flat steps, `Dim` 0 from level 10 and `Dim` 90 from 24, black below (black overhead and in Alexander's band). Hills black. ASCII: bow cells `+` (one pixel) or `#` (both); the sky is blank.
- **Look** *(M16, GEN-R25; a redesign that supersedes the Model's per-pixel LUT, the Motion and the Inks above where they disagree)*: two alternating acts on a looping `Timeline`, all braille dots on black. **Act 1, `inside a raindrop` (12 s)**: one big drop (a thin `Structure` 150 circle, radius min(0.3 × art height, 0.2 × width), centred at 62 % across and 40 % down) in faint parallel sunlight (dotted `Dim` 90 rays from the left that stop at the drop); a white `Star` ray enters at Descartes' impact parameter for the primary (`b = sin i`, `cos² i = (n² − 1)/3` at 550 nm, above the centre), is traced with Snell's law at the surface and mirror reflection inside (vector form), split into six colours (`Hot` 650, `Sun` 590, `Particle` 540, `Wave` 490, `Cold` 455, `Accent` 415 nm, level 230; water's dispersion about 550 nm widened ×4 so they part within one drop), one reflection, and out to the frame's edge, growing along its path from 0.5 s to 4 s; at 4.5 s a dashed `Dim` 120 guide along the antisolar direction from the exit (*round 2*: `Dim` 150), a dotted arc to the 550 nm ray and the label `42°` (ASCII `42d`); from 5.5 s the secondary the same way (entering below the centre, two reflections), its `51°` from 9.5 s; then both hold. **Act 2, `primary and secondary` (16 s)**: the sky above the hills (horizon at 90 % of the height, 46° of sky; *round 2*: was 40°, which clipped the secondary's top), every colour's bow a thin braille circle about the antisolar point (`Sun` elevation from the 50 s loop as before), traced in 360 steps through the az/el projection and clipped at the land: the primary (red outside) at the widened Descartes angles (dispersion ×2.5, secondary ×1.15), spread about the green arc where needed so neighbouring arcs are ≥ 4 dots apart (each colour keeps its own braille cells at any size); the secondary reversed; Alexander's dark band black; two dotted supernumerary arcs inside the primary's violet (`Particle` then `Accent`, level 70, spaced as Airy's maxima, ≥ 4 dots), drawn where the bow is above half strength. Each arc segment's level is the bow's light in three steps (230 from 0.8, 150 from 0.55, 70 below) of `BOW_FLOOR + (1 − BOW_FLOOR)·min(1, lit/0.6)` with `lit` = rain × Sun × (0.55 + 0.45 × the local curtain), ×0.62 for the secondary; so the curtains brighten and dim the bows along their arcs and the Sun breaks the whole bow. The land is a thin `Structure` 110 ridge line; rain streaks are short slanted `Dim` 60 dot lines (one per 30 cells, ≤ 400, 5 dots, 42–98 dots/s) where the curtain is dense, drawn last and only into empty cells. No half blocks; ASCII by dot count. *Round 2 (M16)*: inside the drop the six colours' paths nearly coincide and a braille cell shows one ink, so they are drawn a dot at a time, each offset 0.7 dot sideways by its colour, with the colour drawn last (the one a shared cell shows) rotating along the ray: the interior is a multicoloured ribbon rather than red.
- **Title**: `Rainbow · raindrops split light`; captions `inside a raindrop`, `primary and secondary` (*M16*).
- **Stylisation**: dispersion widened ×2.5 (primary) and ×1.15 (secondary); an analytic angle → wavelength band shape instead of summed Airy fringes; the bow's hues stepped through six semantic colours; supernumeraries lightness-only and ~2.5× wider apart than for real 0.5 mm drops; the storm sky drawn black; the framing shows 40° of sky above a horizon at 90 % of the height.
- **Acceptance**: `BOW-A1` at 589 nm the primary's minimum-deviation angle is 42.0 ± 0.5° and the secondary's 50.5–51.5°; `BOW-A2` *(changed, M16)* in the primary red is outside violet, and in the secondary the order is reversed: in the Descartes angles, in the drawn arcs' angles (at 40, 120 and 400 dots per radian) and in the rays traced through the drop; the primary's rim is `Hot`, its inner arc `Accent`; `BOW-A3` *(changed, M16)* the secondary is never brighter than the primary at the same light, and fainter at full light; `BOW-A4` *(changed, M16)* Airy's supernumerary maxima lie inside the primary, and the drawn supernumerary arcs lie inside the primary's violet arc by at least the arc gap; `BOW-A5` *(changed, M16)* at 120×40 in both acts every lit cell (labels aside) is a braille cell on black, with more than 40 bow cells, each bow cell at one of the three steps (70, 150, 230); `BOW-A6` *(changed, M16)* some column through both bows shows, top to bottom, the secondary's `Accent` … `Hot` then the primary's `Hot` … `Accent` (each colour on its own cells), and the six colours are the spectral roles of their wavelengths; `BOW-A7` *(changed, M16)* the bow's mean level in the left, middle and right thirds changes between 13 s and 21 s (the curtains move along the arc); `BOW-A8` *(changed, M16)* Alexander's band, between the primary's and the secondary's red arcs, holds no bow ink; `BOW-A9` *(changed, M16)* the Sun factor stays in [0.4, 1] and swings by > 0.4 within a minute and by > 0.3 within 10 s somewhere, and in the sky act at its dimmest the bows' summed level is < 90 % of that at its brightest; `BOW-A10` *(new, M16)* a ray at Descartes' impact parameter through the drop leaves at the bow angle (42° primary, 51° secondary, within 0.8°, equal to `bow_angle`), its surface events on the drop and heading down-left to the viewer; by 10 s the diagram shows all six colours, the white sunray and the labels `42°` and `51°`, and at 0.9 s no colour yet; `BOW-A11` *(new, M16)* the acts alternate (12 s drop, 16 s sky, looping) with their captions, and the drop shows only in act 1; `BOW-A12` *(new, M16)* at 120×40 and 160×45 at 10 s, no colour holds more than 40 % of the coloured cells inside the drop.

### meteor_shower  (Earth & Sky, M13, M15)
- **Intent**: a dark summer sky at the Perseids' peak: meteors flash in every part of the sky, yet every streak points back to one radiant; bright ones leave green glowing trains that linger and twist in the upper-atmosphere wind.
- **Model**: meteoroids share one velocity vector (the stream); each meteor is a straight path segment through the ablation layer (Perseids: begins 105–125 km, brighter ones higher; ends 80–95 km, brighter ones deeper), seen through a gnomonic (rectilinear) lens facing north, which keeps paths straight and sends them all back to one vanishing point, the radiant; paths seen near the radiant are foreshortened. The radiant is placed in frame (upper third, off to one side) and marked by a faint ring of 16 braille dots (`Ramp(Starlight)` 80) with a dim `radiant` label; 30 % of meteors start within 22° of it, short foreshortened streaks fanning out of it. Pool of 48 meteors; arrivals are a Poisson process (seeded `ctx.rng`, 2.4/s, slowly swelling and ebbing); magnitudes from a power law (population index 2.2, faintest drawn 3.5, clamped at −4); light curve `√(27/4 u²(1 − u))` along the path. Each streak is drawn in braille: a white-hot head (a 2 × 2 dot blaze) and a tail behind it over 40–75 % of the path (longer for bright meteors), two dots wide just behind the head, then one, thinning toward its end (hashed dots dropped, ever more of them), white-hot at the head (its last 12 %), green (`Ramp(Aurora)` 175–235, magnesium and oxygen) back to 45 % of the tail, then yellow (`Ramp(Sulfur)` 120–190, sodium and iron) to its end; a faint green glow a pixel or two wide is stroked along the bright half of the tail into the trains' layer (a thin anti-aliased thread: bilinear splats every half pixel). Finished tracks join a faint braille composite that fades in 3.5 s (as in a stacked photograph of a shower; ≤ 32 kept), so at any moment several recent tracks show, all pointing back to the radiant. Nothing is drawn over the land: streaks, heads, glare, glow and trains stop at the treeline (only the sky's anti-aliased edge shows below the land's top). Trains (pool 12) from meteors of magnitude ≤ 0.6: 24 points along the brightest part of the track that drift with a sheared wind (two sinusoidal layers, 3.5 and 2.2 km/s time-lapsed, of vertical wavelength 7 and 12.5 km), stroked each step into a second `Glow` as a thin anti-aliased thread one to two pixels wide, dimming as it spreads (time constant 0.8 s) and fading over 1.8–3.2 s (the O I 557.7 nm afterglow, time-lapsed), drawn at half their toned level so the streaks stay the hero. Background: fixed stars (`Blackbody`), a mottled Milky Way (peak 50: a broad glow plus a brighter spine 0.38 widths wide, knotted with star clouds by two octaves of value noise along and across it) with a dark rift and dust lanes (two octaves of value noise stretched along the band, darkening it by up to 80 % near its spine), horizon airglow (44 at the horizon, e-folding 9°) against which an uneven spruce ridge with clearings (`Skyline`, trees 1.4–5.2°, gaps 0.7–2.9°) stands with anti-aliased edges.
- **Motion**: several meteors a second; trains drift, twist and fade. The sky itself is fixed: the loop spans minutes of a night, and a 15°/h turn would only jitter single dots.
- **Resize**: lens, radiant and sky rebuilt; the shower restarts with a train already in the sky and a meteor due at once.
- **Inks** *(M15, ADR-0018: M11's roles in flat steps; were M13/M14's `Starlight`/`Aurora`/`Sulfur`/`Blackbody` inks)*: meteor heads `Star` 255 braille only (a 2 × 2-dot blaze; the half-block glare is gone, M15 review), tails green `Particle` then yellow `Sun` (braille), each in two steps (220 where the trail's `200·f^1.2·(0.5 + 0.5·taper)` reaches 100, 110 below, nothing under level 12), trains (when drawn) one faint `Particle` 30 step, stars `Star` points (200 for the brightest, 50 the rest), composite tracks two dim grey steps (`Structure` 0, 60 from half their peak), land black; the radiant ring (when drawn) `Accent`. ASCII: trains by level; each flying meteor's last stretch as `-/\|` in `Sun` with the head `*`.
- **Title**: `Meteor shower · comet dust burns`.
- **Stylisation**: rate far above a real ZHR (~100/h); paths drawn 5× longer than the ablation layer allows and flown ×3.5 faster so a streak reads on a terminal; streak colours simplified to white, green and yellow; trains brightened and time-lapsed; the radiant marked.
- **M14 geometry** (kept at M15; supersedes the Milky Way and airglow, the wake glow and the radiant mark): a black sky with a sparse starfield (the land is black, stars stop at its top); no sky pass at resize. Each meteor is a 1-dot braille streak, every dot drawn (no dithered thinning): the head white-hot (its 2 × 2-dot blaze the brightest thing), the trail stepping down from the head (`f` = 1) to black at its end, green then yellow past `WAKE_SPLIT`; no half-block wake glow. Persistent trains are modelled but not drawn (they read as blocky smears). Stars are neutral points (one in 15 brighter), no crosses. The rate is 4 per second (was 2.4) and the entry speed ×2.2 (was ×3.5), so a streak stays on screen longer and a frame is rarely empty. The radiant's ring and label are not drawn.
- **Acceptance**: `MET-A1` every meteor's track, extended backward, passes within 1° of the radiant; `MET-A2` meteors near the radiant are shorter on screen than those 60° away (mean ratio < 0.5); `MET-A3` magnitude counts follow the population index within ±20 % over 1000 samples; `MET-A4` trains fade monotonically and drift (and bend: the wind is sheared); `MET-A5` the pools never exceed capacity; `MET-A6` every meteor begins 105–125 km up and (its drawn path divided by the stretch) ends 80–95 km up; `MET-A7` the radiant projects inside the frame above the horizon (it is not marked, M14), and rendered streak cells are only white-hot `Star`, green `Particle` and yellow `Sun` (besides the `Star` points, grey `Structure` tracks and the `Accent` ring), both tail colours appearing *(changed, M15)*; `MET-A8` trains are thin (a stroked thread lights ≤ 2 pixel rows), short-lived (life ≤ 4.5 s), faint (`Particle` ≤ 128, *changed, M15*) and never drawn over the land (M14: not drawn at all).

### noctilucent_clouds  (Earth & Sky, M13, M15)
- **Intent**: late in a northern summer night, around midnight: the landscape and low clouds are dark, but electric-blue clouds glow on a black sky, rippled with soft waves, still in sunlight at 83 km, over a thin warm glow on the northern horizon.
- **Model**: geometry: the Sun at depression `d` below the northern horizon; a point of the 83 km layer seen at elevation `e` and azimuth `a` (spherical Earth) is lit when its ray toward the Sun clears the Earth plus a 20 km screening layer (ozone and haze), softened over 4 km; tropospheric clouds (≤ 12 km) never clear it. Overhead the layer is in shadow, so the lit clouds form a band above the northern horizon. The cloud's density: 6 drifting gravity-wave packets (2 long smooth "bands", λ 35–55 km; 4 "billows", λ 9–16 km, soft crests `0.35 + 0.65 (1 − |w|)^1.5` on a sheet of ice rather than thin lines on dark gaps) whose envelopes wander to and fro about their centres (12 s of drift at 14 km/s, 70 s period, so the field fills the sky all loop and never drifts away), times a patchy drifting value-noise envelope, projected onto the sky; brightness × forward scattering (brightest toward the Sun) × slant path. Evaluated on a lattice capped at 8500 nodes (ADR-0012) from per-node `cos/sin(k·x)` tables (so a node costs two products per packet), then sampled bilinearly to pixels (`SampleMap`); a packet's waves fade out where the lattice resolves fewer than 4–8 nodes per wavelength (far off, near the horizon), so they never alias into stripes. As the Sun sinks the ice's contrast against the darker sky rises (×1.8 at the deepest, stylised), so the late band stays bright; over the lowest degrees (2.8–6.4°) the ice fades out before the warm glow (long, reddened air path). Twilight sky: one smooth lightness (an arch brightest low toward the Sun's azimuth, e-folding 5–9°, plus a horizon glow peaking at 72 and e-folding 3°) whose hue runs by arch elevation from warm `Ember` (< 4.4°, its top an uneven soft edge: a slow value-noise wave of ±0.9° along the horizon plus a ±0.25° per-pixel fray) to deep teal `Aurora` (its dark end, < 11°) to indigo `Plasma` overhead, the bands meeting at equal lightness. One to three long, soft stratus wisps (thickness wandering along them by value noise, hiding ≤ 55 % of the glow) and low boreal hills with sparse spruce (the shared `Skyline`) in silhouette.
- **Motion**: wave packets drift and interfere (phase speed time-lapsed); `d` deepens from 8.2° to 9.8° and recovers over 80 s (the midnight Sun skims below the northern horizon), so the lit band sinks toward the horizon and rises again, while the Sun's azimuth swings from north-west to north-east.
- **Resize**: lattice, view and cover rebuilt; wave packets in km (reseeded).
- **Inks**: black sky. The ice is sampled Catmull-Rom from the lattice (CORE-R102, one row of taps per pixel row) and stretched so the field's brightest node reaches 0.6 (at most ×2.5), so the clouds keep an area in every act; above a floor (0.08) each picture pixel's four braille dots light by a fixed hash against its density `u/0.45` (full from `u` = 0.45), in two flat electric-blue steps *(M15, ADR-0018; was M14's `Ice` 160–205)*: `Wave` 90 where the sheet is thin, `Wave` 220 where its dot density reaches 110 of 255 (the crests), thinning into black. The warm glow is a few pixels over the land's top (so it runs on unbroken behind the hills): its strength `g = e^{−up/1.6 px}`, stronger toward the Sun, its top uneven along the horizon (±0.9°), in three flat orange steps *(M15; was `Blackbody` 1800 K)*: `Hot` 200 from 0.55, `Hot` 90 from 0.28, `Hot` 15 from 0.12, black below. A few faint stars `Star` 50 or 150; land and low clouds black. ASCII: the ice by dot count.
- **Title**: `Noctilucent clouds · sunlit ice`.
- **Stylisation**: wave field procedural; lit/unlit edge softened; time-lapse ×30.
- **Acceptance**: `NLC-A1` the lit test: a point at 83 km is lit at depression 10° for elevations below the computed shadow line (toward the Sun) and unlit above it; a point at 10 km is unlit for any depression > 1°; `NLC-A2` as `d` increases the lit area shrinks monotonically; `NLC-A3` NLC pixels appear only where lit; `NLC-A4` the wave field moves (non-zero change over 1 s); `NLC-A5` *(changed, M15)* the picture holds only black and the `Hot` glow; the ice is `Wave` braille in its two steps, the bright crest step among them; `NLC-A6` ice pixels (dotted) cover > 4 % of the picture at every 5 s through the whole 80 s loop; `NLC-A7` *(changed, M15)* the sky is black over a `Hot` horizon glow at most 8 px deep, in more than 20 columns at 120×40; the warm glow's top edge varies along the horizon within ±0.9°.

### star_trails  (Earth & Sky, M13, M15)
- **Intent**: a long-exposure photograph building before your eyes: concentric coloured arcs grow around the celestial pole above a treeline, blue and orange stars drawing arcs of their own colour, the pole star a tiny bright circle.
- **Model**: stars (≤ 2500, uniform on the cap of sky the frame can show, magnitudes `N(< m) ∝ 10^{0.5 m}` to 6.0, `Blackbody` from a naked-eye spectral mix via `ink::class_kelvin`, saturated away from the 6500 K white point as in a processed photograph) at fixed positions; Polaris 0.7° from the pole. The camera is a rectilinear (gnomonic) lens centred on the pole, which stands at the seeded latitude (35–50°): a star `ρ` from the pole is `f·tan ρ` from it on screen, so every trail is an exact circle about the pole, and the horizon (a great circle) is a straight row `f·tan φ` below it. Earth turns 15.04°/h, time-lapsed ×800, so a 45 s exposure sweeps ~150° arcs. The exposure is a per-dot buffer (the hottest trail's `Blackbody` level per braille dot, allocated once per size): each step paints the dots each star crossed since the last (stars brighter than magnitude 1.5 two dots wide). Stars fainter than magnitude 4.4 draw no braille line: their light goes into a soft `Glow` exposure (bilinear splats along the arc, an even light per unit of arc so the slow stars round the pole do not pile up) toned onto the sky, a dim haze of rings under the few bright coloured trails: a brightness hierarchy by level. The scene opens 10 s into an exposure, so the first frame already shows arcs. After 45 s the trails fade out over 5 s (a hashed share of dots dropped each step) and a new exposure starts. Stars below the treeline (the shared `Skyline`, `src/shared/skyline.rs`: near spruces, anti-aliased) are hidden, and no trail dot is drawn over a land pixel (the paint-time test sees one height per dot column, which can slip between two spires).
- **Motion**: arcs lengthen continuously; circumpolar stars sweep round the pole; others rise and set behind the trees.
- **Resize**: projection rebuilt; the exposure is cleared and restarts. *(M14, GEN-R19)* The opening 10 s of exposure is painted in 0.1 s strides (was 1/60 s): a bright trail is the max of the dots it crosses and a faint one gathers light per dot of arc, so the picture does not depend on the stride, and a resize at 500×150 takes ~7 ms (was 15).
- **Inks** *(M15, ADR-0018: M11's roles in flat steps; were M14's `Blackbody` trails and `Starlight` haze)*: bright trails (magnitude ≤ 4.4) in braille dots drawn straight from the exposure buffer (the brightest thicker), coloured by the star's temperature (the buffer keeps the hottest trail's blackbody level, a model quantity): orange `Hot` 210 below 4300 K, yellow `Sun` 210 below 5500 K, white `Star` 220 below 7200 K, cyan-white `Wave` 200 below 11 000 K, blue `Cold` 230 above; faint trails (magnitude > 4.4) as dim `Dim` 150 braille dots on the same dot lattice as the bright trails (each dot lit where its fixed hash falls below its pixel's toned faint light, `0.36·level` faded with the exposure, none below 12; a cell with a bright trail takes the trail's colour), so the sky stays black between the arcs; the only half-block sky is the barely-lit horizon, one flat `Dim` 0 step (base level ≥ 12, ≤ 22, e-folding 2.5°); trees black; trail colours saturated ×1.15. ASCII: braille dot counts (`.:+#`); the skyglow is not drawn.
- **Title**: `Star trails · Earth turns`.
- **Stylisation**: time-lapse; a stacked exposure; star colours saturated; bright trails carry colour, faint ones only light; faint-trail exposure is even along the arc.
- **Acceptance**: `TRL-A1` every trail point stays at a constant distance from the pole (± 0.5 px), hence a constant angular distance; `TRL-A2` arc length in angle is the same for all stars (15.04°/h × time-lapse × exposure ≈ 150°) and on screen ∝ the projected pole distance `f·tan ρ`; `TRL-A3` stars with declination > 90° − latitude never cross the horizon; `TRL-A4` the exposure buffer stays allocated once (no growth) and resets at the loop; `TRL-A5` *(changed, M15)* braille trail inks are the five temperature steps or the faint `Dim` dots (both present); `TRL-A6` the sky is black at the top (the sky's pixel layer is black above the horizon glow), barely lit just above the land (≤ 24), and no braille trail cell lies over land; `TRL-A7` *(new, M15)* at 120×40 and 20 s the sky's half-block layer is black in the upper half of the frame (no haze between the arcs).

### lenia  (Living Systems, M13, M16)
- **Intent**: smooth, glowing creatures that look alive: Orbium gliders swim across a dark field, bump, and a damage beat tears one apart, after which it regrows its shape.
- **Model**: Lenia (Chan 2019) on a capped periodic lattice (≤ 96×64, `lattice_for`): `A ← clip(A + Δt·G(K * A))`, ring kernel `K` of radius R ≤ 10 (static offset/weight list, ≤ 320 taps), growth `G(u) = 2 e^{−(u−μ)²/(2σ²)} − 1` with Orbium parameters (μ = 0.15, σ = 0.015, Δt = 0.1); seeds from a static pattern table (Orbium). Stepping at 12 Hz from the fixed 60 Hz update (every 5th update) with the field linearly interpolated between steps for display; the stepping frame is benched as the worst frame. Damage beat every ~25 s: a disc of cells zeroed; watchdog reseeds if total mass leaves [0.3, 3]× the seed's.
- **Motion**: gliders drift ~1 cell per step; shimmer from interpolation.
- **Resize**: lattice regridded (ADR-0012); on too-small lattices one glider is reseeded.
- **Inks**: `Ramp(Aurora)` (bioluminescent look, false colour) via `Pixels`. ASCII by level.
- **Title**: `Lenia · life from smooth rules`.
- **Stylisation**: artificial life (no organism is modelled; Living Systems by ADR-0013's artificial-life clause).
- **M15 look** (ADR-0018; supersedes the Inks above and M14's `Ramp(Ember)`): the creature as a `Seq` field in four flat steps, like M11's `gray_scott`: `v` ≥ 0.03 → 60 (a violet membrane), ≥ 0.1 → 125 (red), ≥ 0.3 → 185 (orange), ≥ 0.6 → 240 (a yellow core); black below.
- **Look** *(M16, GEN-R25; supersedes the M15 look and the Inks above)*: no fills: each creature is nested iso-level threads of the displayed field: the current world step's field, cross-faded and birth-dimmed as before, built per lattice node *(changed, M16 round 2: no longer interpolated between the last two 12 Hz steps, whose blend of two shifted creatures drew every edge twice)*, traced by marching squares (`Dots::contour`, CORE-R105) on the lattice (fitted to half-block-sized squares, 2×2 dots, as before): `v = 0.12` a `Wave` 210 membrane and `v = 0.6` a `Particle` 250 core *(round 2: two levels, was three: 0.12 Wave 170, 0.4 Wave 235, 0.75 Particle 250)*, one dot thick on black, about four loops per creature. No wake *(round 2: removed; a trail dim and short enough to stay quiet never cleared the membrane of a creature this slow)*; the gliders now move in 12 Hz steps. ASCII falls back by braille dot count. LNA-A9 counts lit creature-thread cells (≥ 8; was ≥ 20 lit pixels).
- **Acceptance**: `LNA-A1` the Orbium seed on an empty lattice survives 30 s with mass within ±15 % of its start; `LNA-A2` its centre of mass moves (≥ 5 cells in 30 s); `LNA-A3` after the damage beat mass recovers to ≥ 80 % within 10 s; `LNA-A4` the stepping frame at 200×50 is < 2 ms (bench); `LNA-A5` values stay in [0, 1]; `LNA-A6` the staged engulf ends with one creature holding 40–60 % of the pair's mass at 6 s and 10 s, at several lattice sizes and headings; `LNA-A7` the staged glance leaves two creatures with ≥ 90 % of the mass at 8 s; `LNA-A8` a lost glider is replaced after the birth delay, not before (Round 3); `LNA-A9` no frame is empty: over two full act loops at 80×24 and 120×40, sampled every 0.25 s, every frame shows lit creature pixels (Round 4); `LNA-A10` *(changed, M16)* and `LNA-A11` *(new, M16; changed, round 2)* the creature is nested threads: the two levels rise with brighter inks (`Wave`, `Particle`); at 120×40 after 3 s both are on screen, nothing is a half-block or has a background, and under 5 % of the lit dots have all four dot neighbours lit (no fills); `LNA-A12` *(new, M16 round 2)* the threads trace one world step, not a blend of two: with no cross-fade or birth in flight the drawing is the same at 0.1 and 0.9 of the way to the next step.
- **As built (batch 5)**: `R = 13` (the published Orbium table, un-resampled; at `R = 10` the resampled Orbium is lumpy and dies from most bites), so the kernel has ≤ 560 taps (LNA-A4 bounds taps × nodes, ≤ 6144 nodes); up to 3 gliders (one per 2000 nodes). The damage beat is a bite of radius 4 nodes centred 8 nodes from the heaviest node in a random direction (a disc through the core kills an Orbium); besides the reseed band, a glider that dies in a collision or bite is replaced by a new one opposite the heaviest creature.
- **Round 2**: a shoal of up to 3 gliders shares one heading (no mirroring), so they swim in parallel and never merge; a dead glider is replaced in the emptiest place; bites every 12 s; every 50 s the dish fades (1.5 s) and a new shoal on a new heading hatches. Inks: the thin membrane `Ramp(Ice)`, the dense core `Ramp(Aurora)` (two hues, one luminous body).
- **Round 3**: acts of 30 s cycle shoal → engulf → shoal → glance. *Engulf*: two gliders on courses a quarter turn apart, aimed to meet 2.5 s after hatching with an 8-node sideways offset: one glider absorbs the other and a single clean Orbium swims on (LNA-A6). *Glance*: opposite courses offset 12 nodes: they brush past and both survive (LNA-A7). The meetings are deterministic (Lenia has no noise), so these set-ups were chosen once by trial and the whole configuration turns with the heading; they are staged only on lattices ≥ 48 (engulf) / ≥ 56 (glance) nodes a side, else the act is a shoal. A glider lost to a meeting or a bite is replaced by a **birth** 3 s later in the emptiest place, fading in over 1.2 s (display only) (LNA-A8). The watchdog's upper bound drops to 1.6× the seeded mass, so a runaway merger is cleared early. Inks (warmer, distinct): the membrane and halo `Ramp(Eosin)` (rose), the dense core `Ramp(Ember)` (amber to pale gold). (acceptance IDs added to the Acceptance line).
- **Round 4**: the dish never goes dark. The fade-to-black between acts is gone; every reseed (act change or watchdog) cross-fades instead: the outgoing creatures are copied into a ghost world that keeps stepping while it dissolves, the new act condenses over the first second and the ghost fades over the second (display only; the ghost costs one extra world step per stepping frame for 2 s) (LNA-A9).

### firefly_sync  (Living Systems, M13, M18)
- **Intent**: a mangrove riverbank at night: thousands of fireflies flash at random, then patches fall into step, and within a minute the whole tree pulses as one.
- **Model**: Mirollo–Strogatz pulse-coupled oscillators (600, positions on a seeded tree-silhouette mask); phase `φ` advances at `1/T` (T ≈ 0.6 s ± 5 %, the *Pteroptyx* rhythm of ~0.5–0.6 s); on crossing 1 a firefly flashes and neighbours within radius r (via `SpatialHash`) get their state bumped by ε through the concave charging curve `f(φ)`, absorbing when pushed past 1. Order parameter `R = |⟨e^{2πiφ}⟩|` shown as a small right-aligned inset sparkline. Every ~45 s a disturbance (a boat's wake of light) scrambles phases.
- **Motion**: flashes; sync spreads from clusters; order parameter rises to ~1.
- **Resize**: positions kept in mask units; hash rebuilt.
- **Inks** (M15, ADR-0018: M11's roles in flat steps; were a toned `Ramp(Sulfur)` glow, a `Starlight` outline and an `Ember` lantern): a black night. The tree is only its silhouette's 1-px outline (the leafy crown's edge, trunks and prop roots down to the waterline) in `Dim` 70; no sky, moon, stars, water, reflection or R-sparkline. Flashes are the accent, `Particle` green (the yellow-green of *Pteroptyx*): one firefly in every `⌈8 · canopy px / N⌉` is drawn, each a single pixel, at 255 for the first 0.16 s after its flash and 110 until 0.3 s (the afterglow), then dark. Sync is never perfect: 5 laggard fireflies show as dim `Particle` 60 points between the pulses, so the synchronised tree is dark but never blank (FLY-A6). The boat is a `Sun` 255 lantern point on the waterline with a short flat `Hot` 90 wake. The readout is `R 0.93` alone, right-aligned. ASCII: flash `*`, afterglow and outline `.`, lantern `*`, wake `-`.
- **Title**: `Fireflies · flashes fall in step`.
- **Stylisation**: behavioural rule (the flash reset), not photochemistry; ranges and coupling tuned so sync takes ~30 s.
- **Acceptance**: `FLY-A1` without coupling the order parameter stays below 0.3; `FLY-A2` with coupling it exceeds 0.9 within 60 s for 3 seeds; `FLY-A3` a flash resets a firefly's phase to 0; `FLY-A4` the `R` readout stays right of the title span with a gap (GEN-R13); `FLY-A5` the scramble lowers `R` below 0.4; `FLY-A6` *(changed, M15)* a flash is bright (the 255 step) at first, a step dimmer in its afterglow, and has left the screen by half a period, so a synchronised crown is dark between pulses. `FLY-A7` *(changed, M15)* over 8 s at 120×40, seed 42, every lit pixel is the outline (`Dim` 70), a flash, afterglow or laggard (`Particle` at one of its three steps) or the lantern or its wake, and a flash peaks above 190.
- **As built (batch 5)**: coupling radius 0.06 mask units (≈ 18 neighbours; the mask is 2 × 1), `ε = 0.01`, which syncs in 12–31 s over seeds; flashes last ≈ 0.12 s. The boat's lantern crosses the water in 5 s every 45 s (first at 45 s); a firefly it passes gets a random phase and ignores its neighbours for 7 s (startled fireflies flash on their own), which is what drops `R` below 0.4: without it the synchronised majority re-absorbs them at once. The inset is a 16-sample sparkline of `R` (one per second) left of the readout `R 0.93`, both right-aligned on row `h − 1`. No captions.
- **Round 2**: a moonlit scene: moon disc and halo, foliage as dim `Ramp(Aurora)` clumps with moonlit crown edges (not black), water with ripples and a glitter path under the moon; each flash leaves a 0.6 s afterglow (0.18 of the flash), so a wave of flashes leaves a visible wake as sync spreads.
- **Round 3**: `T` = 0.6 s (was 1 s; the model and FLY-A2 hold unchanged, syncing faster in wall time). Each firefly is a one-pixel lamp with a radius-1 halo, light `e^{−s/0.08} + 0.12·e^{−s/0.3}` (FLY-A6): between flashes the crown is dark, so the synchronised flash is a whole-tree pulse. Where the canopy has fewer than 3 pixels per firefly (80×24) only every k-th firefly is drawn. The crown is a leafy silhouette (ellipse edge frayed by value noise, clump and fine leaf texture, moonward rim light); the three trunks are solid and tapered, and the prop roots fan outward like umbrella ribs (no closed loops). The moon is a soft-edged disc with faint maria in an exponential glow. The water carries a rippled, squashed reflection of the flashes (its top row mirrors the crown's underside at y = 0.46, 1.4 rows per water row; artistic, the true mirror image would fall off screen).
- **Look** *(M18, GEN-R26, flash puffs + coupling bonds; supersedes the half-block outline, flash pixels and FLY-A7 above)*: a black night. The tree is its silhouette outline as a 1-px `Dim` 70 braille thread. One firefly in every `⌈28 · canopy px / N⌉` is drawn (≈ 55 at 120×40). A flash is a small round stipple atom (`Dots::stipple_puff`, σ 1.0 dots, peak 0.95; each firefly's fixed-threshold salt is the roundest of 32 seeded candidates, chosen in `resize`, with at least 3 dots) in `Particle` 255 for the first 0.16 s, then an afterglow (peak 0.55, a subset of the same dots) at 110 until 0.3 s, then dark (FLY-A6). Laggards are single `Particle` 60 dots. When a drawn firefly flashes, one 1-px `Dim` 90 bond lights for 0.2 s to a drawn coupling neighbour at least 4 dots away that its pulse reached: one it absorbed, else one it bumped, else one already flashing in the same step (locked in step). The bond stops 1.5σ short of each atom, at most one per flash, from a fixed pool of 256, and is drawn beneath the atoms, so a synchronised flash is a constellation of atoms joined by fine bonds. A phase circle sits right-aligned directly above the `R` readout (radius 10 dots, `Dim` 50 ring dotted at about every third dot); each drawn firefly is a `Particle` 200 hand at angle `2πφ` (clockwise from twelve o'clock) with a 3-dot `Particle` 110 tail, so bunched fireflies sweep round as one bright arc; drawn only when `w ≥ 60`, `h ≥ 16` and it clears the tree. The boat's lantern is a small `Sun` 255 atom with a 1-px `Hot` 90 wake on the waterline.
- **Acceptance** *(M18)*: `FLY-A8` frames are braille-only (≤ 3 % half-block cells) at 120×40 and 200×50; a fresh flash is a `Particle` 255 puff of at least 3 dots within 3σ of the firefly; `FLY-A9` a bond joins a drawn flasher to one drawn neighbour within the coupling radius and at least 4 dots away that its pulse reached (absorbed, else bumped, else already flashing in step), at most one per flash, and is gone 0.2 s later; `FLY-A10` each phase-circle dot lies at angle `2πφ` of its firefly (within one dot), and the dots' circular spread falls as `R` rises; `FLY-A11` over 8 s at 120×40, seed 42, every lit dot is the outline, a flash, afterglow, laggard, bond, the phase circle, the lantern and its wake, or the label.

### heart_fibrillation  (Physics of Life, M13, M18)
- **Intent**: a heart's electrical activity as a phase map: at first regular waves spread from the pacemaker; a premature beat breaks a wave into a spinning spiral (re-entry), which splits into many chaotic spirals (fibrillation); an ECG strip at the bottom turns from neat beats to chaos; a defibrillator shock resets everything.
- **Model**: `excitable` (shared `pub(crate)`): Aliev–Panfilov two-variable model on a heart-shaped mask (capped ≤ 160×80), explicit Euler with sub-steps inside 1/60 s, no-flux boundaries at the mask edge; spiral breakup imposed by patchy refractoriness (`ε` scaled ×0.2 to ×2 in smooth random patches during the VF act), not by restitution alone. Sinus rhythm: periodic stimulus at the sinoatrial corner; re-entry: an S1–S2 cross-field stimulus; VF: breakup; defibrillation: set all cells excited then recover. Phase `θ = atan2(v − v*, u − u*)` for display; phase singularities (points where the phase winds by 2π) marked. ECG: a pseudo-ECG (sum of `∇u·∇(1/r)` at an electrode point) scrolling in the bottom rows above the title.
- **Motion**: `Timeline`: sinus (12 s) → re-entry (10 s) → VF (14 s) → shock and recovery (6 s).
- **Resize**: mask and lattice rebuilt (ADR-0012); the state resets to sinus.
- **Inks** *(M15, ADR-0018: M11's roles in flat steps; supersedes the M14 look below)*: the organ is black. The excitation shows only as its fresh front, a 1-px `Hot` line on the `u = ½` contour (the pixels within 1.05 px of it by `|u − ½|/|∇u|` × pixels per node, `∇u` by central differences each drawn frame): 255 while fresh (`v < ⅓`), 150 after, gone once `v ≥ 1`, so a wave's back and the refractory tail draw nothing. The heart outline is the 1-px rim band (pixels at least half on it) in `Dim` 110; the organ is the pixels at least half inside the curve (no anti-aliasing). The shock flashes the organ `Star` 200 for its first 0.125 s. Spiral tips are single `Star` 255 pixels. The ECG is a 1-dot `Particle` (monitor green) trace, its newer half 160 and older half 90, led by a small `Star` 255 write-head disc (radius 1.1 dots). ASCII: tips `@`, front `#`, flash `*`, rim `.`.
- **Title**: `Fibrillation · spirals break up`; captions `sinus rhythm`, `re-entry`, `fibrillation`, `defibrillation`.
- **Stylisation**: a 2-D sheet shaped like a heart, not a 3-D ventricle; the model is phenomenological; breakup into fibrillation comes from imposed patchy refractoriness (`ε` ×0.2–×2), not from restitution alone; the pseudo-ECG is qualitative.
- **M14 look** (superseded by the M15 Inks above; supersedes the posterised Ember/Cobalt cycle, the ECG strip fill and the thick outline): the organ is black. The excitation shows only as its fresh front, a thin anti-aliased `Ramp(Ember)` line on the `u = ½` contour (full within 0.6 px, gone at 1.3 px of `|u − ½|/|∇u|` × pixels per node, `∇u` by central differences each drawn frame), at `255·√(1 − v)`, so the freshest front's core is near white for a pixel and a wave's back and the refractory tail draw nothing. The heart outline is the 1-px rim band in `Ramp(Starlight)` 40. The ECG is a 1-dot `Ramp(Aurora)` trace at 160 → 70 with age, led by a small white write-head disc (radius 1.1 dots), no glow. Spiral tips are single white pixels.
- **Acceptance**: `HRT-A1` a single stimulus on resting tissue produces one wave that leaves the tissue at rest again; `HRT-A2` the S1–S2 protocol produces a phase singularity that persists ≥ 3 s; `HRT-A3` in the VF act the singularity count is ≥ 4 on average; `HRT-A4` after the shock the count is 0 within 1 s; `HRT-A5` u and v stay finite and in range for all sub-steps; `HRT-A6` *(changed, M15)* the outline is a flat 1-px rim band (under a fifth of the organ's pixels) and, 3 s into re-entry at 120×40, the front shows as > 20 fresh `Hot` 255 pixels while every other lit pixel is the stale front, the rim, a tip or the flash; `HRT-A7` the ECG cell at the write head carries the head ink (Round 3).
- **As built (batch 5)**: breakup comes from patchy refractoriness, not restitution alone: in the fibrillation act `ε` is scaled per node by smooth random patches (×0.2 to ×2), which breaks the re-entrant spiral within ≈ 4 s into 7–16 tips at 200×50 (Aliev–Panfilov `k = 8, a = 0.1, ε₀ = 0.002, μ₁ = 0.2, μ₂ = 0.3, D = 0.5`, `dt = 0.05` tu, 40 tu/s). The lattice is the square view over the heart (≤ 12 800 nodes); the node spacing keeps the heart 120 space units tall (≤ 1.6 per node, so small terminals hold fewer spirals). Sinus beats every 1.5 s from the upper-left lobe; re-entry is the S1–S2 cross-field protocol (S1 at the left edge, S2 on the recovered lower half once S1's tail crosses mid-heart); the shock sets every node to `u = 1, v = 0`. ECG: 4 (or 2) rows above the title, a sweeping braille trace. Readout `spiral tips N` right-aligned on row `h − 1`.
- **Round 2 (inks changed)**: the phase map is drawn in two saturated ramps instead of `Role::Phase`: excited tissue `Ramp(Ember)` (white-gold at the fresh front, orange on the plateau), the refractory tail `Ramp(Cobalt)` fading to near-black resting tissue (`Starlight` 20) with a one-pixel rim; the outline is the analytic heart curve at pixel resolution. The ECG is AC-coupled (0.15 s baseline) and drawn 3 braille dots thick in `Ramp(Aurora)` (monitor green) over a dim glow.
- **Round 3**: the heart outline is antialiased: per view pixel the curve's coverage and a ~1.3 px rim band (the heart minus a copy shrunk about its centre) are 4×4 supersampled once per resize; every tissue ink is dimmed by coverage and the resting rim is `Starlight` 80 × band coverage (HRT-A6). The ECG trace peaks at level 205 and is led by a white write head (`Starlight` 255, a braille disc of radius 2.1 dots) with a green halo in the pixels behind it (HRT-A7). (acceptance IDs added to the Acceptance line).
- **Look** *(M18, GEN-R26, excitation cloud + tip-meander threads; supersedes the M15 Inks and HRT-A6 where they disagree)*: the organ is black. Each wave is a two-lobed probability cloud on the braille lattice, sampled bilinearly from the lattice: the excited tissue a `Hot` 200 stipple, `p = 0.4·u²·(1 − τ/0.15 s)²` for `u > 0.15`, where τ is the time since the node's upstroke (`u` rising through ½; re-armed below 0.3), so freshly depolarised tissue is densest and each wave is a crescent hugging its front (`v` cannot tell fresh tissue from old: it barely rises on the plateau), and the recovering refractory tail a sparse `Cold` 150 stipple, `p = 0.12·min(1, v/v_max)` where `u ≤ 0.15` and `v > 0.4·v_max`, `v_max = 2.0` (the opposite lobe), fixed per-dot thresholds (CORE-R107, one salt per lobe). The fresh front (`u = ½`, `v < ⅓`) is a 1-px `Sun` 255 thread (the M15 front band test on the dot lattice). Each spiral tip is a small `Star` 255 atom (`Dots::stipple_puff`, σ 0.8 dots) trailing its last 0.9 s of positions, smoothed by an exponential moving average (τ = 0.15 s), as a 1-px `Dim` thread, 110 for the newer half and 60 for the older (a fixed ring per tip slot, matched to the nearest raw tip of the previous count, ≤ 3 dots or 1.5 lattice nodes per count, one missed count bridged, else the trail restarts), so fibrillation paints delicate curling, attractor-like rosettes. The heart outline is a 1-px `Dim` 110 braille thread along the analytic curve; the shock flashes the organ as a dense `Star` 200 stipple (`p = 0.5`) for its first 0.125 s. The ECG trace, write head and readout stay as in M15. *As built (M18)*: each dot stores one fixed threshold byte (CORE-R107); the refractory lobe and the shock flash use that byte XOR 0xA5; the front is the dots within 0.75 dots of `u = ½`; tips are counted every 27 sub-steps (about once per drawn frame); ECG samples are stored scaled at write time, so the trace's history never rescales.
- **Acceptance** *(M18)*: `HRT-A8` frames are braille-only (≤ 3 % half-block cells) at 80×24 and 200×50, t = 5 and 30 s; `HRT-A9` the excited lobe's lit share rises with `u` at fixed upstroke age (a window at `u ≈ 0.9` has at least 3× the lit share of one at `u ≈ 0.3`), every lit `Hot` field dot has `u > 0.15`, and every lit `Cold` field dot has `u ≤ 0.15` and `v > 0.25·v_max`; `HRT-A10` every detected tip is a `Star` atom centred within 1 dot of it, and a tip's trail is a connected thread of its own recent positions no older than 0.9 s; `HRT-A11` 3 s into re-entry at 120×40 the fresh front shows as > 20 `Sun` 255 dots and every lit dot is a lobe, the front, a tip or trail, the outline, the ECG or the label.

### motility_clustering  (Physics of Life, M13)
- **Intent**: a drop of swimming bacteria: each swims straight and then turns; where swimmers collide they block each other, and crowds jam into clusters that grow and merge while a gas of free swimmers streams around them.
- **Model**: active Brownian particles (≤ 3000 at 200×50, density-scaled) with self-propulsion `v₀` along heading `θ`, rotational diffusion `D_r` (persistence), and soft repulsion via `SpatialHash`; periodic box. Motility-induced phase separation (Péclet ≥ 80, area fraction ~0.4). Framed as bacteria (elongated glyphs by heading). Acts: dilute (no clusters) → crowded (MIPS) → dispersal: a repellent/stress cue makes them slow (`v₀` ×0.5) and tumble more (`D_r` ×6), `Pe` falls ~12×, and the clusters dissolve.
- **Motion**: swimmers move each frame; clusters coarsen over ~20 s.
- **Resize**: box rescaled; particles kept in box units, count adjusted to keep the area fraction.
- **Inks**: free swimmers by heading `Role::Phase`, clustered swimmers (≥ 5 neighbours) `Ramp(Starlight)` 200 so clusters read as solid, background black. ASCII: `.` / `o`.
- **Title**: `Bacteria crowds · swimmers jam`; captions `dilute`, `clusters`, `dispersal`.
- **Stylisation**: bacteria abstracted as self-propelled discs (no flagella, no hydrodynamics); B by framing (Physics of Life), as the research notes require.
- **M15 look** (ADR-0018; supersedes the Inks above and M14's `Aurora` rods): every swimmer is a braille rod on black, no glow, in M11's complementary pair by crowding (as M11's `molecular_dynamics` atoms): a free swimmer (≤ 2 contacts, a collision is not a crowd) `Cold` 180, one pressed by 3–4 neighbours `Cold` 230, a jammed one (≥ 5) `Hot` 240, so the packed clusters are the one warm, brightest subject (MIPS-A6). Heading is shown by the rod's direction only.
- **Acceptance**: `MIPS-A1` with `v₀ = 0` the system has no clusters (largest cluster < 2 % of particles); `MIPS-A2` at the crowded setting the largest cluster exceeds 20 % within 30 s; `MIPS-A3` a lone swimmer's mean-square displacement is ballistic at short times and diffusive at long; `MIPS-A4` the particle count never exceeds its cap; `MIPS-A5` captions follow the acts; `MIPS-A6` *(changed, M15)* a swimmer's rod ink steps up with its contacts: free (≤ 2) and pressed (3–4) rods `Cold`, jammed rods (≥ 5) `Hot` and brightest.
- **As built (batch 5)**: ≤ 2400 swimmers (not 3000: the stepping budget at 200×50), crowded area fraction 0.6 (0.5 separates only weakly with soft discs), `v₀ = 6 σ/s`, `D_r = 0.18/s` (`Pe = 100`), repulsion `k = 80/s`, 360 sub-steps/s (M14: 160, `k·h` = 0.5, the same clustering at a third of the force passes), heading as a unit vector turned by uniform kicks of the right variance. Acts 6 s dilute (area fraction 0.12) → 34 s clusters (swimmers arrive over 3 s) → 12 s dispersal. **Dispersal changed**: faster running would strengthen MIPS, so the swimmers instead slow (×0.5) and tumble more (`D_r` ×6), `Pe ≈ 8`, and the clusters evaporate. Clusters (MIPS-A1, A2) are contact graphs of swimmers pressed closer than 0.97 σ. Clustered swimmers are Starlight, brighter with more contacts. Readout `N 2264  clustered 46%`.
- **Round 2**: swimmers are ≥ 2.6 px (fewer, larger: 1085 at 120×40, cap 2400), 240 sub-steps/s; free swimmers are one cold hue (`Ramp(Ice)` rods, bright head, dim tail), clustered swimmers expose a soft glow (radius-2 halo, asinh tone) so clusters read as textured luminous bodies; `Role::Phase` is no longer used. Dilute act 6 s.
- **Round 3**: every swimmer is a braille rod (`Dots`, 1.25 σ long) in one ramp, `Ramp(Aurora)` (deep teal → lime-white): its heading picks the hue within a band and its contacts lift the band from dim teal (free, levels 74–130) to bright lime (≥ 8 contacts, 186–255) (MIPS-A6). Crowds (≥ 3 contacts) splat a radius-3 halo into a `Glow`, blurred `[1 2 1]` and toned to a dark Aurora backdrop (≤ 100) under the rods (`Dots::blit_over`), so a cluster is the same rods packed and lit, not a fill. Dilute area fraction 0.08 (was 0.12), so the clusters pop when the crowd arrives. ASCII: the rods' dot-count fallback; the glow is Unicode only.

### dicty_aggregation  (Molecules of Life, M13, M16)
- **Intent**: a lawn of starving slime-mould amoebae: waves of the signal cAMP spiral outward, each amoeba relays the signal and steps toward it, and the lawn breaks into branching streams that pour into mounds, as in the famous dark-field films.
- **Model**: `excitable` (shared `pub(crate)`): a Barkley-type two-variable excitable relay (stands in for the Martiel–Goldbeter reduction) on a capped lattice (≤ 160×80) with diffusion; cells (pool ≤ 3000) sit on the lattice, secrete cAMP when excited, and when the local cAMP rises they move up its gradient for a short burst (chemotaxis is behaviour; cell density raises local excitability, giving streaming). Spiral centres seeded by a few pacemaker cells.
- **Motion**: waves every ~7 s; cells step with each wave; streams form over ~30 s, mounds by ~50 s; reset beat.
- **Resize**: lattice regridded; cells kept in lattice units.
- **Inks** *(M15, ADR-0018: M11's roles in flat steps; supersedes the M14 look below)*: each amoeba is one braille dot in `Hot`, always drawn (the scattered lawn is the subject from the first frame), 150 on the thin lawn and 255 where the smoothed cell density (the cell glow blurred `[1 2 1]`) passes 1.2, so streams and mounds are the brightest subject; within the 2 s either side of a re-scatter the crowds drop to the lawn step; the ground is black. The cAMP wave (false colour, its rising edge only) is a 1-px `Wave` line on the `u = ½` contour (the pixels within 0.6 px of it by `|u − ½|/|∇u|` × pixels per node), 190 where fresh (`v < 0.15`) and 110 up to `v = 0.3`, with no wake. ASCII: cells `o`, crowded `@`, waves `:`.
- **Look** *(M16, GEN-R25; supersedes the M15 Inks where they disagree)*: everything is braille dots on black. Each amoeba is one dot in `Hot`, 110 on the lawn (*round 2*: was 150, so the first wave reads over it) and 255 where its 3×3-node neighbourhood holds ≥ 9 cells (streams and mounds; ≈ 4× a fresh lawn), dropping to the lawn step within 2 s of a re-scatter. A moving amoeba leaves a short trail: a `Hot` 90 dot line through its last four positions (sampled every 0.12 s, so ≈ 0.5 s long), drawn only when it moved ≥ 0.35 nodes over them, so trails show the streaming and vanish as cells stop. The cAMP wave is the `u = ½` iso-contour traced by marching squares (CORE-R105) one dot thick, drawn only on the rising edge (all four nodes of a lattice cell have `v < 0.3`), in `Cold` 255 where fresh (`v < 0.15`) and 190 otherwise: thin spiral fronts in the complementary colour. No half blocks; ASCII by dot count. *Round 2 (M16)*: the aggregate keeps moving and signalling to the reset: each pacemaker moves, when it fires, to the densest 5×5-node crowd within 10 nodes (if it holds ≥ 12 cells and 1.3× its own), as a real aggregate's centre is its pacemaker; from 20 s into the cycle the relay quickens from every 7 s to every 3.5 s and chemotaxis speeds up ×1.6, so the streams shorten into mounds; the cycle is 45 s (was 55 s).
- **Title**: `Slime amoebae · cAMP waves call`.
- **Stylisation**: time-lapse (hours in a minute: real waves come every ~6 min, here every 7 s); cAMP is invisible in reality (shown as its rising edge only); relay kinetics reduced to two variables.
