- **M14 look** (superseded by the M15 Inks above; supersedes the amber density glow, the thick cyan rings and the wake): each amoeba is one braille dot in `Ramp(Sienna)`, at 120 on the thin lawn up to 255 in the streams and mounds by the local cell density (gamma 0.9 over the toned, blurred density), fading with the loop; the ground is black. The cAMP wave is a faint 1-px anti-aliased line on the `u = ½` contour (full within 0.3 px, gone at 0.9 px of `|u − ½|/|∇u|` × pixels per node, `∇u` by central differences each drawn frame), in `Ramp(Ice)` at 70–120 by the freshness `1 − v/0.3` (the rising edge only), with no wake behind it.
- **Acceptance**: `DICT-A1` a single excitation on resting medium produces a wave followed by refractoriness (no immediate re-excitation); `DICT-A2` cells move only up-gradient during a wave; `DICT-A3` *(changed, M16)* the cell density's spatial variance rises by ≥ 3× over 40 s (streaming/aggregation; the cycle is now 45 s); `DICT-A4` cell count is conserved and within the pool; `DICT-A5` cAMP stays non-negative and finite; `DICT-A6` a wave draws only its rising edge: nothing where the cells are refractory (`v ≥ 0.3`); `DICT-A7` *(changed, M16)* at 120×40 after 20 s every drawn cell is a cell dot or trail (`Hot` at one of its three steps, > 50 of them, some crowded) or a wave front (`Cold` at one of its two steps, at least one), nothing else; and the first frame already shows over 100 lawn cells; `DICT-A8` *(new, M16)* at 120×40 at 12, 20 and 30 s every lit cell is a braille cell on a black ground (no half blocks), moving amoebae leave > 20 trail cells, and the fronts are thin threads (fewer than 4 dots per `Cold` cell on average); `DICT-A9` *(new, M16)* at 120×40, in every 5 s window from 10 s to the reset, ≥ 20 % of the cells move ≥ 1 node and a `Cold` crest is lit in at least one frame (sampled every 0.5 s).
- **As built (batch 5)**: the relay is the shared Barkley kernel (`a = 0.75, ε = 0.03`, `h = 0.5`, `τ = 0.025`, 5 tu/s) with excitability `b` from the blurred local cell density (`b = 0.01` dense → `0.42` empty, where fronts die); up to 3 pacemakers fire every 7 s; a cell steps up `∇u` at 18 nodes/s only while `u` rises at a non-refractory cell; nodes with ≥ 8 cells take no more (mound packing). The lawn is re-scattered every 70 s (2 s fade). The cAMP wash is brightest at the rising front. No captions.
- **Round 2**: the lawn starts with 1–2 spiral seeds (broken fronts, painted with the shared `excitable::paint_front`, moved there from `belousov_zhabotinsky`); cells also drift up the density gradient (adhesion, 10 nodes/s per unit excitability gradient) when not chemotaxing, which makes streams coherent; chemotaxis 10 nodes/s; up to 14 cells per node. Streams by ≈ 20 s, mounds by ≈ 40 s; the cycle is 55 s. Cells render as a soft glow; the cAMP front wash reaches `Cobalt` ≈ 170.
- **Round 3**: the classic dark-field look. Cells are a smoothed density (the cell glow blurred `[1 2 1]`) in dim amber, `Ramp(Ember)` ≤ 200 through a gamma of 1.4, so the fresh lawn (0.24 cells per node, was 0.3) is quiet and the streams and mounds glow; bare ground stays black. The cAMP wave draws only its rising edge (`u·(1 − v/0.3)` above 0.2, DICT-A6) as a thin cyan-white `Ramp(Ice)` ring (150–255), with a faint wake (`v` < 0.55, levels ≤ 80) on bare ground. ASCII: cells `.`/`o`/`@` by level, waves `:`.

### molecular_motors  (Life from Matter, M13, M17)
- **Intent**: two molecular machines fuelled by ATP: a kinesin motor walks hand over hand along a microtubule track, one 8 nm step per ATP, pausing between steps and jiggling in thermal noise; then ATP synthase, a rotary motor, turns in 120° steps as protons flow.
- **Model**: kinesin: two heads on a 1-D lattice of binding sites (8 nm period); dwell times exponential with a Michaelis–Menten rate `k = k_cat[ATP]/(K_M + [ATP])` ([ATP] swept on a `Timeline`); during a step the free head diffuses (Langevin, overdamped, in a tethered potential) and binds forward (biased by the neck-linker docking); cargo (a bead) on a spring behind. ATP synthase act: F1 rotor angle advancing in 120° steps each split into 80° + 40° substeps with exponential dwells, driven by a proton-flux rate; c-ring drawn as a wheel.
- **Motion**: kinesin steps at a rate set by [ATP]; thermal jiggle continuously; act 2 rotor steps.
- **Resize**: view scaled to the track; state in nm.
- **Inks** *(M15, ADR-0018: M11's roles, flat fills and 1-px lines, no shading; supersedes the M14 look below)*: kinesin `Particle` (a GFP-labelled look): heads flat discs (radius 3.2 nm-scaled; bound 215, free 140), the second head `Wave` so hand-over-hand stepping reads (false colour: the heads are identical), tether 1-px lines 110, footprints flat discs 130 for their first 40 % of 5 s, then 70; the microtubule `Structure`, its two walls 1-px lines at 90 (the far wall only when it lies above the bottom art row, so it never reads as a frame rule) and one pixel per 4 nm monomer on the track row (β 170, α 110); the cargo vesicle its membrane only, a 1-px `Structure` 150 ring; ATP molecules `Sun` 200 points (above the track). ATP synthase: the α₃β₃ ring as 1-px rings, β `Hot` 200 and α `Cold` 170 (a complementary pair, false colour); γ a flat `Accent` 240 disc with its actin filament a 1–2-px `Accent` line and, while turning, a 1-px `Accent` 100 ghost; substep ticks `Dim` (160 every 120°, 100 between); released ATP `Sun` 200. ASCII: sprites.
- **Title**: `Motor proteins · fuelled by ATP`; captions `kinesin walks`, `synthase turns`.
- **Stylisation**: kinesin dwells slowed 40× (the readout gives the real rate); the heads and rotor are cartoons; the stepping statistics are real in form. The synthase view and its 80° + 40° substeps are from F1 running as an ATP-driven motor (Noji et al. 1997, Yasuda et al. 2001); in the cell the Fo proton flow drives it the other way to make ATP, as shown here, so γ turns clockwise seen from the membrane (Fo) side (the synthesis sense).
- **M14 look** (superseded by the M15 Inks above; supersedes the ball-row microtubule and the shaded cargo): the microtubule is a thin lattice, its two walls 1-px `Starlight` lines (60 and 40) with one pixel per 4 nm monomer on the track row (β 110, α 70); the cargo vesicle is only its membrane, a thin grey ring, the lumen black; kinesin heads are smaller muted `Aurora` discs (radius 3.2 nm-scaled, bound 170, free 130, with a small specular glint on the lit side), the tether `Aurora` 90, footprints fade from 100, ATP molecules `Sulfur` 110 and only above the track.
- **Acceptance**: `KIN-A1` the mean dwell time matches `1/k` at two [ATP] values within 10 % over 2000 samples; `KIN-A2` steps are exactly 8 nm and heads alternate (hand over hand); `KIN-A3` the rotor's angle only increases, in 120° steps with 80°/40° substeps; `KIN-A4` the Langevin jiggle has zero mean drift when bound; `KIN-A5` captions follow the acts; `KIN-A6` γ turns clockwise on screen, viewed from the Fo side; `KIN-A7` every kinesin step leaves one footprint at the moved head's new site (Round 3); ~~KIN-A8~~ *(superseded by the M17 look, KIN-A9)* *(new, M15)* at 120×40, at 5 s, 20 s and 6 s into the synthase act, every lit pixel carries one of the scene's eighteen flat inks.
- **As built (batch 5)**: dwells slowed 40× (`k_cat = 100/s`, `K_M = 60 µM`; `[ATP]` 1000 → 60 → 15 µM over the 24 s kinesin act); the head's swing is overdamped Langevin in a harmonic pull to the next site (display time). ATP synthase is seen from above as in Noji et al. (1997): the α₃β₃ ring, γ and an actin filament; mean dwells 0.45 s (binding, before 80°) and 0.25 s (catalytic, before 40°); each 120° turn releases an ATP. Readouts: `ATP 1000 uM   94 steps/s`, then `ATP made N`.
- **Round 2**: larger view (motor heads 4 nm, vesicle 10 nm radius at `min(w/60, h/32)` px per nm); a vesicle with a bright membrane rim and a mottled lumen (`rng::value_noise`); the microtubule as three staggered protofilaments with per-dimer shade variation; a lazy camera that pans only once the motor strays from the centre, so steps show; ATP synthase's filament drawn thicker with a motion-blur ghost during each substep.
- **Round 3**: a softer microtubule: a dim tube body lit from above fills the gaps, and the tubulin beads are antialiased and low in contrast (α 100, β 132); each site a head binds to leaves a faint `Ramp(Aurora)` footprint on the track that fades over 5 s (the last 8 kept, KIN-A7); the cargo is a 9 nm vesicle with one bright cool bilayer ring (`Ramp(Ice)`), a grainy translucent lumen (two octaves of value noise) and a soft highlight; the tether, heads, α₃β₃ ring and synthase stalk are antialiased (coverage-blended discs and distance-to-segment strokes). γ's screen direction is one function, `gamma_dir`, with the angle growing clockwise seen from the membrane side (KIN-A6). (acceptance IDs added to the Acceptance line).
- **Look** *(M17, GEN-R26, cloud heads + step arches; supersedes the flat heads, ring outlines and Inks above)*: *kinesin act*: the two heads are small Gaussian stipple clouds (σ ≈ 0.95 nm, about 6×3 cells at 200×50; dot density highest at the head centre, thresholds keyed to the offset from the head so a cloud moves rigidly), drawn over everything else, the microtubule-bound head `Cold` and the swinging head `Hot`, as the two signs of an orbital lobe; neck linkers and the coiled-coil stalk are thin `Star` threads (the bright 1-px structure: `Hot`/`Cold` cannot reach the p99 luma floor of 0.6); the cargo vesicle is a hollow stipple shell (dots only in an annulus, sparse `Structure`, its tether a thread); the microtubule is a fine braille lattice of α/β tubulin monomers (short 1-dot dashes with gaps in three staggered protofilament rows, β `Cold` under each 8 nm binding site, α `Structure`), and a site a head has just bound glows brighter for 5 s (KIN-A7); the camera holds still while the motor walks across the view and re-centres in short eased pans, and the vesicle stays fully on screen; every step leaves the swinging head's arched path (low-passed over 30 ms, sampled at 80 Hz) as a thread that steps down through three levels and is gone after 2 s, `Sun` while the neck linker pulls fast and `Hot` as it settles, so the walk writes a chain of arches; ATP molecules are single `Sun` dots diffusing. *Synthase act*: α₃β₃ six teardrop stipple lobes pointed at a clear centre, alternating `Hot` (α) and `Cold` (β); the c-ring a stippled annulus whose density peaks on its 10 c-subunits, turning with γ; γ a short `Accent` thread inside the ring (fading ghosts while it turns); protons `Cold` dots crossing the a-subunit's two `Dim` half-channels in the gap between the lowest lobes, one per 36° of rotation. No flat discs, no box-drawing outlines.
- **Acceptance** *(M17)*: `KIN-A9` no half-block cells in either act; `KIN-A10` the bound head's dots are `Cold` and the swinging head's `Hot` throughout a step; `KIN-A11` during a step lit arch dots lie above the track between the two binding sites, and 3 s after stepping stops the arch trace is gone; `KIN-A12` the cargo is hollow: no lit dots within 60 % of its radius.

### hp_protein_folding  (Life from Matter, M13, M16)
- **Intent**: why a protein folds: a chain of oily (hydrophobic, H) and water-loving (polar, P) residues thrashes as a loose coil while hot, then, as it cools, collapses and tucks its oily residues into a core away from the water, leaving the polar ones on the surface.
- **Model**: the 2-D square-lattice HP model (Lau & Dill 1989): a self-avoiding chain of 48 residues, energy `E = −(number of H–H contacts)` between lattice neighbours that are not chain neighbours. The sequence is the 48-residue benchmark `PPHPPHHPPHHPPPPPHHHHHHHHHHPPPPPPHHPPHHPPHPPHHHHH` (best known 2-D fold: 23 contacts). Metropolis Monte Carlo (P: statistical mechanics) with end, corner, crankshaft and pull moves (Lesh et al. 2003); each attempt moves the chain, is checked for self-avoidance on an occupancy grid, and is accepted with probability `min(1, e^(−ΔE/T))`. Simulated annealing on a looping `Timeline` (C: the hydrophobic effect as a contact energy; B: a protein).
- **Motion**: 40 000 attempted moves per simulated second at `T ≤ 1.6`, fewer when hotter (`× (1.6/T)²`, ≥ 6 %), so the hot coil's beads stay distinct on screen; beads ease towards their lattice sites (50 ms), so the chain writhes smoothly; the camera follows the chain's bounding box closely and zooms in slowly (out quickly). Acts: **unfolded** (10 s, `T = 4`), **collapse** (16 s, `T` from 1.4, just above the coil–globule transition, cooled geometrically to 0.15; it reaches 20–21 contacts for seeds 1–6, 23 is the best known), **folded core** (14 s, `T = 0.15`); then the chain is heated again and the loop repeats.
- **Resize**: the chain lives in lattice units and is untouched; the camera (centre and pixels per lattice step) eases to frame the chain's bounding box, capped so a compact fold fills most of the height.
- **Inks**: H residues `Ramp(Sulfur)` gold cores (brighter the more H neighbours they touch: buried H glows) with a warm `Role::Blackbody` halo; P residues `Ramp(Cobalt)` cores with a faint `Ramp(Cobalt)` hydration halo; the backbone a smooth braille line `Ramp(Starlight)` 140 that stops at the beads; the water a faint `Ramp(Cobalt)` ≤ 22 shimmer (two travelling interference patterns from per-frame row/column sine tables). ASCII: H `@`, P `o`, backbone by dot count, water blank.
- **Look** *(M16, GEN-R25; supersedes the M15 look below)*: two panels, all braille dots on black. Left (62 % of the width; all of it below 60×12 cells, where the panel is dropped): the chain, framed by the same lazy camera (now in dot units, ≥ 1 dot per lattice step), its backbone a uniform Catmull-Rom spline through the beads one dot thick in `Structure` 140 (*round 2*: was 110); residues small braille discs (radius 0.16 lattice steps, 0.5–2.5 dots) in M11's pair: H `Hot` 150/190/255 by H·H contacts (0, 1, ≥ 2; *round 2*: the lowest step was 130, raised above the backbone's level so a bead's cell keeps its colour), P `Cold` 170; the camera frames the chain one cell clear of the title row; each H–H contact a faint dotted `Accent` 90 link (every other dot, stopping short of the beads), drawn only between settled beads (each within 0.35 steps of its site), so easing beads never draw links across the chain. Right (x 66–96 %, y 16–84 % of the art height): the energy `E(t)` over the current loop as a dot line, sampled in 80 equal slices (each the mean energy over its 0.5 s, so the Metropolis flicker averages out), coloured by the temperature of its slice: `Sun` 200 in the hot coil, `Accent` 200 while cooling above `T = 0.5`, `Wave` 230 cold; its head a `Star` dot; a dotted `Dim` 90 energy axis from 0 (top) to −24, a dashed `Dim` floor at the best known −23, and the label `energy` above the axis. The trace restarts with each loop, so the viewer watches it descend the folding funnel. No half blocks.
- **Title**: `Protein folding · oil hides away`; captions `unfolded`, `collapse`, `folded core`. Readout right-aligned on row `h − 1`: `HH contacts N` (or `N`), the current H–H contact count.
- **Stylisation**: a 2-D lattice model, not a real protein: one bead per residue, only an H–H contact energy, no side chains, solvent or hydrogen bonds; Monte Carlo moves are not dynamics, and time and temperature are in model units; the annealing schedule is chosen for the picture and loops; the fold found is a low-energy one, not necessarily the best known.
- **M15 look** (ADR-0018; supersedes the Inks above and M14's `Blackbody`/grey residues): residues are regular 2 × 2-pixel dots on black in M11's complementary pair: H (oily) `Hot`, 130 with no H·H contact, 190 with one, 255 with two or more (buried cores read hot); P (water-loving) `Cold` 170; the chain is a 1-dot `Structure` 150 braille line between them. No water shimmer.
- **Acceptance**: `HPF-A1` every move keeps the chain connected (consecutive residues one lattice step apart) and self-avoiding, and the cached energy equals the energy recomputed from scratch; `HPF-A2` at `T = 0` the energy never rises, while at high `T` uphill moves are accepted; `HPF-A3` annealing collapses the chain: at the end of the collapse act the radius of gyration of the H residues (the core) is < 60 % of the unfolded act's mean and the chain has ≥ 14 H–H contacts; `HPF-A4` in the folded act H residues face the water less than P residues (mean free lattice neighbours per H < per P); `HPF-A5` captions follow the acts; `HPF-A6` *(changed, M16)* at 120×40 after 30 s every lit cell (labels aside) is a braille cell on black: an H (`Hot`, one of three steps) or P (`Cold`) residue, the backbone or a contact link (left of the panel), or the trace, its head or axis (right of the chain's region); both residue kinds show, and the folded core has H at the top step; `HPF-A7` *(new, M16)* each trace sample is the mean energy over its slice of the loop (between the lowest and highest energy seen in it); over a loop the trace averages above −6 in the unfolded act and at or below −14 over the folded act's last 8 s minus 1 s; its inks follow the temperature (hot, cooling, cold); a new loop starts a fresh trace; and its head is drawn right of the chain.
